# Development

How the workspace is laid out, which checks guard it, and how CI runs them. Read [CLAUDE.md](../CLAUDE.md), [PROVENANCE.md](../PROVENANCE.md) and [CONTRIBUTING.md](../CONTRIBUTING.md) first.

## Workspace

Rust stable (pinned in `rust-toolchain.toml`), one Cargo workspace. Package names are prefixed `glasnost-`.

| Crate | Path | Filled in | Role |
|---|---|---|---|
| `fmt` | `crates/fmt` | M0 scaffolding, M1 parsers | File-format parsers, each with a fixture writer; indexed-image model; synthetic generators; content sniffing |
| `vfs` | `crates/vfs` | M1 | Archive mounting (nested archives included), the game's search order (S-0005), lazy reads |
| `ini` | `crates/ini` | M1 | INI reader with the original's parse semantics (S-0001 to S-0003), for the importer only |
| `convert` | `crates/convert` | M1–M2 | Import: INI layers → our rules tree (S-0004 to S-0007), `coverage.csv`; maps in M2 |
| `rules` | `crates/deterministic/rules` | M0 JSONC reader, M1 schema | Our rules format (schema 1), resolve pipeline, `rules explain`, `sim_rules_hash` |
| `map` | `crates/deterministic/map` | M2–M3 | Typed map for the simulation |
| `sim` | `crates/deterministic/sim` | M3–M7 | The deterministic simulation |
| `ai` | `crates/deterministic/ai` | M5+ | Our own opponent; produces Orders only |
| `render` | `crates/render` | M0 quad, M1+ | wgpu renderer: WebGL2 core path, WebGPU where available |
| `audio` | `crates/audio` | M1 preview, M5b | Sample bank (IDX/BAG, AUD, WAVE), integer software mixer, WebAudio and native outputs, sound events for the game client; the viewer's previews |
| `ui` | `crates/ui` | M5 | Game UI |
| `net` | `crates/net` | M9 | Lockstep networking (the relay server gets its own crate in M9) |
| `app` | `crates/app` | M0 demo, M1 viewer | winit shell shared by native and web; the asset viewer |
| `platform-native` | `crates/platform-native` | M0 | Native binary `glasnost` |
| `platform-web` | `crates/platform-web` | M0 | wasm entry point |
| `tools` | `crates/tools` | M0 fixtures, M1 local lane, M3 runner | `glasnost-fixtures`, `glasnost-local-lane`, `glasnost-headless`; later sync-diff |
| `sim-gate` | `crates/sim-gate` | M3 | The determinism gate: per-tick state hashes of a synthetic replay, native and wasm32 |
| `xtask` | `xtask` | M0 | Repository checks and build helpers (`cargo xtask help`) |

`app` is not in PLAN.md section 4.2: it holds the event loop and rendering setup that both platform crates share, so native and web run the same code.

### Simulation crates

Every crate under `crates/deterministic/` is a simulation crate. The checks find them by location, so a new crate placed there is covered automatically. Simulation crates:

- carry their own `[lints]` table (identical across them) instead of the workspace one;
- pick up `crates/deterministic/clippy.toml`, which clippy finds by searching upwards from each crate;
- may depend only on each other and on external crates outside the deny list in `xtask/src/deps.rs`.

## Checks

`cargo xtask check` runs all of the following; each also runs on its own (`cargo xtask deps`, etc.).

### Dependency rule (`deps`)

The `sim` crate (and every other simulation crate) must not depend, directly or transitively, on any non-simulation workspace crate (`render`, `audio`, `net`, `fmt`, `ini`, `convert`, `vfs`, `ui`, `app`, the platform crates, `tools`). It also must not reach graphics, browser bindings, host randomness, hash-seeded maps, threads or clocks (`wgpu`, `winit`, `web-sys`, `getrandom`, `rand`, `ahash`, `rayon`, `tokio`, `chrono`, ...). The check reads `cargo metadata` for every target platform and counts normal and build dependencies. Dev-dependencies don't count. The rule also runs as a unit test in `xtask`, so plain `cargo test` enforces it.

### Determinism (`determinism`, `canary`, clippy)

- **Clippy** bans `f32`/`f64`, `HashMap`/`HashSet`, host-seeded hashers, `Instant`/`SystemTime`, threads and locks, file and network I/O, the environment, and printing macros. It also denies float arithmetic, float comparison, lossy float literals and precision-losing casts. See `crates/deterministic/clippy.toml` and the lint table in each simulation crate's `Cargo.toml`.
- **Float-literal scan.** `let x = 1.5;` uses no float type by name, so clippy alone misses it. `cargo xtask determinism` rejects float literals in simulation code and checks that the simulation lint tables are identical.
- **Canary.** `cargo xtask canary` builds a throwaway crate inside `crates/deterministic/` with one violation per configured entry and requires every expected diagnostic. A misspelt or silently ignored entry fails here instead of letting a float through.
- The narrow float exception from PLAN.md 4.5 (a `Real` wrapper) will be allowed site by site with `#[allow]`, a spec citation and an allowlist entry when a spec requires it.

### Citations (`cite`)

In non-test code of the simulation crates:

- every numeric literal other than `0` and `1`, and
- every `const` and `static` item

must be covered by `// spec: S-xxxx` or `// measure: M-xxxx`. The comment can sit on the same line, or in the comment and attribute block directly above (or opening) an enclosing item, statement, field, variant or match arm. A blank line ends the block.

```rust
// spec: S-0042
const RELOAD_RESETS_ON_MOVE: bool = true;

fn reload_ticks(base: u32) -> u32 {
    base * 15 // measure: M-0007
}
```

- `// nocite: <reason>` in the same places marks a literal that is not behaviour, such as a buffer size of our own design. The reason is required.
- `xtask/citation-allowlist.txt` exempts whole files, again with a reason. It holds the JSONC parser, whose literals are syntax. Stale entries fail the check.
- Every `S-`, `M-` and `Q-` ID mentioned in `crates/**/*.rs` and `data/**/*.jsonc` must exist as a file in `spec/`, `measurements/` or `spec/requests/`, and a cited spec must not be retired.

`spec/` is empty at M0, so the simulation crates hold no behavioural constants yet. Anything that needs one waits for its spec or goes to `spec/requests/`.

### No stock numbers (`stock-guard`)

Stock values can't be recognised without knowing them, and this repository must never know them. So the guard enforces the structure that keeps them out. It covers every file git tracks or would add:

1. **Game-format files** (`.mix`, `.shp`, `.vxl`, `.pal`, `.csf`, `.ini`, `.map`, ... by extension) may only live in fixture directories:
   - In `tests/fixtures/synthetic/` they must match, byte for byte, what our writers generate (`cargo run -p glasnost-tools --bin glasnost-fixtures`), and `FIXTURES.manifest` must be current.
   - In any other `tests/fixtures/` directory they must be text with a `glasnost-synthetic:` line near the top.
2. **Synthetic data** (`data/synthetic/**`) starts with `SPDX-License-Identifier: CC0-1.0`, followed by a `glasnost-synthetic:` line.
3. **Measurements:** nothing under `measurements/local/` is tracked. Records with `value-visibility: local` show no numbers in the value and spread columns of their Result table.
4. **Paths:** no install paths (Steam libraries, Windows program folders, Westwood or EA folders) or home-directory paths in text files. Use `~` or a placeholder. `LOCAL.md` and `.claude/settings.local.json` stay untracked.
5. **Size:** no file over 1 MiB.

Tests that need the user's real install run in a local-only lane (opt-in, never in CI; see "Local-only lane" below); their inputs and outputs are git-ignored. A local comparison of committed synthetic data against the user's imported values needs the converter's (field, value) output. It arrives with the converter in M1 and will run only on the developer's machine.

### Synthetic ruleset

`data/synthetic/ruleset/` is a small, invented ruleset in our JSONC format (CC0). It exercises the format's shape:

- objects keyed by instance name, `inherits` lists and `^abstract` templates;
- `{"+": [...]}` set operations;
- integers in native units, and decimals as quoted strings with a unit.

`crates/deterministic/rules/tests/synthetic_ruleset.rs` checks that every file parses (so every number is an integer), carries the header and is listed in `manifest.jsonc`, and runs the layers through the resolve pipeline: inheritance, set operations, schema 1 (strict, `deny_unknown_fields`), validation, ID interning, the rules hash and `rules explain`. It also checks that `data/schema/ruleset.schema.json`, the JSON Schema generated from the schema types with schemars, is current (`GLASNOST_UPDATE_SCHEMA=1` rewrites it).

### Fixture writers

`glasnost_fmt::fixture` defines `FixtureWriter` (one synthetic file), the registry, the manifest and `check_round_trip` (encode, decode, compare, re-encode). Each format module brings its writers and registers them. `glasnost-fixtures` writes the registry's output to `tests/fixtures/synthetic/`, and the stock guard checks the committed copy byte for byte: after changing a writer, regenerate with `cargo run -p glasnost-tools --bin glasnost-fixtures` and commit the result.

Encrypted MIX fixtures use a key pair of our own (`RsaKeyPair::synthetic`), so they parse only with that key's public half, not with the Westwood key.

### Hostile input and fuzzing

Every parser in `fmt` is panic-free on hostile input: reads are bounds-checked, input-driven arithmetic is checked, allocations are bounded by the input or by `fmt::limits`, and the crate denies `unwrap`, `expect` and `panic` outside tests. `fmt::hostile::run` drives one parser and all its decoders over arbitrary bytes; `ini::hostile::run` reads a document and every stored value in every value form; `rules::hostile::run` parses JSONC, writes it back and resolves it. Two users each:

- `cargo test -p glasnost-fmt --test hostile`, `-p glasnost-ini --test hostile` and `-p glasnost-rules --test hostile` (stable, in CI): synthetic inputs, their prefixes and mutated copies.
- `fuzz/` (cargo-fuzz, nightly, outside the workspace and CI): one target per parser, plus `ini` and `jsonc`; see [fuzz/README.md](../fuzz/README.md).

### Local-only lane

`GLASNOST_GAME_DIR=<install> cargo run --release -p glasnost-tools --bin glasnost-local-lane` mounts a real install and prints counts only: MIX headers decrypted, SHP files and frames (all archives and `conqmd.mix`), VXL files and limbs under `ra2.mix`/`ra2md.mix`, IDX samples, AUD, TMP, PAL, PCX, CSF, HVA and map packs, each by error kind. `cargo test -p glasnost-tools --test local_lane` asserts 100 % on the M1 checks when the variable is set and skips otherwise; CI never sets it. The lane opens only the MIX containers at the top of the folder (`.mix`, `.mmx`, `.yro`); every other file there is counted and never read. It classifies entries with the permissive sniffer (`fmt::sniff`) and counts the strict parsers against that, so a file the sniffer calls SHP but the parser rejects shows up as a failure.

The lane also builds the game's search order (S-0005) and checks the stock outcome (the expansion archive's rules file is the one used, every required archive resolves), imports the rules and art (temperate theater), checks that the result resolves through the rules pipeline, and counts types per kind, the `sim` records of weapons, projectiles, warheads and animations, importer warnings (with the reference-field warnings by kind), art found per kind and coverage outcomes. The binary writes `coverage.csv` (key names and outcomes, no values) to `local-lane/` in the current directory, which git ignores; never commit it.

`cargo test --release -p glasnost-app snapshot -- --ignored` (with the same variable) writes viewer screenshots of a real install to the temp directory, including one of the rules view. Never commit them.

The map lane (M2): the local lane above also decodes every map it finds (`maps decoded (S-0009), no panic`, with importer warnings by kind). `GLASNOST_GAME_DIR=<install> cargo test --release -p glasnost-app --test map_lane -- --nocapture` finds every map by content, loads each with its rules (the map as the map layer), builds its scene, times the build, a CPU redraw of a 1280×720 view and 120 scrolling GPU redraws, counts provisional notes and dropped objects, and writes one screenshot per theater (plus a minimap and a close-up of the first unit) to `local-lane/`. `GLASNOST_MAP_LIMIT=n` limits the maps built, `GLASNOST_MAP_SHOTS=n` the screenshots per theater, and `WGPU_BACKEND=gl` times the GL backend.

The relation lane (M7b step 4b): `GLASNOST_GAME_DIR=<your install> cargo test --release -p glasnost-tools --test relation_lane -- --ignored --nocapture --test-threads=1` runs one test for each interaction pair of [pairs-m7a.md](m7-claims/pairs-m7a.md) and [pairs-m7b.md](m7-claims/pairs-m7b.md) on the installed rules (`arena::reference_rules`). Each test finds its types by their flags, never by name, asserts only the direction of the relation, and prints `relation <test>: PASS`, `PASS (<what was not formed>)` or `SKIP <reason>`, with no type names and no values. Without the variable every test prints `SKIP no install`; CI never runs the lane. The results of the last run are in pairs-m7b.md.

### Provisional placeholders

Behaviour that needs a spec stays out of code; where a spec leaves a gap, the code carries an explicit placeholder tied to a spec request (see the table in "Import" below).

Q-0002 is answered by S-0005: `vfs::game` builds the game's search order by name (Table 1), honours loose files for the archives it mounts, finds members by the signed index bisection and mounts the theater and side groups per game. Loose *data* files are an opt-in mod layer (PLAN.md 4.3); with it off, a loose file that would have won is reported and never read. Archives outside the search order (for example the base game's map archives) stay browsable in the viewer but never answer a lookup.

Q-0001 and Q-0003 are answered by S-0103: `render::palette` loads palette files (×4 in 8-bit arithmetic), keeps the theater palettes in force, builds house-colour palettes from colour definitions and picks each object's palette. Shading by light (S-0104) is not applied yet; the viewer shows unshaded palettes.

## Import

The importer turns the user's install into our rules format (PLAN.md 4.3, 4.6):

1. `vfs` mounts the folder for browsing and builds the game's search order (S-0005). Loose data files are an opt-in mod layer (`GameOptions::mod_layer`, off by default, PLAN.md 4.3); with it off, a loose file that would have won is reported and never read.
2. `convert::inputs` resolves the base rules, a language rules file with a valid digest, the game-mode file (the one the user names, or for a skirmish the file of the mode `convert::modes` picks from the mode list: S-0006 claim 13's sections, mode numbers, items, lobby order and remembered-mode rule, `ImportOptions::mode`) and the map; it reports the databases without which the original refuses to start.
3. `ini` reads each file (S-0001 to S-0003) with importer warnings. `ini::Document::layer` gives S-0002's layered load (claims 9 to 12): each line's section and key lookups follow the name-table model (working order, ordered flag, remembered occurrence, peak count and forgetting peaks, `ini::table`), every table starts with nothing remembered (claim 12), lookups after the load answer as the first lookup after it would, and the importer warns about lines that replace or write into a differently named entry or section by a shared hash and about loads that change a table holding a repeated hash. With hash identity off, hashes compare as (hash, name) pairs. No importer path makes a layered load yet (S-0002 claim 6 has only campaign missions use one).
4. `convert::layers` applies the rules layers of one game (S-0004, S-0006): registration in list order with the rank rule for references, sides, tiberiums, per-key layering with its exceptions (S-0006 claims 3 and 5: the "not given" values, and the resets of countries, warheads, particle systems, particles, the difficulty sections and the twelve land-type sections; the `[Powerups]` crate-effect resets and the voxel-animation resets are not applied yet, because no crate or voxel-animation spec reads those values and the crate entries' item form is not specified), colour definitions (S-0103 claim 5) and the colour-adjustment table. A reset is recorded as a reset, not as a value: no reader takes the difficulty sections' repair- and build-delay resets (0.02 and 0.03), whose unit and use S-0006 Constants leave unresolved, so the importer writes no constant for them.
   Types are created by reference as S-0004 claim 13 says, from the tables in `convert::refs` (one per section kind and per game-wide section that names types). A field takes effect when its type reads its section (or its game-wide section is read); single fields are cut to their text limit (127 bytes; 63 for `HoldsWhat`, 31 for `NextParticle` and `SpawnsParticle`, 19 for `AttachedParticleSystem`, or 24 with `long_name_duplicates` off) and list fields create their items in item order; `none` and `<none>` name no type except in the four particle fields of claim 2. Find-only fields (prerequisites, country lists, `ShareSource`, the building superweapon fields, which warn for an unknown name) create nothing. The weapon fields follow claim 13's weapon-slot table, decided by `TurretCount`, `WeaponCount` and `ClearAllWeapons` as the type holds them once the section is read (`art::int_as_of`, the same reading `convert::sim` uses for the slots), in the table's index order. `ToOverlay` and the `Trailer` of aircraft and projectiles are read from an art section while the type reads its rules section (claim 14): for buildings and aircraft the one named by the image in force at that read, with the snow suffix of S-0007 claims 9 and 10 (`art::ImageWalk`, shared with the art lookup; no suffix for voxel aircraft); for projectiles only the one named by that read's non-empty rules `Image`, unsuffixed, with no fallback to the type name. Animation fields come from the animation's art section. New types fall into the rows of claim 4: `[AI]` then `[General]` before the passes (read in the layer), the passes in rank order (a new type is read in the layer if its kind's rank is at least the creating pass's), then `[CrateRules]` (wood crate, crate, water crate), `[CombatDamage]`, `[Radiation]`, `[AudioVisual]` (`Smoke` read for two fields) and `[SpecialWeapons]`, the second reading of every warhead in a layer with `[SpecialWeapons]` (row 5) and the tiberium list (row 6), none of which are read in the layer. With `unlisted_reference_unread` off, every type created by reference reads the creating layer. After the last layer, `GasCloudSys` is created if missing (claim 3); it reads no section. Within one section, keys that create types of one kind take effect in the order of the `convert::refs` tables (claim 4 leaves that order to us). A long name (claim 8) that would make a type of its own kind create copies of itself without end is stopped (claim 15, a departure, no switch): a reference requesting a kind and name, compared without regard to case, already used to create the referring type or an ancestor in this layer creates nothing, with a warning. Numbered weapon fields above 18 register weapons up to `WeaponCount` (claim 16); only the first 18 fill slots (S-0308). Two creations after the rules layers are not implemented, so `terrain_objects` and `animations` stay listed in the import record's `incomplete_registries`: the terrain objects a map's placements name and the animations the theater control file's tile animations name (S-0004 claim 3).
5. `convert::art` looks up every type's art for one theater (S-0007), with the game's per-game archives mounted. A building's eight other sprites follow claim 11's later-read table (a read whose art section does not set a name, or sets it empty, keeps the sprite). Weapons, warheads and tiberiums have no art files of their own (claim 32). Not implemented yet, because nothing draws them before M6: the art of projectiles, particles, particle systems and voxel animations (claims 26 to 30). The superweapon sidebar icon (claim 31) is read by the game client (`session::read_icons`), not here.
6. `convert::emit` writes a schema-1 tree: names are byte-exact (bytes 128 to 255 map to U+F780 to U+F7FF), keys no spec maps yet stay as INI text, values the layering computes (light values in thousandths, S-0104 claim 12) replace their text (`derived`, `ini_globals_derived`), and every value carries its INI history for `rules explain`. Import errors cover the conditions under which the original would not start, and loose names that differ only in letter case (S-0005 Edge cases).
7. `convert::sim` writes the typed `sim` section (`rules::sim`): the simulation fields of the rules and art data, each written only when the layered data gives it, folded over its history (values a form cannot read and "not given" values keep the held value), with decimals as text in our grammar that reads as S-0003 reads the stored text (a test cross-checks `num::decimal` against `ini::value::decimal`). Land-type rows are the layered values of the twelve land-type sections, whose omitted keys the layering resets (S-0211 claim 2, S-0006 claim 5, `quirks.override_resets_keys`), the anti-air rate follows S-0215 claim 21, locomotion classes S-0211 claim 3, the locomotor field S-0207 claim 14 (a class identifier of table T3; another form or an unknown identifier keeps the locomotor in force, with a warning), and the art fields (infantry sequences, fire frames, `Crawls`, building foundations, overlay `has_art`, an overlay's `DamageLevels` from its image section, S-0210 claim 13) are rebuilt once the art lookup has run. Animation types take the fields their creation draws read from their art sections (`RandomRate`, the bouncing flags, the chain reaction and the middle-event effects with `SpawnsParticle` and `NumParticles`, S-0109 and S-0202 Import mapping, and μ from the art lookup, S-0109 claim 25); particle types take `BehavesLike`, `MaxEC` (a zero value warns unless the behaviour is railgun), the colour count of the last section's `ColorList` and the starting colours (S-0202 Import mapping). Type-name lists and references (a type's `Owner`, `[General] BaseUnit`, the gate and wall tower types of S-0112, an overlay's cell animation `CellAnim`, S-0009 claim 12, whose `none` and `<none>` clear it) are resolved to type keys by S-0003 claim 14's comparison, unknown names dropped with a warning; an overlay's `has_art` is "artwork or a cell animation" (S-0009 claim 12); the game's tech level (`[MultiplayerDialogSettings] TechLevel`), the maximum player count of the unused-house cleanup (`[Maximums] Players`, S-0009 claim 28) and the early phase's passive field (`multiplay_passive_base`, S-0009 claim 33) take the base rules' value only. A terrain type's `Strength` follows S-0102 claim 23: unset until a layer gives it, after each layer holding its section an unset strength takes `[General] TreeStrength` as it stands then (25 when absent), −1 unsets it (`quirks.tree_strength_fixed_at_first_section`); a type left unset is written without a strength, which the simulation reads as −1 (S-0009 claim 42).
   The combat fields (M4, `convert::sim::combat`, S-0301 to S-0315) follow each combat spec's Import mapping: weapons (rate of fire, burst, speed, the lepton-form ranges as decimal text, the effect and choice flags), projectiles (flags, acceleration, arm time, cluster, art `Voxel`, and `Trailer` and `SpawnDelay` from the art section of each read's non-empty rules `Image`, S-0007 claim 33; the rules keys have no effect), warheads (`Verses` as eleven exact decimal expansions of S-0003 claim 13's doubles, spread, falloff, the damage rows, debris), the shared `combat` record of infantry, vehicle, aircraft and building types (armour class by S-0305's words, ammunition and reload, immunities, debris and death fields, targeting flags, the five threat coefficients, art muzzle positions and turret offset), the weapon slots of S-0308 claim 1 (both forms, with the clear-weapons flag applied at each reading of the section), the vehicle burst delays and firing frames, the building art fields (aim shift, height, voxel turret, pixel offsets, delayed fire), country factors and veteran lists, the building `Cost` (S-0311 F1, S-0314 claim 5), the three difficulty rows (`sim.difficulty`) and the game-wide combat values. Weapon `Speed`, the ranges, `AirRangeBonus`, `BallisticScatter` and `FireSupress` given as −1 count as not given. Type names in the new fields resolve to type keys where a type of that name exists and keep the name as written otherwise (a field S-0004 claim 13 does not list as creating, or a name that claim 8 duplicates); every registered weapon, projectile, warhead and animation gets a `sim` record, empty for one that read no section (it keeps the engine defaults of its kind); fields the schema documents as type keys (veteran lists, `HarvesterUnit`, the crew types) drop unknown names with a warning. The importer warns for the bad-data cases of the combat specs' Edge cases. `Decimal::value` reads text at 24 bits (S-0003 claim 6), so a versus value that is not exactly a single needs a 53-bit reading of its text (`num::decimal::nearest_double`) to recover the double.
8. `rules::resolve_ruleset` applies mod patch layers, inheritance and set operations (only in our own collections, never inside imported INI data), types, validates, interns IDs and hashes.

Every quirk switch of the implemented specs has a field in `ini::Quirks` (including S-0003's `percent_list_integer_prefix` and `list_items_untrimmed`, the latter shared by S-0303, S-0311 and S-0312 list items), `vfs::GameQuirks`, `convert::layers::LayerQuirks`, `convert::art::ArtQuirks`, `convert::sim::SimQuirks` (S-0308's `clear_weapons_any_turret_count`, S-0309's `coefficient_zero_replaced`, S-0102's `tree_strength_fixed_at_first_section`, S-0202's `colour_list_item_triples`), `convert::theater::TheaterQuirks`, `convert::map::MapQuirks` or `render::palette::PaletteQuirks`, defaulting to the original's behaviour; the import records them under `import.quirks`. The renderer's and the map viewer's switches are fields of `render::projection::ProjectionQuirks` (S-0101), `render::compose::ComposeQuirks` (S-0102 compositing), `render::light::LightQuirks` (S-0104), `render::voxel::VoxelQuirks` (S-0105), `render::overlay::OverlayQuirks` (S-0112), `render::palette::PaletteQuirks` (S-0103), `render::shroud::ShroudQuirks` (S-0110) and `app::mapview::quirks::ViewQuirks` (S-0102 draw order and placement, S-0108 building frames and slot animations), each with `named()`. `rules::quirks::tests::every_spec_quirk_has_a_switch` checks that every switch the simulation specs, S-0006, S-0009, S-0010 and S-0107 name is a field of `rules::Quirks` or listed with its owner in `EXTERNAL_QUIRKS`; `app::mapview::quirks::tests::every_rendering_spec_quirk_has_a_switch` checks that every switch `spec/rendering/S-01*.md` names is a field of one of the structs above (or of `rules::Quirks`, `LayerQuirks`, `ArtQuirks` or `SimQuirks`) or is listed in `app::mapview::quirks::NOT_IMPLEMENTED` with its reason (the table below). Two S-0009 switches of `MapQuirks` are not consulted yet, because our engine has no lobby and draws no preview markers: `lobby_counts_zero_waypoint` and `preview_markers_from_header`. Three S-0006 switches of `LayerQuirks` are not consulted either, because the importer imports one game, the first of its session, without campaigns: `persist_global_overrides`, `persist_tiberium_overrides` and `mission_rules_before_reset`. The importer warns about an infantry type whose die-1 or die-2 sequence entry has no image frames (S-0107 claim 17).

**Named switches of specs whose behaviour is not implemented yet.** These switches of the rendering specs have no field: the behaviour they switch is not drawn yet, its off state is open (a request or a question for the lead in the spec), or another part of the engine owns them. Where our code already draws one setting, the reason says which.

| Switch | Spec | Why it has no field |
|---|---|---|
| `screen_to_ground_not_exact_inverse` | S-0101 claim 9 | the on state's F6 converts building slot displacements (S-0108 claim 10, `render::projection::screen_to_ground`); the off state's exact inverse is not implemented; the cell under a pixel is F7's pixel formula, the same in both settings |
| `slot_values_replace_animation_adjustments` | S-0102 claim 17 | the on state is drawn (the slot's depth adjustment and y-sort replace the type's, from the first drawn frame); the off state (the slot's values added to the type's) is not implemented |
| `parachute_brightness_fixed` | S-0103 claim 8 | parachutes are not drawn |
| `split_effect_priority` | S-0104 claim 2 | the lighting effects in force (nuke, dominator, ion storm, chrono flash) are not modelled |
| `chrono_flash_no_reevaluation` | S-0104 claims 2 and 5 | no chrono flash and no lighting re-evaluations |
| `dominator_height_terms_linger` | S-0104 claim 16 | no dominator effect |
| `trigger_tint_retints_all_tables` | S-0104 claim 17 | trigger actions do not change the lighting |
| `vehicle_no_recoil` | S-0105 claim 28 | recoil placement is not applied; vehicles never recoil, as with the switch on |
| `barrel_only_no_recoil` | S-0105 claim 37 | recoil placement is not applied; a barrel without a turret never recoils, as with the switch on |
| `voxel_single_pixel` | S-0105 claim 22 | the on state is drawn; the off state (no gaps in magnified sections) is not implemented |
| `voxel_no_intersection_depth` | S-0105 claim 23 | the on state is drawn; the off state (a depth test between sections) is not implemented |
| `no_turret_shadow` | S-0105 claim 29 | turrets and barrels cast no shadow, as with the switch on; the off state's shadow is not specified |
| `tilter_flag_ignored` | S-0106 claim 10 | voxel bodies are not tilted on slopes |
| `full_state_unmaxed` | S-0108 claim 2 | buildings are drawn in their idle state only |
| `turret_building_no_body_wrap` | S-0108 claim 6 | building body ranges are not played |
| `turret_start_clears_damaged` | S-0108 claims 11 and 12 | slot animations are drawn only as started at placement; the turret slot start and damage swaps are not played |
| `damage_swap_drops_occupied` | S-0108 claims 11 and 12 | slot animations are drawn only as started at placement; damage swaps are not played |
| `theater_refresh_buildup_range` | S-0108 claim 5 | no theater refresh |
| `anim_hide_at_level_15` | S-0109 claim 20 | only building slot animations have a translucency level *t* > 0, and they are not played (the played animations have *t* = 0) |
| `anim_low_detail_shows_cloaked` | S-0109 claim 20 | only building slot animations have a translucency level *t* > 0, and they are not played |
| `anim_translucency_levels` | S-0109 claim 20 | only building slot animations have a translucency level *t* > 0, and they are not played |

**Coverage.** `convert::coverage` gives every (category, key) of the rules and art files an outcome: `field` and `render` for keys interpreted per an approved spec, `folded` for type lists and similar, `unsupported` for keys kept as INI text because no approved spec maps them yet (the `spec` column names the specs whose import mapping mentions a key that is not converted yet).

Q-0234 is answered by S-0003 claim 23: the virtual-purifier, extra-credit and computer-timer lists read their items in the decimal-prefix form of claims 20 and 22 (`Form::Maxima`, 511 bytes), and the importer warns for items with no digit, text after the number or a 32-bit wrap, and for a cut beyond 511 bytes.

Q-0216 is answered by S-0105 revision 10, claim 35: top-down, a span's empty count follows its colours for every span, with no first-span exception, which is what `render::voxel::column_steps` already did; the tests check MS-26's two-span and three-span rows.

Q-0415 is answered by S-0102 revision 13, claim 31 and F5: the body of a building on a foundation under 8 cells wide samples image frame 0 of `BUILDNGZ.SHA` (`render::compose::companion_origin`: live constants (198, 446) with the art displacement `ZShapePointMove`; the remembered constants (144, 172) wait for the fog memory, which is not drawn yet). Its bytes are signed adjustments (`companion_adjustment`), every row takes the unrounded upright anchor minus the sample, and body pixels outside the stored rectangle are clipped, with no depth written, on the CPU and on the GPU (the signed images share the atlas). The building shadow and bib are no longer provisional.

Q-0470 is answered by S-0105 revision 13, claim 22 and F6: a part or shadow image keeps at most the 4096 × 4096-pixel window of its copy rectangle, centred on it with an odd extra pixel discarded on the right or bottom (`render::voxel::IMAGE_LIMIT`; the former interim window, centred on the origin shift, was the same window, because the copy rectangle is centred there within half a pixel), and a part or shadow whose placement is not finite is not drawn. The importer does not read voxel models, so the once-per-model warning is a scene note (`mapview::build`, `limit_checked`) raised the first time a drawn part or shadow image of the model exceeds the limit; poses not yet drawn are not checked.

Q-0460 is answered by S-0409 revision 10 (claim 5, F2) and S-0101 revision 10 (claim 3, F2: the exact lift is the drawing lift only; `mapview::tests::drawing_lift_is_not_the_sight_lift`): the sight centre keeps its own floating-point simulation lift (`glasnost_sim::econ::shroud::height_lift`, unchanged, now cited to S-0409 F2 and tested on its check values: level 20 moves the centre 9 cells, not the 10 of the exact drawing lift), while the renderer draws with S-0101 claim 3's exact lift; S-0102 revision 13 retires `quirks.overlay_lift_exact_levels`, so overlays lift exactly 15 per level (`mapview::quirks::overlay_depth_lift`).

Q-0490 is answered by S-0102 revision 14, claim 33: when `BUILDNGZ.SHA` is missing, cannot be read as a shape file, has no image frames (whatever its canvas) or has an image frame 0 that cannot be decoded, the map view and the live game warn once with a scene note naming the file and the failure (`mapview::build::read_companion`, `notes::no_companion`) and draw every eligible body with no companion: the same draw point and claim 13 bias, the plain upright profile of F2, writing depth, and no longer provisional. A valid image frame 0 with an empty rectangle keeps its empty coverage (the body draws nothing and writes no depth, on the CPU and the GPU) with no warning. Foundations 8 or more cells wide use no companion either way. Remembered bodies are not drawn yet, so their recovery waits with them.

Q-0520 is answered by S-0102 revision 15, claim 33 and its Import mapping: for `BUILDNGZ.SHA` only, an image frame 0 with a non-empty rectangle and pixel-data position 0 is failed input, whatever unused bytes follow it, not empty coverage. `mapview::build::read_companion` warns with the scene note naming the file and that failure and the eligible body is drawn with no companion and the plain upright profile of F2, writing depth, like the other failures; it is no longer provisional (`mapview::tests::building_body_companion_without_pixel_data_uses_plain_upright`). The shape decoder for other sprites is unchanged.

**Open requests and their placeholders.** None open here.

S-0501 revision 15 answers Q-0841 (step 5, [docs/m6-claims/step5.md](m6-claims/step5.md)); its row is retired (see "Aircraft shadow anchor" below). S-0501 revision 16 and S-0209 revision 37 answer Q-0872 (step 6a, [docs/m6-claims/step6a.md](m6-claims/step6a.md)): an ordinary aircraft's object-present flag is set in the deck record at rest on a high-bridge deck and in the ground record otherwise (`air::mark_layer`, chosen at each marking or clearing event from the actual position), and its row is retired.

Revision 17 of S-0105 answers Q-0215 question 7 (F8's record-low relation, MS-25 and MS-28); `render::voxel::section_order` already computes it, and `section_order_is_f8_relation`, `section_order_ms25_pair_winners` and `section_order_ms28_rows` check it.

Revision 20 of S-0105 answers Q-0950 (claims 41 and 42, the heading sources of claims 3, 26 and 30, the importer rows); see *Rendering of aircraft attitude (M6c)*. Revision 13 of S-0007 answers Q-0951 with claim 34: the art lookup's own-section rule for an animation's playback and drawing fields; `mapview::build::anim_own_section` and, on the import side, `convert::sim` (`animation`), which already read the own section. The claim 33 rewording of a non-voxel projectile's sprite lookup (theater extension, substituted new-theater name, plain name, each with the generic G fallback tried once) is not built: `convert::art` has no projectile sprite lookup (only the trailer fields of S-0007 claim 33 are read, in `convert::sim_combat`); `quirks.art_generic_fallback_any_name` defaults on, as the rewording says.

## Map viewer (M2)

- `glasnost-map` (a simulation crate) holds the decoded map: footprint, legal and usable cells and their visiting order (S-0009 claims 1 to 4), the cell grid after the initial fill and the terrain stream, overlay stream values, waypoints, header, preview, lighting values in hundredths and thousandths (S-0104 claim 1) and the object lists as read.
- `convert::map` decodes a map document with importer warnings, the object lists by S-0009 claims 34 to 36, 40 and 42; `convert::theater` reads a theater's tile catalogue per S-0010 claims 1 to 5 (theater data file, declared tilesets, tile files with variants and the suffix fallback, role starts) with S-0009's base tiles and tile translation, builds the simulation's `map::terrain::TileCatalogue` (terrain and slope code of every tile cell, and each tile's animation and attaches-to sub-tile from the section its tileset's set name names, S-0010 claim 19) from the tile files, and holds the variant choice of claims 13 and 14 (the 4 × 4 pattern and the 8 × 8 pattern built from the unsynchronised stream, S-0202 claim 11).
- `render::projection` (S-0101), `render::light` (S-0104), `render::compose` (the CPU reference of S-0102's depth compositing), `render::scene` (items in world pixels) and `render::map_gpu` (the WebGL2 map pass, tested pixel for pixel against the CPU reference on a real or software GPU). Both honour `render::compose::ComposeQuirks` (`cell_pass_shadows_write_depth` of S-0102 claim 27: the GPU draws a writer shadow with a pipeline that blends and stores depth). Two S-0102 departures have no switch: the depth reference is one *C* = 32768 for the whole game, whatever the view, scrolls and jumps (F1, `compose::DEPTH_REFERENCE`), and the profile rows *r*₀ and *r*₁ are the drawing rectangle before clipping, so clipping removes pixels only (F2). S-0101 revision 8's departures have none either: the object lift is the exact ⌊(30*z* + 104)/208⌋ (F2, `projection::height_lift`), an image is drawn when its drawn extent meets the view (F4b; the GPU pass skips items whose bounds miss the view, the CPU reference clips per pixel; the ground box of F4a is not applied), and an axis on which the map is narrower than the view keeps F5's fixed centre (claim 8, `projection::limit_centre`). Right-drag scrolling (S-0101 claim 16, F10) is `app::game::scroll::drag_request`: `session::scroll_cycle` asks it once per unfrozen cycle while S-0214 claim 27's gesture is a scroll drag (not during an order block), with method 0, the default of the scrolling-method preference (the settings file is not read, so methods 1 and 2, which move the host pointer back to the anchor, are never chosen), and adds it to the edge and arrow requests before F5; edge scrolling takes no pointer while a mouse button is held (claim 12). Each such update shows claim 17's drag cursor (`cursor::drag`, frames 385 to 393 by the refused one-pixel directions, centre hot spot) in place of the edge cursor. Remembered building animations, which S-0102 claim 16 draws after the whole cell pass, do not arise: fog of war is off in a skirmish and nothing is remembered.
- `app::mapview` builds a map's scene from the decoded map, the rules import and the theater's files; the viewer's *Map viewer* mode lists the install's maps, loads one, scrolls within S-0101's limits, shows a minimap, layer toggles and object info with `rules explain`. Everything that rests on a request is marked provisional and can be hidden. Tiles are drawn from the cells after loading (S-0010 claim 12, when the game was set up; as stored otherwise): the image of claim 13 (a tile cell whose plain image carries the alternate-image mark, the bit of value 4 of its flag value, shows the plain image: its state is clear at map load), the tile cell of claim 24 (the clear tile for a cell without a usable tile), diamonds painted whole, index 0 included (claim 22, `Paint::LitDiamond`), extra images after their diamond (claim 23), at the scaled ground level (S-0104 claim 13). Pre-placed objects are drawn in their S-0009 initial state (claims 37 to 43), with buildings damaged when their starting health says so (S-0108 claim 2's table: a stationary gate that is not occupiable shows `GateStages` + 1 when damaged; claim 3's clamp shows the last body frame, min(*f*, ⌊N/2⌋ − 1), with its shadow, keeps a negative index, and an index below 0 or at or past the frame count draws no body, no shadow and, when negative, no bib; a sprite with no body frames draws neither body nor shadow). Claim 2's occupied hurt civilian frame and claim 4's backward sold gate are departures without switches; neither is drawn yet, because no building is occupied and a building being sold is drawn idle. Animated terrain objects (the type's animated flag) are drawn in the object pass's ground layer as readers with image frame 0, their stage at placement, each body right before its own shadow with nothing between them (S-0102 claims 10, 23 and 30); an ore mine's animation (S-0401 claim 15) is not played.

- **Building slot animations.** A placed building starts its *idle* and *active-1* … *active-4* slots (S-0108 claim 11: the set-up starts for map buildings; grand opening for a building the game completes, where a refinery starts only *active-1*, its slot at fill level 0 by claim 15). `app::mapview::slots` reads the slot definitions (names cut to 15 bytes, empty damaged and occupied names filled with the normal name, displacement, depth adjustment and y-sort) and the animation types' drawing fields. Each started slot whose name is a registered animation type with a sprite is drawn at its first image frame (S-0109 claims 1, 8 and 12: *a*, or *a* + λ<sub>e</sub> − 1 when reversed), at the building's anchor moved by its displacement through S-0101 F6 (S-0108 claim 10), in its type's layer (S-0102 claims 3 and 17: the ground layer sorted by X + Y + the slot's y-sort, the others unsorted in entering order), as a depth reader with the animation rows of S-0102 claim 13 and the slot's depth adjustment, centred at the screen pixel of that position moved down by its type's vertical draw shift (S-0109 claim 26), with the shadow image frame of a shadow type drawn right after the image at the unshifted point (S-0109 claim 10), a tiled type as a stack of copies of its image frame spaced by image frame 0's stored rectangle height *h*, the lowest lifted by ⌊*h*/2⌋, as many as the view's top row allows (S-0109 claim 27; `render::scene::Stack`, expanded per view by the CPU composite and the GPU path, each copy *h* depth units nearer than the one below, S-0102 claim 13; a zero-height first rectangle gives one copy and a note naming the sprite), the palette rows 3 to 5 of S-0103 claim 8 (a tiled type's copies always the anim palette, at the brightness of the row it would otherwise select), and not at all when the type's detail field is above the player's (S-0109 claim 19). Not modelled: playback (no slot animation advances), power (S-0108 claims 13 and 14), damage swaps (claim 12), the turret, special, super and upgrade slots, occupied variants, refinery and silo fill changes, erasure and cloaking translucency (S-0109 claims 20 and 21). A slot animation whose first translucency code (S-0109 claim 20, at the player's detail level) is nonzero is drawn with the translucent blend of S-0102 claim 32 and F8 (`render::compose::Translucency`: each 5-6-5 channel blended with the colour already drawn, no depth written; on the GPU by copying the covered target region to a backdrop texture before each translucent item); its shadow stays a plain halving. Buildings the game places in play draw their slot animations of every layer right after their other items.
- **House colours outside a skirmish.** In the all-owners view and the sandbox game, each object owner shows its imported colour definition (S-0103 claim 14, S-0009 claims 47 and 48, `build::map_owner_colours`: a listed house's country is its own section's `Country` selector, index 0 when absent, a created default country at position 0 when unknown; the country's `Color` selector through the rules layers, else position 0, replaced by a valid `Color` selector of the house's own section; with no house list, one house per country). An owner matching no created house exactly is unresolved: the original drops its objects, and the viewer keeps them in its own grey with a scene note; the live radar draws each house's objects in its house display RGB (S-0103 claim 15, S-0410 claim 8: ramp index 16 at shade row 26, max(0, *u* − 1) kept to 5-6-5 bits). The viewer's static minimap (M2) is a picture of the map's terrain only: S-0410's object listing needs a viewing house and its discovery marks (claim 7), which a map without a game has not got.
- **Live sandbox game (M3).** Loading a map also starts the simulation's sandbox game (`glasnost_sim::load::sandbox_setup`: one house per object owner, human-controlled except the neutral and special houses, fixed seed) from the same game inputs (`convert::game::load`). `app::mapview::build` keeps the static part of the scene (cell pass and building voxel parts) and a `Painter` with its caches; `app::mapview::live` draws vehicles, infantry and aircraft from the simulation after each tick (position from the lepton position, S-0101; voxel poses from the body and turret facings, S-0105; bodies are drawn untilted: `render::tilt` holds S-0106's tilt state and transition orientations (claims 6 and 7, F3; a restart runs on from the interrupted orientation, with no switch), but no locomotor keeps the state yet and no pose uses it; voxel part and shadow images kept in each type's image cache under the keys of S-0105 claims 26, 31 and 37, and drawn fresh, each fresh draw starting fully transparent, with no image-coordinate wrap (claims 22 and 34; each map load starts with empty image caches), and the depth-buffered mode on only during a fresh part draw of a type with the depth option, never for a shadow, claims 32 and 33 (Q-0056); the cache-disable options are not honoured, because the scene keeps every image it is given; a vehicle's shadow is made after its body, turret and barrel, the cache keeps the unmasked footprint, and every appearance excludes every pixel the current completed part images cover, S-0105 claim 39 (kept per unmasked-shadow and part-image combination); an aircraft's shadow still comes before its body, which covers it, S-0102 claim 18; infantry frames from the sequence state, S-0107; vehicles drawn as sprites from S-0102 claim 22's frame table, reading a walk counter the view keeps per vehicle (`mapview::walk`, advanced once per tick; the voxel body frame of S-0105 claim 16 and the harvesting image read the same counter, S-0102 claim 22a, which also sets its advance: the locomotor's *moving now*, the hover-attack and deploy-to-land tests, and the held states; presentation state, started again at 0 on a save, load or join), keeps the object pass's ground layer as S-0102 claim 6 says (after each tick: leavers dropped, every member's current key taken, newcomers after the existing members, then a full stable sort by key, so equal keys keep their previous order and a newcomer follows them; a building's slot animations are ordered by their slot's y-sort from the first drawn frame, claim 17) and paces ticks by S-0201 (16 × s ms, no catch-up; at most one tick per drawn frame, so index 0, whose period is 8 ms, runs at the display rate instead of 125 ticks per second). The GPU map pass uploads only the images and palette rows a scene gained (`MapRenderer::sync`). Problems that keep the game from starting are listed and the static view stays. While a game runs, every owner's objects are drawn, whatever the skirmish-view option says.
- **Harvesting sprite.** A loading vehicle harvester shows `OREGATH.SHP` (S-0402 claim 5a and F8, answering Q-0550 and Q-0580): `glasnost_sim::econ::harvest::display_shown` says when (eligible from loading start until the phase leaves loading, hidden while moving, turning or teleporting), `app::mapview::harvesting` gives the frame and the anchor (X + 30 × cosT θ, Y − 30 × sinT θ with S-0105's table trigonometry, rounded toward zero), and `build::harvesting_item` draws it before the vehicle's shadow and body with the anim palette, no house remap, at the vehicle's brightness (S-0104 claim 13), depth-tested flat at the vehicle's *U* − 2 without writing depth (S-0102 claim 13). A ground driver counts as moving while on a track whose latest drive tick earned an integer step *s* above 0 (`DriveState::step`, S-0402 claim 5a, answering Q-0590). Not modelled yet: the walk-animation counter ν (taken as 0), erasing weapons (none exist yet), and the vehicle light's bridge-deck addition and shadowed-cell penalty, which the body lacks too.
- **Controls.** The viewer follows S-0214 claim 26 as the game client does: a left click on a cell or object goes through `glasnost_sim::targeting::click` with the selection, which selects or gives the selection's orders (shift force-select, ctrl force-fire, alt force-move), issued in the controlled house's name between ticks; a right click clears the selection. Dragging with either button scrolls the viewer (the game client scrolls with S-0214 claim 27's right drag only). S, X, G and D are stop, scatter, guard and deploy (`key_command`), C is the house's cheer (`CheerMemory`, kept for the session). The panel has pause, one-tick, the game-speed index for pacing (the game's own speed setting is not changed), the controlled house (default: the first house owning a mobile unit), the selected object's kind, type, owner, cell, behaviour and destination, and the game's and the drawing's notes. Selection markers are our own presentation.
- **Combat (M4).** `app::mapview::live` drains `World::effects` after every tick it runs and draws, after the object pass: a provisional placeholder of our own for each projectile in flight at its projected point with its height (S-0101; a 7 × 7 diamond, cyan, orange when arcing, magenta when steering; an invisible projectile is not drawn; no game art, because the importer does not look up projectile art yet, S-0007 claims 26 and 27), a muzzle cross at a shot's fire point for 3 ticks, a growing burst ellipse for a detonation for 8 ticks, sized from its spread radius by S-0101's ground projection, and, since M6b, the animation of each animation event played with its art (section *Animation playback (M6b)*); the viewer still shows a magenta square for 12 ticks for an animation event whose type has no sprite (its debug layer). The game client (`Live::set_debug_markers(false)`) draws no animation marker, and no burst where an animation with art started in the detonation's cell in the same tick; it keeps the projectile diamonds (no projectile art lookup, S-0007 claims 26 to 30) and the muzzle crosses (no muzzle art path). Then S-0111's status extras (`app::mapview::combat`): the health bar of every selected object (with the unit bracket image) and of the object under the pointer (without it), at any health (claim 3), with the filled segments of F1 in soft-float under the rules' rounding mode, the colours of claim 5, the unit places of claim 6 (δ from `PixelSelectionBracketDelta`) and the building pip line of claim 7 (foundation and art `Height`), and every object's veterancy insignia (claim 18), from `PIPS.SHP` and `PIPBRD.SHP` in the unshaded overlay palette `PALETTE.PAL` (a missing sprite draws nothing). Not drawn yet: claim 1's culling, claim 2's shading (brackets and insignia are drawn from the unshaded overlay palette, not through a neutral-brightness shade row, S-0104 claim 15, so the shroud covers of S-0110 do not darken them), building corner brackets (claim 8; our own markers stay for buildings and for units when the bracket sprite is missing). Since M8 step A3 intake 46 (S-0111 r21) the whole pip scale is drawn with the health bar where claim 9 lets the viewer see it (`app::mapview::pip_scale`, `Live::pip_rows`): the spawned-aircraft, occupant, passenger and by-kind rows of claim 11 in table order, each covering the rows above it, from `PIPS2.SHP` for units and `PIPS.SHP` for buildings at claim 10's places (ammunition 3 pixels higher, always from `PIPS2.SHP`); the self-heal marker of claim 16, a shadow during its blink; and last the control-group number of claim 17 in the game font (`combat::GroupFont`, `group_number_image`, F6: the digit's glyph, or the fallback, left-aligned at the placement point in an opaque black box with a one-pixel ring in the owner's house display RGB; every stored row paints; no box when the pitch leaves none; nothing without a usable font). Status extras follow S-0102's object order (`Live::status_order`), not object ID order. Ledger [m8-claims/intake46-S-0111.md](m8-claims/intake46-S-0111.md). The health bar has no quirk switch. Destroyed objects leave the picture: buildings the game removed are dropped from the cell pass and the ground layer; infantry dying are drawn by their death sequence (S-0107) until the game removes them. The layer toggles *combat effects* and *status extras* hide them. Attack orders are a left click on an enemy with a selection (`targeting::click`, S-0214 claim 26); units in guard pick their own targets. The panel shows the selected object's health of its strength, target, ammunition, experience and rank, the event counts and every house's kills, losses and score.
- **Live lane.** `GLASNOST_GAME_DIR=<install> cargo test --release -p glasnost-app --test live_lane -- --nocapture` loads a few maps with their game, selects units through the input rules, orders a move, runs 240 ticks and writes views before, during and after to `local-lane/screens/`, with per-tick step and redraw times (`GLASNOST_LIVE_MAPS`, `GLASNOST_LIVE_TICKS`, `GLASNOST_LIVE_FILTER`, `GLASNOST_LIVE_INFANTRY=1`). Its combat run (`live_maps_fight_and_draw`) picks the closest armed unit and enemy unit of two player houses that are not allied, orders up to 8 armed units near the first to attack with a click through `targeting::click`, runs `GLASNOST_COMBAT_TICKS` (360) ticks on `GLASNOST_COMBAT_MAPS` (2) maps and writes `combat-<map index>-<tick>.png` before the order, at the first visible projectile, 8 ticks later and at the end, printing shot, detonation, animation and kill counts and timings. With a real install the import holds no weapons yet (weapons, projectiles and warheads exist only by reference, and the importer does not yet create them from the weapon reference fields of S-0004 claim 13 and its Import mapping), so no unit fires; the run then takes any two units of houses that are not allied and still shows the status extras. The pip-scale lane (`--test pip_scale_lane`, S-0111 claims 10 to 17) puts one house's building, nearest vehicle and infantry, and its first harvester, in control groups 1 to 4, sets the vehicle to half strength, gives the house unit self-heal and the harvester a part load, and writes `pips-<map index>-group.png` and `pips-<map index>-building.png` to `local-lane/m8/a3-46/` (`GLASNOST_LANE_OUT`, `GLASNOST_LANE_MAPS` (2)) without running the game.

## Animation playback (M6b)

`app::mapview::anims` is S-0109's playback clock for the drawing (since M8 step A5 the clock is `glasnost_sim::anim_clock`, re-exported, and the simulation plays every animation in play with it: section *Animation playback in the simulation (M8 step A5)*): `Player` holds a playing animation's type name, frame, stage delay, pass, loop count and clock, and `Player::update` is one update of claim 5 (the first update after creation does not advance; later ones advance when *t* − last ≥ *d* and *d* > 0). It plays claims 1 to 12, 15 and 16: frame *a* + *k*, the stage delay of claim 2 (⌊900/ρ⌋, absent or −1 rate 1, `glasnost_sim::rules::anim_delay`), the random rate of claim 3 (the simulation's creation draw, carried in `Effect::Anim::delay`), the game-speed normalisation of claim 4 (`glasnost_sim::infantry::normalised` under `quirks.speed_normalised_delays`), the loop delay counted in updates (the update reaching 0 does not advance), F3's pass ends, ping-pong turns, reversed playback from *a* + λ<sub>e</sub> − 1, loop counts, and the follow-up type of claim 15 (*k* = *a*′, the step kept, the delay drawn afresh, the clock restarted). An ended animation is removed. Its switches are `AnimQuirks` (`anim_loop_count_wrap`, `anim_reverse_raw_loop_end`, `anim_pingpong_lower_turn`, `anim_loop_end_from_sprite`, `anim_followup_start_doubled`, `anim_followup_keeps_step`, all on by default).

`Live` creates a playing animation for every `Effect::Anim` of a tick (the type looked up by name with ASCII case folding, the first registered wins, S-0003 claim 14), updates every animation once per shown tick, and draws each in `Live::redraw` through `Builder::live_anim`, the slot animations' code path (`Builder::anim_items`): unattached, so in its type's layer (default air, S-0102 claim 3), the ground layer keyed X + Y + `YSortAdjust` and the other layers in entering order after their static entries (S-0102 claims 4, 5 and 7), a depth reader with the animation rows of S-0102 claim 13 and the type's `ZAdjust`, the anim palette rows 4 and 5 of S-0103 claim 8 at neutral brightness, the shadow frame *f* + ⌊N/2⌋ of a shadow type (S-0109 claim 10), tiled and flat types as for slot animations, the translucency of claim 20 at *t* = 0, and nothing when the type's detail field is above the player's (claim 19). A type with no sprite draws nothing in the game client; the viewer's debug layer shows our magenta marker for it. The load reads every animation type's sprite when a game starts (`build::anim_files`). The counts *animations with art*, *without sprite* and *drawn* are in `Counts`.

Not modelled in the drawing: held animations, translucency levels above 0 (only slot animations have them), and map-load, tile and cell animations, which the simulation does not report as effects. Attachment, damage over time and the start and middle events are the simulation's since M8 step A5; the drawing follows the simulated animation's stage and position (`LiveAnim::sim`). Building slot animations stay drawn at their first frame (section *Map viewer (M2)*).

`GLASNOST_GAME_DIR=<install> cargo test --release -p glasnost-app --test anim_lane -- --ignored --nocapture` re-shoots the ship-sinking and V3-rocket scenes of the M6a lane into `local-lane/m6b/ship-sinking-anims.png` and `v3-rocket-anims.png` and prints the animations created, with art and without a sprite. The ledger is [docs/m6-claims/S-0109-presentation.md](m6-claims/S-0109-presentation.md).

S-0109 revision 24 answers Q-0770 (claim 28) and Q-0771 (claim 29):
- every ordinary playback draw (the between-pass loop delay, the follow-up's random rate) is a simulation effect on the scenario stream, chosen in the animation's own turn, whether or not it is drawn;
- an animation made before or during the turn pass is eligible for its first update later in that pass, after the objects already in the update order (so S-0203 claim 10's removals can cost it that update); one made after the pass is first eligible next tick.

Both needed animation playback inside the simulation's turn pass. M8 step A5 builds it (next section), and the two interim rows that stood here (the drawing's cosmetic stream for playback draws, and the first update taken in the effect's tick) are retired.

## Animation playback in the simulation (M8 step A5)

`glasnost_sim::anims` plays S-0109's ordinary animations in the simulation. `World::anims` (`AnimWorld`, save format 69) holds every animation in play by object number: its type, its `anim_clock::Player`, its position, its owner and displacement (claim 22), its damage accumulator (F5) and its role (an ordinary animation, a first-pass tile animation, a parachute, a warp source marker, a drain animation). `anims::spawn` is the creation of claim 29: the creation draws (S-0202 claim 10), the start event when the creator gives no loop delay (claim 13), the clock made with the creator's `anim_clock::Creation` (an initial loop delay, a repeat factor and a reverse request), then the animation is appended to the update order (S-0203 claims 3 and 4). In its turn (`tick::object_turn`, `anims::turn`) an attached animation first takes its owner's position plus its displacement, or is deleted with nothing else when its owner has left play; then one clock update. An advance delivers its damage (claim 17, F5: the accumulator starts at 1.0 under `quirks.anim_damage_starts_at_one`, at 0.0 after a follow-up switch, 5δ when attached to a terrain object; an area detonation with resource reduction allowed and no attacker or house) and then its middle event when *a* + *k* = μ ≠ 0 (claim 14; not for a bouncing type). A loop delay's end and a follow-up switch give the start event. Between-pass loop delays and a follow-up's random rate are ranged draws from the scenario stream in the animation's own turn (claim 28). An animation whose clock has ended leaves the update order in its own turn, a counting removal (S-0203 claim 10): the next object misses that turn under `quirks.update_order_removal_skip`.

Every creation that is not played by a specialised model goes through `anims::spawn` (`load::anim_created` with `played` false): weapon, warhead, death, debris, warp, wake and splash animations, the map's tile and overlay-cell animations and glints at loading (the first pass's tile animations are removed before the second, S-0010), shot animations attached to a firer that is not a building (S-0301 claim 22), projectile trailers with the loop delay of 1 (claim 29, `combat::effects::trailer`), deploy presentations with their reverse request (S-0517 claim 7), the drain animation attached to the drainer until the link ends (`drain`), a house's warp source marker until a new pick or the launch (`superweapons::warp`), and a dropped paratrooper's parachute (S-0522 claim 13: attached to the trooper, `fall.chute` while it plays, its remaining passes set to 0 on landing). The specialised models keep their own playback and are not entered twice: infantry-making animations (`mutation`), virus-death clouds (S-0613, `gas`), burning walkers, domination, depot slot animations and storm clouds (S-0513, played by the storm model). `Effect::Anim` and `Effect::ShotAnim` carry the simulated animation's number, as does `SwEvent::Parachute`; the client's `LiveAnim::sim` follows it (`Live::follow_sim_anims`: the simulated type, stage and position each tick, removed when the simulation's ends) instead of playing a clock of its own.

S-0109 revision 41 (M8 step A3 intake 22) answers Q-1716 and Q-1717, and their two interim rows are retired:
- within one advance the damage detonation comes first, with its draws and kills, then the middle event, then the turn and pass tests; the same holds on an ending advance and before a follow-up switch (State and ordering);
- an animation that leaves play in its own detonation does nothing more in that update (`Env::left_play`, our engine's rule); an attached animation whose detonation removes its owner goes on at the owner's last position and is deleted at its next update (our design);
- the warhead of an animation's damage is chosen on each delivery by the current type's stored name: `C4Warhead` for the built-in invisibility-animation type (`rules::INVISIBILITY_ANIM_NAME`, an exact comparison with the first-registered spelling), `FlameDamage2` for every other (`anims::damage_warhead`, also used by the burning walker's expiry damage).

The ledger is [docs/m8-claims/intake22-S-0109.md](m8-claims/intake22-S-0109.md).

Seams: bouncing motion and landing (the stages play, without damage or advance middle events), trigger-played animations (map triggers are not built), a warp source marker's end on leaving aiming (a sidebar action), rocket trailers (`rocket` is not an S-0306 projectile, so its trailer has no loop delay), resource glints (no position: no damage, only their particle draws at a middle event), building slot animations and held animations (claim 23; not simulated). The clock's quirk switches are the ruleset's `quirks.anim_*` since seams sweep 3 ([docs/m8-claims/seams-3.md](m8-claims/seams-3.md)). The client still draws its own cosmetic pictures for the drain and warp-marker animations and draws no tile animations or glints. S-0312 claim 18 (survivors) stays a seam: F4 and F5 need a house's side index, which `SimRules` does not map. The ledger is [docs/m8-claims/S-0109-playback.md](m8-claims/S-0109-playback.md).

## Simulation (M3)

`glasnost-sim` runs the game rules on the typed ruleset and map. Everything in it is integers and the soft-float types of `glasnost-num` (S-0205: IEEE binary32/64 in integers, round toward zero under `quirks.chop_rounding`), with ordered containers only.

- **Rules and data.** `rules::SimRules` is built from a resolved ruleset (`SimRules::new(&resolved.ruleset, &resolved.sim_rules_hash)`): typed per-kind tables, the 443 quirk switches of `rules::Quirks` (every one defaulting to the original; `refused_death_keeps_unit` of S-0107 claim 17 is not consulted, since M3 has no death handling), and engine defaults cited to their specs. A type without a locomotor field has the teleporting locomotor (S-0207 claim 14); vehicles with the driving locomotor drive and infantry with the walking one walk, and every other locomotor is not moved in M3. `consts` holds the spec constants given as decimals, as checked bit patterns.
- **A game.** `load::new_game(rules, map, catalogue, setup)` follows S-0009 and S-0202's table *Draws during map loading*: the early phase of start positions on blank cells (lists 1 and 2, the assignment against list 2, with the base rules' passive fields, claims 17 to 20 and 33; `starts`, with the start-cell variant of the nearby-cell search, `path::start_cell`), the initial fill, the overlay type stream placed overlay by overlay (`overlays`: the slope condition of claim 12, high-bridge cells as each overlay is placed, wall connection bits, low-bridge end markers with their pieces' draws), the overlay data stream, the first recalculation pass, the vein rebuild with its draws (claim 14), the tile animations each recalculation starts with their creation draws (S-0010 claim 19, S-0202 claim 10: the first recalculation of a cell before the second pass, then at most one per cell from the second pass on, in play too; `load::recalc_cells`), the pre-placed objects (with the visceroid draw of a vehicle's placement and its follower-car link, claim 41), the second pass, the unused-house cleanup (claim 28 and S-0202 claim 14: only in a game with no AI house, S-0412 claim 12, and fewer human slots than the base rules' maximum player count; a removed house loses its pre-placed objects, takes no position and gets no starting units, and taking its objects out of their ground cells recalculates those cells; `House::removed`), the late phase (list 3), the owners of the map's walls (S-0210 claim 14), the starting units (`starting`, claims 45 and 46: the placement search's acceptance test reads the ground occupants of the ground layer only and takes as the nearest the one whose reference point lies nearest the cell corner by the table root, ties by the ground list's order), the rest of game start (S-0412 claim 14: crates, then the secret-lab draws), the game-start pass of S-0010 claim 12 (a third recalculation of every legal cell, with its tile animations, after those draws by S-0202 claim 14) and the bridge spans of S-0010 claim 25, whose low spans give their bands the bridge-body mark (`Cells::bridge_body`, `glasnost_map::bridges`; the burrowing test of S-0010 claim 17 is to read it). Cell animations (S-0009 claim 12) take their creation draws when their overlay is placed, before the tile animation of the same placement. Terrain recalculation itself is `glasnost-map`'s (S-0010, S-0211). `GameSetup` gives each house a role (player, neutral, special), whether it is an AI house (`HouseSetup::ai`), the unit-count setting and the bases setting. `load::sandbox_setup` is our viewer's setup: one house per object owner of the map, no start positions and no starting units.
- **Wall connection bits.** `walls` keeps a wall's overlay data value as 16 × damage stage + connection bits (S-0112 claim 7), recomputed when a wall is placed from the map's overlay list, when crushing removes one, and when a gate or wall tower is placed or destroyed (`quirks.gate_tower_fixed_cells`). The map viewer draws the cells after loading, so walls show the bits the simulation gives them.
- **A tick.** `tick::step` runs S-0203's parts in their places: the turn pass over the update order (with S-0203's removal skips and second turns), each object's behaviour part (`process`, S-0215), its locomotor (`drive` with `tracks`, S-0207 and S-0206 claim 12: a driving vehicle in the same behaviours stops at the track target where its mobile target comes in range, S-0207 claim 19 a, with the walker's conditions and range test, `walk::arrival_stop_holds`; the hovering locomotor is `hover`, S-0503; a vehicle with the teleport-capable flag drives on every assignment but a qualified docking one, S-0207 claim 28, `refresh::begin`, [m8-claims/intake39-S-0207.md](m8-claims/intake39-S-0207.md); ships move by the driving code since M6a (section *Simulation (M6a: naval units)*); `walk`, S-0208: a walk ends on its arrival tick in the destination's cell, claim 16, and an approaching walker in *attack*, *area guard*, *hunt* or *rescue* stops at the standing position where its mobile target comes in range, claim 17; the walker's speed fraction of S-0310 is `WalkState::fraction`), its infantry sequence (`infantry`, S-0107), then the orders (`orders`, S-0213), then the tick number. Occupancy is `cells`/`occupancy` (S-0209), crushing `crush` (S-0210), passability and regions `passability`/`regions` (S-0211, S-0212), pathfinding `path` (S-0212) behind the `Pathfinder` trait, over (cell, search layer) pairs, with the corridor of claims 13 to 14 in `corridor` (sectors and connections are derived data, rebuilt with the regions). Since step 1i (S-0212 revision 49), `quirks.corridor_retry_no_fallback` (default on, the original) decides what follows a failed repeated coarse search: on, the computation fails; off, one more cell search without a corridor, final (departure D2's chain-length failure fails whatever the switch; `corridor::coarse_why` tells it apart). Since S-0212 r82 (M8 A3 intake 34) every building gives the corridor exemption to its exemption rectangle and an aircraft round its placement and landing cells, so a unit leaving its base or passing a short line of buildings finds the way on the first cell search; the switch's case is now a strip of ground the unit cannot enter, and `quirks.corridor_free_part_ignores_height` (default on) a ledge's. Each house keeps threat records (claim 16a, module `threat`), read by the coarse search's threat term and the straightening passes for a unit whose coefficient exceeds 0.00001 ([m8-claims/intake34-S-0212.md](m8-claims/intake34-S-0212.md)). Since r87 (intake 44) a walker's failed gate clears its destination after a fresh search or a re-path, a failed search ends its movement in progress, and a new destination waits out a running retry delay for every locomotor ([m8-claims/intake44-S-0212.md](m8-claims/intake44-S-0212.md)). Regions and sectors follow the rules of claims 3 and 13b and exact ties the recording order of claim 12a (departures D4 and D7, accepted by the project lead).
- **Bridges in the path search.** Each placed high-bridge overlay gives its four cells the deck-entry mark (three of them) and the bridge axis (S-0212 Terms); once at game start `bridges::find_spans` finds the bridge spans of S-0010 claim 25 from the bridge and wooden-bridge role tiles, makes them S-0212's bridge links (enabled when high) and gives the bands of low spans the bridge-body mark and their axis. Links, marks and axes are state (`Cells`); regions join the end cells of enabled links, sectors gain their connections (with claim 13b's side cells, which the place of the first end's tile in its bridge role decides), decks are read through the links where S-0212 claims 4, 6, 14 and 25 say so (`regions::reach_read`), and the coarse end cells and F8's anchors follow claim 13b. The burrowing test (`path::burrow_ok`) is S-0010 claim 17's, the tileset's allow-burrowing flag carried per tile in the tile catalogue. Links do not change in play yet: no bridge is destroyed or repaired before the bridge specs (S-0212 claim 3's table is a seam).
- **Orders and input.** Every change a player or our AI makes goes through `orders::issue` (pending lists, S-0213). `targeting` turns a click or key command into orders by S-0214 and S-0213 table T2, for people and AI houses alike; the selection is kept by the caller.
- **Saves, replays, hashes.** `save::save`/`load` store the whole state with the rules hash; `save::state_hash` is SHA-256 of the state's bytes (derived data such as regions and the path search's memory is not state). `save::Replay` holds the rules hash, the setup and every order with its tick.
- **Marks of the map load.** The loader marks the high-bridge cells of each high-bridge overlay as it is placed (S-0211 claim 15) and gives the map's walls their owners once the buildings are placed, before any starting unit (S-0210 claim 14). Gates start closed (S-0215 claim 35); a gate's door moves at every turn of the gate, before its per-tick start and processing (claim 35 (d), which answers Q-0030); S-0209's gate rows read `process::gate_open`, and a unit that meets the *closed friendly gate* outcome as it moves (not while it only asks) calls `process::gate_met`.
- **Animation start events.** An animation created with no loop delay (`load::anim_created_at`) takes its creation draws (S-0202 claim 10), then its start event (S-0109 claim 13): with μ = 0 the immediate middle event of S-0109 claim 14 and S-0202 claim 15 (the requested particles' creation draws, then, below 30 leptons above the terrain at its position, the scorch-or-crater choice when the type has both flags and a crater's removal of 6 bales from the containing cell by S-0401 claim 3), then its chain reaction. μ is ⌊n/2⌋ of the sprite of the type's last sprite-found moment (S-0109 claim 25), which the importer reads during the art lookup (`middle_frame`, 0 when no sprite was found; absent without a theater, when the middle event takes no draw and leaves a note). Tile animations stand at S-0010's *Tile animation position* (the tile's `TileNNXOffset`/`TileNNYOffset` through S-0101 F6), cell animations at the overlay cell's centre moved by 384 leptons along +x and +y, at the surface height of the overlay cell's centre (S-0009 claim 12). Seams: the smudge selection (S-0312 claim 26; smudge types are not modelled, so no type fits and it takes no draw, as for destructions; for the same reason `quirks.smudge_footprints_beyond_map_edge` of S-0312 revision 17 is not consulted) and a chain reaction on a resource cell (S-0401 claim 20, the economy topic) take no draw and leave a note; a resource glint (S-0202 claim 14) has no position in any spec, so its middle event takes only its particle draws and notes the rest. Animations created in play with no loop delay (warp, weapon, warhead and death animations) take the same draws through the same function (S-0202 claim 15). A middle event later in playback (μ > 0) comes on the advance that reaches μ, in the animation's turn (`anims`, M8 step A5).
- **Owners that are not human-controlled.** An AI house counts as human-controlled (glossary *AI house*); the neutral and special houses do not, and keep the original's rules for such owners (glossary *Player houses only*): skill level 0 (S-0209 claim 11), starting units in *area guard* (S-0009 claim 45), crates blocking their walkers (S-0209 row WO1), the idle scatter of a frightened fraidycat (S-0107 claim 14), lying down while moving (S-0107 claim 15), the computer-controlled branch of S-0208 claims 11 and 15, *area guard* when a unit gives up after a failed path search (S-0212 claim 23 row 2; its house's centre choice is S-0215 claim 38's, `base::selected_centre`), the resting rows 7 to 10 of S-0215 claim 14, a harvester's harvest wherever it stands (S-0215 claim 15 row 4) and the auto-deploy row of S-0215 claim 31 (with the auto-deploy list's entry for difficulty index 1; without one the row does not apply), *hunt* for the crews and survivors a death releases (S-0312 claim 25), and the attack-target crushing approach and the omni-crusher's opportunity-target exemption (S-0210 claim 11; both crushing approaches replace the approach step of S-0215 claim 37 (h), `approach::step`). Their house rate-of-fire and firepower factors are exactly 1.0 (S-0301 claim 3, S-0302 claim 3, S-0412 claim 5). `House::human` carries the distinction. A player house with `human` clear is a *test-only computer-controlled player house* (glossary revision 58; S-0215 and S-0309 Terms, M7b step 1c): it takes every one of these computer-owner rules too, the acquisition rules of S-0309 claim 25 (`combat::acquire::player_owned` is "a player house with `human` set") and the retaliation of S-0215 claim 18 included, but keeps its player-house setup (row factors, credits). Only the oracle arena makes one, and it sets the oracle house's skill level and difficulty index. The auto-deploy delay reads the list's entry for the owner's difficulty index (index 0 for such a house; `GeneralRules::auto_deploy_delays`). Seams for such a house: S-0309 claim 25 parts g and i read difficulty index 1 only, the automatic repair start uses R(0, 0) where the original computer player uses its difficulty row's repair-start delay (S-0314 claim 15 and F4; the difficulty rows are not imported), and the damage scatter of vehicles (S-0215 claim 19) is not built.
- **Selection.** `targeting::selectable` is S-0214's *can be selected*: a unit or building in play whose type's selection flag (`selectable`, default yes) permits it, but not a building whose type is invisible in the game; a building awaiting removal with 0 health stays selectable (claim 16). Units below the surface and cloaked units are an open question of S-0214; M3 has neither state. The acknowledgement's response (S-0214 claim 14, F5) is `targeting::selection_response` with n from `targeting::selection_responses`, the count of the type's imported selection-response list (`voice_select`, every entry kept). The power, alliance and surrender orders of S-0213 table T1 now point to S-0405 claim 10 and S-0414 claims 11 to 14 and are not implemented (no order variant yet), nor are S-0214 claim 13's sell, repair and power modes.
