- **Hut and cursor (Q-0715, Q-0716).** The hut cell is the cell of the hut's position, its top-left foundation cell (Terms), and the engineer's own cell is the repair's start (claim 18), as `bridges::hut` already did. The repair cursor (`repair_offered`, claim 21) takes the last qualifying cell of the hut's 5 × 5 block, in increasing y and then x: a tile of either bridge role, otherwise a low piece. A low candidate decides by its own row's run. A high candidate's role position picks a probe and a direction from the claim's table, and the test follows enabled links whose end is the traversal cell until it finds a disabled link (repair), no link (none) or, with `quirks.bridge_cursor_unrelated_link`, an enabled link with neither end there (repair). A repeated traversal cell refuses repair; the warning the Edge cases ask for is not written, because the cursor test is a read-only query.

## Simulation (M6b: cloak and stealth)

S-0516 (cloak and stealth) is built in `glasnost_sim::cloak`. The general cloak fields, the per-type cloak, sensor, detector and disguise keys, and the weapon and warhead keys are imported by `glasnost_convert`. The ledger is [docs/m6-claims/S-0516.md](m6-claims/S-0516.md); the tests are in `crates/deterministic/sim/tests/claims_s0516_*.rs`, on the invented rules of `tests/cloak_common/mod.rs`.

- **The machine (claims 1 to 18 and 41 to 43).** `cloak::machine` holds the four states and their stages. `machine::turn` is the ordinary cloak update (claim 42): a mobile object's runs in `tick::object_turn` after its due behaviour processing and before locomotion and the separate firing; a building's runs at the end of its turn, after its behaviour. `machine::late_turn` (aircraft target checks, disguise) follows at the end of every object's turn. `machine::uncloak` is the one call of every uncloak cause. Hooks:
  - movement for the cells entered; the cell-arrival update (`machine::cell_movement_completed`, the sensor disc move, even within the same cell, and the adjacent-sensor reveal) comes from the locomotor by claim 22's table: `drive` at a track target, `walk` at an arrival and snap, `hover` at S-0503 claim 15's non-refusing choices, `teleport::jump` in the jump tick (after a vehicle's arrival reporting and crushing, before the entered-by raise), the superweapon warp at the member's arrival-effects update (`superweapons::warp::arrive`, after the fate test, for survivors only; its removal and appearance leave the disc at the old recorded cell), `fall::turn` at a completed landing (after falling ends, before the parachute is detached); ordinary flight and rocket flight have none (`machine::flight_cell_crossed`, `quirks.flying_sensor_disc_stationary`); a jumpjet's touchdown, underground emergence, mechanical walking and drop-pod descent are seams of unbuilt locomotors, and entering play (unloading included) adds the disc with no adjacency test;
  - `combat::damage` for hits, `combat::fire` for firing, `crush` for crushing;
  - `econ::events` for entering and leaving the map and owner changes.

  The delay `CloakDelay` is read in single precision (F3), so the test rules' delay of 0.01 minutes is 8 ticks, not 9. An underwater vessel is submerged while it is not uncloaked (`naval::submerged`, claim 41).
- **Coverage (claims 19 to 23 and 40).** `cloak::field` grows and shrinks cloak fields ring by ring per house, in increasing Y then X, stopping at own building centres, the generator's own included (`quirks.cloak_field_building_interrupt`). Each generator records whether growth or shrinking is pending (`cloak::FieldPhase`) and whether it was in operation at its last turn (`Generator::was_on`): a new generator is idle at radius 0, and the turn that detects a change in operation applies claim 20's table (`field::turn`; staying in or out of operation requests nothing, pending growth has no power gate). An accepted manual power-off makes shrinking pending even at radius 0, the radius becoming the type radius first (`field::switched_off`, called from `econ::power::switch`; `quirks.cloak_generator_zero_radius_shutdown`). Only an idle one in operation, at any radius, receives a neighbour restart. Removal and capture are immediate collapses that run the neighbour-restart test, also for a type radius of 0 (strict reach 16 square cells); a captured generator grows for its new owner only if in operation; `cloak::sensor` keeps per-house, per-cell sensor and disguise-detector counts (`World::cloak`).
- **Hidden objects (claims 24 to 29).** `cloak::hidden_from` is the game-state test, used by `combat::check`, `combat::acquire`, `air` and `targeting::selectable_by`. `cloak::view` holds the presentation queries (`look`, `shadow`, `pickable`, `deselects`, `on_radar`). They take a viewing house and are never read by the simulation.
- **Disguise (claims 30 to 40).** `cloak::disguise` holds the disguise state (`Object::disguise`), disguising by a hit, permanent disguise, disguise when still, loss, and `appears_disguised`. `appears_disguised` feeds targeting, threat, the walker rows of S-0209 and the radar colour (`econ::minimap`).
- **App, audio and AI.**
  - The live view draws through the local player's eyes (`mapview::live`, `set_viewer`, `apply_cloak`). The "rippling" look is drawn as 50 % translucent.
  - Cloak events take objects out of the selection (claim 26).
  - Audio plays the game-wide cloak sound (claim 15).
  - The own AI does not see a cloaked enemy it does not sense.
- **Seams.** These are named functions with doc comments in `cloak/mod.rs`:
  - C4 planting (S-0519) and the special-weapon causes call `machine::uncloak`;
  - cloaking crates (S-0520, built) call `machine::grant_own_flag`;
  - undeploying (S-0517) calls `disguise::clear`;
  - a house leaving (S-0414) calls `disguise::house_left`;
  - the crush response to a hit (S-0210 claim 11, S-0215 claim 39; `crush::respond_to_hit`) reads `appears_disguised`;
  - seizing (S-0607), paralysis (S-0214), the chrono warp (S-0514), infiltration (S-0510), mind control (S-0515), and bunkers and occupied buildings (S-0508, S-0509).
- **Quirks.** All fourteen are implemented, each on by default:
  - `quirks.building_sensor_ring_owner_only`;
  - `quirks.cloak_field_building_interrupt`;
  - `quirks.cloak_field_overlap_loss`;
  - `quirks.cloak_flag_ground_mobile_only`;
  - `quirks.cloak_generator_zero_radius_shutdown`;
  - `quirks.flying_sensor_disc_stationary`;
  - `quirks.cloak_zero_speed_stall`;
  - `quirks.heal_reveals_cloaked`;
  - `quirks.mirage_adjacent_check_cadence`;
  - `quirks.mobile_sensor_removal_centre`;
  - `quirks.sensor_array_removal_radius`;
  - `quirks.sensor_building_activation_once`;
  - `quirks.sensor_count_negative`;
  - `quirks.uncloak_from_cloaking_full_duration`.
- **Save format 22.** `Object::cloak`, `Object::disguise` and `World::cloak` are new state, and they are hashed. Format 28 adds `cloak::Generator::phase` (S-0516 claim 20: growth or shrinking pending). Revision 8 adds `cloak::Generator::was_on` (`serde(default)`; the format number is not yet bumped).
- **Not built.**
  - The app does not draw the disguise image (claim 37), and the cursor, insignia and hover name of claim 36 are not wired into the status drawing.
  - Buildings drawn as static map scenery get no translucency.
  - The importer does not warn about an empty mirage disguise list.
  - The importer warns about a `CloakingStages` of 0 or below (imported as 1) and a negative `CloakRadiusInCells` (no field), claims 20 and 43.
- **Local lane.** `GLASNOST_GAME_DIR=<install> GLASNOST_LANE_SCENE=cloaked-as-owner,cloaked-as-enemy cargo test --release -p glasnost-app --test m6b_lane -- --ignored --nocapture` places a cloaking vehicle near the local start, with a plain vehicle beside it. It uses a driving vehicle type with the cloak flag if the ruleset has one, else it gives a plain vehicle its own flag through the crate seam.
  - `cloaked-as-owner.png` is the local house's own vehicle, fully cloaked after 36 ticks. It is drawn 50 % translucent.
  - For an enemy house's vehicle, the lane uncloaks it and waits for the fade back. `cloaking-as-enemy.png` shows it translucent mid-fade, 21 ticks later. `cloaked-as-enemy.png` shows it fully cloaked 14 ticks after that, when it is no longer drawn.

  On the first run on the user's install, the scenes took 10 s in all.

S-0516 revision 8 answers Q-0795 and Q-0796, and their rows are retired. `cloak::machine::arrival_source` is gone: no cell-arrival update comes from the cloak turn any more; each source above calls `cell_movement_completed` itself. Unbuilt locomotors (S-0502's jumpjet touchdown and the other modes) are seams. The generator's phases follow claim 20's table, edge-triggered on the recorded operation state.

S-0516 revision 7 answers Q-0791 (claim 22's table of cell-arrival updates by locomotor, `quirks.flying_sensor_disc_stationary`, same-cell relocation) and Q-0792 (own-centre interruption on the first radius-1 update; idle means no growth or shrinking pending, whatever the radius). Their rows are retired. It also lets uncloaking at stage 0 complete with speed 0 (`quirks.cloak_zero_speed_stall` stalls only a positive stage).

## Simulation (M6b: superweapons)

S-0512 (charge and readiness) and the S-0513 effects of the Allied and Soviet kinds are built in `glasnost_sim::superweapons`. The kinds are the nuclear strike, the weather storm, invulnerability (iron curtain), the teleport (chronosphere), the force shield, the paradrop and the spy plane. The ledgers are [docs/m6-claims/S-0512.md](m6-claims/S-0512.md) and [docs/m6-claims/S-0513.md](m6-claims/S-0513.md); the revision 7 and 10 changes are classified in [docs/m6-claims/step2-sw.md](m6-claims/step2-sw.md). The tests are `crates/deterministic/sim/tests/claims_s0512.rs`, `claims_s0512_words.rs` and `claims_s0513_{nuke,storm,protect,warp,planes}.rs`, on the invented rules of `tests/sw_common/mod.rs`.

- **Words (S-0512 claims 23 and 24).** The twelve kind and link words and the aim-action vocabulary are `glasnost_rules::sim_econ::SUPERWEAPON_KIND_WORDS` and `SUPERWEAPON_ACTION_WORDS`. The importer (`convert::sim_econ`) keeps the previous layer's kind or link for a word outside the vocabulary and keeps the previous action for an empty one; a nonempty word outside the action vocabulary becomes the explicit word `none`. An icon with no aim action fires at cell (0, 0) without aiming (`Play::cameo_click`).
- **Records and charge (S-0512).** `superweapons::charge` holds one `Record` per house and type in `World::sw`. Grants, suspension, loss, the one-time and permanent trigger grants, set and reset charge, drain and manual modes, the flash window and the countdown rows all run in the house update (`charge::house_update`, `charge::after_tech`). A fire order (`orders::Order::FireSuperweapon`) is carried out by `launch::fire`, which consumes the charge and dispatches the kind's effect.
- **Nuclear strike (claims 7 to 13).** `superweapons::nuke`. The silo is the building owned longest. It launches an upward missile on its next update and stays busy until guard would be queued, 8 ticks after it accepted the launch (claim 47); the missile-maker burst creates the falling payload (F8). A one-time record drops the payload from 20 000 leptons (F2). The warning marker is an animation. The payload leaves radiation like any radiation-weapon detonation (`radiation::detonation`, M6c: radiation fields).
- **Weather storm (claims 14 to 21).** `superweapons::storm`. The storm states of T1 run in the weather update at row 3 of the tick pipeline, before the turn pass. It covers the countdown and warnings, the radar outage of the other houses, the centre and scatter clouds (F5, F6), strikes with the bolt, area damage and debris, and clearing. A cloud plays as an ordinary forward one-pass animation (claim 46): its stage delay comes from the creation draw, a constant random-rate delay or the `Rate` field, and it is tested against the frame count of its image and the canvas height of the first bolt image, both imported from the sprites (`AnimationType::frame_count`, `canvas_height`, `rate`, `normalized`). Playback fields beyond the rate (start, end, loops, reverse, ping-pong, shadow, follow-up) are a seam (S-0109 claim 28). The strike witness (claim 18) is the ground-listed candidate nearest the cell's top corner. Trigger actions 44 and 45 start and stop a storm; action 109 is the waypoint curtain.
- **Invulnerability and the force shield (claims 22 to 32).** `superweapons::protect`. The protection timer is `Object::sw.protection` (F7). Its consumers are the hit table, the small-detonation nullification, the firing answers, crushing and the occupancy row. The curtain releases a parasite through `parasite::host_curtain_started`. The force shield blacks out the launching house and shields allied buildings within F3's radius. PLAN.md lists the force shield under M7; it is built here because S-0513 specifies it with the others.
- **Teleport (claims 33 to 41).** `superweapons::warp`. A source pick emits the player-local marker event. The warp walks the source block (F1, claim 4). For each examined object it first releases a naval parasite with 500 ticks of suppression (`parasite::superweapon_teleport`). It then kills organic objects (claim 35), leaves the excluded ones in place, and makes the rest members (`Object::sw.warp`). Each member's stages (T2) run at the start of its own living turn (`warp::turn`, from `tick::object_turn`): leaving, the 60-tick wait, departure, the landing check and redirect, placement, arrival fate, and the arrival hold. `teleport::phasing_in` and `being_erased` read the member's stage. The departure does not release a drone parasite (S-0614 claim 12, P7), so it does not use `objects::take_inside`. The chrono weapons of S-0514 are the seam `warp::chrono_seam`.
- **Paradrop and spy plane (claims 42 to 45; S-0522 claims 1 to 3).** `superweapons::planes`. Planes are sent from the house's edge cell. Paratroopers board the plane. The passes and the drop are S-0522, in the section below. The spy plane's camera reveals through `econ::shroud::camera` in its approach and overfly.
- **Quirks.** Every switch of S-0512 and S-0513 is implemented and on by default.
- **Save format 26 (changed since by step 2).** The records, the storm, the protection timers, the warp members and the held delay are new state, and they are hashed. Step 2 adds the silo's busy-until tick (`ObjSw::silo_busy_until`) and changes the meaning of `Cloud::delay` (the effective stage delay); the format number is not bumped here. `warp_state_survives_save_and_load` saves mid-warp and checks the hash.
- **Own AI.** The brain (`glasnost_ai::brain`) builds, one after the other, the cheapest building it can build and place that gives a kind of superweapon it has no building for yet (M6c step 2b: every Allied and Soviet kind, that is the nuclear strike, the storm, the iron curtain, the teleport, the force shield, the spy plane and the two paradrops; the Yuri kinds stay unbuilt, `launch::yuri_seam`). The kinds come from the buildings' superweapon fields and the types' kind words in the imported rules (`Brain::giving_kinds`, `fired_kind`), no building name is in the code. It does so once its harvesters are kept and while it is not selling out. A giving building that the tech tree still blocks is reached through the building that clears the blocker (the tech climb's `unblocker`, depth `CLIMB_DEPTH`), as a person climbs to a superweapon, and the cheapest of the buildable giving buildings and these clearing buildings is built next. The teleport's first stage wants two armed vehicles in a block (the curtain still three, `TELEPORT_MIN`). What the local lane showed (`ai-sides` on map 8, seed 7, one game per pair of sides, counts in `local-lane/m6c/ai-sides-map8-b.txt`): the paradrop (American), the spy plane and the teleport were built and launched (7, 5 and 1 launches in one game), the force shield was built in all three games and ready for 16 000 to 57 000 house-ticks with one launch in one game (its cell needs two own buildings in a block with a seen enemy near and nothing of anyone else, which a house that wins away from home rarely has), the missile, the storm and the curtain were never built in these games: both houses of a game have near zero credits for most of the game (three army lines spend them), so a costly building waits; two houses had a placeable giving building and no credits. Not changed: the AI does not hold back its army to save for a superweapon. This build comes before the tech climb but after a building the plan wants for power, a refinery or a factory. Each tick, it fires every granted, ready and not suspended missile or storm record. It fires through `Order::FireSuperweapon`, at the seen enemy cell with the most seen enemies within 3 cells and none of its own objects (`Brain::strike_cell`). A storm is fired only while none is under way. The other kinds (M6b2, `crates/deterministic/ai/tests/ai_sw.rs`; the building of each is built since M6c, `ai_sw_build.rs`) are fired when ready and the house owns the giving building: an iron curtain on the block (a cell and its eight neighbours, S-0513 claim 4) with the most of its armed non-organic vehicles, at least 3, with a seen enemy within 10 cells and none of its infantry (claim 23) and nothing of anyone else in the block (`Brain::curtain_cell`); a force shield on the block with the most of its buildings, at least 2, with a seen enemy within 12 cells and nothing of anyone else in it (`shield_cell`); a spy plane at the cell the next wave would attack; the teleport's first stage on a vehicle block like the curtain's but with no fight needed, only while no source is held, and its second stage at the first cell 4 to 9 cells around the enemy base whose whole block is free usable land (`group_cell`, `landing_cell`). The numbers are tuning of our own opponent. Tests: `fires_a_ready_superweapon_at_seen_enemies` (debug) and `builds_and_fires_a_superweapon` (release) in `crates/deterministic/ai/tests/ai.rs`; `curtains_a_fighting_group_of_vehicles`, `shields_buildings_with_an_enemy_at_them`, `sends_the_spy_plane_at_the_enemy` and `teleports_a_group_to_the_enemy_base` in `ai_sw.rs`.
- **Fixture and gate.** The synthetic skirmish (`glasnost_ai::fixture`) adds NUKE (the missile kind, charge 1 minute = 900 ticks), its silo SILO (needs LAB), the upward weapon LIFT and the falling NukePayload. Every gate duel saves and loads at the first tick with a granted record still charging. The straight and the diagonal duels must launch a superweapon (`SwEvent::Launch`). The naval duel is short of credits and never reaches LAB, so it neither charges nor launches, and it still does not fly a rocket. Its `flies` flag stays off.
- **Local lane.** `GLASNOST_GAME_DIR=<install> GLASNOST_LANE_SCENE=sw-nuke,sw-storm,sw-iron-curtain,sw-chronosphere,sw-force-shield,sw-paradrop-plane,sw-spy-plane cargo test --release -p glasnost-app --test m6b_lane -- --ignored --nocapture` readies the local house's first type of each kind. It places the building that gives the type, with power plants until the house has enough power, sets a full charge and aims through the client (`Tool::Superweapon`, then a left click). It writes `local-lane/m6b/sw-*.png`. On the run after merging main, on the user's install (map 0):
  - nuclear strike: the launch event came 1 tick after the order, and the strike 399 ticks after it; it damaged all three enemy vehicles and destroyed none;
  - weather storm: the storm started 251 ticks after the order, with the first lightning at 285 ticks;
  - iron curtain: 3 of 3 vehicles were protected 1 tick after the order;
  - teleport: the source pick armed list position 4 (S-0513 claim 33), and the destination click warped 3 of 3 vehicles, which stood at the destination 66 ticks later;
  - force shield: the shield started 1 tick after the order, aimed at an own building;
  - paradrop and spy plane: no type found (the `paratroopers-dropping` scene of S-0522 sends the plane without a type). The types at list positions 4, 5, 6, 8 and 9 (5 of 12) had a kind word that the interim words did not recognise. Revision 7 of S-0512 (claim 24) gives the words, so these types resolve now; the lane has not been run since, and its fallback through `planes` stays in case a type is not found.

  The screenshots show the explosion, a bolt under the clouds, the curtain's invoke animation, the group landed with the source markers fading, and the shielded building. The iron-curtain and force-shield shots have no tint (not built: S-0513 claim 29's brightness phases and F9, S-0102 claim 34).
- **Not built.**
  - The Yuri kinds (S-0610, S-0611, S-0612) are `launch::yuri_seam`.
  - Tank bunker links (S-0606) do not exist, so the warp has none to end.

Provisional rules of M6b superweapons, each tied to a request: none are open (Q-0870 and Q-0871 answered by S-0513 revision 14).

S-0203 revision 32, S-0301 revision 18 and S-0215 revision 48 (with S-0513 revision 14 claim 51) answer Q-0870 (step 6a, [docs/m6-claims/step6a.md](m6-claims/step6a.md)): the warp update is the whole member turn from the first pending update through arrival effects (`tick::object_turn`), the reload countdown still elapses (`combat::fire::reload_elapsed`), and the expected-health bookkeeping and the protection envelope's phases are ordinary work that resumes after the hold-end test. Its row is retired.

S-0512 revision 8 and S-0513 revision 11 (step 3, [docs/m6-claims/step3-sw.md](m6-claims/step3-sw.md)) answer Q-0738 (claim 48) and Q-0741 (claims 33 and 49); the rows above keep only what those claims mark unmeasured, under the new requests Q-0850 and Q-0851. S-0513 revision 12 answers Q-0800 (step 4, [docs/m6-claims/step4.md](m6-claims/step4.md)): `storm::cell_state` measures a building witness at its foundation centre (`World::reference_point`, remainders modulo 256 compared with (0, 0)) and a building is listed in every actual foundation cell; its row is retired. Q-0850 and Q-0851 stayed open until revision 13 (step 5, [docs/m6-claims/step5.md](m6-claims/step5.md)), whose claims 50 and 51 are the engine rules `warp::*` now builds: the fallback cell (0, 0) for an off-map destination and a failed search, an illegal landing cell is blocked and never placed, a refused placement returns the member to the landing retry (alive, no cap, no timeout), the first fallback and the first refusal per member and warp are reported as world notes, launch clears the path, the queued destinations and the walking, driving and hover steps, and target and destination are dropped in the first update. Their interim rows are retired; Q-0870 and Q-0871 are new.

S-0512 revision 7 answers Q-0730, Q-0731 and Q-0737, and S-0513 revision 10 answers Q-0732, Q-0734, Q-0735, Q-0739 and Q-0740 (the lead closes the request files); their interim rows are gone. Revision 10 also answers Q-0733 fully (claim 29 for buildings, the force shield and missed turns) and part 1 of Q-0741 (claim 33: the source marker takes its creation draws on every pick).

S-0513 revision 9 answers Q-0733 (claim 29): the draw R(−5, 5) is taken on entering Initial dimming, in the turn *q* + 10 for an object with uninterrupted turns, *q* being its first turn while this application protects it. A launch applied after the turn pass in tick *t*₀ therefore draws in tick *t*₀ + 11. `protect::tint_turn` advances `Protection::tint` (before the first turn, Initial rise of 6 ticks, Bright hold of 4 ticks) by at most one phase per turn and takes the draw on entering Initial dimming. The later phases, their brightness factors and F9 are presentation and not built. The request file still reads open; the lead closes it. Tests: `claim29_tint_draw_in_turn_q_plus_ten`, `claim29_launch_after_the_turn_pass_draws_at_t0_plus_eleven`, `claim29_reapplication_restarts_the_envelope`.

## Simulation (M6b: parachutes and drops)

S-0522 is built in `glasnost_sim::paradrop`, on the plane sending of S-0513 (`superweapons::planes`). The ledger is [docs/m6-claims/S-0522.md](m6-claims/S-0522.md). The tests are `crates/deterministic/sim/tests/claims_s0522.rs`, with the launch side in `claims_s0513_planes.rs` and the arrival edge in `claims_s0501.rs`.

- **Passes (claims 6 to 8).** `AirState` holds the pass counter (`passes`, 5 at sending and after each successful drop) and the paradrop flag. `air::process` dispatches *paradrop approach* to `paradrop::approach` (every 3 ticks) and *paradrop overfly* to `paradrop::overfly` (every 5 ticks). A plane that gives up retreats to its edge; there its paratroopers are removed silently before the plane (claim 8).
- **Drop attempt (claims 9 and 10).** `paradrop::attempt` takes one round, picks the side by the ammunition's parity, and steps 128 leptons sideways to the drop point. The entry outcome is tested first and takes no draw; then the standing position is selected (its draw), then the usable area, the deck-entry mark and the nearby-cell qualification. On success, `transport::drop_out` places the paratrooper at the plane's height, facing 32768, on the deck layer when it is at or above the deck height over a bridge cell and on the ground layer below it. The fall starts, the parachute sequence is requested, and the parachute animation's draws are taken. The `SwEvent::Parachute` and `SwEvent::Chute` events are emitted. On failure, the round is given back.
- **Shot drop (claim 11).** In `combat::fire::shot`, an aircraft carrying passengers makes a drop attempt in place of the whole ordinary shot: no delay or draw, burst advance, report, muzzle animation, spray draw or projectile. A successful drop expires the rearm countdown now; a refused one changes nothing, and both report a shot, so the run's pending round is made.
- **Fall and parachute (claims 12 and 13).** `FallState::chute` marks an attached parachute. While it is set, `fall` uses the parachute recurrence, and the landing surface is the deck's for deck-layer objects. The chute is cleared on landing. `infantry` refuses other sequences while a paratrooper falls with its parachute sequence, and requests stand once it has landed (S-0107 claim 6). For a held parachute type both settings of `quirks.paratrooper_any_animation_slow_fall` give the same result, because the parachute is the only animation the simulation attaches. With no type held, the setting on gives the parachute recurrence once in the release-tick update (`FallState::chute_once`) and then the no-parachute one; off gives the no-parachute one from the first update. A name the rules give that no type lists was created as an animation type by the importer and is attached normally.
- **Losses (claims 14 and 15).** A paratrooper killed in descent is removed like any infantry. A shot-down plane kills only the passengers still aboard.
- **Drawing.** The app attaches the parachute animation to its paratrooper on the `SwEvent::Parachute` event (`Live::attach_parachute`, S-0109 claim 22). The animation follows the unit in the ground layer, ordered by the ground sort key plus its sort adjustment, with the carrier's house-colour unit palette and the cell light sampled at attachment (claim 18), and disappears with the unit. When the unit lands, its current pass becomes its last (`Live::follow_owners`). The chute sound event is not played, because the app plays no superweapon sounds yet.
- **Revision 5 (step 3, [docs/m6-claims/step3-sw.md](m6-claims/step3-sw.md)).** A falling infantry unit is not ready to change (S-0215 claim 11, `process::ready`), so a fresh paratrooper's queued rest starts only after landing and its due processing in the air chooses 450 ticks (S-0215 claim 4, scenario B44). The release adds the sensor disc at once and the landing is a cell-arrival update (already built, tested in `claims_s0522.rs`). The S-0301 revision 17 drop-attempt rules (no ordinary delay, burst step or draw; a round spent at once; not a void shot) are tested there too.
- **Quirks.** `quirks.paradrop_fallback_point`, `quirks.paradrop_west_edge_start_row` and `quirks.paratrooper_any_animation_slow_fall` are implemented, each on by default. No switch is new.
- **Save format 27 (changed since by step 2).** The pass counter, the paradrop flag and the chute mark are new state, and they are hashed. Step 2 adds the one-update chute mark (`FallState::chute_once`); the format number is not bumped here. `save_load_mid_descent_round_trips` saves mid-descent and checks the hash.
- **Own AI.** The brain fires every ready paradrop or American paradrop record at a cell 3 to 8 cells around its home: the first usable cell with no object on it (`Brain::drop_cell`). Infantry types listed in a paradrop payload count as army when they are armed (`scripted::of_role`), so landed paratroopers join the army. Test: `fires_a_paradrop_whose_troops_join_the_army` in `crates/deterministic/ai/tests/ai.rs`. The brain does not build a building for a paradrop.
- **Local lane.** `GLASNOST_GAME_DIR=<install> GLASNOST_LANE_SCENE=paratroopers-dropping cargo test --release -p glasnost-app --test m6b_lane -- --ignored --nocapture` sends a plane to a cell near home. No type had a paradrop kind on the user's install (Q-0730), so the scene sent it straight through `planes::paradrop`. It writes `local-lane/m6b/paratroopers-dropping.png` and, 60 ticks later, `paratroopers-descending.png`. On the user's install (map 0):
  - one plane was sent, and the first drop came 398 ticks after the order;
  - 3 paratroopers were descending at 433 ticks, and 4 at 493;
  - 6 were dropped, and all 6 stood on the ground 1055 ticks after the order;
  - at both shots, every parachute animation had art and was drawn.

  The second screenshot shows four paratroopers under their parachutes and the plane leaving.
- **Seams.**
  - Parachute reinforcements from triggers (S-0011 claim 30) are not built.
  - The not-human-type exception of claim 14 keeps S-0107 claim 17's open outcome.
  - The simulation has no animation clock (S-0109 claim 28), so the chute mark lasts until landing.
  - The falling-damage multiplier is not applied by `fall`; the magnetic-lift impact applies it in `lift` (S-0607 F2), for lifted objects only.
  - Once landed, a paratrooper is ordinary infantry, so the M6c infantry features (garrisons S-0508, capture S-0509, infiltration S-0510, deployers S-0517) need nothing from `paradrop`. A falling paratrooper is on the map in the ground or deck layer, so M6c area effects (chrono weapons S-0514, radiation S-0525) and M7 effects reach it through the ordinary object lists.

Provisional rules of M6b parachutes and drops, each tied to a request: none are open since S-0522 revision 4 (Q-0736, Q-0750 to Q-0754 are answered and their interim rows are gone).

## Simulation (M6b: multiplayer triggers)

S-0524 is built in `glasnost_sim::mp_triggers`, on the trigger runtime of S-0011 (`triggers`). The ledger is [docs/m6-claims/S-0524.md](m6-claims/S-0524.md). The tests are `crates/deterministic/sim/tests/claims_s0524.rs`.

- **Import.** The converter reads each action's fourth to seventh items into `Action::items` (code 40's rectangle) and the trigger's field 8 into `TriggerDef::carry_over`. The demolition warhead's `Bright` and `CombatLightSize` are read into `WarheadRules::bright` and `light_size`.
- **Storm, strike and nuclear actions (claims 1 to 3).** Code 102 requests a storm through `storm::request`, owned by the trigger's owner house. Code 90 makes a cloud through `mp_triggers::lightning_strike`, which records the waypoint for `quirks.repeated_lightning_strike_suppressed`. Code 95 launches the payload through `nuke::waypoint`.
- **Demolition blast and flash (claims 5 and 6).** An undefined marker acts at the stand-in point (128, 128, 0) and makes no bridge attempt. `demolition_blast` makes five area detonations with the demolition warhead and no source or house, then calls `bridges::cell_attempts`. `light_flash` pushes an `Effect::Trigger` with code 66, the waypoint point and the flash-size level as its number; the presentation does not draw it yet.
- **Ambient (claim 7).** `TriggerState::mp.ambient` holds the base and target values code 73 set; the renderer does not read them yet.
- **Local area (claim 8).** Shrinking touches no object (it does not remove, move, stop or switch off buildings or units; an excluded power source and an excluded building still count; an ordered attack on an object outside is still an attack). `resize_local_area` sets the footprint's local area, recalculates every legal cell, recomputes the passability categories and rebuilds the reach regions.
- **Restoration (claims 9 and 13).** The record is in creation order with top-left cells; a building of the type and house that covers the recorded cell with any foundation cell is healed; otherwise a fresh building is placed by ordinary during-play placement (construction queued; claim 15, Q-0840 answered: the ready mark is clear at entry, the placed case of S-0406 claim 17, even without a build-up sprite) and the ground-layer occupants of its foundation are re-placed in reverse lifting order by the 1 x 1 nearby search from their old cells, facing 0, removed when no cell is found. `record_starting_buildings` runs in `load::new_game` after the game-start terrain pass. `restore_starting_buildings` heals a recorded building that is still there, and otherwise replaces it, displacing mobile ground units.
- **Entries (claims 10 to 14).** An infantry unit arriving at a transport raises its tag once before the acceptance test (`transport::arrival`); vehicle boarding, depot and refinery docking and pad landing raise nothing (tested in the owning claim files); capture makes no ownership-change raise; `vehicle_like_entry_seam` is new. `carry_over` moves a flagged tag to the captured or hijacked object. `capture_seam`, `hijack_seam`, `consuming_entry_seam` and `retaining_entry_seam` hold the raise order for S-0509, S-0312 claim 13's hijack, S-0510 and S-0508 when those are built.
- **Quirks.** `restoration_failed_return_removes_unit` (on), `repeated_lightning_strike_suppressed`, `trigger_crate_respects_setting` and `trigger_crate_expiry_while_disabled` (off: off is the original) are new (443 with the upgrade switches of S-0215). The two crate switches are not consulted, because crates (S-0520) are not built.
- **Save format 28.** The trigger definitions' new fields, the warhead's flash fields and `TriggerState::mp` are new state, and they are hashed.
- **Local lane.** `GLASNOST_GAME_DIR=<install> GLASNOST_LANE_SCENE=trigger-coverage cargo test --release -p glasnost-app --test m6b_lane -- --ignored --nocapture` starts every offered map and counts its trigger events and actions as implemented, seamed or outside the subset (`mp_triggers::event_coverage`, `action_coverage`).
- **Seams.**
  - Code 108 (crates) places a crate through `crates::place_trigger` (S-0520).
  - Engineer capture (S-0509), hijacking (S-0312 claim 13), garrisons (S-0508), infiltration (S-0510) and sabotage call nothing yet; their seams are in `mp_triggers`.
  - Teams, computer targeting, change owner, destroy attached objects, explosions, cheering and the waypoint animations stay as S-0011 left them.

Provisional rules of M6b multiplayer triggers, each tied to a request:

| Where | Interim rule | Request |
|---|---|---|
| `mp_triggers::resolve_house` | position values A to H take the house at that start position; S-0009 claim 29's rule for header counts of 9 or more is not applied | — |

Aircraft shadow anchor (S-0105 claim 40, Q-0799 answered): `mapview::build::vehicle_voxels` draws an aircraft shadow at the projection of its horizontal position at the terrain height there (`Mobile::shadow`, from `live::shadow_anchor`; a map-only scene reads the terrain from the map), 60 pixels higher over a qualifying high bridge, whatever its flight height. Q-0841 is answered by S-0501 revision 15, claim 43: an aircraft counts as marked on a deck only by its own deck mark (`AirState::deck`, read by `live::shadow_anchor`), never by its layer or its listing. The mark is set by a descent that reaches height 0 above a high-bridge deck or a landing that finishes at deck height, cleared by an ordinary climbing tick or a refused touchdown, and left alone by horizontal flight (`air::tick`, `air::touchdown`). Altitude calculations use the deck as their reference for a marked aircraft, and for an unmarked one at least 416 leptons above a high-bridge cell (`air::height`); `air::listing_height` and `air::sync_listing` list a flying aircraft in the ground layer, the deck layer or neither (S-0209 claim 13). Vehicle shadow anchors stay as the body point (open in S-0105). The paradrop plane's flight height (1500 leptons in the lane) is drawn. The pitch of a rocket body and the pitch and bank of a flying aircraft are drawn since M6c step 1, section *Rendering of aircraft attitude (M6c)* below.

Save format 29 to 31 (M6b2): one bump for the step-2 to step-4 state changes: the silo's busy-until tick (`ObjSw::silo_busy_until`), `FallState::chute_once`, `Cloud::delay` meaning the effective stage delay, and the cloak generator's `was_on` phase mark. Format 31 adds the warp member's `entry_ok` (the claim 50 placement check). Format 30 adds `AirState::deck` (the aircraft deck mark, S-0501 claim 43) and the warp member's `fell_back` and `refused` marks (S-0513 claims 50 and 51). Format 28 is the multiplayer trigger and driving-destination state.

## Simulation (M6c: garrisons)

S-0508 (revision 7) is built in `glasnost_sim::structures` (`garrison`, `entry`, `ownership`); the ledger is [docs/m6-claims/S-0508.md](m6-claims/S-0508.md), the tests are `claims_s0508.rs` and `claims_s0508_fire.rs` (fixtures in `structures_common`). New state is `Object::structure` (`StructState`: a building's occupants in order, the shared occupant/muzzle index, an occupant's building); save format version 32.

- **Entry (claims 1 to 6).** `walk::tick` calls `structures::entry::arrival` at the arrival update, after the boarding test and before the arrival raise of S-0208 claim 22: an infantry unit in *capture* whose destination is a building that stands in its cell. An occupier or assaulter type makes the garrison attempt (`garrison::attempt`: the occupation test of S-0508 claim 1, `targeting::may_garrison`, shared with the cursor of S-0214 row I8; one entry raise; the unit leaves the map through `objects::take_inside` and is appended to the occupants; or the refusal of claim 4, `entry::refuse`). The announcement is `Effect::Sound::Garrisoned`, which the audio crate plays for the entering unit's house (`BuildingGarrisonedSound`, `EVA_StructureGarrisoned`).
- **The building's turn (claims 7 to 9).** `econ::events::turn` calls `garrison::turn` for a civilian garrison building (garrisonable, tech level exactly −1): a badly damaged one evicts, the owner follows the first occupant while it belongs to the civilian house, an empty one returns to the civilian house (`ownership::change`, which runs `econ::events::owner_changed`) with `Effect::Sound::Abandoned` for the owner it lost it from (`BuildingAbandonedSound`; the announcement only when the *garrison abandoned* radar event is created, F7).
- **Eviction (claims 10 to 13).** `garrison::evict` with the options of `Evict` (`PLAIN`: deploy order, forced evacuation, destruction, the map's action 111; `SALE`: the inside fallback; `HUNT`: the house-wide hunt action 6). The exit cell is the highest-ranked cell of `garrison::exit_ring` that qualifies for the first occupant (`garrison::qualifies`, revision 7 claim 10): there is no independent legal-cell or usable-area gate; a candidate with no map cell reads as the stand-in blank cell of S-0009 claim 44; terrain level −6 or lower is clear before the other tests (`quirks.garrison_negative_level_exit`, S-0208 claim 19's bypass); an ordinary occupant, which went in from inside the usable area and keeps that status (its retained position), is refused a cell outside the usable area (S-0209 claim 4), a legal one included; otherwise the *clear* entry outcome at reference level −1; the occupants are put at its centre from the last to the first, each with its scatter request (claims 11 and 22: the placement has no second entry test and no terrain, capacity or usable-area refusal; an occupant that is already on the map, an inconsistent state, is removed, not destroyed). A building's *unload* is `garrison::process_unload`; a sale calls `garrison::sold` (S-0408 claim 9, after absorbed passengers, before the survivors); a destroyed building calls `garrison::destroyed` (S-0312 claim 17); the trigger actions 111 and 6 are in `triggers::action`. The quirk switches `garrison_capacity_equality`, `garrison_exit_skips_corner`, `garrison_negative_level_exit`, `garrison_no_exit_removes` and `assaulter_reversed_kill_credit` are consulted.
- **Assault, erasure, defeat (claims 14, 15, 19).** `garrison::attempt` for an assaulter type runs `assault` (`assault_eligible`; the occupants removed with the weapon's occupant animation, `OccupantAnim`; reversed or ordinary kill credit through `damage::credit` and `death::destruction_raises`). `garrison::erased` is the call the erasure topic (S-0514, not built) will make; `objects::remove` also removes the occupants of a building that leaves play. The assault removes the occupants from the last list position to position 0, the occupant at position *i* showing its animation at muzzle point *i* (`garrison::assault_shift`: no wrap, an unwritten point is (0, 0); a position of 10 or later uses point *i* mod 10, S-0508 claim 25, below). An occupant killed inside (defeat's single hit on its owner's objects) goes through `death::passenger_death`: credited, destruction raises, removed from the world; claim 23 (an engine design rule, no switch) removes its place in the same update (`garrison::removing`, called from `objects::remove`): the others keep their order, the index is kept when below the new count and is 0 otherwise (a range check, no modulo), and the count, the capacity test, the delay quotient, the muzzle modulus and the experience recipient use the count after removal. With no civilian house the owner of a civilian garrison building is kept at every turn with no abandoned sound (claim 24, a design rule); the forced evacuation still empties it.
- **Firing from inside (S-0308 claim 13, S-0313 claim 6, S-0301 claim 7, S-0302 claim 2, S-0307 claim 4, S-0311 claim 8, S-0215 claim 32, S-0405 T1).** `combat::weapon::slot_weapon` takes the weapon of the current occupant (`occupant_weapon`) for a garrisoned building whose type lets occupants fire, `choose_slot` answers slot 0, `fire::full_delay` divides by the occupant count and the occupy factor, `damage::shot_damage` applies the garrison multiplier, `range::in_range` the garrison range, `position::fire_point` the muzzle point of the index, `fire::shot` advances the index (not for a disc laser), `veterancy::destruction_credited` gives an untrainable garrisoned building's experience to its occupant.
- **Importer.** `OccupantAnim` (weapon) and `MuzzleFlash`*n* (building art, one pixel pair per place of the occupant capacity, an unwritten point (0, 0), `garrison_muzzles`) are fields now; the audio names `BuildingGarrisonedSound` and `BuildingAbandonedSound` and the announcements `EVA_StructureGarrisoned` and `EVA_StructureAbandoned` are read by the audio crate.
- **Client.** The click is S-0214 row I8 (already in `targeting::click`, with the capture cursor). The live view draws the map's occupiable buildings with the game's own buildings (`mapview::live`, `live_gates`), and the body frame of an occupiable building is 2·[occupied] + [hurt] (S-0108 claim 2, `quirks::occupiable_frame`). The lane scene `garrison` (`crates/app/tests/m6c_lane.rs`) sends three occupiers into a map-placed building, puts them out with a deploy order and writes `local-lane/m6c/garrison-empty.png`, `garrison-occupied.png` and `garrison-evicted.png`.
- **Own AI.** See *Own AI (M6c: special abilities)* below.

There are no provisional rules of the garrisons left: Q-0970 to Q-0973 are answered by S-0508 revision 7, and Q-0990 (assault animation at list position 10 or later) by revision 8's claim 25, engine design rule with no quirk switch: `garrison::assault` shows the animation of list position *i* at muzzle point *i* mod 10, whatever the number of configured points, the occupant count or the firing index (S-0313 claim 6 points to it).

## Simulation (M6c: engineers and capture)

S-0509 is built in `glasnost_sim::structures` (`entry`, `engineer`, `ownership`); the ledger is [docs/m6-claims/S-0509.md](m6-claims/S-0509.md), the tests are `claims_s0509.rs`. New state: `House::lost_to_capture` (the mark of claim 11); save format version 33; the presentation event `Effect::Captured`.

- **The entry (claims 1 and 2).** `structures::entry::arrival` is the one dispatcher of an infantry unit's arrival at a building (called from `walk::tick`, after the boarding test): the behaviour is *capture*, *area guard* or *patrol* and the building, in a cell the unit stands in, is its destination or its target. An occupier or assaulter in *capture* with a building destination makes the garrison attempt (S-0508); a vehicle-like destination building is taken over (claim 17, `engineer::takeover`); an engineer at a bridge repair hut is S-0511's (`bridges::hut::engineer_entered_hut`, whose entry raise is the general raise); otherwise the general entry raise (`mp_triggers::entry_raise`) comes first and then an engineer follows `engineer::entry`, a spy S-0510 (`structures::spy`) and any other unit is refused and scattered (`entry::refuse`).
- **Engineer outcomes (claims 3 to 7).** `engineer::entry` judges the row after the general raise: arrival-friendly buildings (own house, allied by the engineer's house, or garrisonable and passive) are refused at full health and repaired below it (`quirks.engineer_repairs_during_sale` for a sale in progress); then the multi-engineer row (never reached in a skirmish, `engineer::multi_engineer` is false; the hit, F1 and F2, is `engineer::damaging_hit`), a non-capturable building (the engineer is used up, `quirks.engineer_lost_on_uncapturable`), a building being sold or erased (refused), and the capture. A capture is a second entry raise, the lost-to-capture mark on the house that owns the building then, the tag carry-over, `ownership::change` (cause `Capture`), the scatter of the infantry on the building (claim 9) and the used-up engineer (destroyed raise, removal).
- **Ownership change (claims 12 to 16).** `ownership::change` handles the contacts of the building (claim 14, F3), runs `econ::events::owner_changed` (power, shroud, factories, production, counts, the captured mark, cloak, superweapons through the tech recheck), clears a silo's slots (S-0506 claim 13) and pushes `Effect::Captured` for the audio crate (the capture and loss announcements of S-0701 claim 39, the *captured* radar event) and the client (claim 15: `Play::captured_yard_cancels_placement` cancels the placement in progress when the local house gains a construction yard). The planted-bomb disarming (claim 13) is a seam until S-0519; claim 16 (the computer-only sale) is the original's and never applies to our AI houses. The map's change-owner action (S-0011 claim 24, code 14) is built (`triggers::action`, cause `Trigger`, no announcement).
- **Importer and audio.** `BuildingRepairedSound` and `CaptureEvaEvent` are read by `glasnost_audio::names` from the imported text (a game-wide sound at the repaired building's centre; the tech building's capture announcement). The other keys were read by earlier specs.
- **Own AI.** See *Own AI (M6c: special abilities)*.
- **Client.** The click is S-0214 rows I4 to I7 (already in `targeting::click`: capture, damaging entry and repair building all give the *capture* behaviour order for an engineer, S-0214 table M), with the capture cursor frames. The lane scene `capture` of `crates/app/tests/m6c_lane.rs` sends an engineer into an enemy building and writes `local-lane/m6c/capture-before.png` and `capture-after.png` (the building drawn in the local house's colour).

## Simulation (M6c: spies and infiltration)

S-0510 is built in `glasnost_sim::structures::spy` (the entry dispatcher `structures::entry` hands a spy there after the general entry raise); the ledger is [docs/m6-claims/S-0510.md](m6-claims/S-0510.md), the tests are `claims_s0510.rs`. New state: `House::infiltrated_vehicle_factory` and `infiltrated_infantry_factory` (claim 5); save format version 34; the presentation event `Effect::Infiltrated`.

- **Rows (claims 1 to 4).** `spy::rows` lists the effects E1 to E7 of the building (radar recover, power blackout, stolen tech, superweapon reset, cash theft, vehicle and infantry factory flags); with `quirks.infiltration_single_effect` only the first applies, otherwise all do. The spy is used up (destroyed raise, removal, no kill or score). An allied or own-house building gives no effect.
- **Theft (claims 6 and 7).** `spy::theft_amount` reads `SpyMoneyStealPercent` through `Rules::spy_steal` in single precision (`Decimal::single_value`, absent 0.5); `quirks.spy_theft_unbacked_gain` decides what an out-of-range fraction does. The importer warns for a fraction outside 0 to 1.
- **Factory flags (claim 5).** `objects::veteran_at_creation` reads the flags: infantry after a barracks, trainable non-naval vehicles and undeploying buildings after a war factory. `spy::alternate_icon` gives the sidebar the alternate icon (S-0007 claim 25); the client draws it (`game/session.rs`).
- **Audio.** The infiltration lines and the *infiltrated* radar event are in `glasnost_audio::events` (`infiltrated`, `announce_entry`).
- **Own AI.** See *Own AI (M6c: special abilities)*.
- **Client.** The click is S-0214's (the infiltrate cursor, already in `targeting`). The lane scene `infiltrate` of `crates/app/tests/m6c_lane.rs` sends a spy into an enemy radar building and writes `local-lane/m6c/infiltrate-before.png` and `infiltrate-after.png`.

## Simulation (M6c: tech buildings)

S-0515 is built in `glasnost_sim::structures::tech`; the ledger is [docs/m6-claims/S-0515.md](m6-claims/S-0515.md), the tests are `claims_s0515.rs`. New state: `BuildingSwitches::income_restart` (the tick of the last restart of a building's income countdown); save format version 35. The manned state of claim 2 was built with S-0405 (`BuildingSwitches::manned`, set by `power::owner_changed`).

- **Bonus and restart (claims 3, 4, 7).** `econ::events::owner_changed` calls `tech::owner_changed` for a building whose owner really changes: the acquisition bonus (`ProduceCashStartup`) is added to the new owner's balance (signed 32-bit wrapping, no factor) when the old owner is a passive house (`setup::passive`), then the countdown restarts. `econ::events::entered_map` restarts it at a building's first entry to the map (claim 4 a); a countdown never restarted reads 0 and has no cycle.
- **The cycle (claims 5, 6, F1, F2).** `econ::events::turn` calls `tech::turn` in a building's own turn, after the power topic: with `D = ProduceCashDelay` at least 1, the cycle falls at the restart tick + *D* - 1 (`quirks.cash_cycle_one_short`; off: + *D*) and later ticks have none (`quirks.cash_cycle_lapse`; off: the first turn at or after the due tick has it). A building being erased (`power::being_erased`) has none; otherwise the cycle restarts the countdown and pays the current owner `ProduceCashAmount` by F2 when the owner is not passive and the building is in operation.
- **Secret labs (claim 8).** Nothing to build: the secret type is assigned at game start and stored on the building; an ownership change neither touches it nor draws (tested).
- **Importer.** `ProduceCashStartup`, `ProduceCashAmount` and `ProduceCashDelay` are integer fields of a building type (`glasnost_convert::sim`), absent 0.
- **Seams.** The pulse that stops a building (S-0405 claim 8) is still a seam, so no test pulses a derrick; the erasure of S-0514's temporal beam will reach `power::being_erased` by itself (a warp member is the only erasure built).
- **Own AI.** See *Own AI (M6c: special abilities)*.
- **Client.** Nothing of its own: the click is the engineer's (S-0214, capture). The lane scene `tech` of `crates/app/tests/m6c_lane.rs` finds a cash-producing tech type by its rules fields, has an engineer capture it and prints the credits at the capture and at three income cycles; it writes `local-lane/m6c/tech-before.png`, `tech-captured.png` and `tech-income.png`.

## Simulation (M6c: jumpjets)

S-0502 is built in `glasnost_sim::jump` (state, queries, orders, occupancy marks in `jump.rs`; the flight update in `jump/fly.rs`); the ledger is [docs/m6-claims/S-0502.md](m6-claims/S-0502.md), the tests are `claims_s0502*.rs` (fixtures in `jump_common`) and, for the importer, `jumpjet_type_keys` and its neighbours in `crates/convert/src/sim_econ_tests.rs`. New state: `Object::jump` (`JumpState`: the flight state, whether it holds a move order, the destination point, the current and maximum speed, the target height, the wobble phase, the movement heading, the speed fraction, the ground mark, the landing reservation, the hover slot, the deck flag, the landing request of S-0517, a pending crash start) and `World::jump_slots` (the hover slot of each cell); save format version 36 (38 since step 1b: `JumpState::listed`). Revision 5 (taken in at M6c step 1b, [docs/m6-claims/step1b-m6c.md](m6-claims/step1b-m6c.md)) answers Q-0975 to Q-0981; revision 6 (taken in at step 1c, [docs/m6-claims/step1c-m6c.md](m6-claims/step1c-m6c.md)) answers Q-1010 to Q-1012. The new locomotor is `rules::Locomotor::Jump`: **the type's imported locomotor chooses it** (`JumpJet` flag or not), for infantry and vehicles.

- **Update (claims 3 and 5 to 15, F1 to F6).** `tick::object_turn` calls `jump::tick` for the locomotor. An update takes a physics step with the previous state's maximum speed and target height, then the state's outcome: speed (F1, the fraction before the ground-hold reset), wobble phase and desired height (F2), the ground reference (F3, the direct reference or the look-ahead; the quirk `jumpjet_lookahead_bridge`), the height step and ground hold (F4), the step along the heading (F5, `walk::step_point`; the off setting of `jumpjet_step_truncation` is the exact rounding of `glasnost_num::bigmath::rounded_sin_cos`) and the body facing; then the outcome of grounded (take-off), ascending, hovering (the six rows), cruising (the F6 bands, `jumpjet_turn_slowdown_one_sided`), descending (water and beach, the landing check, the deploy direction, touchdown) and crashing. A jumpjet that holds no move order and is grounded or hovering takes no update.
- **Orders (claims 16, 17, 21, 23, 24).** `infantry::destination_assigned` calls `jump::take_destination`: an order-level destination goes to `order_point` (claim 16): the landing reservation is released (unless grounded), the nearby-cell search runs with the settings of claim 23 and does not lift the unit's own occupancy (revision 6, Q-1011: an ordinary vehicle ordered to its own cell gets a nearby cell, only an entering or balloon-hover vehicle keeps the exact point; a jumpjet-type infantry unit, by the imported `JumpJet` flag and outside an ion storm (none exists), ignores a nonempty assignment to its own cell before any search or draw, `jump::own_cell_filtered`, and S-0213 claim 21's get-up skips it) (`jump::Use`: a vehicle that is not balloon-hover, hover class, flying zone, no deck mode, bridge cells excluded; infantry and balloon-hover vehicles, deck mode at a high-bridge centre, bridges allowed; the stopped or failed landing, the type's own class and zone; none has a reach region or a height condition), the exact point for a balloon-hover vehicle and an entering vehicle, the centre of the found cell for a vehicle, and for infantry the standing choice `jump::standing_choice` (`walk::choose_position` with the centre class: the vehicle-present and object-present refusals take no draw, otherwise one ranged draw over 0 to 3 picks table O's row R0 to R3, the first free side position wins, all taken is a failed adjustment; the ground record, the deck record only for a deck at or below world height 0), the re-ascent of a descending unit and the *unload*-to-*guard* replacement. A failed adjustment keeps the ordered point as the movement destination and changes nothing else; one that would retain an off-map point is refused (the preceding point, moving flag, state, speeds, target height and landing reservation stay; claim 24's safety departure). An order-level destination that becomes empty is not claim 16's direct no-point instruction (`jump::no_point`, refused while moving now): for a moving jumpjet it is claim 17's reroute (`jump::stop_with`, the stop order's `jump::stop_ordered` from `orders::apply_other`, the deploy order's `jump::destination_cleared` from `orders::apply_other` and every other clear through `take_destination`; revision 6, Q-1012: an unchanged assignment of an ordinary vehicle, an empty-to-empty clear included, is suppressed, with no reroute, no draw and the preceding point and commitment kept, `stop_ordered` and `take_destination`'s comparison with the order the locomotor last took), which assigns a replacement movement point through `give_point` and leaves the order-level destination empty; `jump::stop` is the failed landing (it also sets the order-level destination): the nearby cell, or the destruction by its health, or `quirks.jumpjet_no_cell_self_destruct` off: hovering again. The neighbour step is one ranged draw over 0 to 7 in the tick of the transition (`jump::neighbour_step`), followed by an infantry unit's own draw of the standing choice.
- **Occupancy (claims 4, 22, 26).** `JumpState::listed` is the cell list the jumpjet is in. In every physics step but hovering and cruising the old placement goes first (`fly::remove_placement`: the mark, the listing, a recompute of the cell of the position, S-0211 claim 5), the clearance is evaluated without it (`jump::clearance` reads the actual ground listings and has no self-exclusion, claim 22), and the new position is placed only when it qualifies (`fly::place_after_step`, `jump::placement_qualifies`: revision 6, Q-1010, exactly when *r* < 208 leptons or the bridge-adjusted *r*′ = 0, the 208 test reading *r*; starting a crash does not set the falling state that suppresses the subtraction; the on-deck state ends at the first rise): listed on the deck or the ground and marked (`lay_mark`: an infantry unit never takes a standing position that another unit holds), and its cell recomputed; a crash's extra drop adds its own removal and placement. Hovering and cruising make no new listing or mark, keep a listing they have in the old cell and do not recompute on a crossing (`jump::move_to` moves nothing but the position). A descending one holds its landing reservation (the same mark in the landing cell, made by the landing check; its own actual-position mark does not take the standing position from it, and `clear_mark` clears a shared position once). `occupancy::in_progress` reads *moving now* (every state except grounded and hovering), so readiness (S-0215 claim 11), the balloon-hover test of S-0310 claim 7 and the move fall-back use it. Touchdown (`settle`) and take-off (`leave_ground`) keep their own recomputes.
- **Crash (claim 18, S-0312 claims 15 and 16).** `combat::death` starts the crash of a killed crashable jumpjet above the terrain (`jump::start_crash`: passengers, three tumbling draws except for infantry, a pending start); the unit stays in play (`tick::object_turn` calls `jump::tick` for a dead crashing jumpjet), takes the extra drop *k* in its next update, enters *crashing* with target height −5, spins by claim 18's lead, and ends below 1 lepton or by crossing a deck with S-0312 claim 16's impact (`jump::fly::impact`). `jump::seized` reads magnetic lift (S-0607, built at M7a step 3.8; see *Simulation (M7a: Magnetic lift)*).
- **Revision 14 (M7a step 1o, [step1o-m7a.md](m7-claims/step1o-m7a.md)).** F3's look-ahead runs in every state but grounded and descending only when the movement point (`JumpState.dest`), not the order-level destination, lies in another cell, or for a balloon-hover type; crashing is included, even at zero speed (only the own cell's clearance then counts). With no point the direct reference applies (`jump::height_step`). Claim 17's magnetic-release safety departure: when a lifter ends a grip and the stop finds no cell, nothing self-destructs in either quirk position (`jump::stop_with` with `lifter_ended`, `jump::lift_release_stop`). Claim 24's new row: a grounded unit with a movement commitment keeps it and its point and takes off at its next eligible update (claim 5, even with no order-level destination). With `jumpjet_no_cell_self_destruct` off, the hover re-entry over the current cell now also clears the movement point and commitment, so the unit no longer flies on to its old point. Requests: Q-1342 and Q-1343 (S-0607 cases; see *Simulation (M7a: Magnetic lift)*), answered by revision 16. Revision 15 (step 1p) is editorial: the "Changes for other specs" section is gone (S-0607 took it in at revision 6).
- **Revision 16 (M7a step 1q, [step1q-m7a.md](m7-claims/step1q-m7a.md)).** With `jumpjet_no_cell_self_destruct` off and no cell for claim 17's stop, the jumpjet clears its point and commitment; in the air it hovers again over its current cell, and a *grounded* jumpjet stays grounded with no hover slot (`jump::stop_with`; before, it was put into *hovering* on the ground). A seized set-down unit that hovers is not restored and retries the search at each fall-back to rest (S-0607 claims 9 and 20). Claims 3, 9 and 17 now name the no-cell magnetic-release safety fall (an update every tick, the direct reference with no point). That was already so: our fall enters *crashing* at once. Answers Q-1342 and Q-1343 with S-0607 revision 8. Q-1344 filed (whether *ascending* at height 0 is in the air; answered by revision 17).
- **Revision 17 (M7a step 1r, [step1r-m7a.md](m7-claims/step1r-m7a.md)).** With `jumpjet_no_cell_self_destruct` off and no cell for claim 17's stop, the integer height above the surface decides (`jump::stop_with`): 1 lepton or more hovers over the current cell; 0 or less stays put and becomes *grounded* with target height 0 (as touchdown leaves it, so a later take-off keeps claim 5's timing), also from *ascending* at height 0, releasing any hover slot. Every seized set-down unit hovering in that outcome, by any route, is not restored and retries the search (S-0607 claim 20). Answers Q-1344 with S-0607 revision 9. Claim 2 and the Key case no longer speak of the stock data; claim 6 restates F1 (as built).
- **Elsewhere.** `combat::check` (S-0310 claim 8: the infantry speed fraction above 0.1 and the jumpjet-turn flag with *moving now*), `combat::range` (S-0307 claim 24: no detour limit for a jumpjet), `infantry` (S-0107: a moving jumpjet asks for *hover* or *fly* by its speed fraction, *stand* becomes *hover* when airborne), `econ::shroud::refresh_sight` and `cloak::machine::cell_movement_completed` at touchdown, `objects` (marks released when a jumpjet leaves the map or goes inside).
- **Importer.** `JumpjetSpeed`, `JumpjetClimb`, `JumpjetCrash`, `JumpjetHeight`, `JumpjetAccel`, `JumpjetWobbles`, `JumpjetDeviation`, `JumpjetNoWobbles`, `DeployToLand` (type), `[AudioVisual] DeployDir` (`general.economy.deploy_dir`), the six `[JumpjetControls]` values as *ignored* in the coverage table; a key written with different letter case (such as `JumpJetAccel`) sets nothing, so the absent-key default applies or an earlier layer's value remains (revision 17, S-0001 claim 13); the importer warns for every type with a speed or turn rate of 0 (claim 25: the engine gives a take-off fraction and every cruise speed of 0 for a speed of 0 and an empty middle cruise band for a turn rate of 0, no quirk switch).
- **Quirks.** `jumpjet_bridge_early_cruise_height`, `jumpjet_lookahead_bridge`, `jumpjet_no_cell_self_destruct`, `jumpjet_step_truncation` and `jumpjet_turn_slowdown_one_sided` are consulted and tested in both settings.
- **Seams.** The flying-object registry (Open questions), ion storms (out of scope), the infantry *air-death-end* timing.
- **Own AI.** See *Own AI (M6c: special abilities)*.
- **Client.** The click is S-0214's (a jumpjet is a free mover). The map view draws the unit at its world height, so the flight shows with no new drawing code. The lane scene `jumpjet` of `crates/app/tests/m6c_lane.rs` finds a non-balloon jumpjet type by its locomotor, orders it by a click to a cell across an obstacle (another reach region of the ground) and writes `local-lane/m6c/jumpjet-before.png`, `jumpjet-flight.png` and `jumpjet-landed.png`; it prints the states and heights by tick.

There are no provisional rules of the jumpjets left (Q-0975 to Q-0981 are answered by S-0502 revision 5 and Q-1010 to Q-1012 by revision 6 and Q-1030 and Q-1031 by revision 7 (step 1e: jumpjet-type infantry has no unchanged-destination suppression, `jump::clear_rejected` is the balloon-hover rejection of claim 28), and their interim rules are retired; Q-1090 is answered by S-0207 revision 32 and S-0502 revision 8 (step 1f, section *Simulation (M6c: destination refresh and the dock fallback)* below); a pending aerial crash start waking an idle jumpjet is claim 3's safety departure, an engine design rule with no quirk switch, as S-0312 revision 29 says). A landing request no longer sets the movement commitment (S-0517 revision 7): `jump::request_landing` only sets the request, so an idle uncommitted hover is not woken by it (S-0502 claim 3). The render layer of S-0102 revision 20 (claim 3 and its table) is not modelled: the map view draws mobile objects in the ground layer (`quirks.layer_change_on_events_only`, the table above), and the predicate it shares with S-0502 claim 4 is `jump::placement_qualifies`.


## Simulation (M6c: destination refresh and the dock fallback)

Step 1f (S-0207 revision 32 and S-0502 revision 8, answering Q-1090; S-0417 revisions 17 to 19; S-0402 revision 26). The module is `glasnost_sim::refresh`; the two runtime flags, `econ.refresh` (the destination-refresh state) and `econ.point_suppression` (the one-application movement-point suppression), are per-object hashed state (save format 45).

- **Assignment event.** `infantry::destination_assigned` (the hook of every `assign_destination`) calls `refresh::begin` for a vehicle after the order-level destination is stored and before any locomotor takes it. An assignment is effective when the destination changes or refresh is pending (`refresh::pending`; `jump::take_destination`, `jump::stop_ordered` and `teleport::take_destination` read it to bypass their unchanged-destination rejection). An unchanged assignment without refresh is ignored by the dock test; the driving locomotor's own re-installation of an unchanged point (which the engine always did) is unchanged. `refresh::finish` clears the refresh the assignment consumed, unless the dock fallback newly enabled it in that very assignment.
- **Dock test (S-0207 claim 27).** The type has the `Teleporter` flag (`raw.teleporter`), the vehicle has no warp-pending immobilization (S-0207 revision 34 Terms: `superweapons::warp_pending_or_leaving`, the accepted superweapon warp from its acceptance through the leaving wait until departure, read whatever the current locomotor; phasing in after a jump or a warp's departure, the arrival hold and the parasite's held countdown are not it; the same state leaves a teleporting vehicle's landing point unchanged under S-0207 claim 20 (b), `teleport::take_destination`), not gripped or seized (`lift::lifter_of`, `lift::seized`; S-0207 r82), its contact is a building with `DockUnload`, the offered destination is a cell with no ground-layer vehicle (any house, the mover included) and the current locomotor is not teleporting (`teleport::is_teleporter`, false during temporary driving). With a suspended predecessor and no movement report the predecessor is restored (`econ::release::restore`), with no pending state. Otherwise `refresh::fallback` clears the driving locomotor's destination point (`drive::stop_point`) or a jumpjet's point (`jump::no_point`), clears the current behaviour, queues *enter* without an immediate start and sets both flags. `drive::take` and `jump::take_destination` then consume the suppression (`refresh::skip_point`) instead of applying the assignment's own nonempty point; an empty destination does not consume it.
- **Restoration (S-0417 claim 5, S-0207 claim 27 row 1).** `econ::release::restorable`: a temporary driving locomotor, not seized, no restoration lock (the lock of the new Terms entry is never set in our engine) and no driving movement report (`drive::movement_report`: no locomotor destination point, no heading point, the track's target, horizontally different from the actual position). The fall-back's restoration (`restore_locomotor`) follows it, so a teleporting vehicle that left its factory on the exit track keeps driving until its destination point is gone (a stop, or the arrival of a later move at its destination); before revision 19 it was restored at the first fall-back. The dock test inside an assignment judges the stored point only (`assigning`), because the assignment's own point is not installed yet. The restored teleporting locomotor then takes that same assignment (S-0207 revision 34 claim 27, *The restoring assignment's destination*): `refresh::begin` forgets the destination the suspended state last took, so `teleport::take_destination` fixes the landing point for the offer (replacing a pending earlier teleport) and the vehicle jumps at its next movement update; no driving point is installed first. Suppression already pending before the assignment still skips that application (`teleport::take_destination` calls `refresh::skip_point`).
- **Placement.** `objects::place_with` enables refresh for a vehicle around the placement fall-back (`refresh::placement_begin` and `placement_end`); the fall-back rows that clear the destination (`process::fall_back_vehicle`: unarmed, harvester) make the clear an effective empty-to-empty assignment while refresh is enabled, which consumes it and enables no suppression. A fresh vehicle has nothing to stop, so nothing is observable. The ordering before a visceroid's placement-only action-timing draw is not built (the engine has no such draw).
- **Harvest row 2 (S-0402 claim 10, revision 26).** `harvest::enter` offers the docking cell at every processing when pending refresh makes the repeat effective; an unchanged offer without refresh is not repeated (it was never), so ordinary harvesters re-search nothing new. For a qualified teleport-capable harvester that is not teleporting the offers keep qualifying (no once-per-entry limit): each installs the order-level cell destination and skips its point. Since intake 39 (S-0207 r82) the fallback also stops a driving vehicle (`refresh::stopped`), and a flagged vehicle with a suspended teleporting locomotor jumps at the next offer at which it stands still.
- **Refinery sale (S-0402 revision 76 claim 11, M8 A3 intake 36, [intake36-S-0402.md](m8-claims/intake36-S-0402.md)).** `harvest::refinery_sold` (the sale's first processing) starts an unloading harvester's *harvest* before its scatter request; one in *enter* queues *guard* and loses a destination naming the refinery. `harvest::sale_breaks_contact` ends every contact at the sale's next processing; a harvester tethered then holds back the build-down one more processing.
- **Factory-exit lifetime (S-0417 revision 20 claim 16, S-0207 revision 34 claim 25).** Nothing new is built: the exit point is kept after the no-order track completion (no timer clears it); a rally, scatter or ore destination replaces it in the driving locomotor; the completion of that later goal or a stop clears it; a later eligible fall-back restores the teleporting locomotor. A factory-built chrono miner therefore drives its first leg to the ore and loads while still driving (the harvest queue and the start of loading restore nothing); it gets its teleporting locomotor back at a qualified docking assignment or a later eligible fall-back. S-0417 r64 adds that each later destination other than a qualified docking assignment installs temporary driving again (S-0207 claim 28): built in A3 intake 39 ([m8-claims/intake39-S-0207.md](m8-claims/intake39-S-0207.md)).
- **Request Q-1110** (answered by S-0207 revision 34 and S-0417 revision 20): its interim rules are retired.
- **Request Q-1130** (answered by S-0207 revision 36 and S-0402 revision 28, M6c step 1h; its interim rules are retired). The answers:
  - Point suppression applies to every nonempty vehicle assignment, a teleporting landing point included. `teleport::take_destination` tests `refresh::skip_point` before claim 20 (a)'s disabling refusal (phasing in, held, and now being erased by a temporal beam).
  - So in any state a pending suppression keeps the order-level destination, fixes no landing point and is consumed. `teleport::tick` keeps such a destination until a later effective assignment. There is no switch for it (S-0207 revision 37 Edge cases, answering Q-1131).
  - An empty assignment while a jump is pending cancels the jump. The landing point's reservation stays until the next accepted destination (claim 20 h).
  - A vehicle being erased or in the leaving stage keeps its destination under a pending suppression (S-0215 claim 2, S-0514 claim 17: `temporal::turn`, `superweapons::warp::turn`).
  - A harvester whose active locomotor teleports drops its destination, an adopted remembered cell included, at every seeking processing past the *full* test, and searches from where it stands (S-0402 claim 17, the seeking drop: `harvest::seeking`).

- **Request Q-1131** (answered by S-0207 revision 37, M6c step 1h). S-0207 revision 36 had proposed `quirks.teleport_suppression_keeps_destination` and left it to the lead; revision 37 decides: the original behaviour, with no switch (no ordinary or scripted play reaches the state), so the S-0215 claim 2 and S-0514 claim 17 exceptions apply unconditionally. The switch built in the meantime is reverted.

Tests: `claims_s0207_r36.rs` (step 1h: the Q-1130 table of S-0207 claim 27, claim 20 h, S-0215 claim 2's exception), `claims_s0402_r28.rs` (step 1h: the seeking drop), `claims_s0207_r34.rs` (step 1g: the immobilization predicate, the restoring assignment's destination), `claims_s0417_r20.rs` (step 1g: the factory-exit lifetime with a factory-built chrono miner), `claims_s0207_r32.rs` (placement leaves no pending state, the fallback and its separate consumption events, repeats through refresh, contact alone, every predicate, empty clear, jumpjet refresh, restoration versus fallback), `claim_10_row_2_offers_reach_the_dock_test` (`claims_s0402.rs`) and `claims_s0417_r19.rs` (`claim_5_restoration_needs_no_movement_report`, `claim_5_a_different_heading_point_is_a_report`).

## Simulation (M6c: deployable units)

S-0517 is built in `glasnost_sim::econ::inplace` (the in-place cycle, the firing refusals, the deploy-to-fire cell test and the drawing helpers), with hooks in `econ::deploy` (the order of S-0415 claim 1: rank 2, the deploy-fire shot of claim 2, and rank 5, the in-place cycle), `process` (the in-transition readiness, the *move* fallback, the deployed-firing rule of claim 16), `combat::check`, `combat::behave`, `combat::range`, `approach`, `orders` and `jump` (landing for deploy). The ledger is [docs/m6-claims/S-0517.md](m6-claims/S-0517.md); the tests are `claims_s0517.rs` and `claims_s0517_more.rs` (fixture `deploy_common`, built on `jump_common`) and, for the importer, `deployable_unit_keys` in `crates/convert/src/sim_econ_tests.rs`. New state: `DeployState::form` (`inplace::Form`: mobile, deploying, deployed, undeploying, the last two with their start tick); save format version 37.

- **The cycle (claims 3 to 8, F1, F2).** `inplace::process` takes the deploy step (mobile) or the undeploy step (deployed) at each processing of *unload*, then chooses 1 tick while the vehicle is in transition or landing for deploy and 450 ticks with *guard* started otherwise (`quirks.deploy_transition_discards_orders`). A transition completes at the first processing at or after t0 + (E - 1) d (deploy) or t0 + (E - 2) d (undeploy), never in the processing that began it; with *E* at most 1 (or 2) at t0 + 1; a zero stage delay stalls it (`quirks.deploy_stall_zero_delay`). *E* is the animation's end field, or its sprite's frame count (half of it for a shadow type) when the field is absent or -1. The presentation is an `Effect::Anim` (`reverse` for an undeploy); the sounds are `Sound::Deploy` and `Sound::Undeploy` (`quirks.deploy_sound_repeats`). A landing-first type above the ground requests landing (`jump::request_landing`; the request ends at touchdown); any other airborne type drops the order (`quirks.airborne_deploy_order_dropped`).
- **Orders and destinations (claims 9, 10, 15).** `econ::assign_destination` replaces a destination given to a deployed or changing vehicle by none, `inplace::enforce` clears one written directly at the start of the vehicle's turn, and *move* processed for such a vehicle queues *guard* with 1 tick. No rule starts a vehicle's unload or deploy cycle, and `DeployTime` is read and used for no vehicle.
- **Deploy fire (claim 2).** A deploy-fire vehicle without the in-place flag fires slot 0 at its own cell through the firing check (range test included) at each processing, then chooses 14 + u ticks (one ranged draw over 0 to 2, taken whatever the check answered).
- **Refusals and drawing (claims 11, 12).** A vehicle in transition answers *illegal* at the rank of S-0301 claim 9's group; a magnetic-beam weapon is refused against a vehicle in transition or an in-place deployer whose current behaviour is *unload* (`inplace::beam_refused`, read by the firing check for the magnetic beam of S-0607). The map view hides the body in transition (`inplace::body_hidden`) and draws a deployed vehicle with its deployed image type (`inplace::deployed_image`, learned like any other image).
- **Deploy to fire (claims 13, 14).** The firing step queues *unload* for a deploy-to-fire vehicle without starting it (a deployed in-place one again with `quirks.deploy_to_fire_repacks`); the cell test (`inplace::deploy_test_cell`: flat, no building among the occupants, a land type with the buildable flag) feeds the *must deploy* answer, S-0215 claim 37 f2's adjusted reach and the primary-cell qualification of the approach search.
- **Deployed infantry (claim 16).** `process::deployed_firing`: a target in range of the weapon selected with no target stays; otherwise an S-0309 range search runs at once, an out-of-range target is replaced or dropped, and a unit left targetless falls back unless it is on *guard* or a jumpjet.
- **Importer.** `DeployingAnim` (an animation type), `UnloadingClass` (a vehicle type), `DeployTime` (read, no effect) and the animation keys `Start`, `End` and `Shadow` (`rules::AnimationType.start/end/shadow`). The quirks `deploy_sound_repeats`, `deploy_stall_zero_delay`, `deploy_to_fire_repacks`, `deploy_transition_discards_orders` and `airborne_deploy_order_dropped` are consulted and tested in both settings.
- **Own AI.** See *Own AI (M6c: special abilities)*.
- **Client.** The click is S-0214's (the cursor and the click of an in-place deployer, a deployed unit and the deploy-ready check already exist in `targeting`). The map view draws the deploying presentation (reversed for an undeploy), hides the body in transition and draws the deployed image type.
