At step 1i (S-0212 revision 49 with `corridor_retry_no_fallback`, S-0605 revision 5's approach, S-0506 revision 10's flagged slot) the goldens were regenerated and no golden file changed: no gate game reaches the changed rows, and every figure above (seed 3 and the sweep) is the same.

## Simulation (M7a: Vehicle bunker)

S-0606 (revision 3, revisions 4 and 5 since step 1k, 6 and 7 since step 1l) is built in `glasnost_sim::structures::bunker`; the ledger is [docs/m7-claims/S-0606.md](m7-claims/S-0606.md), the tests are `claims_s0606.rs`, four pairs in `pairs_m7a.rs` (a gattling in a bunker spins up as in the open; a controlled vehicle goes into its controller's bunker; a warp takes a bunkered vehicle out; a berserk weapon is refused at a bunkered vehicle and at its bunker), `a_temporal_shot_at_a_bunkered_vehicle_erases_its_bunker` (`claims_s0514_more.rs`), the audio test `bunker_walls_sounds` and, for the importer, `bunker_fields`, `bunker_wall_anims_are_converted`, `bunker_foundation_warning` and `bunker_depot_warning`. The state is in `StructState`: the bunker's vehicle (`bunker_occupant`), the vehicle's bunker (`bunkered_in`) and the bunker's stage of table S1 (`bunker_stage`: clearing, waiting, turning in, driving in, turning south, raising walls, occupied); the entrant's contact is the ordinary contact field, and the entry and exit tracks (T1, T2) are `Track.bunker` in `drive`. All of it is saved and hashed. Save format version 53 (54 since step 1j).

- **Who may go in (claims 1 to 3).** `bunker::eligible`: the vehicle's bunkerable flag (absent counts as on), a turret, a turret weapon, not hover or balloon, no parasite on it. `bunker::permits` is the permission table (rows P1 to P10, first match wins); `targeting` reads it, so the *enter* cursor and click follow it. `bunker::request` takes the contact; a second vehicle is turned away and a switched-off bunker refuses.
- **Entry (claims 4 to 8, F1 to F3, T1).** The docking point (F1) from the vehicle's quadrant; the arrival (`bunker::arrival`, from `drive` only: the vehicle stops, keeps the bunker as destination and stays in *enter*, the bunker queues *repair*; claim 5, revision 4); then S1, one row per *repair* processing of the bunker with a delay of 15 (`bunker::process_repair`): clearing (objects in the foundation are asked to scatter and waited for; the candidates are the infantry, vehicles, aircraft and buildings of each foundation cell's ground list; `quirks.bunker_clear_chosen_only`, default on, asks only each cell's chosen object, and `quirks.bunker_clear_far_point`, default on, measures F5's distance from 896 leptons per axis off the cell's top corner, off from its centre; both tested both ways), the turn in, the drive along T1 (every point driven, claim 21), the turn to 32768, the walls going up (`Effect::Bunkered`, the walls-up sound and the special-2 to special-4 animations). The occupied bunker blocks its foundation (`occupancy`); the vehicle keeps *guard*, cannot be moved, is left out of band selection, and the client drops it from the selection (`game/play.rs`). `bunker::clear_report` is claim 8's report (384 leptons, the run-away reply); no route reaches it yet.
- **Inside (claim 9).** The range bonus (`combat::range`), the rate of fire (`combat::fire`), the hit rows (`combat::damage`: a penetrating warhead at an occupied bunker leaves it unaffected, a non-penetrating one at the bunkered vehicle likewise), the threat and the redirection to the bunker (`combat::acquire`, `temporal::deliver`). A bunkered vehicle has no approach (`approach`) and refuses every non-empty destination (claim 18), and counts for `transport::tank_bunker`.
- **Release (claims 11 to 15, F4, T2).** The deploy click on a bunkered vehicle gives *unload* (S-0214 row VD5); every order ends a bunkered vehicle's contact while the link holds (claim 17), and an order to a vehicle still entering ends its contact, so the bunker returns to clearing and stops special-1 and special-2 if the walls were going up (claim 16). `bunker::process_unload` lowers the walls (walls-down sound and effects), drives T2 to facing 192, gives the nearby cell as destination (none found: the placeholder cell (0, 0)) and queues *move*, and ends every contact of the bunker; an ordinary type then chooses 450 ticks, a harvester or weeder, deploy-into or simple-deployer type goes on with that rule; the deploy-fire rule and passenger unloading are skipped (claim 12, revision 7, answering Q-1164; S-0415 claim 1). A released simple-deployer takes its first in-place step in the release processing, whatever its passenger capacity, and under `quirks.deploy_transition_discards_orders` its return to *guard* replaces the queued *move* (S-0517 revision 10 claim 1; `claim_12_released_simple_deployer_deploys_in_place`, `claim_12_released_simple_deployer_with_presentation`). A destroyed, sold or erased bunker lets the vehicle out (`bunker::let_out`, from `combat::death`, `econ::sell` and `temporal`); a destroyed, erased or warped vehicle is released in place (`bunker::release_in_place`; walls down after `Effect::Killed`, S-0312). An owner change of either side keeps the link (claim 15); other removals end it silently on both sides, and a surviving bunker returns to clearing with *guard* queued (`bunker::removing`, claim 19, our departure).
- **Seams filled.** Psychic (`psychic::firing_refused`, S-0610 claim 3: a berserk weapon is refused at a bunkered vehicle and at its bunker); warp (`superweapons::warp`, S-0513 claim 34: the block takes a bunkered vehicle out in place); mind (`mind::can_take` refuses a bunkered vehicle, S-0601 claim 3; a controlled vehicle may enter its controller's bunker); temporal (S-0514 claims 1 and 12: a shot at a bunkered vehicle erases its bunker); death (S-0312); sell (S-0408); range and fire (S-0301); approach (S-0215); transport (S-0507); parasite (claim 1's parasite row); the gattling (S-0609 claim 5: no exclusion or delay of the firing step inside).
- **Seams left (M7b).** S-0612 (the Psychic Dominator's sweep of a bunkered vehicle, `bunker::holds_through_owner_change`). Magnetic lift (S-0607) is built at step 3.8: a lift hit at a bunkered vehicle goes to its bunker, which is not seized. Release under a pulse is the spec's open question. The drain of a bunker building (S-0608) is built at M7b step 3.3 (`approach::draining`; the pairs `a_drained_bunker_refuses_fire_and_sale_but_no_blackout` and `a_drained_bunker_keeps_its_vehicle_firing`; its occupants keep their own rules, S-0608 claim 18, taken in at M7b step 1d).
- **Importer and audio.** Buildings `Bunker`, vehicles `Bunkerable`, `[AudioVisual] BunkerWallsUpSound` and `BunkerWallsDownSound` (played as `Sound::BunkerWallsUp` and `Sound::BunkerWallsDown`); the importer warns when a bunker type's foundation is not 2 × 2 (Edge cases) and when a type is both a bunker and a repair depot (claim 20).
- **Bunker and depot on one type (claim 20).** The bunker's permission rows (`depot::accepts`, `targeting`), the repeated request also refused by claim 3's eligibility check, the bunker's docking point (`depot::docking_point`), the depot's movement-arrival exchange (`bunker::arrival` skips a depot), and a *repair* processing that runs one S1 row and then the depot's processing on the state that row left, whose delay is returned and whose own request replaces S1's queued *guard* (revision 7, answering Q-1163; `claim_20_one_s1_row_then_the_depot`).
- **Requests.** Q-1300 to Q-1308 are answered by revision 4 (step 1k), Q-1163 and Q-1164 by revision 7 (step 1l); no interim is left.
- **Own AI.** `glasnost_ai::bunker` (AI game format 22); see *Own AI (M6c: special abilities)*.
- **Client and lane.** The local lane scene `bunker` (`crates/app/tests/m7a_lane.rs`, screenshots `local-lane/m7a/bunker-*.png`: before, driving in, occupied, firing, released): a vehicle clicked onto the install's tank bunker (cursor *enter*) reaches it and the bunker steps through S1: waiting at 149 ticks, turning in 164, driving in 179, turning south 194, raising walls 224, occupied 239, the body at facing 32768. An enemy vehicle placed four cells off is hit from inside 40 ticks later; the deploy click on the bunkered vehicle (cursor *deploy*) ends the link on the next tick, the bunker goes back to clearing, and the vehicle drives out beside it. The bunker's wall animations (special-1 to special-4, `Effect::SpecialAnims`) are not drawn: the client skips the effect, so the screenshots show the vehicle in the open pit. AI lane (map 8, seed 7, Yuri house 1; outputs `local-lane/m7a/s0606/ai-sides-0-2.txt` and `ai-sides-1-2.txt`): sides 0,2: Allied won at 17 384; the Yuri house built 2 tank bunkers, gave 1 entry order and 1 exit order, 1 vehicle was bunkered and let out with both standing. Sides 1,2: Soviet won at 21 750; 2 bunkers, 2 entry orders, 2 exit orders, 2 vehicles bunkered and let out. House 0 (Allied, then Soviet) built no tank bunker in either game. Replays and mid-game saves matched in both. The same numbers before and after merging step 1i (the gate minima and the goldens did not move either).
- **Gate.** Both sides of the M7a duel may build BUNK (2 × 2, cost 600, strength 1 500, no impassable rows); GUNR and Alpha's gattling GATV may go in. The run counts bunker build, entry and exit orders and vehicles bunkered, and saves and loads at the first tick a vehicle is bunkered. Minimum / median over seeds 1 to 16 as measured when the bunker was built (gate seed 3 plays all 12 000 ticks; seeds 4, 12, 15 and 16 end with Alpha's win at 10 643, 10 578, 11 615 and 11 468). Since step 1k only seed 4 ends early (at 11 250); the bunker minima are still 4, 3, 3 and 3, and the full table is in [step1k-m7a.md](m7-claims/step1k-m7a.md):
  - berserk orders 15/27, units made berserk 30/49;
  - sensor orders 2/2 (the known exception), intent ticks 2 346/3 644;
  - reveal orders 8/12, reveals fired 8/12;
  - slave-miner buildings 3/5, slave ticks 1 321/6 764;
  - gattling stage rises 6/12, top-stage ticks 570/1 704;
  - takeover orders 5/17, takeovers 9/14;
  - gas clouds 34/87, gas hits 74/305;
  - absorber entry orders 5/19, infantry absorbed 4/8;
  - grinder entry orders 4/5, units ground 4/5;
  - bunker build orders 4/4, bunker entry orders 3/8, vehicles bunkered 3/7, bunker exit orders 3/7.

  Before the AI's join exit and early builds, the exit orders stayed at 0: bunkers came late and nothing called the vehicles out. The base defence also took vehicles on their way in as attackers. The goldens were regenerated.

## Simulation (M7a: S-0215 revisions 63 and 67)

S-0215 revision 63 (taken in at M7a step 1k, [step1k-m7a.md](m7-claims/step1k-m7a.md)) widened claim 11's "moving" for a vehicle with the driving locomotor to a body still turning towards its given facing, for any reason. Revision 67 (step 1l, [step1l-m7a.md](m7-claims/step1l-m7a.md), answering Q-1160 and Q-1161) replaces the rest of the driving test: such a vehicle is moving for readiness while its body still turns, or while all three of these hold: its locomotor moves it in S-0206's sense (a locomotor destination, or a track target whose x and y differ from its own), it has a heading point (it is on a track, S-0207 Terms) and its integer movement step *s* (S-0207 claim 2, `drive::progress` at the read) is above 0. `process::moving` calls `process::drive_moving` for a driving vehicle (by type, or temporarily, S-0417 claim 5) and keeps `occupancy::in_progress` for every other actor. A stored path, a retained destination or a turn-in-place mark no longer holds a driving vehicle back, and nor does the first, truncated-to-zero step of an accelerating vehicle; a vehicle between two tracks (S-0207 claim 12's two-track cap), blocked or waiting for a retry is ready. A body turn ends by tick number (S-0206 claim 1), so the first start of the tick in which it ends already sees it ended and processes the started behaviour in that tick (B47). The late start reads the same current predicate, not a remembered displacement. `process::ready` keeps its exceptions ahead of the moving test. Every start that asks for readiness goes through `process::ready`: the first per-tick start and the late start, and the harvest unload rows (S-0402 revision 31), the grinder's queued *guard* (S-0603 revision 8), the absorber's entrant, the depot's retry and arrival *sleep* (S-0314 revision 29), the release rows, mind control and the bunker. `occupancy::in_progress` itself is unchanged, so S-0206 claim 9's body turn and the scatter refusals keep S-0206's term. An attack order during a pre-step turn removes both destinations and the route; `drive::tick` then ends the turn in place at its next update, the body completes its turn and the cancelled step is not taken (S-0207 revision 39 claim 11; D11 c). S-0207 lets the old path steps survive; ours are cleared with the destination, which the claim allows. Tests: `claims_s0215_r63.rs` (a turn to face a target, the exceptions, ships and hovering vehicles stay ready, B47 with its same-tick processing) and `claims_s0215_r67.rs` (a retained goal and path alone are ready; a heading point with *s* above 0 is not, with *s* = 0 it is; B48's fresh move from *area guard*), `claim_11_queued_guard_starts_by_the_current_predicate` (`claims_s0610_r8.rs`).

- **Formats and gate.** No new state at either step: save format 54 and AI game format 22, unchanged. The goldens were regenerated at both steps (`duel`, `duel-m6c` and `duel-m7a` play differently). Step 1k: over seeds 1 to 16 every minimum is at least 3 except the sensor orders (2); seed 3 plays all 12 000 ticks, and seed 4 ends at 11 250 with Alpha's win. Step 1l: the gate seed 3 plays all 12 000 ticks with every minimum but the sensor orders at least 3, but over seeds 1 to 16 seed 8 gives no takeover orders and seed 9 no grinder entry orders or ground units (no setup-only fix found; left to the lead). `combat` also changes at step 1l. The tables are in the step 1k and step 1l ledgers.

## Simulation (M7a: Magnetic lift)

S-0607 (revision 18, taken in at M8 A3 intake 49, [intake49-S-0607.md](m8-claims/intake49-S-0607.md): claim 11's no approach step against the gripped unit, in `approach::step`, with part h's approaches ending the grip, tests in `claims_s0607_r18.rs`; revision 14 at intake 45, [intake45-S-0607.md](m8-claims/intake45-S-0607.md); revision 2 built at step 3.8, revision 3 taken in at step 1m, [step1m-m7a.md](m7-claims/step1m-m7a.md), revision 4 at step 1n, [step1n-m7a.md](m7-claims/step1n-m7a.md), revisions 5 and 6 at step 1o, [step1o-m7a.md](m7-claims/step1o-m7a.md), revision 7 at step 1p, [step1p-m7a.md](m7-claims/step1p-m7a.md), revision 8 at step 1q, [step1q-m7a.md](m7-claims/step1q-m7a.md), revision 9 at step 1r, [step1r-m7a.md](m7-claims/step1r-m7a.md)) is built in `glasnost_sim::lift`, with hooks in `jump` and `jump::fly`. The ledger is [docs/m7-claims/S-0607.md](m7-claims/S-0607.md). Tests: `claims_s0607.rs` (45), `claims_s0607_standin.rs` (15), `claims_s0607_release.rs` (18), `claims_s0607_r7.rs` (19) and `claims_s0607_r18.rs` (7), fixtures in `lift_common/mod.rs`; five pairs in `pairs_m7a.rs` (a lifted controlled vehicle dies and frees its controller's slot; a lift attack on a bunkered vehicle goes to its bunker; a seized gattling spins down; a seized vehicle ignores its owner's recycle click; a drone stays on a seized host); the importer tests `lift_fields`, `lift_fields_absent`, `lift_locomotor_malformed_keeps_the_earlier_layer` and `lift_death_weapon_given`; the AI tests `crates/deterministic/ai/tests/lifter.rs`; the tally test `counts_seized_and_dropped_units`. The state is `Object.lift` (`LiftState`: seized, set down, dropped, the stand-in and the stashed own locomotor states, the grip kept with no destination `grip_without_dest`, the gripped unit, the lifter, the dropper and its house, the crash rate) and the magnetic waves (`World.waves`, keyed by their own object id: `Wave`, firer, target and fade). All of it is saved and hashed (save format 58).

- **The seize (claims 1 to 6, F1).** `combat::projectile` sends a hit whose warhead has the locomotor flag to `lift::seize_hit`. The hit does no damage. `lift::eligible` is claim 3 (a ground vehicle, not invulnerable, not in factory contact, not already seized, type size below the shot's damage, F1). A takeover warhead suppresses the seize (`mind`). A temporarily driving harvester ends its replacement or stops (claim 4). The seized unit stops, gives up its mark, starts *guard*, loses its spawned objects, releases a naval parasite, and gets the jumpjet locomotor with its own type's jumpjet fields. A second seize by the same lifter drops the first unit in the same tick. A house that is not human queues *hunt* (claim 6). Claim 3's conditions come before claim 4 (State and ordering). The warhead's locomotor decides the rest (claim 22, `combat::data::SeizeLocomotor`): the jumpjet one seizes as above; one that can stand in (drive, walk, ship, teleport, drop pod, and an absent key, which is the earlier layer's value, else teleport) seizes with that stand-in (claims 22 to 24, below); one that cannot (hover, mechanical walking, fly, rocket, tunnel, an unknown identifier) stops the target and puts it in *guard*, takes its spawned objects and parasite and releases the firer's previous victim, but does not seize it (`lift::not_seized`). Claim 4 comes first whatever the locomotor, an unknown identifier included (revision 5).
- **Other stand-ins (claims 22 to 24, revision 4).** `lift::install_stand_in` gives up the unit's marks as claim 5 does (`jump::give_up_marks` for a jumpjet type), stashes its own locomotor states in `LiftState.own` (`OwnLocomotor`) and installs a fresh driving state (drive, ship) or teleporting state (also for a landed aircraft, whose own teleport state is stashed; revision 7). Occupancy (`quirks.lift_standin_unmarked_cell`, default on; revision 7, Q-1338): installing it marks no cell, so the unit holds no mark until its stand-in's own movement marks one, keeps its last cell's mark after moving, and an unmoved unit stays unmarked for good and clears no mark it does not hold (`lift::stand_in_marks`); the teleporting landing cell is marked at the seize without releasing anything else (`teleport::fix_landing_with`); the drop pod's own locomotor marks the cell again. Off, driving, walking and ship keep their own cell's vehicle mark at the seize. The unit stays listed: while the lifter is its destination a walking or teleporting stand-in is between steps (`lift::stand_in_between_steps`, read by `drive::between_steps_or_chain`), so an allied mover meets *moving blocker*, and after the grip *stationary friendly*. The order-level destination becomes the lifter object, and the stand-in gets the lifter's point of claim 7 (`lift::instruct_stand_in`; for teleport `teleport::stand_in_instruction`). `tick` sends a unit with a moving stand-in to `lift::stand_in_tick` (drive, `naval::tick` for ship, `teleport::tick`; walk does nothing, design; a landed aircraft jumps with teleport and does nothing with drive, walk or ship, design, revision 7). `lift::current_locomotor` is the one answer to "which locomotor moves this unit now": readiness (`process::driving`, S-0215 claim 11 revision 68), crushing and the teleporter test read it. `lift::enforce`, after movement, at the start of the behaviour turn and in `econ::assign_destination`, keeps the destination on the lifter while gripped and ends the grip with the unit set down when a rule removes it (claim 12's no-destination row; for teleport that is the jump, whose target drop also clears the lifter's target, or the first movement update when the landing point is the own position, revision 7). A landing search with no cell at the seize keeps the grip with no destination (`LiftState.grip_without_dest`); a later empty assignment ends it (`lift::given_no_destination`). Drop pod ends at once (`lift::drop_pod_ends`: own marks back, grip ended, set down, still seized; the lifter's target is cleared, claim 12's drop-pod row, revision 7). Restoration: `quirks.lift_standin_stays_seized` (default on) never restores drive, walk, ship or teleport and leaves drop pod seized; off, claim 20 applies with the stand-in's own moving test (`lift::stand_in_moving`) and `lift::end_stand_in` puts the own states and marks back.
- **The pull (claims 7 to 11).** At the seize the movement point is fixed (`jump::lift_point`): S-0502 claim 16's search around the lifter's point (`lift::lifter_point`: a mobile lifter's heading point, a building's foundation centre, or its docking point when it has the pad, repair-depot or vehicle-bunker flag, revision 5: the obsolete-unloading fixed point first, then the resource-unloading point at the centre +128 x, then the bunker's quadrant point, then the docking places, with a held contact choosing among several). The order-level destination is the cell found. It is not refreshed; `lift::steer` only keeps the destination on it. Within 20 leptons the grip ends and the unit is dropped over that cell, normally a free cell beside the lifter, so it lands unhurt (claim 8). A seized unit ignores destinations (`lift::refuses_destination`, claim 9; also a recycle click, and the deployed or changing rules of `econ`). It does not fire (claim 10). Against its own gripped unit the lifter answers *rearming* with any weapon, right after the busy test and ahead of the layer, height and projectile tests (claim 11, `combat::check`); the magnetic beam's answer against other targets stays after them. Deploying, undeploying and unloading are not refused (claim 10).
- **The end of a grip (claims 12 to 14).** `lift::end_grip`, from every row of claim 12: arrival, an accepted destination (`lift::lifter_accepts`), a new hit, another seize, either side leaving play (`lift::removing`, from `objects`), no destination, a fall-back to rest (`lift::lifter_rest`, from `process::fall_back`), and the two building rows (`lift::building_turn`, `lift::building_destroyed`). Above 1 lepton the unit is dropped, otherwise set down (claim 13). The stop on dropping clears its destination, route and target, breaks off its contact and announces `Effect::Dropped` (the client deselects); then S-0502 claim 17's stop runs on its jumpjet movement, at a drop and at a set-down alike (revisions 5 and 6, `jump::lift_release_stop`): a found point is kept with the movement commitment, flight state, speeds and target height untouched; with no cell (lifter-ended, design) nothing self-destructs in either quirk position, both a dropped and a set-down unit lose their point and commitment (revision 8, Q-1342 answered); a dropped unit falls, with the direct reference over its cell centre (off it, the detour's point is its own cell's centre) and an update every tick, and a set-down one stops. Claim 9's no-destination exception takes S-0502 claim 17's ordinary outcome, quirk included. With the switch off and no cell (revision 8, design; Q-1343 answered), a claim 9 unit in the air keeps hovering over its current cell, set down and seized, with no point and no commitment, and neither descends nor flies (`lift::set_down_released`); one on the ground stays grounded and is restored as usual (S-0502 revision 16: `jump::stop_with` keeps a *grounded* jumpjet grounded). A kept point in another cell makes the fall use S-0502 claim 9's look-ahead at zero speed (revision 14's F3, `jump::height_step`); `jump::lift_fall` keeps the point and commitment. A set-down unit still moving takes off, flies to the point and lands. The dropper house gets the credit (claim 14).
- **The fall and the impact (claims 15 to 19, F2).** `jump::fly` takes the unit to its cell centre with a growing crash rate (`lift::rate`, starting afresh at each seize at the type's crash value narrowed to single precision; the drop comes before the growth) and a target height of 0 (claim 15). A fall that crosses a bridge deck lands on it. `lift::fall_ended` reads what lies underneath (claim 16): infantry are erased (quirk `lift_drop_erases_cell_tail`), a vehicle blocks, anything else blocks. Clear land: the unit descends and touches down unhurt (claim 17). Blocked water: it sinks (`lift::sunk`). Blocked land (every S-0209 outcome but clear, claim 17): the impact (claim 18) hurts the unit by the falling damage F2 (`FallingDamageMultiplier`, `CurrentStrengthDamage`) with the crushing warhead, detonates the death weapon (none: a plain area detonation, claim 18's design rule, with an importer warning), crashes a crashable type, and removes a survivor (quirk `lift_impact_removes_survivor`, credited once). A unit a detonation has killed gets the impact credit a second time (quirk `lift_impact_double_credit`, default on; `lift::impact_credit` credits from the snapshot when the unit is gone). A dropped aircraft detonates the demolition warhead first (claim 19). The ground layer is read even on a deck (quirk `lift_deck_impact_reads_ground`). All four quirks are tested both ways.
- **Restoration (claim 20).** A set-down seized unit whose jumpjet movement is not moving (revision 6: not while a movement commitment lasts) gets its own locomotor back. Revision 14 (answers Q-2035): the test is made at the end of each of its turns (`lift::end_of_turn`, last in `tick::object_turn`), whatever its behaviour and house, and still at a fall-back to rest (`lift::restore_at_rest`, from `process::fall_back`); both use `lift::restoration_test`. A clear-cell touchdown is restored at the end of the touchdown turn, *guard* and *hunt* alike; restoration starts no behaviour, so a hunter hunts from its next processing. Turns that end early have no test (lost, dead, sinking, being erased, warp-only updates); a unit being erased or in a chrono move's leaving stage is not restored at either test. Revisions 8 and 9 (design, `jumpjet_no_cell_self_destruct` off; Q-1344): a set-down unit hovering with no commitment, by any route, is not tested; at the end of each of its turns (revision 14; not at a fall-back to rest) it repeats the stop's search (`jump::lift_retry_stop`, S-0502 claim 17's search around its cell): with a cell it re-orders itself to the cell's centre (a movement point only), flies there, lands and is restored at the end of its landing turn; with none it keeps hovering. The check is the derived state (`jump::hovering_uncommitted`: set down, no lifter, *hovering*, no movement point, not moving), so it adds no saved field. At height 0 or less the stop leaves the unit *grounded* instead (`jump::stop_with`), and it is restored as usual. With the switch on claim 20's two original conditions decide. A seized survivor is put on the surface or the deck. Readiness asks the current locomotor (S-0215 claim 11, revision 68; `process::driving` reads `lift::current_locomotor`): a jumpjet-seized vehicle uses the jumpjet's *moving now* (claim 20), a driving stand-in the driving predicate; tests `claim_5_seized_turning_vehicle_is_ready`, `s0215_claim_11_current_locomotor`.
- **The magnetic wave (claim 21, F3, F4).** `combat::fire` calls `lift::shot`: no wave when the distance test F3 fails (a sum that wraps negative takes the root of its negation). The wave gets a new object id, is appended to the update order and updates once at creation; then `lift::wave_turn` runs in its own turn (`tick`'s object turn), fades it over 20 updates (F4) and removes it (claim 21). Its firer or target leaving play, which includes going inside a transport, bunker or building, ends it. A live wave counts for S-0301 claims 5 and 11.
- **Seams filled.** Jumpjet (`jump::seized` reads lift); in-place deployers (`inplace::beam_refused`, S-0517 claim 11); bunker (a lift hit at a bunkered vehicle goes to its bunker, which is not seized); mind control, gattling, parasites, spawners, naval sinking credit, temporal, superweapons, grinder, the factory release, cloak and approach take the seized state through their ordinary hooks.
- **Gaps.** The S-0309 claim 24 redirect to a bunker is applied by the target search only, not to an ordered target; `lift` redirects at the seize. The client does not draw the wave.
- **Importer.** Warheads `IsLocomotor` and `Locomotor` (a locomotor class identifier of S-0207 table T3, classed by claim 22; blanks trimmed; an unknown well-formed identifier warns and does not seize; a malformed value is ignored as if absent, keeping an earlier layer's value, and warns on a seizing warhead; an empty value is no entry under S-0001 claim 8 and does not warn, claim 22 revision 7), `[CombatDamage] FallingDamageMultiplier`, `CurrentStrengthDamage` and `DeathWeapon` (absent: a warning, claim 18).
- **Requests.** Q-1320 and Q-1322 to Q-1331 are answered by revision 3 (step 1m), Q-1321 by revision 4 (step 1n); their interim rules are retired. Revision 4 answers Q-1334 (claims 23 and 24) and Q-1335 (mechanical walking cannot stand in) in substance; both are built (the request files are left to the lead). Revisions 5 and 6 answer Q-1332 to Q-1336 (step 1o; Q-1334 and Q-1335 were already built) and Q-1338 in substance (claim 23's Occupancy bullet); their interims are retired. Revision 7 answers Q-1337 to Q-1341 (step 1p); revision 8 answers Q-1342 and Q-1343 (step 1q); revision 9 answers Q-1344 (step 1r); their interims are retired. S-0207 revision 41 (claim 20 b) and S-0209 revision 43 (claim 1) point at S-0607 claim 23 for the no-cell landing search and the stand-in marks; both are built through it. No request of the spec is open.
- **Own AI.** `glasnost_ai::lifter` (AI game format 23); see *Own AI (M6c: special abilities)*.
- **Client and lane.** (Recorded under revision 2; not re-run at step 1m, where the pull goes to a fixed cell beside the lifter.) The local lane scene `lift` (`crates/app/tests/m7a_lane.rs`, screenshots `local-lane/m7a/lift-before.png`, `lift-seized.png`, `lift-drop.png`, `lift-impact.png`) uses the install's lifter type and an unarmed liftable vehicle. The attack click shows the *attack* cursor. The vehicle is seized 14 ticks later, unhurt. After 201 ticks it is over a second vehicle at height 883. A move click on the lifter (cursor *move*) ends the grip 2 ticks later (dropped). The impact follows 43 ticks later: the dropped unit leaves play and the vehicle underneath is at 0 per mille. AI lane (map 8, seed 7, Yuri house 1; outputs `local-lane/m7a/s0607/ai-sides-0-2.txt` and `ai-sides-1-2.txt`): after merging step 1l: sides 0,2: Allied won at 18 682; the Yuri house built 2 lifters, gave 1 lift order and 1 drop order; 2 units were seized and 2 dropped. Sides 1,2: Soviet won at 32 468; 1 lifter, 1 lift order, no drop order, 4 seized, 4 dropped (lifters also fire at what they acquire themselves, and a dropped unit's grip may end by arrival or the lifter's death). Before step 1l: sides 0,2: Allied won at 16 081 (1 lifter, 1 lift order, 2 drop orders, 4 seized, 4 dropped); sides 1,2: Yuri won at 40 802 (2 lifters, 4 lift orders, 4 drop orders, 12 seized, 8 dropped). House 0 (Allied, then Soviet) cannot build a lifter. Replays and mid-game saves matched in both. Before the first lifter was queued behind the war factory's work, the Yuri house built none in either game.
- **Gate.** Both sides of the M7a duel may build MAGN (cost 400, strength 400, beam MAGRAY of damage 10, so every fixture vehicle of size 1 can be seized). With an Alpha-only lifter, Alpha won the gate seed before its last tick. The run counts lifter build orders, lift orders, vehicles seized and seized units dropped, and saves and loads at the first tick a unit is seized and the first tick one is dropped. Minimum / median / maximum over seeds 1 to 16, after merging step 1l (gate seed 3 plays all 12 000 ticks; seed 15 ends with Alpha's win at 11 968):
  - berserk orders 14/28/64, units made berserk 42/47/59;
  - sensor orders 2/2/2 (the known exception), intent ticks 2 296/4 701/5 667;

  - slave-miner buildings 3/4/12, slave ticks 1 980/7 127/9 293;
  - gattling stage rises 6/17/31, top-stage ticks 282/2 797/3 758;

  - grinder entry orders 5/11/21, units ground 5/9/12;

  **Unresolved:** bunker exit orders are 2 in seeds 4 and 13, and vehicles bunkered 2 in seed 13 (on main after step 1l, without lift: 3 and 3). Step 1l's shortfalls (takeover orders 0 in seed 8, takeover orders 1 and grinder entry orders 0 in seed 9) do not occur on this branch. After merging step 1k (before step 1l) the shortfall was elsewhere: gattling stage rises 2 and gas clouds 1 in seeds 11 and 14. A probe then found one GATV seized in each of those seeds, so the lifters were not dropping the gattlings; the course of the games had changed. Making GATV too heavy to lift (size 10) raised those counters, but Alpha then won the gate seed at 11 583, so the change was reverted. Each fixture or AI change moves which seeds fall short, so none was kept for the gate.

## Simulation (M7b: particle systems)

M7b step 3.1 builds the rest of S-0616 (revision 7; revision 8 taken in at step 1b, [step1b-m7b.md](m7-claims/step1b-m7b.md)): every creator of claim 1 whose owning part an approved spec covers, in the new module `glasnost_sim::emitters`, plus the fire emitter's sweep, swing and insertion in `glasnost_sim::particles`. The ledger is [docs/m7-claims/S-0616.md](m7-claims/S-0616.md); the tests are `claims_s0616_emitters.rs` (19; 29 since step 1b) and the importer tests `creator_keys` and (in the sim) `absent_creator_defaults`.

- **Effect weapons (claim 1, Terms).** `combat::fire::shot` calls `emitters::effect_weapon_emitter` after the projectile: for each of the weapon's fire, spark and railgun flags, in that order (revision 8), whose place is empty, one emitter of its `AttachedParticleSystem` at the fire point (the firer's position for a dropping projectile), owner object the firer, target point and target object the target's (railgun: F14's beam end point, below), and holds it in the firer's place of that kind (`CombatState.fire_emitter` with the firing slot and remembered target, `spark_emitter`, `railgun_emitter`). No first particle and no draw. The fire emitter is let go by a move order (`orders::apply_behaviour`), by a new target out of the weapon's reach (`emitters::retarget_let_go` in the fire turn), and by the owner's death; the spark emitter ends by its counter and the railgun by its line. A place empties when its system leaves play (`emitters::release_places` from `gas::system_turn`). The importer warns about an effect weapon without an attached system.
- **The railgun shot (S-0306 claim 27, S-0616 F14; step 1b).** With the railgun place empty, `combat::railgun::shot` walks F14's 50 samples from the emitter's position to the target's, tests a sample only on entering a new cell, and ends at the first tested sample below the terrain height (never a deck), else at the target's position; that end point is the emitter's fixed target point. With no terrain stop, every qualifying object of the entered cells (deck layer at (level + 4) × 104 leptons or above in a bridge cell; infantry, vehicles, aircraft and buildings, not the firer; buildings at distance 0; others strictly nearer the beam line than `railgundamageradius`, 128 when absent; no alliance test; the target always, last when not met) takes one ordinary hit of the weapon's raw damage with its warhead and the firer as attacker. A terrain stop cancels every hit. One draw R(0, 99) for a destroyable-cliff stop cell, or after the hits for a destroyable-cliff cell target; below `CollapseChance` (100 when absent) the cliff collapses, which is the seam `railgun::cliff_collapse` (no approved spec gives what a collapse changes).
- **S-0301 claim 5's gate and S-0519 claim 13.** `combat::check` answers *rearming* after the rank-5 countdown while any place of the weapon's own flags is held, and *impossible* (last of rank 4) while any place of the other weapon's flags is held (S-0301 revision 24). `combat::fire::arrival_delay`'s row 2 reads the same places. Sonic waves are not modelled.
- **The place, not the kind (S-0301 r63, Q-2181; M8 A3 intake 63).** A let-go of the fire place marks whatever system the place holds, whatever its kind (`emitters::fire_place_let_go`, from `gas::system_turn` for systems that are not fire-kind; `particles::fire_turn` lets go only when the place holds it). The spark and railgun places are freed only when their system leaves play, so a fire-kind system there is not marked by its own turn while its owner lives. At creation the railgun place passes no target object. Tests `claims_s0301_r63.rs` (Scenario F4's unmatched part).
- **Fire turn (claims 10 and 11, F5 to F7).** The owner decides the mark; a living owner's target object is refreshed; a turning owner with a target object sweeps (`particles::flame_sweep`, F5 with `quirks.flame_sweep_facing`, both positions tested; the system moves to the first weapon's fire point for the owner's current burst counter) and the system then emits on multiples of 3 as well as of its spawn interval. Each emission makes one held particle 1 lepton above the system aimed at the swing point (F6, `particles::swing` and `step_direction`) and moves it among the newest by one raw draw (F7).
- **Other creators (claim 1).** `barrel_particle_system` (S-0303 claim 19, after the 25 % draw; no owner house), `destruction_smoke` (S-0312 claim 19 f, from `combat::death`: smoke-kind entries only, nothing else stops it; a building leaving play at once marks it before it emits, an exploding or sold one lets it emit until its removal), `refinery_smoke` (smoke events: each payment row of `econ::harvest`, before the payment, and the empty-load row that starts *finishing*, so *k* + 1 per unloading; one per slave delivery after its payments, only when one paid something), `natural_smoke` (at placement in `econ::events::entered_map`; ×1.5 timer after each *now yellow* hit in `combat::damage`, compounding, never on *now red*; removed at full cloaking and remade at full uncloaking in `cloak::machine`, S-0516 claim 18), trigger actions 88 and 89 (`triggers`, both `Implemented`). Claim 4 also clears a removed target object.
- **Sparks in the turn (claim 1, Q-1404 answered).** `deactivated_sparks` runs first in the living branch of `tick::object_turn`, before the warp, the expected-health bookkeeping and the tint draw (a deactivated vehicle is one disabled by S-0405 claim 13, nothing else). `damage_sparks` (on-map cyborg infantry only) runs after the behaviour processing (`process::turn` or `tail`), so after the overload and self-healing, and before the radiation hit, which now follows it (an erasing turn still takes the radiation hit before it returns), the movement, the parasite step and the infantry firing.
- **Healing marks of damage smoke (claim 1, S-0314 claims 9 and 10).** After the object's own self-healing gain, and after a non-organic vehicle's house-wide gain, `heal::turn` marks its damage smoke when the health ratio is strictly above yellow or the object is more than 10 leptons below the surface. Infantry and organic vehicles healed house-wide take no test.
- **Spark turns of 0 or less (claim 20).** A spark system of such a type (absent `SparkSpawnFrames` included) is marked in its first turn with no burst and no draw, so an emitter leaves then and frees the spark place; the importer warns.
- **Seams left.** `emitters::electric_bolt_sparks` and `emitters::voxel_animation_system`: no approved spec covers their owning parts (the electric-bolt shot; voxel animations as objects of play). `electric_bolt_sparks` was filled at M8 step C5 part 1 (S-0316; section *Electric bolts (M8 step C5 part 1)*), `voxel_animation_system` at M8 step C5 part 2 (S-0317; section *Debris pieces with voxel models (M8 step C5 part 2)*).
- **State.** `ParticleSystem.target_object`; the four places on `CombatState` (`#[serde(default)]`). Save format 59; 60 at step 1b, which drops the natural smoke's `yellowed` mark.
- **Import.** Weapons `AttachedParticleSystem`; buildings `NaturalParticleSystem`, `NaturalParticleLocation`, `RefinerySmokeParticleSystem`, `RefinerySmokeOffsetOne` … `Four`; types `DestroySmokeOffset` (S-0312); `[General] RefinerySmokeFrames` (absent 25), `ConditionRedSparkingProbability` (0.02), `ConditionYellowSparkingProbability` (0.01); the spark-kind entries of `DamageParticleSystems`; at step 1b `[CombatDamage] railgundamageradius` (128) and `CollapseChance` (100) for S-0306 claim 27, and the warning for a spark-kind type whose `SparkSpawnFrames` is absent, 0 or negative. `data/schema/ruleset.schema.json` regenerated.
- **Q-1405 (answered by S-0616 revision 10 and S-0306 revision 15, M7b step 1c).** δ is the fixed 10956549 / 536870912; every *t*ᵢ, *t*₄₉ included, follows the rounding mode, so under round-toward-zero *s*₄₉ can fall one cell short of *Q* while the no-stop end point stays the exact *Q*. The line distance of S-0306 claim 27 follows its mixed precision (`railgun::line_distance`); a distance that is not a number (exact collinearity, a zero-length beam) qualifies under `quirks.railgun_collinear_hits` (default on; off: the finite Euclidean distance, to the start for a zero-length beam). Q-1400 to Q-1404 are answered by S-0616 revision 8 (step 1b). No interim is left.
- **Client.** The view draws each particle by its system's kind (our placeholders: white puff smoke, orange fire, yellow spark, cyan railgun, green gas) and spreads a railgun system's particles from its position to its target point, since the line's shape is drawing only (`mapview::live`).
- **Stock data and lane.** The only stock effect weapon is a spark repair weapon in a gunner vehicle's second slot (chosen by an engineer-like passenger's mode); no stock Soviet or Yuri type carries an effect weapon, and no stock type carries natural smoke, a smoke-kind destruction entry or a cyborg infantry spark entry. The lane scenes are in `crates/app/tests/m7b_lane.rs`; where no stock type carries a creator they flag one in the lane's own copy of the rules.

## Simulation (M7b: special-hit effects)

M7b step 3.2 builds S-0615 (revision 2): the death-kind animations, the burning walker and the disc laser. The ledger is [docs/m7-claims/S-0615.md](m7-claims/S-0615.md); the tests are `claims_s0615.rs` (37; 40 since step 1c), two pairs in `pairs_m7b.rs` ([pairs-m7b.md](m7-claims/pairs-m7b.md)), the importer tests `sim_special_tests.rs`, two audio tests and a tally test.

- **Death kinds (claims 1 to 3).** `combat::death::death_kind_effect` runs after S-0107 claim 17's choice of kind: kinds 3, 4, 6, 7 and 10 make their game-wide animation (`[General]` `InfantryExplode`, `FlamingInfantry`, `InfantryHeadPop`, `InfantryNuked`, `InfantryBrute`), kind 5 the animation type at registration index 1, at the unit's position, with the usual creation draws and none of its own. A kind whose type is absent or unlisted still removes the unit. Kind 9 is a seam ("Seam: S-0611 is not built yet (M7b)").
- **Burning walker (claims 4 to 11).** A kind-4 animation whose type has `IsFlamingGuy` becomes a walker (`glasnost_sim::walker`, `World.walkers`), with an object number in the update order; its turn runs in `tick::object_turn`. The water search (F2), the step and fallback (one ranged draw), passability, running (F3, soft-float table trigonometry), arrival and the retry limit, the deck fall (S-0511's descent) and expiry follow the claims. Damage over time (S-0109 claim 17, F5) is delivered on expiring advances only, with `quirks.anim_damage_starts_at_one` now implemented (both positions tested; it left the NOT_IMPLEMENTED list). The client draws the walker's animation at its position with the F1 frame.
- **Disc laser (claims 12 to 18).** `combat::fire` hands a `DiskLaser` weapon's shot to `disc::shot` (no projectile, no shot report); `disc::update` runs as S-0203 row 12, newest first, before the turn pass: re-check, eight arc steps two updates apart, the final beam and detonation in tick T + 17, removal in T + 18. `quirks.disc_laser_no_house` (default on) leaves the detonation without an attacking house; both positions tested. `Effect::Disc` carries the charge-up and report sounds; `Effect::Beam` the beams.
- **State.** `World.walkers`, `World.discs` (`#[serde(default)]`), saved and hashed. Save format 60; AI game format unchanged (23).
- **Import.** `[General]` `FlamingInfantry`, `InfantryHeadPop`, `InfantryNuked`, `InfantryBrute`; `[AudioVisual]` `DiskLaserChargeUp`; weapons `DiskLaser`, `LaserDuration`, `LaserInnerColor`, `LaserOuterColor`, `LaserOuterSpread`, `IsHouseColor`; art `IsFlamingGuy`, `RunningFrames`, `Damage`. The importer warns about a walker type without a positive running-frame count. `data/schema/ruleset.schema.json` regenerated.
- **Q-1420 to Q-1427 (answered by S-0615 revision 3 and S-0109 revision 31, M7b step 1c; [step1c-m7b.md](m7-claims/step1c-m7b.md)).** Q-1420: the disc detonation is area damage only (S-0303 revision 18, S-0514 revision 9). Q-1421: a parachutist's virus effect comes first, before its kind 3 or 5 animation; a not-human ground death with kind 8 makes its die-1 request, then one virus effect. Q-1422: the disc measures from the firer's position to the target's position (a cell target at its centre at terrain height, its final point raised by 416 over a deck); a loss applies at the moment it happens and for good. Q-1423: a negative death kind acts as kind 0; the importer warns. Q-1424: a walker's fall descends in the update that starts it, from rate 0. Q-1425: walker damage allows resource reduction and names no attacker or house. Q-1426: `quirks.disc_laser_unlimited_range` (default on: −512 compared as it is, always cancelled; off: never cancelled on distance). Q-1427: a walker type with no or an unknown frame count has N = 0 and is removed at its first removal test; the importer warns.
- **Own AI (M7b).** No decision added for the special hits. The Floating Disc's disc laser fires through the generic attack logic, as a person's unit would on an attack order. Its drain (S-0608) is built at step 3.3 and the own AI sends drainers (*Simulation (M7b: drain)*). Walkers and death animations are not choices.
- **Stock data and lane.** Every death kind with an animation has a stock warhead; the stock Floating Disc fires its disc laser and the hit lands (lane scenes `deaths`, `walker` and `disc` in `crates/app/tests/m7b_lane.rs`). The skirmish lane prints "special hits: burning walkers N; disc lasers shot N, hit N, cancelled N".

## Simulation (M7b: drain)

M7b step 3.3 builds S-0608 (revision 3; revision 5 taken in at M7b step 1d): drain links, their payouts and their ends. The ledger is [docs/m7-claims/S-0608.md](m7-claims/S-0608.md). The tests are `claims_s0608.rs` (34), six pairs in `pairs_m7b.rs` ([pairs-m7b.md](m7-claims/pairs-m7b.md)), the importer tests `sim_drain_tests.rs`, `a_draining_neutral_object_does_not_retaliate` (S-0309), `a_drained_building_does_not_support` (S-0518), seven AI tests and a tally test.

- **The attempt and the link (claims 1 to 6).** `combat::fire::shot` hands a draining weapon's shot to `drain::attempt`, which makes no projectile, damage or draws. Against a cell or an object of a type that is not drainable it does nothing and keeps the target. Against a drainable object it clears the target, and forms a link only when the drainer's cell, height aside, is a cell of the building's foundation. The firing check (`combat::check`, rank 4) refuses the building the firer drains, an object already being drained and a type that is not drainable. S-0308 table P row 8 (`combat::weapon`) makes a draining drainer use its other weapon. Forming a link makes the house's power due, releases a controller building's victims (`mind::drain_link_formed`, S-0601 claim 10) and starts the drain animation with its creation draw.
- **Payouts (claims 7 to 10, F1, F2).** In a drained resource-destination building's own turn of every tick with *n* mod |*P*| = 0, `drain::building_turn` moves min(*A*, available credits) from its house to the drainer's, in wrapping 32-bit arithmetic with no clamp. Payouts skip a building that is being erased or materialising. *P* = 0 never pays, and the importer warns (the lead question's proposed departure).
- **The end of the link (claims 11 to 14, F3).** `drain::alliance_test` runs in every turn of the drainer that is not being erased. A vehicle drainer also runs `drain::position_check` on ticks with *n* mod 16 = 2. A killing hit (`drain::killed`, before the death effects), a removal (`drain::removing`) or successful temporary storage (`drain::stored`, step 1d) of either party ends its links. `drain::end` restores power (due), the firing answer, sale and support.
- **Quirks.** `quirks.drain_relink_strands_previous` (default on: a second link leaves the first building drained, paying and out of both tests until the drainer is removed or stored) and `quirks.drain_position_check_vehicles_only` (default on). Both positions are tested. The view removes an old drain animation in both positions, since that is a display defect.
- **Consumers.** `being_drained` and `draining` feed S-0405 (the drain blackout, the building's illegal firing answer), S-0408 claim 3 (no sale), S-0518 claim 2 (no support), S-0215 claim 37 (d) (no ordinary approach while draining), S-0309 claim 25 (h) (a draining neutral object does not retaliate) and S-0601 claim 10.
- **State.** `World.drain` (`DrainWorld`: every drained building with its drainer, and each drainer's newest partner), saved and hashed. Save format 62 (64 since M7b step 1c, 65 after its merge with S-0612: S-0405 revision 20 drops the building switches' `last_cell`, since disabling now follows the cell-arrival event); AI game format 24.
- **Import.** Weapons `DrainWeapon`; types `Drainable`, `ResourceDestination`; `[CombatDamage]` `DrainMoneyFrameDelay` (absent 100), `DrainMoneyAmount` (absent 0), `DrainAnimationType` (absent: none). `data/schema/ruleset.schema.json` carries the new fields.
- **Revision 5 (requests Q-1440 to Q-1444, taken in at M7b step 1d; [step1d-m7b.md](m7-claims/step1d-m7b.md)).**
  - Claim 15 (Q-1440): in `tick::object_turn` the drainer's position check (`drain::position_check`) runs even when the drainer is being erased, and the alliance test (`drain::alliance_test`) follows it unless it is; both come before the behaviour start. A building's periodic income (`tech::turn`) and then its payout (`drain::building_turn`) come after the behaviour start and before the due behaviour processing (`process::tail`); the later power and economy turn no longer runs `tech::turn`. The superweapon warp-only stages skip both tests (S-0513 claim 51).
  - Claim 3 (Q-1441): part (a) answers right after the deactivated-vehicle answer (*rearming* while phasing in, else *illegal*) and reads the newest link only; parts (b) and (c) answer after the temporal hold, before the spawner answers (`combat::check`).
  - Claim 16 (Q-1442): the suicide hit replaces the attempt; the attempt sets no last shot, reveals nothing and uncloaks nothing. An aircraft whose object target the attempt clears departs (S-0501 claim 12: *done*, 1-tick delay, the round pending; `air::drain_departure`). `RevealOnFire` absent is now yes (S-0409 revision 16).
  - Claim 17 (Q-1443): `objects::take_inside` ends every link of the stored party at once (`drain::stored`), the stranded one included. Failed boarding keeps the link; the warp is not storage.
  - Claim 18 (Q-1444): no occupant gate; a bunkered vehicle keeps its own firing answer, and absorber and grinder entries go on as at any other time (no code change).
- **Client.** The view draws the drain animation on every drainer that has a partner and drops it when the link ends (`mapview::live`). The audio plays the animation's start sound at the drainer. The skirmish lane prints "drain: links formed N, ended N; credits drained N; drained-building ticks N", and the uses line adds the drainer builds and drain orders.
- **Own AI (M7b: drain).** `glasnost_ai::drain` (`Uses::drainer_builds`, `Uses::drain_orders`). An idle drainer that drains nothing gets the attack click at a seen enemy building it could drain now: one of a drainable type, not drained already, against which it would choose its draining weapon and which the firing check does not refuse. A building that pays credits comes first, then a power plant, then any other; within each the nearest comes first, and no two drainers go for one building. `drain::filter_orders` drops every other part's order to a draining drainer (a move would end the link), keeps a drainer on its way out of the waves, and refuses a second drainer at a claimed building. The house keeps up to 3 drainers, built like lifters. One fix came out of the lane. A drain click and a later order of another part in the same think went to the same disc, so the disc only kept the refinery as its target, in *guard*, and never flew there. Now a drainer counts as on its way only in *attack*, and a drainer given a drain order takes no later order in that think (`a_drainer_that_only_keeps_its_target_is_sent_again`).
- **Gate.** `duel-m7b` (`crates/sim-gate/src/m7b.rs`, the synthetic map of `glasnost_ai::fixture_m7b`: M7a's duel plus three Alpha discs with a draining secondary of a tenth of a cell, Beta's drainable power plant and refinery, *P* 30, *A* 40). It saves and loads at the first tick a link exists. The full run asserts that drain orders, links formed, credits drained and drained-building ticks are each at least 3. Seed 11, 8 000 ticks: at 12 000 Alpha won every seed between 8 896 and 10 314, so the game is cut while both sides play. Over seeds 1–16 the minimum and median are: drain orders 5 and 8, links 3 and 5, credits drained 7 120 and 7 920, drained-building ticks 16 173 and 17 929. Seed 11 gives 24, 8, 7 120 and 16 173, with its first link at tick 1 167. The median equals the maximum, 7 920, so at least half the seeds reach it. Drainer builds are 0 in every seed: Alpha keeps its three start discs. The `duel-m7a` sweep over seeds 1–16 is unchanged. Since M7b AI round 2 Beta's construction yard is drainable too (see *Own AI (M7b)*, AI round 2, for the current minima).
- **Stock data and lane.** Lane scenes `drain-power`, `drain-refinery` and `drain-blackout` (`crates/app/tests/m7b_lane.rs`) spawn a stock drainer and a stock drainable building, click the attack and wait for the link. The refinery linked after 190 ticks and credits moved. The blackout scene linked after 139 ticks and the house's output dropped to 0. On the power plant the first attempt lost its target without a link: the disc fired before it was over the foundation, which is the spec's open question 2. A second click linked within 2 ticks. Shots are in `local-lane/m7b/drain-*.png`. In the AI lane (map 8, seed 7, Yuri house 1), against the Allied side, Allied won at 19 465. The Yuri house built no drainer and gave no drain order: its credits sat near 0 and the drainer was startable only briefly, the same gate as for the lifters. Against the Soviet side, Soviet won at 102 291. The Yuri house had 1 drainer build, 4 drain orders, 1 link (ended), 0 credits drained and 83 388 drained-building ticks.

## Simulation (M7b: mutation)

M7b step 3.4 builds S-0611 (revision 1; revision 2 taken in at M7b step 1d; revision 3 after M7b, [step1g-m7b.md](m7-claims/step1g-m7b.md); revision 4 at step 1j, [step1j-m7b-sim.md](m7-claims/step1j-m7b-sim.md), which answers Q-1530 as its interim already built): mutating deaths, infantry-making animations and the genetic mutation launch. The ledger is [docs/m7-claims/S-0611.md](m7-claims/S-0611.md). The tests are `claims_s0611.rs` (31), `claims_creation_set.rs` (4), four pairs in `pairs_m7b.rs` ([pairs-m7b.md](m7-claims/pairs-m7b.md)), the importer tests `sim_mutation_tests.rs`, the audio test `genetic_mutation_sounds`, six AI tests and a tally test.

- **Conversion (claims 1 and 5 to 8).** S-0107 claim 17's row 7 hands a kind-9 death to `mutation::convert`. Its test reads the foot land multiplier, then S-0208 claim 3's position choice on the cell's ground record with its draw, then a building in the ground layer. A converted victim leaves play in its death tick (`combat::death::leave_play`) and a conversion animation starts at its position. A refused victim takes the die-2 request; its standing position is freed at once and again at its removal (`mutation::removing`).
- **Infantry-making animations (claims 8 to 13).** S-0109's stage clock moved from the client into the simulation (`glasnost_sim::anim_clock`; `mapview::anims` re-exports it), so these animations play in the simulation with scenario-stream draws (`World.mutation.makers`; each maker has an object number in the update order and updates in its own turn, `tick::object_turn`). In each update a maker marks its position and the cell's infantry house. At its end it resolves its owner (the civilian house when it has none or its owner is defeated), creates the made type (F4) facing 24 576, places it under S-0614 claim 20's rules, and gives *hunt* only for the neutral and special houses. A refused placement steps one frame back and tries again. Each attempt creates the made type's whole creation set (revision 3, claim 14 and F5: `objects::create` makes the unit, then its spawn slot objects in slot order, then its slaves in place order, each with its own set first); a refused set is discarded with its launcher records, its draws and numbers kept. A type that closes a cycle of spawned and slave types gets no fill inside the chain and the rules warn (`MissileRules::warnings`; Q-1530 interim: no controller or group either).
- **The launch (claims 2 to 4).** `superweapons::launch` hands the genetic kind to `mutation::launch`: the launch animation at P + 5, the sound and radar event, then one area hit of 10 000 in explosion mode, or one hit of each listed infantry unit's strength over the effect block in cell mode, with no list cut.
- **Quirks.** `quirks.mutation_test_ground_record`, `quirks.refused_mutation_frees_position` and `quirks.infantry_animation_occupancy` (all default on). Each is tested in both positions; none is on the NOT_IMPLEMENTED list.
- **State.** `World.mutation` (`MutationWorld`: makers and refused victims' freed positions), saved and hashed. Save format 63 (64 since M7b step 1c, which merged it with step 1c's own format 63; 65 after the merge with S-0612); AI game format 25 (`Uses::mutator_launches`).
- **Import.** `[General]` `MutateExplosion`, `InfantryMutate`, `AnimToInfantry`, `IonBlast`; `[SpecialWeapons]` `MutateExplosionWarhead`, `MutateWarhead`; `[AudioVisual]` `GeneticMutatorActivateSound`; art `MakeInfantry` (with S-0109's `LoopStart`, `LoopEnd`, `LoopCount`, `Reverse`, `PingPong`, `RandomLoopDelay`, `Next`). The rules warn about a missing mode warhead, a missing conversion animation, an unknown made type and an index outside the list. `data/schema/ruleset.schema.json` regenerated.
- **Revision 2 (Q-1460 and Q-1461, taken in at M7b step 1d).** Claim 14 (Q-1460): every creation attempt takes the raw creation draw, then the position-choice draw only where S-0208 claim 3 takes it; a refused attempt keeps its object number, none is given back. Claims 7 and 8 (Q-1461): a crashable victim crashes only with height above the surface, keeps its position and is not taken out of play, and its conversion animation still starts and marks its position at once (`mutation::mark_now`). Q-1460's request file still says open.
- **Client.** The view draws makers from `World.mutation` in their owner's colour; the audio plays the activation sound and the makers' start sounds. The skirmish lane prints "mutation: mutator launches N, infantry-making animations N, infantry mutated N", and the uses line adds the mutator launches.
- **Gate.** `duel-m7b` gains the mutator by setup only (`glasnost_ai::fixture_m7b`): Alpha's GENEB (cost 300, no prerequisite) gives GENE (cell mode, charge 0.2 minutes, needs no power); the cell warhead MUTATE has kind 9; MUTANIM makes a MADMAN. Beta starts with four crowds of three unarmed CROWD walkers around its start, because Beta builds no barracks within the run and its six start riflemen alone are too few for three launches. The run saves and loads at the first tick a mutation animation exists and asserts at least 3 mutator launches and 3 infantry mutated, besides the drain counters. Seed 11, 8 000 ticks: 4 launches, 12 mutated. Over seeds 1–16 (min / median): drain orders 4 / 7, links 3 / 5, credits drained 7 280 / 7 320, drained-building ticks 16 702 / 16 962, mutator launches 3 / 4, infantry mutated 9 / 12. The drain medians moved (they were 8, 5, 7 920 and 17 929) because the crowds change the game; every minimum stays at 3 or more. The `duel-m7a` sweep over seeds 1–16 is unchanged. The goldens changed because the state hash covers the new state.
- **Stock data and lane.** `side-audit 2 8 7`: the stock mutator building is offered, built and placed for the Yuri side, with superweapon kind `geneticconverter`. Lane scene `mutator` (`crates/app/tests/m7b_lane.rs`) places the giving building for the local house, cuts the charge short and turns its power need off in the lane's own copy of the rules, and fires at five enemy human infantry. All five converted at once, and five units of the local house appeared 56 ticks later (`local-lane/m7b/mutator-*.png`).

## Simulation (M7b: psychic dominator)

M7b step 3.5 builds S-0612 (revision 2): the psychic dominator's launch, phases, area damage and takeover sweep, with the S-0104 global lighting it times. The ledger is [docs/m7-claims/S-0612.md](m7-claims/S-0612.md). The tests are `claims_s0612.rs` (30), three pairs in `pairs_m7b.rs` ([pairs-m7b.md](m7-claims/pairs-m7b.md)), the importer tests `dominator_keys` and `absent_dominator_keys`, the audio test `dominator_sounds`, eight AI tests (`crates/deterministic/ai/tests/dominator.rs`) and a tally test.

- **The launch (claims 1 to 3).** `superweapons::launch` hands the dominator kind to `domination::launch` (the `yuri_seam` is gone): the radar event, the domination with its fixed house and cell, the lead-in animation at the cell centre on the terrain surface, the lighting re-evaluation, the announcement and the positional sound. Either animation type unset or unknown starts no domination (the importer warns). While a domination is in force every house's launch is refused and keeps its charge.
- **Phases (claims 4 to 7, F1, F2).** `domination::update` runs in row 3 after `lighting::update` and before the storm. The sweep comes at the first update after the first whose lead-in stage reaches F1's threshold (compared in F64, as the spec gives it), or at the next update once the lead-in has ended. The final phase begins at F2's *t*₁ of the sweep animation; the domination is over when the current ambient reaches the target. The domination animations take turns in the turn pass (`tick::object_turn`, `World.domination.anims`), so S-0203 claim 10's removal skip (`quirks.update_order_removal_skip`) moves the final phase by one tick; the test covers both positions. A zero ambient rate freezes the ambient, and a domination then ends at the next update.
- **The sweep (claims 8 to 15, F3).** At the sweep: the blast ring at the target cell (row 15, 79 updates, no draws), the sweep animation, then one area hit of the damage amount and warhead at the centre, attributed to the house of the storm in force or last requested (`quirks.dominator_damage_storm_house`), then the takeovers. F3's rings, from ring 0 out to the capture range (the largest ring 10), list the cells; in each cell the ground layer (`quirks.dominator_ground_layer_only` off: the bridge deck after it), from the start object (`quirks.dominator_sweep_from_nearest`). Each object S-0601 claim 13 calls dominable (`mind::dominable`, split out of `mind::dominate`) is taken through `mind::dominate`: owner change, permanent control, one ring (the double ring of the original is fixed with no switch), its own house's objects marked by `quirks.dominator_marks_own_objects`. A dying object is taken only with `quirks.dominator_takes_dying`; an object this sweep's own damage destroyed is never taken. Taken objects of the neutral and special houses hunt.
- **Coexistence (claims 16 to 18).** The dominating house is fixed at launch, so removing the launching building changes nothing. The storm outranks the dominator in the global lighting choice; the sweep's timing and takeovers do not change. A taken bunkered vehicle keeps its link (`bunker::holds_through_owner_change`); an absorbed passenger is not reached; a taken controller keeps its victims and a controlled unit is released by its temporary controller first (three pairs).
- **Lighting (S-0104).** `glasnost_sim::lighting` (`World.lighting`, `LightWorld`): the base, current and target ambient, the global choice (normal, ion storm, dominator, nuke), the ambient step every interval with `quirks.dominator_rate_thousandths` and `quirks.zero_rate_freezes_ambient` (both now off the NOT_IMPLEMENTED list), the nuke flash's re-evaluation and the set-base action. The view (`mapview::live`) relights its cells and terrain tiles when the current ambient or the chosen set changes (`Painter::relight`: a full recompute with the chosen set's terms and tint).
- **State.** `World.domination` (`DominationWorld`: the domination, its animations, the blast rings) and `World.lighting`, saved and hashed. Save format 64 (65 since M7b step 1c merged it with step 1c's own format 64); AI game format 26 (`Uses::dominator_launches`).
- **Import.** `[General]` `DominatorFirstAnim`, `DominatorSecondAnim`, `DominatorFireAtPercentage` (absent 50), `DominatorCaptureRange` (absent 2), `DominatorDamage` (absent 50), `DominatorWarhead`; `[AudioVisual]` `PsychicDominatorActivateSound`, `AmbientChangeRate`, `AmbientChangeStep`. The rules warn about an unset animation type, a percentage the lead-in cannot reach, a sweep animation that cannot reach its threshold stage and a negative capture range. `data/schema/ruleset.schema.json` regenerated.
- **Client.** The view draws the domination animations with the simulation's type and image frame (`mapview::live::Live::follow_dominations`; the blast ring is not drawn yet, a view seam) and relights for the dominator's lighting; the audio plays the activation sound at the cell. The effect stream adds `Effect::Domination` (started, sweep, damage with the damaged and killed counts, each taken object, final, over, ring ended; presentation only). The skirmish lane prints "psychic domination: dominator launches N, objects taken over N, objects damaged N, killed N", and the uses line adds the dominator launches.
- **Gate.** `duel-m7b` gains the dominator by setup only (`glasnost_ai::fixture_m7b`): Alpha's DOMB (cost 100, no prerequisite, so the superweapon step builds it before GENEB) gives DOM (charge 0.3 minutes, needs no power); the lead-in DOMLEAD has 8 frames, the sweep DOMSWEEP 12; the warhead PSIWH and the damage 30; the percentage and range are the absent-key defaults. A skirmish drops pre-placed structures of every player house (S-0009 claim 27; an AI house counts as human-controlled), so Alpha cannot start with the building; its superweapon step builds it at about tick 5 600. Beta's crowds grow from four to eleven, because the mutator and the dominator take from the same crowds; the new ones stand on Beta's flanks, away from the discs' path (two in mid-map cut the drain links to 2). The run saves and loads at the first tick a domination is in force and asserts at least 3 dominator launches and 3 objects taken over, besides the drain and mutator counters. Seed 11, 8 000 ticks: first domination at tick 5 878, 4 launches, 12 taken; 4 mutator launches, 12 mutated. Over seeds 1–16 (min / median): drain orders 3 / 5, links 3 / 4, credits drained 7 280 / 7 280, drained-building ticks 16 915 / 17 436, mutator launches 4 / 7, infantry mutated 12 / 21, dominator launches 4 / 5, objects taken over 11 / 15. The `duel-m7a` sweep over seeds 1–16 is unchanged (every seed line and every min, median and max equal to 2091560's). The goldens changed because the state hash covers the new state.
- **Stock data and lane.** `side-audit 2 8 7`: the stock dominator building (superweapon kind `psychicdominator`) is offered at tick 6 915, ordered at 13 215, built at 19 480 and placed at 19 486 for the Yuri side. Of the side's 79 types, 29 are offered and built and 28 placed or released; the one left is a naval yard with no cell on map 8; every type never offered belongs to another side or country (forbidden or required country, stolen tech, another side's building) or is above the tech level. Lane scene `dominator` (`crates/app/tests/m7b_lane.rs`) places the giving building for the local house, cuts the charge short and turns its power need off in the lane's own copy of the rules, and fires at three enemy infantry and two enemy vehicles: the record is ready after 31 ticks; at the launch the global choice turns to the dominator (ambient 68, target 150), the sweep comes 36 ticks later and the domination is over 64 ticks after that; 4 of the 5 were taken (the stock capture range is 1, and one infantry unit walked out of the swept cells before the sweep). Screenshots `local-lane/m7b/dominator-launch.png`, `dominator-lighting.png` (the dominator set's tint on the relit terrain; cells near a light source keep their own colour; trees and other static objects of the cell pass are not relit, a view seam) and `dominator-taken.png`. AI games on map 8, quick mode, seeds 7 to 10: Yuri against Allied, Allied won all four (ticks 21 906, 20 582, 25 628, 24 332); Yuri against Soviet, Soviet won seed 7 (34 233) and Yuri seeds 8, 9 and 10 (41 059, 32 710, 32 053). The dominator counters stayed 0 in all eight, because the Yuri house never finished a dominator building: the lane now names the dominator and genetic kinds in its "building gives" and "ready" counts, and in the Soviet game of seed 8 the Yuri house held a genetic building but never a dominator building. It was short of money all game: income 26 174 over 41 059 ticks, credits 97, 9, 4 and 83 at ticks 20 000, 30 000, 36 000 and 40 000, with the dominator building the one giving type left that it could build and place (the superweapon step builds the cheapest giving type first, and the genetic building is cheaper). With no dominator ever ready, there was nothing for a person to fire, so the firing rule is unchanged. The same game at 2091560 ends at tick 47 731 (Yuri won); it changes on this branch because the brain now fires the dominator kind, so the superweapon step counts the dominator building as a kind it still lacks. Seen on the way: the genetic mutator stood ready for 2 907 to 10 051 house-ticks in those games with no launch, and the same seed-8 game at 2091560 also launched none (step 3.4's chooser, not this branch; not investigated here).

## Own AI (M5b)

`glasnost_ai::game::Game` runs every game with AI houses: the world plus one `brain::Brain` per AI house of the setup (`HouseSetup.ai` and the player role, glossary *AI house*). In each tick, after the person's orders, every AI house that is not defeated or removed thinks and its orders go through `orders::issue` like a person's; then `tick::step` runs. The brains are saved with the game (`Game::save`, `Game::load`) and hashed with the world (`Game::state_hash`), and a replay holds only the setup and the person's orders: `Game::run_replay` runs the AI again. The AI draws from no random stream.

The brain plays in two layers (PLAN §4.7 stage 1 and enough of stage 2):

- **The plan** (`scripted::Script`, M5a's scripted builder), every `THINK_PERIOD` ticks: deploy the MCV, build power, a refinery and factories, keep two harvesters, build the army round-robin and attack in waves (see `scripted.rs`). It rebuilds lost power, refineries, factories and harvesters, since it asks for each role whenever the house has none (fewer harvesters than the plan's).
- **Selling out** (part of the plan's think): a house whose harvesters' summed loads and credits show no income for `BROKE_PATIENCE` ticks (a rise within `SALE_WINDOW` ticks of its own sale counts as the refund) and that cannot afford anything it could build sells its costliest sellable building (`sell::sellable`, S-0408 claim 3), one per patience period, keeping one factory per kind while it has army units, and from then on builds no buildings, harvesters or construction yard, only army units. Income ends the mode. A house with nothing left to fight with thus sells out, so a game between two broke houses ends. The loads of its slaves on the map count with the harvesters', so a house whose slaves are delivering is not broke.
- **The reactions**, every tick (no reaction delay): place a finished building at once; defend the base (every army unit within 30 cells of home that is not already fighting one attacks the nearest seen enemy within 12 cells of a house building); rebuild a lost construction yard (build the cheapest buildable unit that deploys into one, queued behind what the line holds); and move an idle deploying unit that could not deploy where it stands to the nearest spiral cell where the deploy's cell test (S-0415 claim 3, the answer a person's deploy cursor gives) passes.

- **New units (M6a).** Aircraft are army of their own kind in the round-robin; a type the house cannot build yet makes the tech climb build its blocker, so a pad for an aircraft and a naval yard for a ship (ordered only when the placement spiral has a cell for it, which for a yard means water). An idle aircraft with ammunition attacks the nearest seen enemy it can hit and flies back to rearm by itself (S-0501); aircraft are kept out of the land waves. A launcher that spawns rockets (S-0506) is army like any armed vehicle. A unit whose goal lies outside its reach region (`Script::reachable`, S-0212 claim 4: a ship for a goal on land) goes to the nearest cell of its own region instead, as a person sends ships to the water off an enemy base. Defence and attacks pick only targets one of the unit's weapons can hit (`scripted::can_attack`: `weapon::would_use` gives a weapon and the firing check answers neither *illegal* nor *impossible*), so ground units do not chase hovering aircraft.

What it knows is what a person in its seat knows: its own objects, its production lines and sidebar offer, enemy objects on cells its house has explored (`econ::shroud::explored`; fog of war is off in a skirmish, S-0409 claim 19, so a person sees what stands there), the map's start waypoints (lobby knowledge), and the answers of order-legality checks a person's cursor gives (`placement::place_legal`, `placement::cell_test`). Its placement spacing (`Script::free`) counts only its own objects and those on explored cells; it keeps a gap of two cells between buildings, and `place_cell` narrows the gap to one cell and then none when the base is too cramped for any cell with the wider gap (M6c step 2d). A unit resting in guard that holds a target out of its weapon's range counts as idle (M6c step 2a, `stale_guard_target`). A refinery is a building with the refinery flag or a member of the refinery group (S-0407 Import mapping, `PROC`); a role the house cannot build a type for yet does not hold up the next one, and the army waits for harvesters only while a harvester type is buildable, or a vehicle factory is while the house has none. Refused orders are not an error: the next think tries again. Every number in `brain.rs` and `scripted.rs` is tuning of our own opponent (`nocite`), not game behaviour. Types are found by role from the rules among those the house can build, so every faction plays with its own types. A slave miner (S-0402 claim 14) plays the harvester role and its deployed building counts towards the harvesters kept; it is built without a refinery, and a resting one whose slaves are home is sent to harvest with the nearest explored resource cell as its destination, the order a person gives it (no other order starts a player house's slave miner); it then finds its site and deploys by S-0402 claim 22. A slave with a master is never army, even when its type is armed. Only a vehicle that deploys into a construction yard is deployed as the MCV or kept as the lost yard's replacement.

Tests: `crates/deterministic/ai/tests/ai.rs` on the synthetic skirmish (`fixture`): the build-up, a rebuilt power plant, base defence, and shroud honesty (two worlds that differ only by an enemy unit on a cell the house has not explored give identical orders over a think period) in debug; a broke house selling a building (and a house with income not selling), the rebuilt harvesters, the rebuilt construction yard, a slave-miner side earning income from its slaves (and rebuilding a lost slave miner) and two whole games played to one winner with each house's loop checked in release (`cargo test --release -p glasnost-ai -- --include-ignored`). The determinism gate adds three AI duels (`duel`, `duel-diagonal`, `duel-naval`: the AI against itself on `fixture::map`, `fixture::map_diagonal` and the water map `fixture::map_naval`, hashed with `Game::state_hash`, saved and loaded halfway and at the first tick with a rocket in flight and with an aircraft in the air; the loaded game must hash alike and the run goes on from it, and a full run of the straight duel must have flown both; the diagonal game ends before a launcher is sent, and since S-0507 revision 4 the naval game no longer reaches a launcher shot within its run). The fixture (M6a) adds a pad, an armed plane, a V3-style launcher with its set-A rocket type (not scored, S-0414 claim 4), a naval yard and a gunboat, its own tile catalogue (`fixture::catalogue`, water tile `fixture::WATER_TILE`) and the release tests `flies_aircraft_from_pads` and `builds_a_navy_on_a_water_map` in `tests/skirmish.rs`. M6b adds HOVR, a gun hovercraft (hovering locomotor, amphibious zone, needs NAVY and LAB), as army of the vehicle factory, and the debug test `a_hover_crosses_the_sea_to_a_land_goal`.

Transports and hovers in the local lane (M6c step 2c). The ferry (`Brain::ferry`) is built and works; the AI-lane zero readings of the M6b lane on maps 3 and 5 were short games. At the start of M6c (seed 7, 120 000 ticks) map 2 had 40 loads and 5 launches, map 3 ended at tick 13 129 and map 5 at tick 11 721, both with 0 loads: the first ferry needs the harvesters kept, idle infantry at home, a type the tech tree allows and its build time, which a rush by tick 12 000 does not leave. On map 3 the run never crosses water (`diagnosis` of a 6 000, 12 000 and 18 000 tick run: infantry reaches the enemy base by its zone, no ferry type buildable before tick 18 000), so no crossing was due. With the final code (seed 7, all seven maps end with a winner; counts in `local-lane/m6c/ai-final-maps.txt`) the loads are 69 on map 0 (5 of them onto a hovering transport), 48 on map 2, 1 on map 3 (onto a hovering transport), 32 on map 5, 40 on maps 6 and 7 and 48 on map 8, and hovering or amphibious units were seen on water on maps 0 (4 units), 3 (1) and 5 (1). The lane now counts the loads, the loads onto a hovering transport and the hovering or amphibious units seen on water (`Tally`, the `hover or amphibious on water` feature); tests `counts_the_loads_onto_a_hovering_transport` and `counts_a_launch_by_kind` (`crates/tools/tests/tally.rs`); the ferry itself is tested by `ferries_infantry_across_a_strait`. The brain's diagnosis names the ferry state (idle infantry at home, whether the run crosses water, ferries owned, ferry types buildable). Not done in this step: a transport for vehicles or for a naval landing (the AI carries infantry only).

Maps 6 and 7 of the local lane (M6c step 2d). In the M6b lane both reached no winner in 120 000 ticks (AI against AI, seed 7).
