Diagnosis (`GLASNOST_AI_QUICK=1 glasnost-headless ai 24000 7 6 7`, the lane prints a counts-only `diagnosis` for a game that does not end): the maps are not islands and no house was out of resources. On map 6 both houses held a yard, a power plant, a refinery, a barracks and one harvester, 29 000 credits and 389 resource cells worth 105 225 on the map; on map 7 one house stood so and the other held only its yard with 10 000 credits and no power plant. Neither had a war factory: of the 624 cells of the placement spiral, 156 to 164 were legal for it, 10 to 28 passed the cell test (their start sits in a small clearing), and none kept the two-cell gap to the buildings already standing (`Script::free`). So the second harvester, which needs the war factory, never came, and the army waits for harvesters, so no unit was built and the houses did not meet. Class: a defect in our AI (a fixed gap in a cramped base), not a stalemate. Fix: `Script::place_cell` narrows the gap to one cell, then to none, when no cell keeps it (the diagnosis names the cell counts per stage). Result with seed 7: map 6 over at tick 23 804 (house 1 won), map 7 over at tick 31 784 (house 1 won), 24 and 16 transport loads, 3 superweapon launches each. Test `builds_every_building_in_a_cramped_base` (`ai.rs`, the clearing of `fixture::map_cramped`). Open: a house whose factory has no cell even without a gap still waits for its harvester before any army (the army rule `economy_first`); not reached on these maps. Cost per tick (release, final code, no replay): map 7 6.0 ms, maps 0, 3 and 5 2.8 to 3.0 ms, map 2 2.1 ms, maps 6 and 8 0.4 to 0.6 ms; the M6b lane read 1.0 ms on map 0 and 1.3 ms on map 7 with one house idle there, but the games differ in length and army, and the share of the three placement passes in `place_cell` (called for each giving building type every think and in `water_goal`) was not measured.

Whole-game AI tests (M6c step 2a). The release games `builds_a_navy_on_a_water_map` (`crates/deterministic/ai/tests/skirmish.rs`, seed 6) and `builds_and_fires_a_superweapon` (`ai.rs`, seed 5) run one fixed seed again; `naval_seed_sweep` and `superweapon_seed_sweep` (`GLASNOST_SWEEP=<from>-<to>`, `--ignored --nocapture`) print the pass count of a seed range and the claims that fail. Cause, found by running the same seeds at 1783c23^, 1783c23 and the tip: the S-0207 claim 26 goal cleanup (`drive::goal_cleanup`) follows its spec and is not a defect; it only shifted which seed wins the first clash. The chain, from the first diverging tick of the scripted naval game (seed 5, the replay's orders equal up to tick 8145): claim 26 keeps the order-level destination of a driving vehicle for one locomotion update after its last track ends, S-0402 claim 3 makes a seeking harvester with a destination wait for its next processing, so a harvester's decisions come later (about ten ticks in the traced case), harvest and income timing move, the Build orders move by tens of ticks, and with them the winner of the first clash. Whether the two specs compose that way was asked in Q-0900, which S-0402 revision 25 claim 3b (with S-0207 revision 30 claim 26 and S-0215 revision 49 claim 2) answers as coded: the seeking test reads the order-level destination, a due processing in the tick after the last track still sees it and stays seeking with the standard delay, and loading starts at the first processing from the tick after that (test `claim_3b_loading_starts_after_the_destination_is_removed`). In 20 of 32 seeds one house loses its construction yard to the first wave (near tick 10 000, the yard at a few health points) both before and after that commit (20 of 32 at 1783c23^, 22 at 1783c23), and the loser never reaches its naval yard or silo; the claim that both houses build a navy held in 12 of 32 seeds before and 9 after, by luck of the seed. Two AI gaps found on the way. (1) An armed unit resting in guard keeps an acquired target that is out of range and does not pursue it (S-0215 claim 24), so `scripted::idle` never saw it as free: a home guard stood still while the wave destroyed the base (the firing check answered out of range for most of the guard-with-target unit-ticks). `idle` now treats a resting unit whose target it cannot reach as idle (`stale_guard_target`), as a person would give it an order. (2) The army round-robin shared one counter over three kinds of army, which skipped some types every round (the hovercraft was rarely built); it is one counter per kind now (`next_army`, game save format 2). Pass counts over seeds 1 to 32. The naval game under the original claim set (a navy and a ship, a hovercraft, a ship crossed, fired and one sank, yards on water): 7 before, 8 after. Under the new claim set (the hovercraft claim moved out): 29 before and 29 after (the three that fail, 5, 7 and 17, send no ship out before the game ends); so the 29 comes from the moved claim, not from a fix. One silo built and one superweapon fired (the old test wanted a silo of each house too; its count was not taken): 21 before, 26 after the idle-guard fix, 29 with the later superweapon changes (step 2b). The hovercraft claim moved out of the whole naval game (its lab comes after the naval yard and most games end first) into `builds_a_hovercraft_once_a_naval_yard_and_a_lab_stand`; the claims that both houses build a navy or a silo are dropped as seed luck. The gate's straight duel no longer flies a rocket and the diagonal duel no longer launches (`crates/sim-gate/src/lib.rs`). A stock-map AI duel still plays to a winner in every case run (local lane).

Lane findings of M6c step 5 (AI, fixed). (1) **Infantry left at the shore.** After step 1f's stronger economy, map 5 ran out of ore and reached no winner: army infantry that could not walk to the attack goal stood at the nearest shore, out of the ferry's reach (`Brain::ferry` takes idle infantry within twice `FERRY_HOME` of home). Now an idle infantry unit that does not fly and whose reach region does not hold the goal (`Script::needs_ferry`) walks back to within `FERRY_GATHER` cells of home and waits there for a ferry (test `infantry_that_cannot_walk_to_the_goal_waits_at_home`; `a_wave_sends_its_jumpjets_across` checks that the wave gives such a walker no destination). (2) **A barracks without a rally point.** The lane's unit-class count (`glasnost-headless ai`, line *unit types by class*, and for each never-built type its kind, class, weapon, AI role and first offered tick) showed one early infantry type of the Allied side never built in 9 of 14 games, although the army builder ordered it. Its release was refused: with no rally point a released infantry unit gets no destination (S-0417 claim 10, S-0404 claim 13) and rests at the barracks while still in contact with it, so when the next item was done no contact place was free (S-0417 claim 14), the release was busy and the item was cancelled with its refund and not retried (S-0404 claim 7). The cancelled item was whatever the round robin had next; this type came after a unit that stayed every time. A person sets a rally point, and now the AI does too: `Script::rally_points` clicks (`targeting::click`, S-0213 claim 20 (a), the click's own nearby-cell search of S-0212 table T5) on a cell `RALLY_AHEAD` cells beyond each barracks without one, toward the attack goal (tests `sets_a_rally_point_on_its_barracks`, `a_released_infantry_unit_frees_its_barracks`; both fail without it). With the rally point the infantry fights from the start, so the games are shorter and some house-local tests now remove the armies while they wait (`builds_a_hovercraft_once_a_naval_yard_and_a_lab_stand`, `builds_one_building_of_every_kind`). (3) **A ferry-wait goal that flew away.** The ferry-wait test of (1) and `Brain::ferry` aimed at `attack_goal`, the nearest seen enemy of any kind; on map 8 that was an aircraft or a unit across the water, so the walkers stayed home for good and the game reached no winner. Both now aim at `Script::base_goal`: the seen enemy building nearest home, else the next enemy start position. (4) **Army types never reached.** The army builder took one type per kind in turn; on the Soviet side the vehicle list grows with the tech tree and one line builds them all, so in a short game the later types were never reached, and on a water map a naval yard's pick moved the same counter as the war factory's, so the war factory skipped the hovercraft every round (the AI test above failed once the games were shorter). Now, of the types whose line is free, the builder takes the one the house has fewest of on the map, then the one it has built fewest of (S-0407 claim 10's ever-built count), then the next in turn. The lane's same-side pairing (`GLASNOST_AI_SIDES=1,1`: the first two open countries of side 1) showed that the winning Soviet house builds every class, jumpjet units included; the Soviet house that loses to an Allied house falls before its last vehicle types come up (Allied against Soviet the Allied house wins every game run; the AI plays both the same way, so this reads as balance of the data under our AI, not as an exclusion). (5) **A loaded ferry that never left.** On map 5 a loaded hover transport stood 5 to 11 cells from home, just outside `FERRY_HOME`, so `Brain::ferry` took it for a ferry that had arrived and clicked it to unload; the infantry were then within the gathering radius and were boarded again, and the game reached no winner in 120 000 ticks. A loaded ferry within the gathering radius (twice `FERRY_HOME`) that is not at home now leaves for the drop ring near the goal (test `a_loaded_ferry_near_home_leaves_for_the_goal`, which fails without it), and a ferry that has stood in its unload for more than `UNLOAD_STUCK` ticks with passengers still aboard (a passenger with no exit blocks the rest, S-0507 claim 10) is moved to land nearby, where the unload click is made again (test `a_ferry_stuck_unloading_is_moved_on`). Map 5 then ended with a winner at tick 95 700. (A loaded ferry near home with no drop cell to go to falls back to the earlier rule and lets its passengers out.) (6) **A wave sent to the shore by a ship.** On map 3 the wave goal was the seen enemy nearest home, of any kind; for most of the game that was an object off the house's own shore. About a hundred infantry stood at the shore by it, arrived and so idle, and every wave sent them there again: the game reached no winner in 120 000 ticks although one house was down to four buildings. Now each unit of a wave takes the seen enemy nearest home that lies in its own reach region (S-0212 claim 4; any for a unit that flies, and a ship keeps the old rule, as it fires from the water off an enemy base) and that it can attack (`can_attack`), else the next enemy start cell as before (test `a_wave_goes_for_an_enemy_it_can_reach`, which fails without it). Map 3 then ended at tick 33 915 and map 5 at 48 382 (quick runs).

Lane findings of M7a step 2 (seed 7, quick runs: `GLASNOST_AI_QUICK=1`, no replay or save check; outputs under the git-ignored `local-lane/m7a/`). (a) **The ferry fallback** of M6c step 5 (5) now has a test: `a_loaded_ferry_with_no_drop_cell_lets_its_passengers_out` (ai.rs) fills every land cell across the strait, and the loaded ferry near home is told to unload; without the fallback the brain gives it no order. (b) **The M6c duel's margins** come from its setup alone (start units, a second tech and spy building per side, the HULKs, a second jumpjet type); see *Margins* under *Determinism gate*. (c) **Charges and disarms at 0.** The runner's new line *occasions (S-0518, S-0519)* counts per house the buildable charger, overpowerable (and of them the prism type), disarmer and bomb-detector types, and the house-ticks with an occasion. Over the 16 games (Allied against Allied on maps 0, 2, 3, 5, 6, 7 and 8, Allied against Soviet on the same maps, Soviet against Soviet on maps 2 and 8): the 21 Allied houses could build 0 charger types and 0 overpowerable types; each of the 11 Soviet houses could build one charger type and one overpowerable type that is not the prism type, had chargers on the map for 3 601 to 17 924 house-ticks and an overpowerable building for 0, because the linked module ordered only the prism type. A person who builds chargers builds the building they charge, so the module now orders it (see *Linked weapons*). In the re-run with the fix 2 of the 11 Soviet houses built one (12 charge orders); the others never held twice its price with the building line free (one game sampled every 300 ticks: 75 of 78 samples short of the credits, the line busy in 52), and chargers stood idle within 12 cells of the building in 3 and 87 house-ticks, the rest of the time they were with the army. Disarms: every house could build one disarmer type and one detector type; 5 own objects carried a bomb over the 16 games (9 in the re-run), and in no house-tick did the carrier's house see one of them (`bomb::visible`), so no person in the seat had an occasion to disarm; no change. (d) **Map 8, Allied against Soviet: the hover class never built.** Nothing excludes it: in the 5 668 ticks from its first offer to the end the Allied war factory's line was free for a new pick only twice, because each item it built waited for money (8 army vehicles in the whole game, 1 200 to 4 000 ticks each; three times an item's progress stood still for 300 ticks or more; credits 92 at the end). At the first pick the hover type and another vehicle type were tied at 0 on the map and 0 built and the round robin took the other; at the second (tick 15 240) it took the hover type, 29 ticks before the game ended. No change. (e) **An order flood in the Soviet against Yuri game** (map 0): 1253 bomb, 340 suicide and 263 air-strike orders from 6 units, fixed in the memory (see *Orders a unit did not take*); 119, 1 and 10 after. Re-run of the 16 pairings with (c) and (e), every game over with a winner: Allied against Allied map 0 house 1 at 10 978, 2 house 0 at 63 719, 3 house 0 at 41 804, 5 house 1 at 32 646, 6 house 0 at 50 604, 7 house 0 at 28 646, 8 house 0 at 44 242; Allied against Soviet house 0 on every map, at 23 630 (0), 20 168 (2), 24 607 (3), 15 583 (5), 16 217 (6), 17 272 (7), 15 269 (8); Soviet against Soviet map 2 house 0 at 86 860, map 8 house 0 at 29 107; Soviet against Yuri map 0 house 1 (Yuri) at 83 108. Unit classes (line *unit types by class*): the winning house built at least one type of every class it could build in 12 of the 16 games (both Soviet against Soviet winners built 2 of 2 jumpjet types). The four others: Allied against Allied map 0 (the shortest game: hover 0 of 1, teleporter 0 of 1) and Allied against Soviet maps 0 (hover 0/1, ship 0/4, teleporter 0/1), 5 (hover 0/1) and 8 (hover 0/1, as in (d)). The losing Soviet houses of the Allied against Soviet games built no jumpjet type (0 of 2), as at M6c step 5.

Yuri side (M7a step 4; seed 7; outputs under the git-ignored `local-lane/m7a/yuri4/`). (a) **Audit.** `glasnost-headless side-audit [side] [map index] [seed]` (`glasnost_tools::side_audit`) lists every type whose owner list names the side's first open country with its kind, plan role, flags and weapon and warhead flags, and plays a sandbox game (1 000 000 credits, an idle opponent, each factory given a rally point) that orders each offered type once, places finished buildings on the plan's spiral and releases finished units; for a type never offered it names the unmet prerequisites and the country and stolen-tech conditions. Map 0, side 2: 79 types (53 buildings, 16 infantry, 10 vehicles, no aircraft), 78 within the house tech level. 31 are offered, and all 31 are built and placed or released (0 without a cell, 0 left on the line). Not offered: 1 above the tech level, 3 that need stolen technology (S-0407 claim 6; infiltration is S-0510), 4 that the country's forbidden list refuses, 2 that only other countries may build, and the rest need buildings of another side (a captured yard). Every offered type is covered by an approved spec: the base, power, factories, naval yard, wall and radar by S-0404/S-0407 (the radar's psychic reveal and sensor by S-0610), the slave miner and its building by S-0402, the cloning building by S-0404 claim 14, the force shield by S-0512, the hover transport by S-0507, the ship by the naval and spawn specs (S-0503, S-0506), the deployers by S-0517, the jumpjet vehicle by S-0502, the berserk vehicle by S-0610, and the rest by the batch-6 specs that are approved but not built yet: mind control (S-0601, being built in parallel), grinder (S-0603), infantry absorber (S-0605, the power plant), vehicle bunker (S-0606), magnetic lift (S-0607), drain (S-0608), gattling (S-0609), mutation (S-0611), the dominator (S-0612), gas clouds (S-0613) and special hits (S-0615). Those types import, are offered and are built today; what is missing is their ability, a seam of the step that builds each spec. No type lacks a spec, so no request. (b) **The jumpjet vehicle.** No rule excludes it: map 2 against the Allied side (a quick run before (c)), it was picked at the first free pick of the war factory after its offer (tick 11 221; offered at 6 601) and cancelled with a refund at 45 of 54 (no AI code cancels; most likely a prerequisite was lost); on map 8 against the Soviet side it was picked at 13 741 and was still waiting for money when the game ended. The house's credits sat near 0 from about tick 6 600 and each vehicle of its kind took about 4 000 ticks while it waited for money. (`GLASNOST_AI_LINES=h` prints house h's production line changes: started, ended, and every 300 ticks progress, waiting and credits.) In the full re-run below the Yuri house built it in each of the four games in which it was offered (against the Allied side on maps 0 and 8 the game ended before it was). (c) **Fixed: a refinery ordered while every slave miner was moving.** When all of a house's slave miners had packed up to move on (S-0402 claims 22 and 24), the plan saw no refinery and ordered the side's refinery building, which is the slave miner's costly deployed form; on map 2 such an order (tick 12 721) sat at progress 2 with no money and held the building line until 13 261. A slave miner acts as its slaves' refinery whether it is a vehicle or deployed (S-0402 claim 14), and a person does not buy a refinery while theirs drives to the next field, so `Script::has_refinery` now counts a slave miner of the house (test `a_moving_slave_miner_stands_for_the_refinery`, ai.rs: with a driving slave miner the plan orders no refinery; with none it does). Looked at, no change: the slaves are not counted as harvesters (only the miner, and its deployed building); the plan's refinery-less harvester rule already lets a slave-miner side build its miner; the absorbing power plant is S-0605's; no side's AI builds defences (a general gap, not the Yuri side's). (d) **Lane, full mode** (`ai-sides 120000 7 0 2 8`, replay and mid-game save checks on; both true in all six games). Allied (house 0) against Yuri (house 1): house 0 won on map 0 at 13 998, on map 2 at 21 228 and on map 8 at 18 333. Soviet (house 0) against Yuri (house 1): house 1 won on map 0 at 41 519 and on map 2 at 51 707; house 0 won on map 8 at 16 915. The runner's per-role counts are distinct objects a house ever held, so every deploy and undeploy of a slave miner adds a refinery and a harvester (the Yuri house on map 0 against the Soviet side: 49 and 48 over 41 519 ticks); the counts do not tell relocations from purchases. Unit classes (line *unit types by class*), the Yuri house: every class it could build had a built type in five of the six games (jumpjet 1/1 in the four where it was offered; ship 1/1 and transport 1/1 on map 0 against the Soviet side); against the Soviet side on map 8 (the shortest of its games) one armed and one deployer type were not built. The Allied and Soviet houses: against Yuri the Allied side left 5 types unbuilt on map 0 (a 14 000-tick game) and the hover type on map 8; the Soviet side left an armed, a jumpjet, three ship and a transport type unbuilt on map 0, both jumpjet types on map 2 and one jumpjet type on map 8 (it lost two of three games). Psychic weapons in these games: 0 to 6 units gone berserk, 4 553 to 32 843 house-ticks with a hostile-intent line, 0 to 13 psychic reveals per game. Not traced: on map 2 against the Soviet side the Yuri slaves spent 293 517 slave-ticks in *guard* while returning (about six slaves for the whole game), against 3 592 on the same map against the Allied side; S-0402's relocation is the simulation's and was left alone.

## Own AI (M6c: special abilities)

`glasnost_ai::special` (one module of its own, hooked into `Brain::think` once per think period) uses the special abilities the way a person in the house's seat could: it clicks (`targeting::cursor` and `targeting::click`, so the cursor rules of S-0214 decide what a click means) on objects of cells its house has explored (`econ::shroud::explored`) and issues the resulting orders through `orders::issue`. It reads no hidden state and draws no random value. Its counters (`Uses`: garrison, capture and infiltration orders) are kept with the brain, saved and hashed with the game (`ai::game::GAME_FORMAT_VERSION` 2), and the headless runner's AI games print them (`special abilities (orders): …; garrison occupants inside at the end: …` in `local-lane/ai-duel.txt`). Tests: `crates/deterministic/ai/tests/special.rs`.

- **Garrisons (S-0508).** Every idle infantry unit of an occupier type that the house can select is sent to the nearest building it may garrison (S-0508 claim 1, the cursor of row I8) on an explored cell within 24 cells, civilian garrison buildings before the house's own, with a free place (occupants plus units already on their way, the orders of the same think included). Numbers are tuning of our own opponent (`nocite`). It keeps at most six occupants in garrisons, and looks for buildings near its home.
- **Engineers (S-0509).** Every idle engineer is sent to the nearest seen enemy building whose cursor shows capture or damaging entry (S-0214 rows I6 and I7: the enemy's capturable buildings), else to the nearest own building whose cursor shows repair, never to a building another engineer of the house is already bound for. A house with no engineer (on the map, off it or in its line) and no engineer queued that sees a capturable enemy building orders the cheapest engineer type its sidebar offers, once, when it holds at least twice its price in credits and is not selling out.
- **Tech buildings (S-0515).** `glasnost_ai::tech_buildings` ranks the engineer's targets: a tech building held by a passive house that pays (an acquisition bonus or an income delay) comes before one that only serves (among captures; repair stays last), then the nearest; a house with no engineer orders one also when it sees such a building (the earlier rule looked only at buildings of player houses). `Uses::tech` counts the orders to tech buildings (a subset of `engineers`; AI game format 3) and the runner prints it as "tech buildings". Tests: `sends_an_engineer_to_a_paying_tech_building_first`, `takes_a_serving_tech_building_and_only_an_explored_one`, `orders_an_engineer_for_a_tech_building`.
- **Jumpjets (S-0502).** `glasnost_ai::jumpjets` (a new file; `Special::jumpjets` is called from `Special::think`): every idle armed jumpjet unit is sent against the nearest seen enemy that a walking unit of the house's cannot reach (another reach region of the ground, `across_obstacle`) and that it may attack (`scripted::can_attack`), with the order a person gives; a wave's jumpjets go to the goal itself (`Script::reachable` returns the goal for a unit that flies); a house with no jumpjet unit (on the map or in its line) that sees such an enemy would order the cheapest armed jumpjet type its sidebar offers, once, under the credit reserve (held back at M6c step 2e under the interim rule of Q-1070, orders again since step 1f: see *A jumpjet unit and its factory*). `Uses::jumps` counts the orders (AI game format 4) and the runner prints it as "jumpjets". Tests: `crates/deterministic/ai/tests/jumpjets.rs` on the strait map (`sends_a_jumpjet_over_the_strait`, `flies_only_at_what_it_has_explored`, `a_wave_sends_its_jumpjets_across`, `builds_a_jumpjet_unit_and_its_factory_stays_free`).
- **Deployable units (S-0517).** `glasnost_ai::deployers` (a new file; `Special::deployers` is called from `Special::think`): a standing in-place vehicle, deploy-fire vehicle or firing infantry unit (no destination, resting or attacking, with or without a target) that has a seen enemy it can attack within 9 cells is sent the deploy order a person gives (`Order::Deploy`); a deployed in-place vehicle with no such enemy within 13 cells is sent the same order again to pack up, so that it joins the next wave (`scripted::idle` leaves a deployed or changing vehicle out of the waves). Every order is counted by `Uses::deploys`; the runner prints it as "deploys". It reads only what the house sees and draws no random value.
- **Radiation (S-0525).** `glasnost_ai::radiation` (a new file; `Special::radiation` is called from `Special::think`): every idle infantry unit or vehicle that radiation hurts (not immune, on the map) and that stands on a cell its house has explored where the cell radiation is at least 1 is sent to the nearest cell within 8 cells that is usable, explored, clean and in its own reach region, with a *move* order. A radiation-immune deployer is deployed by the deploy rules near a seen enemy and then fires its radiation weapon at its own cell (S-0215 claim 31). `Uses::radiation` counts the orders (AI game format 7); the runner prints it as "radiation exits". Tests: `crates/deterministic/ai/tests/radiation.rs` (`leaves_a_field`, `stays_when_it_is_safe`, `reads_only_explored_cells`).
- **Chrono weapons (S-0514).** `glasnost_ai::chrono` (a new file; `Special::chrono` is called from `Special::think`): every idle unit with a temporal beam that holds none and is selectable is sent the attack order against the costliest enemy its house sees (explored cells) that it may attack and that can be erased (an erasable type, not protected, in the ground layer), the nearest on equal cost. `Uses::erasures` counts the orders (AI game format 8); the runner prints it as "erasures". Tests: `crates/deterministic/ai/tests/chrono.rs`.
- **Linked weapons (S-0518).** `glasnost_ai::linked` (a new file; `Special::linked` is called from `Special::think`): a house that can build the prism type and holds at least twice its price orders one while it has fewer than three (on the map or queued), the finished ones being placed by the ordinary place reaction near the base (so within the support reach of each other, which the spiral keeps small); every idle charger (infantry or vehicle with an electric-charge secondary) is sent the attack order at the nearest own overpowerable building within 12 cells that has fewer than three chargers. `Uses::prisms` and `Uses::charges` count the orders (AI game format 9); the runner prints them as "prisms" and "charges". Since M7a step 2c a house that has a charger and no overpowerable building (on the map or queued) orders the cheapest overpowerable building type other than the prism type that its sidebar offers, under the same rules as the prism building (a free building line, not selling out, twice its price in hand; one such building wanted); `Uses::overpowerables` counts these orders (AI game format 15) and the runner prints "overpowerable buildings". Tests: `crates/deterministic/ai/tests/linked.rs` (`a_house_with_a_charger_orders_an_overpowerable_building` fails without the step 2c rule).
- **Terror and strikes (S-0519).** `glasnost_ai::terror` (a new file; `Special::terror` is called from `Special::think`): every idle selectable unit is given the order a person would give by its role: a designator (strike size above 0) attacks the costliest seen enemy building it may attack; a bomb planter (primary weapon with a bomb-planting warhead) the costliest seen enemy it may attack (a bombed carrier is illegal by the firing check, so it is passed over); a disarmer an own object that carries a bomb the house sees (`bomb::visible`); a suicide unit the nearest seen enemy it may attack; infantry with explosive charges the nearest seen enemy building the cursor offers sabotage on. `Uses::bombs`, `disarms`, `suicides`, `sabotages` and `strikes` count the orders (AI game format 10); the runner prints them. Tests: `crates/deterministic/ai/tests/terror.rs`.
- **Crates (S-0520).** `glasnost_ai::crates` (a new file; `Special::crates` is called from `Special::think`): every crate on a cell the house has explored (crate places whose cell holds a crate overlay) that no own unit already runs to is given to the fastest idle selectable ground unit within 30 cells (infantry or vehicle, not a harvester or an engineer; the nearest on equal speed), with a move order to the crate's cell; one unit per crate per think. `Uses::crates` counts the orders (AI game format 11); the runner prints "crates". Tests: `crates/deterministic/ai/tests/crates.rs`.
- **Orders a unit did not take (PLAN §4.7; AI game format 13, 14 since M7a step 2e).** `glasnost_ai::memory::Memory` (kept in `Special`, saved and hashed with the brain): every behaviour order a special-ability module gives is noted with the cell the unit stood on; at the next think a unit that is idle again on that very cell did not take the order (no path out of a pocket of buildings, a target it cannot attack), and the pair (unit, goal, cell) is blocked for 4500 ticks. Every module (garrison, engineers, spies, jumpjets, chrono, linked, terror, radiation, crates) asks `Memory::ok` when it picks a goal, so the unit is sent at the next best goal, or none, instead of the same click every think. A unit that moved, entered something, died or fired (its rearm tick changed) leaves no memory. A unit that is still busy on the same cell without having fired is judged again at the next think, for at most 4500 ticks, and a new order for the unit replaces its note (M7a step 2e; until then a busy unit's note was dropped at once). Found by the lane at step 2e: in the Soviet against Yuri game on map 0 (seed 7) the Soviet house gave 1253 bomb, 340 suicide and 263 air-strike orders; with a print of every such order, 2 planters took all 1253 bomb orders (one unit at one goal 499 times), 1 suicide unit all 340 (111 at one goal) and 3 designators all 263 (132 at one goal). Each held its attack order through the next think on the cell it stood on (busy, so the note was dropped) and then fell back to guarding there, idle, and was ordered again. With the change the same game gives 119 bomb (at most 4 orders for one unit and goal), 1 suicide and 10 air-strike orders; it now ends at tick 83 108 with the Yuri house the winner (before: 56 956, the Soviet house). Crates also skip a unit whose reach region does not hold the crate. Found by the lane: a starting engineer boxed in by buildings (the path search returned no steps, `Capture` lasted one tick) was ordered at the same tech building every think, 1250 times in map 2's 21 000 ticks, 5540 times in map 5. Tests: `does_not_reorder_an_engineer_that_did_not_take_the_order`, `an_engineer_that_gave_up_a_building_goes_for_another`, `an_engineer_on_its_way_is_not_given_up` (special.rs), `does_not_resend_a_unit_that_did_not_take_the_order`, `does_not_resend_a_unit_that_dropped_the_order_where_it_stood` (fails without the step 2e change), `a_unit_on_its_way_to_a_crate_is_not_given_up` (crates.rs).
- **A jumpjet unit and its factory (Q-1070 answered, S-0417 revision 19).** The interim hold of M6c step 2e is gone. S-0417 claim 15 gives a released jumpjet infantry unit or vehicle (type marked `JumpJet` or `BalloonHover`, in contact with a vehicle factory or a building with a barracks door kind) a clear report in its first ascending update whose adjusted height lies in trunc(*h*t / *q*) < *h* < *h*t (*q* = 2 for a balloon-hover vehicle, 4 otherwise); contact and tether end together (`econ::release::ascent_report`, called from `jump::fly::ascending_outcome`, so the factory closes by table S1 or the infantry place is free, whatever the unit does next). The design fallback (the update in which *h* reaches or passes *h*t reports too) has no quirk switch. The saved guard spot becomes the destination, otherwise the order-level destination is discarded (`jump::discard_order_destination`: the movement point and commitment stay) and a forced scatter request follows. The own AI therefore builds and uses jumpjet units like a person: `scripted::jams_factory` is removed, the army builder and the jumpjet module's order take the type again. Test: `builds_a_jumpjet_unit_and_its_factory_stays_free` (the barracks of that test has a door kind; the link must end while the unit is ascending); sim tests in `claims_s0417_r19.rs`. The lane counts of jumpjet units built and used are in step 5 of the M6c step ledger.
- **Psychic weapons (S-0610, M7a; AI game format 16 at merge).** `glasnost_ai::psychic` (a new file; `Special::berserk` is called from `Special::think`): every idle unit with a berserk weapon (a weapon slot with a berserk-causing warhead), or one resting in guard on a target it took by itself, is sent the attack order against the seen enemy unit within 12 cells that has the most seen enemy units within 3 cells of it (then the nearest), that it may attack (`scripted::can_attack`, so psionics-immune targets are passed over) and that is not berserk already. Once the harvesters are kept, `Brain::build_sensor` orders the cheapest psychic sensor type the house can build and place when it has none (replacing a tech-climb building of that think, like a superweapon building). `fire_superweapons` fires a ready psychic reveal at the enemy start cell nearest home that the house does not see, then at the unexplored cell (on a 4-cell lattice) nearest an enemy start, and keeps it when nothing is unexplored. `Brain::think` drops every order to a berserk unit (S-0214 claim 15). The deployed psychic pulse is the deployers' (firing infantry, S-0517 claim 16). `Uses::berserks`, `sensors` and `reveals` count the orders; the lane prints them and, per game, the units gone berserk and the house-ticks with an intent line (`skirmish_lane::Tally`). Numbers are tuning of our own.
- **Mind control (S-0601, M7a; AI game format 17).** `glasnost_ai::mind` (a new file; `Special::mind_control` is called from `Special::think` after the berserk weapons): every idle or guarding controller of the house that is selectable, not berserk, not at capacity (claim 2) and, if unlimited, safe to take one more (`mind::safe_more`: the overload level for N + 1 victims does no damage, claim 16) is sent with the attack order at the costliest seen enemy within 10 cells that the takeover test accepts (claim 3) and its firing check allows, then the nearest; no two controllers at the same enemy in one think. The brain drops any other attack order at an object to an unlimited controller that may not safely take more. Controlled objects belong to the house and join the waves like its own; controller types are built as army types. `Uses::takeovers` counts the orders; the skirmish lane prints it and a `mind control:` line (takeovers, most objects held at once and controllers per house). Tests `crates/deterministic/ai/tests/mind.rs` and the tally test `counts_takeovers_and_controllers`.
- **Gattling weapons (S-0609, M7a; AI game format 18).** `glasnost_ai::gattling` (a new file). (1) **Kept spin:** a gattling unit firing at an enemy object (`gattling::engaged`: its firing check gives can fire, rearming, not facing or still turning, the spin-up answers of claim 5) is given no attack order at another object by any part of the brain (the base defence used to re-target such units at an intruder, which would make them drive and spin down); `Uses::spins_kept` counts the dropped orders. (2) **Defences:** the brain orders the cheapest buildable armed gattling building type, up to two (`DEFENCES_WANTED`), once the house keeps its harvesters, holds twice the price, can place it and gave no other building or placement order in that think (a tech-climb building aside, which it replaces; never instead of a superweapon building or a psychic sensor); `Uses::gattling_defences` counts the orders. (3) **Armed by slots:** a gattling type whose weapons are only its stage slots (no primary weapon) is now armed for the army role, the jumpjet search and the ferry test (`scripted::armed`), so the production round-robin builds gattling units and the waves use them. The skirmish lane prints `gattling defences` and `spins kept` per house and a `gattling:` line (house-ticks with a gattling at its top stage and the gattling types each house had). Tests `crates/deterministic/ai/tests/gattling.rs` and the tally test `counts_gattling_types_and_top_stage_ticks`.
- **Gas clouds (S-0613, M7a; AI game format 18, unchanged).** No new decision: a gas weapon is an ordinary weapon, and a virus infantry type is an armed army type the brain builds and sends like any other. The AI lane counts per house its virus units, and in all gas hits, clouds and the most particles at once (`skirmish_lane::Tally`).
- **Absorbing buildings (S-0605, M7a; AI game format 20 since step 1g).** `glasnost_ai::absorber` (a new file; `Special::absorbers` is called from `Special::think` after mind control). The absorber is built by the plan's power role (a power plant in the stock data). Every idle, selectable, controlled enemy infantry unit is sent in (its controller is then free for another victim; its power goes to the house); own idle infantry at home (within 16 cells) are sent while the house is short of power, or beyond the 4 it keeps, cheapest first; engineers, spies, mind controllers and units already waiting in line or in contact are kept out. At most 3 per think, each to the nearest own infantry absorber with room (capacity less passengers and entrants on their way), by the *enter* click. Since step 1g (S-0605 revision 2, claim 17: a contact outlives *enter*) a unit of the house's own that holds an absorber's contact with neither *enter* current nor queued gets the *enter* click on that building again before the others, once per cell it stands on (`absorber::stalled`, the order memory's `Memory::pending`); the new order ends the stale contact (S-0213 table T3 (b)). `Uses::absorbs` counts the orders; the AI lane prints them, the infantry that went in, releases beside a standing building, the most passengers at once and the absorbing buildings per house (`skirmish_lane::Tally`, test `counts_absorber_entries_and_releases`). Tests: `crates/deterministic/ai/tests/absorber.rs`.
- **Tank bunkers (S-0606, M7a; AI game format 22).** `glasnost_ai::bunker` (a new file; `Special::bunkers` is called from `Special::think` after the grinders). `Brain::build_bunker` orders the cheapest tank bunker the house can place, up to 2 (on the map or queued), with twice its price in hand, under the gattling defence's conditions and not in a think that ordered one, but without waiting for the harvesters (the price reserve keeps the economy fed); the ordinary placement puts it in the base. An empty bunker with no vehicle on its way gets the nearest idle, selectable vehicle of the house within 14 cells whose *enter* cursor it shows (so the bunker's permission rows decide; harvesters, slave miners, construction vehicles, mind controllers, loaded transports and vehicles at or below 40 % are never sent). A bunkered vehicle is let out by the deploy click (S-0214 row VD5, *unload*) to be repaired (at or below 40 %), to retreat (its bunker at or below 25 %), to attack (a seen enemy within 10 cells of the bunker or another building of the house and no target of its own), or to join the army after 1 200 ticks inside with nothing to shoot; a vehicle let out is not sent back in for 1 500 ticks. A bunkered vehicle is not idle (`scripted::idle`), so waves, repair and the grinder leave it; the base defence leaves vehicles entering or inside a bunker alone. All numbers are `nocite` tuning. `Uses::bunker_builds`, `bunker_entries` and `bunker_exits` count the orders; the AI lane prints them, the vehicles bunkered, the releases with both standing and the bunkers per house (`skirmish_lane::Tally`, test `counts_bunkered_vehicles_and_releases`). Tests: `crates/deterministic/ai/tests/bunker.rs` (8).
- **Magnetic lifters (S-0607, M7a; AI game format 23).** `glasnost_ai::lifter` (a new file; `Special::lifters` is called from `Special::think`, `Brain::build_lifter` from the build step). A lifter is any vehicle type whose primary weapon has a locomotor warhead (`lifter::lifter_type`). The house keeps up to 2 (`WANTED`, on the map or queued) of the cheapest it can build, not while selling out and only once the plan keeps its harvesters: the first is queued behind whatever the war factory builds (paid as the money comes), a further one waits for a free line and twice its price in hand (`RESERVE`). With the reserve for the first one too, the install's Yuri house never built a lifter in the lane: the army round keeps its war factory busy and its credits near 0. Lifters are built by this rule only: the army round of `scripted` leaves them out, and the tank bunker does not take them. An idle lifter, or one aiming at something it cannot lift, is sent by the attack click at a seen enemy vehicle it can seize (`lift::eligible` with the beam's damage, on the ground, not already held, and not refused by the firing check) within 22 cells of home (`HUNT_REACH`) or 8 cells of itself (`LIFT_REACH`): the dearest type first, then the nearest; infantry never. An idle lifter more than 8 cells from home (`HOME_REACH`) goes back. A holding lifter is given a move order to its own cell, which ends the grip (claim 12), when the held vehicle is at least 200 leptons up (`DROP_HEIGHT`) over water or over a cell with an enemy vehicle or building, or within 512 leptons of the lifter (`LET_GO`): pulled all the way it would be dropped onto the lifter itself (claim 8). No other part of the brain orders a holding lifter (`lifter::keeps_order`, a filter on the brain's output). All numbers are `nocite` tuning. `Uses::lifter_builds`, `lift_orders` and `lift_drops` count the orders; the AI lane prints them and the vehicles seized and dropped (`skirmish_lane::Tally`, test `counts_seized_and_dropped_units`). Tests: `crates/deterministic/ai/tests/lifter.rs` (3).
- **Lane diagnostics.** `GLASNOST_AI_DUMP=t1,t2,...` prints every object of the player houses (type, cell, behaviour, destination, target, power state and production items) at those ticks, and `GLASNOST_AI_WATCH=id,from,to` prints one object every tick in a range (with `GLASNOST_AI_QUICK=1` to skip the replay and save checks).

- **Spies (S-0510).** Every idle spy is sent to the nearest seen enemy building with an infiltration effect, ranked by what a person wants (stolen technology, a factory flag it lacks, money, power, a superweapon, radar), never one another spy of the house is bound for. A house with no spy and none in its line that sees such a building orders the cheapest spy type its sidebar offers, once, under the same credit reserve as engineers.

## Own AI (M7b)

- **Genetic mutator (S-0611; AI game format 25).** `glasnost_ai::mutator` (a new file; `Brain::fire_superweapons` calls `mutator::mutate_cell` for the genetic kind, `Uses::mutator_launches`). The giving building comes from the brain's superweapon step like every other kind. A ready mutator is fired at the seen enemy infantry cell whose reach holds the most seen enemy infantry, at least `MIN_GROUP` (3), with none of the house's or its allies' infantry in the reach (in explosion mode, none of their objects). The reach is what a person reads off the cursor and the map: in cell mode the effect block, each cell in its swept layer; in explosion mode the blast radius around the cell centre, in the one layer it hits (S-0303 claims 3 and 4). It holds fire while no group is worth it. Weapons with a mutating warhead are ordinary weapons. The explosion-mode layer rule came from the lane: aimed beside a bridge, four launches converted only one unit each, because the infantry on the deck was out of the blast's layer.
- **Psychic dominator (S-0612; AI game format 26).** `glasnost_ai::dominator` (a new file; `Brain::fire_superweapons` calls `dominator::dominate_cell` for the dominator kind, `Uses::dominator_launches`; `Brain::fired_kind` now names the dominator). The giving building comes from the superweapon step like every other kind. A ready dominator is fired at the seen enemy unit cell whose sweep (`domination::swept`: the cells and layers a person reads off the cursor) takes the most value: at least `MIN_UNITS` (3) seen, dominable, non-building enemy objects, and only where the enemy value it takes (cost) is larger than the house's and its allies' own value in the same reach. It holds fire while a domination is in force or no group is worth it. In one think a launch's reach is spent: the mutator and the dominator never fire at the same group (the gate showed the mutator killing the crowd the dominator was about to take).

- **AI audit: water walk (S-0406).** `Script::water_goal` grows the base toward water only where an explored site would take the naval yard but for the distance to the base (what the placement cursor shows, S-0406 claims 3 to 5). On a stock map the Yuri house used to walk a dozen power plants toward a strip of water too narrow for the yard, and starved. Tests: `tests/water.rs`.
- **AI audit: economy first (S-0404; AI game format 27).** `Brain::economy_first`: while the house is short of the plan's harvesters and a harvester on its line waits for money, every other running item is put on the hold order (S-0404 claim 5) and resumed with a build order once the economy is whole (claims 3 and 5); `Uses::economy_holds`, `Uses::resumes`. `Script::harvesters_wanted` adds harvesters to an explored field: one for every 20 explored resource cells within 14 cells of the house's refineries, slave masters and home, between the plan's count and twice it (tuning of our own opponent). Tests: `tests/economy.rs`. The extra harvesters cost the duel-m7a sweep margin (seeds 1 to 16, minimum/median/maximum, before → after): gattling stage rises 2/13/32 → 0/2/6, gas clouds 1/160/250 → 0/2/91, bunker entry orders 2/7/12 → 0/4/7, lift orders 6/11/26 → 0/5/10: the fixture fields grow to about 228 resource cells, each house keeps up to 4 harvesters and its army comes later. The gate seed still passes; the fixture setup is left as a question for the main session.
- **AI audit: force shield aim (S-0513).** `Brain::shield_cell` counts the buildings of the house and its allies whose reference point lies within the shield radius of the target cell's centre (claim 31, F3: the radial indicator a person reads), at one of the house's buildings with a seen enemy within 12 cells, at least 2 buildings. The old aim counted building cells in a 3 × 3 block, which two buildings with a gap never share: the shield was ready in every Yuri lane game and never fired (now 80 launches in 24 games). Test: `tests/ai_sw.rs` `shields_buildings_with_a_gap_between_them`.
- **AI audit: gunner vehicles (S-0308).** `glasnost_ai::gunner` (a new file; `Special::gunners` is called last in `Special::think`): an idle, empty gunner vehicle at home whose type switches its weapon with its passenger (claims 5 and 6) gets the nearest infantry unit of the house, idle or at home on the army's rally (moving or guarding, no capture or entry of its own), whose mode gives it a healing weapon (negative damage), by the *enter* click; one healer for every 5 armed vehicles. The click replaces the army's order of the same think for that unit, and the capture step leaves it be (`Special::gunner_reserved`): in the stock data the side's healer is its engineer, an army type the army script rallies and the capture step sends away. With no such infantry the cheapest type is queued without the credit reserve of the other abilities (`Special::queue_unit`). `Uses::gunner_loads`. Tests: `tests/gunner.rs`.
- **AI audit: lane and audit tools.** The lane's launch count now counts only a fall of the ready mark to not ready while the record stays granted (low-power suspensions used to count as launches). New lane lines: house-ticks granted (and suspended) by superweapon kind; `GLASNOST_AI_ECON=n` (economy line per house: income, spending by category, harvesters, slaves, explored resource cells near the workers, lines); `GLASNOST_AI_SWPROBE=n` (what the mutator and dominator aim sees, `mutator::probe`, `dominator::probe`); `GLASNOST_AI_GUNPROBE=n` (`gunner::probe`). The side audit prints the side's psychic-detection buildings (S-0610 claim 9) and its gunner vehicle types with the infantry types that would heal in them.
- **AI round 2: power, workers and the curtain.** Power is read off the house's own plants, not off the house total that a timed or drain blackout sets to 0 (S-0405 claims 5 and 6, S-0513 claim 30): the lane had an Allied house with 47 power plants built under a drain blackout. A building whose drain the plants cannot carry waits for a power plant, which is built first (`power::plant_first`; a defence keeps its own line and the plant is ordered beside it). A slave-miner house works every field it knows (no cap at twice its count, S-0402 claim 14); a house that knew a field and knows none any more wants no harvester (the map 2 stalemates with money); past the plan's harvesters, a house with money for a harvester and an army unit builds both. The army goes for a seen enemy shooting at the house's workers (harvesters, slaves, miners) or draining one of its plants (S-0608), one order per unit and target. The iron curtain goes on a fighting pair of vehicles or a building under attack, never on a block with an enemy vehicle or building in it (they would be protected, S-0513 claim 25), and enemy infantry in the block does not stop it (infantry of any house dies, claim 23; block F1, claim 4). New lane fields: the drainers of a house's plants and who targets them; the uses `plants_first`, `economy_defence` and `curtained_buildings`. The defence gives no order to a berserk unit (S-0610 claim 6: it takes none; the brain dropped such orders, but the defence had counted and noted them, and alternated a berserk unit between two raiders every tick, 480 to 600 counted orders in a 12 000-tick duel); a unit that lost its target while the raider still shoots is sent again at once. The 4d games and the lane still counted up to 2 479 defence orders a game (one house, Yuri against Allied on map 0): a raider the unit was sent at turned on the unit, was no raider any more, and the unit was sent at the next raider; the next tick the two had swapped, and so on every tick; a unit under an attack order whose target had dropped was sent again on every cell it walked. The exclusions are by state, no timer: a unit is left alone while it shoots at an enemy that is shooting at the house's own, while it is under an attack order whose destination is a threat (S-0213 table T3 (f): the order's destination stays), and while its order from the cell it stands on still names a raider (the memory's judging window, `BLOCKED_FOR`, bounds only how long that order is judged; it is no reaction delay). The same games afterwards: 58, 82, 85 and 36 orders for the worst houses (2 479, 678, 234 and 276 before). AI game format 28. **Gate (setup only):** Beta's construction yard YARD is drainable in `fixture_m7b` (Beta no longer builds a plant for each drain blackout, so the third start disc had no target and drain orders and links fell to 2 in 13 seeds); `duel-m7b` seeds 1–16 (min / median, final code): drain orders 4/6, links 3/3, credits drained 7 280/7 960, drained-building ticks 19 825/20 532, mutator launches 4/6, infantry mutated 12/18, dominator launches 3/5, objects taken over 11/15; every drain counter at least 3 in every seed; seed 11: 11, 4, 7 960, 20 470, 6, 18, 5, 20. `M7A_SEED` 16 → 4 → 27: seed 4 was the only seed of 1 to 16 with every counter at least 3 after the first round-2 changes; after the economy-defence change it gives no takeover order, no seed of 1 to 16 has every counter at least 3 (seeds 1, 11 and 13 miss only by the sensor orders' 2), and of seeds 17 to 40 only seed 27 has (seed 35 ends at tick 11 100). The `duel-m7a` sweep, seeds 1–16 on the final code (min / median): berserk orders 16/29, units made berserk 32/66, sensor orders 2/2, intent-line ticks 794/3 627, reveal orders 4/13, reveals 4/13, slave-miner buildings 4/13, slave ticks 2 702/7 263, stage rises 14/26, top-stage ticks 2 069/3 961, takeover orders 0/4, takeovers 6/15, gas clouds 115/184, gas hits 301/506, absorber entries 5/13, absorbed 3/7, grinder entries 0/9, ground 0/3, bunker builds 3/4, bunker entries 0/5, bunkered 0/3, exits 0/3, lifter builds 2/4, lift orders 2/13, seized 4/16, dropped 3/15. It is weaker than step 2a's: three seeds end early with Alpha's win (2 at tick 10 078, 7 at 10 926, 8 at 9 766; step 2a and the first round-2 sweep had none on the final setup), and there are zeros: takeover orders in seeds 4, 5, 9 and 16 (the takeovers themselves stay at least 6, from the controllers' own guard search), grinder entries and units ground in seed 5, units ground in seed 12, bunker entries, bunkered and exits in seeds 7 and 8 (both end early). Seed 27: berserk orders 51, units made berserk 61, sensor orders 3, intent-line ticks 4 159, reveal orders 14, reveals 14, slave-miner buildings 14, slave ticks 6 522, stage rises 24, top-stage ticks 4 178, takeover orders 17, takeovers 21, gas clouds 193, gas hits 693, absorber entries 13, absorbed 5, grinder entries 16, ground 5, bunker builds 4, bunker entries 9, bunkered 5, exits 5, lifter builds 4, lift orders 17, seized 15, dropped 15. The diagonal duel runs on its own seed 8, so that it still loads and unloads a transport (above).
- **AI round 3: superweapon aim and saving, expansion, anti-air (AI game format 29).** (1) *Dominator by value* (`dominator::dominate_cell`): the cell is chosen by the value a person reads off the sweep, the cost of the enemy units it would take (S-0612 claims 13 and 14: buildings are never taken, allied units are, own objects change no owner) plus the area damage at the cell centre on the ground layer (claim 9), less the own and allied losses; no launch while a domination is in force (claim 3). *Mutator by value* (`mutator::mutate_cell`): the gain is the cost of the enemy infantry in the reach (claims 3 and 4, the infantry type made by claims 8 and 9 and F4), the loss that of own and allied infantry. Both are tested on synthetic data (`tests/dominator.rs`, `tests/mutator.rs`). (2) *Superweapon saving* (`Brain::economy_first`): while a superweapon building of the house is being built and the house is not outnumbered (its own army units fewer than the seen enemy army units), other running items are put on hold (S-0404 claim 5) and resumed after; `Uses::superweapon_holds`. Quick lane A/B, maps 2, 8 and 0, seeds 7 to 10 (Yuri's wins against Allied / against Soviet, dominator launches): saving off 4/12, 11/12, 4; saving on 5/12, 7/12, 15; saving while not outnumbered (kept) 4/12, 8/12, 23. (3) *Expansion* (`Brain::expand`, `Script::far_field`): a rich house (credits at least twice the yard vehicle's price) with its harvesters kept and no intruder builds one yard vehicle (the code had no saving test, though this line said so until M8 step B1: saving acted only by keeping the credits low; *Own AI (M8 steps B1 and B2)* below) (the cheapest type deploying into a building factory; S-0403 claim 6 counts the yards), drives it to the explored resource field that no worked field reaches, on its own half of the map, and deploys it there (the deploy's cell test only, no adjacency: S-0415 claims 1 to 3, S-0406 claim 4); at most 2 yards (a person stops there). `tests/expansion.rs`. (4) *Anti-air against drainers* (`glasnost_ai::antiair`): a house that sees enemy drainers in the air (S-0307 Terms: height at least 208 leptons) and has fewer units, defences and line items able to hit them than drainers builds the cheapest startable type that can (an anti-air projectile, S-0306 claim 23; a versus value not zero, S-0304 claim 1; damage above 0); the base defence already sends only units the firing check lets attack. `tests/antiair.rs`.

### Ability audit

The M7b AI audit lane: maps 0, 2 and 8, Yuri against the Allied side and against the Soviet side, seeds 7 to 10 (24 games, at most 120 000 ticks; 12 games per Allied and Soviet side, 24 for Yuri), with the final AI. Counts are the orders of `special::Uses` (games with any in brackets) or true launches; "0 because" gives the reason found. Ability names are generic (no stock type names).

- **Dominator:** granted in 3 games (Allied map 2, Soviet maps 2 and 8) and launched in all 3 (4 launches), each soon after it was ready (house-ticks ready 2, 1 and 1). It took no object: the 900-tick probe shows the seen enemy units at the best cell mostly refused as immune by data or in the air (0 or 1 dominable unit there), so the value aim fires for the area damage of claim 9 (2 to 9 kills a game). **Mutator:** 16 launches in 5 of 6 games (granted in all 6), 18 infantry mutated.
- **Expansion and anti-air:** no expansion yard vehicle was built in these games, and the unarmed deployer vehicle is again listed as never built in 4 games (buildable from ticks 11 701 to 22 501). The cause was not probed; the brain does not expand while it saves for a superweapon building, and Yuri saved in every game (9 to 54 holds), which is one possible cause. M8 step B1 probed it: the brain had no saving test, and the causes were the yard vehicle's prerequisites and the wealth test (*Own AI (M8 steps B1 and B2)*). In the quick lane below it built 7 in 1 game and moved yard vehicles 54 times in 3. Yuri built no anti-air unit (no enemy drainer); the Allied and Soviet houses built 10 in 4 games and 23 in 5 in the quick lane.
- **Lane on the same binary** (quick mode, seeds 7 to 10, 24 games; `local-lane/m7b/ai3/q-*`): every game ends with a winner; Yuri wins 4 of 12 against Allied (all 4 on map 2) and 9 of 12 against Soviet (map 8 4, map 2 3, map 0 2); v9 at fa9a9f0 had 5 of 12 and 11 of 12. Launches: dominator 25 (Yuri took 4 objects with it), mutator 69 (102 infantry mutated), psychic reveal 188.

- **Psychic sensor orders** are 0 in every game: the side's psychic detection comes from its radar building (side audit below), so no sensor is needed.
- **Dominator: no launch in any game.** It was granted in two games: the 900-tick probe (`GLASNOST_AI_SWPROBE`) found it never ready in the Allied map 0 game (2 samples) and ready in 6 of 29 samples in the Soviet map 2 game, never with at least 3 dominable enemies at the best cell (the aim's minimum). **Mutator:** 12 launches in 4 games; no probe sample found it ready, unsuspended and with a clear group of at least 3 (it fires when one appears, and waits while Yuri's own infantry is in the group).
- **Never built by Yuri while buildable:** the unarmed deployer vehicle (it has no AI role) in 4 games, buildable from ticks 16 501 to 24 301; in the Allied map 2 game, which Yuri lost at 22 314, an armed vehicle and the armed jumpjet, buildable from tick 8 101. In the Soviet games on maps 0 and 2, several types became buildable only after tick 43 201 or 61 501 and were not built before the game ended.
- **Side audit** (`side-audit 2 8 7` at fa9a9f0, the same result as the earlier run of this round): 79 types of the side, 76 within the house tech level; 29 offered, 29 built, 28 placed or released. The naval yard (#65) has no cell on map 8. Three buildings are above the tech level (#43, #51, #66). Never offered: types forbidden to the house's country or stolen-tech types, the other sides' buildings and what needs them, and two vehicles (#17, #23) whose prerequisite is a side building the house is never offered. No gunner vehicles.
- **Seams (*Seams reachable by stock data*, below) in these games:** hovers stood in 4 games and repair depots in 3, so the hover chase cut and `any_docked` are reachable there (no counter in the simulation tells how often they ran). No pulse source appeared (`under_pulse` not reached). The other listed seams leave no trace in the lane output. No game stalled or failed its replay or save.
- **Lane on the same binary** (v9: quick mode, seeds 7 to 10, 24 games): every game ends with a winner; Yuri wins 5 of 12 against Allied and 11 of 12 against Soviet (the same as v8 at 56e9852); the most economy-defence orders for one house are 85 (2 479 in v8).

### Seams reachable by stock data

A grep of the 22 `Seam:` comments under `crates/deterministic` (no counter in the simulation: that would change it). Reachable by stock data: the hover chase cut (`hover.rs`, likely every game); morphable tilesets in placement (`econ/placement.rs`); the sidebar display-name tie (`econ/tech.rs`); the scenery-disguise terrain-fire pass (`targeting.rs`); tubes (`seams.rs`, `transport.rs`: map tube definitions are not imported); `any_docked` (`econ/sell.rs`, repair depots and pads); `under_pulse` (`econ/power.rs`, no pulse source). Not reachable or not wired: the capture, hijack and retaining-entry trigger notifications (`mp_triggers.rs`, no callers), the trigger cell attempts (`bridges/mod.rs`, trigger actions only), the all-base-units mode (`econ/setup.rs`, refused), the teleport type exclusion (`superweapons/warp.rs`, no type named), the transport `disabled` states (neither built), `house_left` (`cloak/disguise.rs`, no callers), the load map RNG (`load.rs`). Stale comments: `spawn.rs` (`owner_changed` has callers), `cloak/machine.rs` (`grant_own_flag` is called by `crates.rs`), `bridges/hut.rs` (`engineer_entered_hut` is called by `structures/entry.rs`).

## Own AI (M8 Track B)

The sections of steps B1 to B7 (*Own AI (M8 steps B1 and B2)*, *Own AI (M8 step B3: the dominator takes units)*, *Own AI (M8 step B5: tempo after expansion)*, *Own AI (M8 step B4: gate margins)*, *Own AI (M8 step B6: the anti-air count and the tempo misses)*, *Own AI (M8 step B7: the dominator's grant and the think tick)*) are in [own-ai-m8-track-b.md](own-ai-m8-track-b.md).

## Map triggers (M5b)

`glasnost_sim::triggers` runs the map triggers of S-0011 in a skirmish; the converter
(`crates/convert/src/map/triggers.rs`) resolves the map's `[Triggers]`, `[Events]`, `[Actions]`,
`[Tags]`, `[CellTags]` and `[VariableNames]` into registration-indexed definitions
(`glasnost_map::triggers`), which the world keeps, so a save carries them. Tags are created at the
cell tags, at pre-placed objects and at the end of loading (with their draws, claim 34), polled in
row 1 of the tick, raised by their owner house every eighth tick, and deleted at the end of the
tick when a one-time tag fired. Covered events: any event, elapsed time, random delay, game time,
variable set and clear, type exists and does not exist, and the code-matched evaluation with its
latches for raises with a code. Covered actions: enable and disable, set and clear variable, the
mission timer (`TriggerState::mission`), reveal around a waypoint, and the presentation-only
actions as `Effect::Trigger` (the audio crate plays sound and sound-at-waypoint by name).
Interim rules and gaps:

- Carrier raises: `triggers::cell_entered` (S-0011 claim 18; called at the arrival of S-0207 and
  S-0208 claim 22) and `triggers::destruction_raises` (S-0011 claim 19 with S-0312 claim 29's
  groups; called in the killing hit before death debris). The bridge-destroyed raise waits for
  bridge destruction.
- Sound at a waypoint plays the sound once at the cell's centre; giving a building or terrain object
  on the cell the sound as its ambient sound (claim 32) waits for ambient sounds in the audio crate.
  Text messages, speech and radar events reach the presentation as effects but are not shown yet.
- An owner country is matched against the countries' type names only; our rules data has no country
  name field yet.
- Type exists and does not exist look through buildings, vehicles, infantry and aircraft in that
  order and take the last exact match (claim 21 leaves the order open).
- Not implemented yet (recognised, no effect, a note): change owner, destroy attached objects,
  explosion at a waypoint, make a house cheer, play an animation and the drop-zone animation
  (claims 24, 27 to 29 and 36), and the hand-over actions of claim 30 (teams, crates and
  preferred targets). The lightning storm and the other multiplayer actions are S-0524's (see
  *Simulation (M6b: multiplayer triggers)*). The stock skirmish maps in use need only elapsed time and
  sound at a waypoint.

## Audio (M5b)

`glasnost-audio` turns what the game reports into sound, following S-0701 (sound selection, revision 3). It never feeds back into the simulation, and nothing in it is simulation state. The claim 29 test checks that a game's state hash is the same with and without audio.

- **Samples (`bank`).** `SoundBank` maps names (case-insensitive) to samples and decodes each one on first use (`clip`), caching the result. A name that does not decode is remembered as a failure. Sources: IDX/BAG banks (`add_idx`), AUD and plain PCM WAVE files (`add_file`, with WAVE read by `glasnost_fmt::pcm::Pcm::from_wav`), and decoded PCM (`add_pcm`). `bank::discover(&Vfs)` builds the one sample bank of S-0701 claim 34 from `AUDIO.IDX` and `AUDIO.BAG`, each found through the game's file lookup (S-0005), so either may be an archive member or a loose file. It returns a `DiscoverReport` (index found, index without a readable data file, sample count). Sound entries play only bank samples.
- **Sound entries (`catalog`).** `SoundCatalog::from_ini` reads the sound database (S-0701 claims 1 to 3): samples, control and placement words (the last of `shroud` and `unshroud` applies), levels (F1, and with `quirks.full_volume_16383` off an entry of 100 or more is at full), priority, limit, range, delay, pitch shift and attack/decay. The install reads the sound and speech databases (S-0005 Import mapping) into `Install::sounds` and `Install::speech`. The client builds the catalogs at each game start, under that game's quirk switches.
- **Announcement entries (`speech`).** `SpeechCatalog::from_ini` reads the speech database (claim 26). `Announcer` keeps one line playing at a time and queues the others by priority (claim 27). Each announcement requests a fixed entry (`Announcement::entry`, S-0701 Import mapping), and the sample is chosen by the local house's voice set. Announcement samples are separate files, the eight-character name plus `.wav` (`speech::file_name`, claim 34), never bank samples. The client's start job reads the local voice set's files (`SpeechCatalog::files`) through the file lookup and hands them to `Audio::set_voices`. Announcements skip the master level and the sound volume option (claim 35); our engine has no options screen, so the voice option stays at full.
- **Names by role (`names`).** `SoundNames::from_ruleset` reads the sound keys the specs name from the imported rules: the per-type keys (S-0701 Import mapping, S-0415, S-0107, S-0210, S-0207, S-0214), the weapon reports, the animations' start sounds (S-0109 claim 13) and the game-wide keys (S-0408, S-0410 claim 3, S-0416 claim 4, S-0701). Lists follow S-0003 claim 12 and `quirks.list_items_untrimmed`.
- **Mixer (`mixer`).** `Mixer` is integer only and mixes interleaved stereo at the output rate. It provides:
  - placement on screen in half-pixels (F2), levels and truncation (F3), and attenuation and balance (F4);
  - 16 channels and a pool of 300 instances, of which 200 may follow an object; the per-entry limit (claim 8) and the victim choice (claim 9);
  - the per-instance draws in their order: pitch, shift, pre-delay, attack/body/decay, the startup draw, the shuffle (claims 10 to 14);
  - loops and the dormant restart (claim 15);
  - a separate announcement voice;
  - `render`, which tests call offline with no device.
- **Music (`music`, `music_files`, M8 step C1).** The mixer's music voice, mixed after the announcement at our music volume; the player runs at the end of each audio pass. See "Music (M8 step C1 part 1)".
- **Outputs (`output`, `web`, `pipe`).** `Output::pump` is called once per frame and pushes enough mixed audio to stay about 0.1 s ahead.
  - **wasm32:** `web::WebOutput` schedules 1024-frame AudioBuffers on an AudioContext. The context starts suspended and is resumed by the first pointer, key or touch gesture. `Audio::resume` can also be called from an input handler.
  - **Native:** the `native-output` feature (off by default) pipes 16-bit stereo to `pw-cat`, `pacat` or `aplay`. This is a stopgap: an in-process device crate (cpal) could not be fetched offline when M5b was written, and the default build needs no system audio headers.
  - **Otherwise:** `NullOutput`, which discards voices, is used and its state names the reason.
- **Events (`Audio`).** The game client owns one `Audio` (`Audio::platform()` picks the output). At each game start it calls `set_quirks`, `set_catalog`, `set_speech`, `set_bank`, `set_voices`, `set_names` and `start(world, local_house)`. Then it calls:
  - after each tick, `tick(world, effects, unsync)`. This plays:
    - the hit, shot, death and animation effects (claims 20 to 22), and the game-wide building damage and death sounds for building types with none of their own (claim 37);
    - the response slots (claim 16);
    - the movement, turret, unit-ready, building and promotion sounds (claim 23), and the promotion announcement (claim 36);
    - the low-funds, low-power, base-attack and unit-lost announcements (claim 28, F7).
  - after input:
    - `selected` for the first object each input selects (claim 17);
    - `ordered` for a click that gave orders (claim 18, F6). The repair vehicle (`FV`) repairing plays the repair response; an attack with a secondary weapon by a non-elite object plays its secondary-weapon response;
    - `key` for the guard, stop and scatter commands (claim 19);
    - `cue(UiCue)` for the interface sounds (claim 24, S-0410 claim 3, S-0416 claim 4) and the building placement sound. Under claim 24 the start, queue, resume, hold and cancel clicks play the build click with their announcement (start and resume request the training entry for infantry and the building entry for any other kind; a queued copy on an already active line requests no announcement). A busy production line gives only the unable-to-comply announcement, and a refused placement click only the cannot-deploy-here announcement. A page request already at the strip's end plays the refusal sound, an accepted page move the tab sound, and nothing plays while the icons fit (S-0411 claim 18).
  - every frame, `set_view` (the view's origin and size, with S-0101's projection) and `frame(now_ms, unsync)`, which runs the audio passes and pumps the output.

  Game-side choices and the mixer's per-instance draws all take raw values from the client's one unsynchronised stream (S-0202 claim 11, S-0701 claim 30). `frame` and `service` lend that stream to the mixer for the pass and take it back. `now_ms` is host time; S-0701 revision 14 claim 30 (answering Q-0671) specifies the deterministic logical schedule that replaces it, which is not built yet (row below). Since intake 25 the stand-stills are left out of it: `frame_in(now_ms, stand, unsync)` makes no audio pass in the options menu or without focus and holds sound playback there (`glasnost_audio::stand`).
- **Not modelled yet.**
  - Claim 22's animation stop sound: the simulation reports no animation end.
  - Revision 15's Import mapping for `Report`, `StartSound` and `StopSound` per import layer (an unknown name keeps an earlier layer's reference, and the `Report` alias is read only while no start reference is held) is not built: `SoundNames` reads the merged section, where the last layer's value wins and an unknown name resolves to no entry at play time. The 127-character cut is the common text limit.
  - Claim 25's lightning sounds: there is no weather storm.
  - Enslaved and deactivated objects do not exist yet, so the normal response lists always apply (claim 17).
  - Sounds the simulation does not report yet: crushing (S-0210), chrono departure and arrival (S-0207), entering and leaving water, idle comments and undeploying (S-0107).
  - Claim 24's refusals that need a signal from the simulation: a production request refused as it is applied (full queue or build limit, S-0404), and a place order that fails as it is applied. The route-mode refusal (S-0213 claim 16) is absent too, because the client has no route mode yet.
  - Claim 35's voice volume option: there is no options screen, so it is at full.
  - Claim 36's defeat, victory and player-defeated announcements, and the play-speech trigger action as an interrupt. Alliance announcements are not raised either; S-0414 revision 12 (claims 5 and 13) leaves their selection and entry bindings unspecified, apart from the break announcement's condition and the post-defeat muting. Structure-sold announcements are not raised either.
- **Viewer.** The asset viewer's `Player` (`preview.rs`) is unchanged and still plays previews natively through a command-line player.

S-0701 answers these earlier interim rules:
