Provisional rules of the audio, each tied to a request:

| Where | Interim rule | Request |
|---|---|---|
| `bank::SoundBank::add_idx` | when the index lists a name twice, the first copy wins | Q-0263 |
| `mixer` | a delayed instance plays the immediate mode's partition, keeps one body sample across loops, takes no startup draw and has no waits | Q-0329 |
| `mixer` | the crowded master level reaches its target at once (S-0701 F3 gives the settled target only; the transition ramp is outside its coverage) | Q-0333 |
| `Audio::frame`, `Mixer::service` | audio passes still run on host time (`now_ms`, a pass when more than 33 ms have passed) and samples complete when the device finishes them, so the per-instance draws' place in the unsynchronised stream can vary with host timing. S-0701 revision 14 claim 30 answers Q-0671 with the engine's schedule, which is not built yet: logical audio time advancing by *P(s)* (8 ms at speed 0, 16s ms otherwise) at each cycle end with a pass when *C* − *L* > 33 ms; logical sample durations max(1, ⌈100000 × *N* / (*f* × *p*)⌉) ms completing at the first pass at or past the deadline; waits expiring at the first pass past *C₀* + *d*; game-side choices before the pass's per-instance choices, in request-number order. The scenario stream and game rules are unaffected (claim 29) | none (S-0701 claim 30, not built) |

S-0701 claims 21 and 32 answer Q-0327: a weapon report plays entry *r* mod *n* of the weapon's list, *r* the firer's retained report value (`Object::report_value`), carried on `Effect::Shot::report`, with no draw. Its row is retired. Q-0329 stays partial (claim 2 and E3 defer the delayed sequence), Q-0333's ramp is outside F3, and S-0005 claim 10a does not say which copy of a name listed twice in the one index wins (Q-0263), so those rows stay.

## Game client (M5b)

A person plays a skirmish against our AI from the main menu to the end screen, natively (`cargo run -p glasnost-platform-native -- --game --game-dir DIR`) and on the web (`?mode=game`, docs/web.md). The interface is our own code, `glasnost-ui` (no egui), drawn by `glasnost_render::batch2d` within the WebGL2 core.

- **Screens (`glasnost_ui::shell`).** Main menu, then skirmish setup (S-0412), then the game, then the end screen (S-0414), then back to the menu. The setup offers every multiplayer map with at least two start positions (S-0009 claim 23, `install::start_count`). Maps are known by their position in the list and, in the game, by a content hash (`session::content_hash`), never by a stock name. Lobby colours are the colour slots of S-0103 claim 10.
- **Modules (`crates/app/src/game`).**
  - `install` reads what the menus need from the mounted install: the base rules (setup values, S-0412 Import mapping), the maps and their start counts, the sound names, the sound and speech databases and the sample bank.
  - `play` is the running game without drawing: selection, tools (select, place, sell, repair, power), sidebar clicks (`econ::sidebar::click`), status messages and the end screen. Every order goes through `glasnost_ai::game::Game::issue`, so it is in the replay.
  - `session` is a game on a loaded map: camera (S-0101 limits), the live scene, radar, gestures, and the HUD's draw list.
  - `paint` turns draw lists into batch quads, and draws them on the CPU for tests.
  - `client` holds the screens, jobs, pacing, sound and input, without a window.
  - `window` runs winit and wgpu. The battlefield is drawn on the GPU (`map_gpu::MapRenderer` into a target the batch shows through `Batch2d::set_image_view`); if that fails, it falls back to the CPU compose.
- **Pacing.** `mapview::live::Pacer` gives one tick per drawn frame at most, measured from each cycle's start (S-0201 claims 2 to 4). In a skirmish nothing ticks while the options menu is open (claim 8) or the window is out of focus (claim 9). The speed comes from the setup and the options menu. A network game paces by its own table (`setup::net_period_ms`, S-0201 claim 17 and F5), and neither the menu nor lost focus stops it (claims 15 and 16).
- **Sound.** `Audio` is created at the first game start and resumed on the first gesture. At each game start the client sets it up from the install's sound and speech databases, bank and names, under the game's quirk switches (S-0701). After each tick it calls `tick`. After input it calls `selected`, `ordered`, `key` and `cue`, with what `play` collected in `selected`, `acks`, `keys` and `cues`. Each frame it calls `set_view` and `frame`. Game-side choices use the unsynchronised stream seeded with the game's seed (S-0202 claim 8).
- **Input.** Clicks go through `glasnost_sim::targeting` (S-0214). A left release selects or orders (claim 26), a left drag is the band box, a right release clears the selection or ends a sidebar or placement mode (claims 26 and 28), and a right drag past F8 (8 pixels on either axis, `band::RightDrag`) scrolls the view (S-0101 claims 16 and 17) and is released with no effect (claim 27). The key commands are S-0213 table T2. Box selection, scrolling and key bindings follow those claims; the client's remaining interim rules are in the table below.
- **Route mode, type select and control groups (M8 A3 intake 50, [intake50-S-0213-S-0214-S-0512-S-0522.md](m8-claims/intake50-S-0213-S-0214-S-0512-S-0522.md)).**
  - *Route mode* (S-0213 claims 16 to 17, table T2): `Action::RouteMode` (Z by default) held makes battlefield clicks go through `glasnost_sim::routes::click`, which refuses them (kinds, same route state, the 128 limit, the point-click test) or gives `Order::RoutePoint` and `Order::RouteJoin`; a deploy click gives nothing (`RouteClick::Ignored`, S-0214 VD1). Releasing the key or losing the focus issues `Order::RouteCommit`; `Key::Delete` issues `Order::RouteDelete` for the selected point. The guard, stop, scatter and deploy keys are refused with their messages. The state is `World.routes` (`routes::RouteWorld`: points, each holder's steps and loop, the per-house holder count), saved and hashed (save format 88). Every other order naming an actor ends its route (`routes::order_ends`, claim 17) except `Order::Routed`, which a route issues for its next point at the fall-back to rest (S-0215 claim 13's route row, `routes::fall_back`). `Session::draw` draws the routes of the local house and its allies while the key is held (`routes::drawn`). Quirks `spent_route_blocks_join` and `route_holder_count_drift`. Tests `claims_s0213_routes.rs`, `route_mode_adds_points_and_commits_on_release`.
  - *Type select* (S-0214 claim 32): a release of the type-select key at most 500 ms after its press (`session::TYPE_SELECT_TAP_MS`) is a tap: `PlayState::type_tap` adds the selection's types (`targeting::tap_types`, with DeploysInto) from the screen candidates (`targeting::tap_candidate`), or across the map, by the reach (`play::Reach`, reset by `sel_reset` after an addition or clearing). Tests `claim_32_*`, `claims_s0214_tap.rs`.
  - *Control groups* (S-0214 claim 33, F9; S-0213 claim 22): Ctrl+digit makes, Shift+digit adds the group to the selection, Alt+digit centres on it, the digit selects; a select less than 800 ms after the last (`session::GROUP_REPEAT_MS`) centres the view on `targeting::group_centre`. `PlayState.groups` maps each member to its `GroupMark` (number and the house that made it). The bindings are fixed for now. Quirks `group_center_keeps_extra_selection` and `group_kept_on_owner_change`. Tests `claim_33_*`, `control_group_keys_make_add_select_and_centre`.
  - *Arming from an icon* (S-0512 claim 25) clears the selection; *falling* is no longer part of S-0214's *movable* and *able to fire* (S-0522 claim 17), and claim 6 (e) asks `combat::check::check_ignoring_fall`.
- **Route mode, held type-select drags and icon sounds (M8 A3 intake 54, [intake54-S-0213-S-0214-S-0512-S-0522.md](m8-claims/intake54-S-0213-S-0214-S-0512-S-0522.md)).**
  - *Route points* (S-0213 r60 claims 16a to 16f):
    - Only point adds applied in the same tick share a point (`RoutePoint.made`).
    - Being committed belongs to each step (`Step.committed`).
    - A join copies the longest holder's route, the smallest `Step.since` from `RouteWorld.stamp`. A loop that starts before the joined point is rotated, so the copy is the whole loop. The copied steps are uncommitted.
    - The commit and each fall-back issue start with `routes::wait`, a queue of *wait* with an immediate start.
    - Issuing a loop point moves it to the end of the route (`Route.next` is gone).
    - `drop_orphans` runs after every issue.
    - Two points at one place: intake 54's interim pick (`routes::point_for`, Q-2166) is gone; intake 56 builds S-0213 r74's rule (below).
    - Save format 89, AI game format 51.
- **Route-mode presses (M8 A3 intake 56, [intake56-S-0213.md](m8-claims/intake56-S-0213.md)).**
  - *Click areas and presses* (S-0213 r74 claim 16b): a point's click area is the 16 by 16 pixel square from 8 left and above to 7 right and below its place's screen position (`Session::points_under`, `routes::in_click_area`). At a left press in route mode the session asks `routes::press_kind_refusal` (claim 16d's kind test, skipped inside a click area), then `routes::press`: `Take` (the earliest-made drawn point, own and accepted), `Refused` (the earliest-made point of any house refuses the selection) or `Ordinary`. At the release a taken point is selected (nothing selected) or `routes::press_orders` sends one `Order::RouteJoin { actor, point, place }` for each selected object and each holder of a selected route's last point (`routes::press_list`). A join names its point by place and acts on the house's earliest-made point there. An ordinary click goes through `routes::click`: behaviour orders become point adds, a detonate is sent as it is, and the same-route-state and holder-limit tests are made.
  - *Joins* (`routes::apply_join`): a loop close when the actor holds the point, else the copy after the actor's own points, past 16 points and with a loop that can begin in its own points (`Route.loop_len`; intake 63 below).
  - *Selection and the end* (claims 16e and 16h): selecting any object clears the point selection; selecting a holder while the key is held also selects the holders of its last point (`Play::add_holders`); a short right click (under 4 pixels) with nothing selected clears the point selection; a delete applied selects the next point (`RouteNote::Selected`). Route mode ends with the commit at the key's release, a focus loss, the options menu and the local house's defeat (`RouteMode.ended`).
  - Quirks `hidden_point_refuses_click`, `click_area_skips_kind_test`, `delete_selects_after_release`, `stacked_point_takes_earliest`, `press_copy_ignores_route_state`. Save format 90, AI game format 52, wire version 11. Tests `claims_s0213_routes.rs`, `claims_s0213_route_detonate.rs`, the `route_mode_*` tests in `client_play_notes_tests.rs`.
- **Recorded route orders, loop attack moves, aircraft and building holders (M8 A3 intake 63, [intake63-S-0213-S-0301.md](m8-claims/intake63-S-0213-S-0301.md)).**
  - *Recorded order and place* (S-0213 r84 claim 16b): a point issues the order recorded in its `Step` (`routes::issue_next` sends it as it is; S-0215 claim 13's classification, service guards and cell fallback do not apply). `Order::RoutePoint.place` is the order's place (`routes::order_place`): the target's; a *move*'s clicked cell, or the clicked object's cell; any other order's destination. An attack-move click has the place the same click gives without entry (r89, Q-2230), so the order's attack-move mark is not an input. `None` adds nothing. A building's rally-point click on a cell, and its *move* on an object (`Order::GuardSpot`, r87), are sent at the release after claim 16d's tests, as is its *deploy*'s primary order (`Order::Primary`, r87; a building's deploy is not refused in route mode). An absorbing building's *deploy* (r87) is a point add of *unload* naming the building itself, at its own place.
  - *Attack moves in a loop* (claim 16c): `routes::apply_join`'s loop close calls `loop_attack_moves`. When a holder of the loop's first point passes `orders::attack_move_eligible`, every *move* step at the loop's points, in every route of the house, gets `attack_move`.
  - *A loop that ends early* (claim 16c, Q-2191): `Route.loop_len` (was `loop_to`) is the loop's number of points. Once `loop_from` is 0, every issued point goes to the route's end. `loop_len` counts only for deletes (`apply_delete`: each delete at or after the loop's first point lowers it; below 3 the loop is cancelled) and for join copies (`join_copy`). `Route::loop_ends_early` tells such a loop. A delete of a route's last point selects the point (*n* mod *C*) places after the loop's first, with *n* the route's points from the loop's first and *C* `loop_len` (`next_after`, r89, Q-2226).
  - *Aircraft and buildings* (claim 16d, Q-2192): `routes::fall_back` issues only for infantry and vehicles. `routes::issue_later` (the queue of *wait*, then the issue) is called from `air::landing_trigger` (unless *enter*; S-0501 claim 6's planned-route step, before the pad-bound test, with no landing begun: r89, Q-2229) and from `combat::behave::building_process`'s no-target branch for non-SAM buildings (no *guard* queued when *wait* is then current). A building under *move* does nothing (S-0215 claim 4). A turreted one gets no ready mark there, so a later *attack* stays queued and it never fires; one without a turret starts it (r89, Q-2228).
  - *S-0213 r89 (M8 A3 intake 66, [intake66-S-0213.md](m8-claims/intake66-S-0213.md))* answers Q-2226 to Q-2230 with the rules above. A vehicle that issues an engineer's copied *capture* gets *capture* with the building as its destination and no target, and never captures it (Q-2227). Next to the building, S-0207 claim 15 table T4 still makes it attack the building as an enemy obstacle, against r89's part ac: request Q-2247, full-window test ignored. No new state, so no format change. Tests `claims_s0213_r89.rs`.
  - *Q-2193*: `routes::press_kind_refusal` counts every house's click areas (only drawn ones with `quirks.hidden_point_refuses_click` off); right presses are never tested (`Session`).
  - Save format 92, AI game format 54, wire version 12. Tests `claims_s0213_r84.rs`, `claims_s0213_routes.rs`.
  - *Falling* (S-0214 r64 claim 6 (e), S-0522 r14 claim 17): `check_ignoring_fall` is gone. Claim 6 (e) asks `combat::check::check` again, so during the fall an ordinary click on an enemy gives a move order and force-fire gives an attack order.
  - *Held type-select drags* (S-0214 r64 claim 32): `Play::type_drag` seeds every in-box candidate's type, of any house or kind, and adds own selectable objects of those types from the reach's pool (`Session::type_pool`). It adds no deploys-into types, and claim 19's clearing and empty-box rules still apply.
  - *Icon sounds* (S-0512 r21 claim 25): `Play::cameo_click` pushes `UiCue::SuperweaponClick` (the build click) on every left click, then `UiCue::SelectTarget` (`EVA_SelectTarget`) when it arms.
- **Superweapons (S-0512, S-0513, S-0410).**
  - `glasnost_ui::hud::entries` puts the house's granted superweapons first in the second tab (`hud::SW_TAB`, S-0004 claim 11). They sort by charge time, with ties per `quirks.sidebar_tie_newest_first`; display names are not read, so they compare equal.
  - A superweapon icon is the type's sidebar image cut to 24 bytes plus `.shp`, with `XXICON.SHP` when that file is missing (S-0007 claim 31). It shows the F3 charge stage as the build-clock veil, "On hold" while suspended, "Ready", and "Charging" or "Active" for drain-mode types (S-0512 claim 16). A ready superweapon in its flash window flashes its tab (claim 17).
  - A left click on an icon that offers targeting arms it (`play::Tool::Superweapon`). The tool is dropped when the record stops offering targeting. A source pick of the local house (`SwEvent::SourceMarker`) arms the warp type by `quirks.superweapon_kind_as_list_position` (S-0513 claim 33).
  - With the tool armed, a left click on the battlefield issues `Order::FireSuperweapon` at the cell and returns to selecting (S-0513 claims 1 and 2). A force shield is offered only over an own or allied building; elsewhere a click sends no order and the aim stays. A non-empty unit selection takes precedence, and a right click cancels. A radar click fires the kinds of S-0410 claim 12 table C1 (`play::Play::radar_aims`, `click_radar`).
  - Effect animations come from the simulation's `Effect::Anim` and are played like any other animation. The teleport source marker takes its creation draws in the simulation on every pick (S-0513 claim 33, Q-0741 part 1); only its picture and sound are the launching viewer's. `mapview::live::Live` plays the `chrono_placement` animation at a `SwEvent::SourceMarker` of the viewer's house, replacing an earlier one, and removes it at `SwEvent::MarkerEnd` (S-0513 claims 33 and 34).
  - Protection appearance (S-0513 claim 29 and F9, S-0102 claim 34 and F9): see *Protection appearance* under *Visual review 2026-10-19*, with S-0513 revision 20's rows and the building slot-animation rows.
  - Not built: the aim cursor images (S-0214 claim 31 table CS keys them by aim action, and the action words are still interim, Q-0731). The egui map viewer (`mapview::sidebar`) has its own sidebar model without superweapons.
- **Line effects.** Laser beams, disc-laser beams, prism support beams, radiation-beam flashes, the magnetic wave and the half see-through erased body: see *Beams, beam flashes and the magnetic wave* under *Visual review 2026-10-19*.
- **Tests.**
  - `game::play` tests: select, move, deploy, build and place, box selection, superweapon arming and firing (`s0512_claim_16_*`, `s0513_claim_1_*`, `s0513_claim_33_*`), and surrender to an end screen, all on the synthetic skirmish.
  - `game::client` tests: menu, setup and the start job on a synthetic install.
  - `game::paint` tests and `crates/render/tests/offscreen_batch2d.rs`: the batch's pixels against the CPU reference, image views included.
  - The local lane: `GLASNOST_GAME_DIR=<install> cargo test -p glasnost-app --test ui_lane -- --ignored --nocapture` drives the client from the menu through a game (deploy, a few hundred ticks, a building ready for placement, surrender) to the end screen. It writes `menu.png`, `setup.png`, `game.png`, `placement.png` and `end.png` to `local-lane/ui/` (git-ignored; never commit them). With a GPU adapter it also draws the game frame through the window's path (`game::GpuDraw`) offscreen, to `game-gpu.png`, and compares it with the CPU frame. It writes the first icon's palette indices as grey levels to `icon-indices.png`, which separates the icon palette (Q-0275) from the decoding. `GLASNOST_UI_MAP=n` picks the n-th offered map and `GLASNOST_UI_TICKS=n` sets the ticks before `game.png`.

Interim rules of the game client, each tied to a request:

| Where | Interim rule | Request |
|---|---|---|
| `session::read_icons`, `register_art` | an icon is frame 0 of its file, index 0 transparent, its canvas centred in the slot and clipped; a darkened icon gets a translucent black veil; the alternate icon is unused (the palette part is answered, see below; frame selection, placement and the darkening mask are S-0411 claim 16's and not yet brought over) | Q-0275 |
| `glasnost_ui::hud::Layout` | our own sidebar layout: the radar, credits and power bar (the meter's three parts follow S-0405 claim 19 and F4; a drain mark inside the supplied part at full power and an output/drain tooltip on hover, `hud::drain_mark`, `hud::power_lines`), sell, repair and power buttons and four tabs drawn as symbols of our own with their names on hover (`hud::symbol`, Q-2238), a grid of icon slots with scroll buttons, and the options button; the build clock is a veil of stage/55; "Ready" and "On hold" labels | Q-0276 |
| `session::update_cursor` | battlefield and sidebar-mode cursors follow S-0214 claims 21 and 22 and the edge-scroll cursor S-0213 claim 24 (`game::cursor`); the parts not yet brought over keep the system pointer: the radar, building-placement hover and route-point editing | Q-0277 |
| `session::update_cursor` | while a superweapon is armed the system pointer stays: S-0214 claim 31's table CS keys the aim images by aim action, and which words name those actions is open | Q-0731 |

Answered requests of the game client: Q-0570 by S-0101 revision 11, claim 8 and F5 (`session::outside_local_area`, `render::projection::local_area_rect`: on each oversized axis, every battlefield pixel beyond that axis's interval of the local-area rectangle is black, over the battlefield image and under the overlays; the bands of both axes form a union and a non-oversized axis is not clipped); Q-0530 by S-0014 revision 3, claims 4 and 6, and S-0213 revision 25, claim 25 (Vertical metrics) and Scenario O21 d (`glasnost_fmt::fnt::Font::parse` rejects the whole font for another identifying sequence, an incomplete header, mapping or record payload, any selector beyond the record count, a record span below 1 + *HB*, any stored width beyond 8*B* or an absent capital-X mapping, checking every selector and record whether used or not; a zero-width mapped X is accepted; `game::status_font::read` then reports one diagnostic naming the file and the reason and status text is omitted, with no partial use and no substitute font; `status_font::draw` keeps the line origins a fixed 19 pixels apart, puts each glyph's row 0 at its line origin and clips each line to its first min(*H*, *P*) rows, so a zero pitch paints nothing, a pitch below *H* cuts the bottom without the original's extra row at *P*, and tall masks may overlap the next line without re-spacing it; the pitch changes no splitting, capacity or lifetime); Q-0501 by S-0014 and S-0213 claim 25 (`glasnost_fmt::fnt`: the `fonT` header, the 65536 16-bit selectors and the one-bit records; `game::status_font`: `GAME.FNT` resolved through the ordinary precedence (S-0005 claim 18a), each 16-bit text unit selecting its glyph without low-byte reduction, a zero selector drawing the generated complement of the mapped capital X with X's width; `glasnost_ui::status`: lines kept as 16-bit units, the zero unit ending the text, carriage returns and line feeds with no advance and no glyph, other control characters drawn with their glyphs; `play::Play::say` measures with the font's widths and `status_font::draw` draws the masks 3 pixels in from the battlefield's top left with 19 pixels between line origins, zero bits leaving the battlefield unchanged and zero-width glyphs painting nothing; without a usable font the session logs a missing-asset diagnostic and status text is omitted. Our own messages, "Game started." and "Nothing to do there.", use our text colour as foreground: no route-refusal notice, whose foreground is the local player's colour definition, is shown yet); Q-0393 by S-0213 claim 25, Line splitting and font (`glasnost_ui::status::split`, `play::Play::status_view`, `session::resize`: the budget is the view width minus 22 at game start and minus 16 after a size change, which clears the lines; each character advances its glyph width plus one pixel, tabs go to 64-pixel stops, at most 111 UTF-16 units per line, word breaks after the last character of value 32 or less, explicit breaks, continuation control characters skipped, an empty text one blank line; every line has its own place and lifetime; an unfittable remainder is left out with a logged warning); Q-0392 by S-0214 claims 24 and 25 and F7 (`session::box_candidates`, `band::admit`, `band::mobile_point_in`, `band::building_point_in`, `live::Live::drawn_mobiles`: the in-play buildings on the map whose type is not invisible in the game with their point in the view, 0 to *W* and 0 to *H* inclusive, in creation order, then the drawn mobile objects with their point from −32 to *W* + 32 and −32 to *H* + 32, in drawing order, cut at 500; the drawing order runs layer by layer, underground, surface, ground (sorted, S-0102 claim 6), air and top (entering order, claim 7), with each mobile object in the layer `mapview::layers` last put it into (claims 3 and 4), and transient drawing displacements are ignored (S-0214 claim 19)); Q-0391 by S-0213 claim 23 and S-0201 claim 14 (`client::key`, `scroll::arrow_event_accepted`, `orders::blocked`: a view-arrow press or release made while orders are blocked is discarded under `quirks.blocked_arrow_events`, so an accepted arrow keeps scrolling and a newly pressed one adds nothing until a fresh press after the block; the window drops auto-repeated key-downs, so holding a key is never a new press); Q-0281 in part by S-0412 claim 15 (`install::offered_countries`, `glasnost_ui::setup`: the offered countries by index and listing flag, the map's rows with hidden computer slots beyond them, no team and four teams); Q-0276 in part by S-0405 claim 19 and F4 (`power::meter_base`, `power::meter`, `hud::power_meter`: the power bar's three parts; the rest of the layout keeps the interim row); Q-0339 by S-0411 claim 18 (`play::Play::scroll_icons`: a page move, the end sound at the end, no effect when the icons fit, one silent row per wheel step); Q-0285 by S-0414 claims 19 to 23 (`econ::stats`: the result screen's own built total, kills, losses and running score, the displayed score fixed once after play stops with one scenario-stream draw per undefeated listed house, and `Play::clock`'s active 16-ms clock for the duration), Q-0283 by S-0411 claim 11 (`glasnost_ui::hud::tab_lit`: a tab flash keeps the tick that started it, with the initial extra period and `quirks.tab_flash_initial_extra_period`; the lit look is our own design), Q-0279 by S-0101 claims 12 to 15 (`game::scroll`: edge scrolling at the outermost client pixel with F9's direction, momentum and magnitude table scaled by the imported `ScrollMultiplier`, 21 px per held arrow, both once per unfrozen cycle; the scroll index is the default 3, since our engine has no settings file yet; an order block turns edge scrolling off, S-0201 claim 14, and no timed freeze is modelled), and Q-0280 by S-0103 claims 11 and 12 (`install::slot_swatches`: the fixed slot colours after the 16-bit step).

Known limits:

- Icons are drawn through the icon palette of S-0103 claim 13 (`palette::icon_palette_565`: `CAMEO.PAL` through claim 1's conversion, the middle shade row, the 16-bit step; black with an importer warning when the file is missing, our engine's departure). The file is read with each game's files, not once at program start, so a side's archives that hold a different copy would change it between games (claim 13's session lifetime is not modelled).
- With no radar building, the radar box says "No radar" (S-0410).
- The seed of a game comes from the host clock when Start is clicked.
- Natively, the install is read when the window opens and the game is loaded synchronously after the frame that shows "Loading". The window does not respond while it loads (a few seconds in a debug build).

### Performance (M8 C4)

The benchmark (`crates/app/src/game/bench.rs`, our own tooling: nothing in it is game behaviour). It starts a skirmish of our AI houses only on the install's `map`-th offered map, runs `late` ticks straight on the game without drawing, then times frames from fixed views at a fixed size: for each view, `warmup` frames recorded but left out, then `frames` timed ones. The views are found in the game when each starts: **base** (the watched house's densest cluster of buildings), **battle** (the mobile unit with the most units of other player houses in view), **open** (the candidate centre with the fewest objects) and **pan** (a sweep across the map's width through its middle, there and back). Ticks follow the game's own pacer against a virtual 60 frames-a-second clock, so every run and machine ticks on the same frames and draws the same pixels for the same frame; the state hash at the end is printed, and the offscreen host writes a picture of each view's last warmup frame for comparing builds. Maps are known by index and content hash only.

Run lines (DIR is the install folder; nothing of it is copied):

```sh
# Native, in the game window (closes at the end):
cargo run --release -p glasnost-platform-native -- --game --game-dir DIR --bench default
# Native, without a window (also writes one picture per view):
cargo run --release -p glasnost-platform-native -- --game --game-dir DIR --bench default --offscreen
# Firefox: build and serve, open the page at 1920x1080 (a 1080p screen, full screen with F11), click Game folder and pick DIR:
cargo xtask web --release --serve      # then http://127.0.0.1:8000/?mode=game&bench=default
# The browser run, scripted (Playwright; --gpu uses the machine's GPU, without it the browser's software renderer;
# --headed because a headless Firefox may have no WebGL, or only software WebGL):
node web-tests/bench.mjs --install DIR --browser firefox --gpu --headed
```

Check that the summary's `size:` line reads 1920x1080: a display scaled above 100 % can give the page fewer pixels.

The spec is `key=value` words separated by spaces, commas or semicolons (`default` alone means all defaults): `map` (offered-map index, 9), `seed` (7), `players` (AI houses, 2 to 8, 8), `late` (ticks before timing, 9000), `frames` (timed frames per view, 600), `warmup` (60), `views` (`base+battle+open+pan`), `size` (`WxH`, 1920x1080; the window or page uses its own size and the summary says which), `label` (output name; default `native-window`, `native-offscreen` or `web`), `out` (native output folder, `local-lane/m8/c4`), `tick` (`paced` or `every`: a tick every frame), `vsync` (off), `shots` (pictures, on). Outputs: `<label>.txt`, the summary (per view and all: mean, median, p95, p99, worst and frames over 16.7 ms of the frame time; the means of each part; GPU timestamps where the device has them; the renderer's counts), and `<label>.csv`, one row per frame (the host-clock times of the client update, tick, scene, radar, draw list, scene uploads, light alpha, map recording, batch, acquire, encode, submit and present; instances, draw calls, passes, backdrop copies, uploads and bytes; the GPU time). Natively they go to `out`; on the web they are downloads, and the page's `data-glasnost-bench` turns `done` or `failed` with the summary and rows in `data-glasnost-bench-summary` and `-csv` (`web-tests/bench.mjs` reads those).

**Where the time went (this machine, a desktop with a discrete GPU; native offscreen, default spec, 1920x1080; measured on main 63c1df4, before and after on the same game).** Before the fixes every timed frame was over 16.7 ms: the client alone (frame work minus the tick) took 17.6 to 18.4 ms on average. Per frame: the scene build 6.7 ms, the light alpha of shroud and fog (S-0110) 6.3 ms, the HUD's draw list 2.0 ms (most of it a copy of the rules each frame), 2.1 MB of texture uploads, and 6.5 render passes on average (up to 154), one per translucent drawing or spot batch (each needs a copy of the target's pixels below it).

Fixes, each drawing the same pixels (below):

1. The session draws with the rules by reference instead of copying them every frame (draw list 2.0 to 0.1 ms).
2. `mapview::live` keeps the start overlays by the grid's dense cell index and compares each cell in place instead of rebuilding a map of them every frame (most of the scene build's saving, 6.7 to 2.5 ms).
3. `render::shroud::light_alpha` clips each frame's canvas and the view once and runs its rows as slices, and skips frames that change nothing (all-shroud and all-clear frames); the window keeps the light alpha while the covers and the view are unchanged, and `MapRenderer` uploads the alpha texture only when its values change (light alpha 6.3 to 0.5 ms; uploads 2.1 to 0.6 MB a frame).
4. `MapRenderer` takes a blending run's backdrop copy at the last pass break when nothing drawn since then meets the run's rectangle (the pixels there are the same at both moments), so translucent drawings and spot batches apart from each other share one pass break (`map_gpu::passes`). Passes per frame 6.5 to 3.9 on average; submit and present 1.30 to 0.51 ms; GPU time 0.59 to 0.43 ms.

| Run (3 each, load 1.5 to 8.5) | mean | median | p95 | p99 | worst | over 16.7 ms (of 2399) | client mean | client p95 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| before | 28.17 to 29.52 | 19.35 to 20.25 | 98.5 to 104.2 | 149.4 to 171.5 | 296 to 308 | 2399 | 17.61 to 18.39 | 23.2 to 24.7 |
| fixes 1 to 3 | 16.64 to 16.75 | 7.69 to 7.74 | 89.1 to 90.5 | 135.3 to 137.1 | 289 to 373 | 258 to 260 | 5.87 to 5.89 | 11.39 to 11.43 |
| fixes 1 to 4 | 15.81 to 16.66 | 7.25 to 7.73 | 87.9 to 91.7 | 231.6 to 237.7 | 291 to 342 | 201 to 204 | 5.09 to 5.44 | 5.84 to 6.16 |

The client columns are the frame's work minus its tick. On main f74fb61 (after B4) the same benchmark plays the same game (same state hash and pictures); one run there: mean 15.67, median 7.12, p95 88.7, p99 230.6, worst 284.9, 201 over, client mean 4.98 (p95 5.66). The p99 rose with fix 4 because of one span of 15 frames in the battle view (ticks 9960 to 9974), each with about 150 spot batches that overlap one another, so they still need about 147 passes (154 before): there the submit grew from about 34 ms to about 90 ms on every other frame (frames of 44 ms became frames of 100 ms; over budget either way). A run of the same build with the hoisting switched off reproduced the earlier frames one for one, so the change causes it; the time is spent in the driver's submit, not in GPU time (about 3 ms either way). On the software Vulkan adapter (below) the same A/B shows no such span (the submit of those frames is the same with and without the hoisting) and a small gain overall (frame mean 28.2 to 27.7 ms, GPU time 15.5 to 15.2 ms; the run without the hoisting had a load near 10), so the slower submit belongs to this machine's driver and kernel; whether an iGPU's driver behaves like it is open. Fewer passes there needs fewer spot batch breaks, not more hoisting (a seam).

The tick's share did not move (10.6 to 11.1 ms on average over timed frames before and after, the same game: the state hash at the end is the same in every run). What is left over 16.7 ms is almost all ticks: of about 202 such frames, 189 have a tick over 16.7 ms and 13 have the client alone over (62 before fix 4). Late-game ticks are spiky (the AI's planning and pathing; median 2.6 ms, p95 near 88 ms in the base view), and the late run itself averages 18.8 ms a tick natively. That is the simulation's cost and outside this step (no simulation change: `crates/deterministic` and `crates/sim-gate` are untouched).

How drawing was checked unchanged: every view's picture of every run after a fix is byte-identical to the first run before (`cmp`), with the same state hash; the GPU map tests (`crates/render/tests/map_gpu.rs`, against the CPU reference compositor, on the discrete GPU and on lavapipe) and the shroud tests pass, with a new test comparing the clipped `light_alpha` with a plain per-pixel order over clipped and overlapping frames (`clipped_rows_match_the_plain_order`) and one for the pass breaks (`backdrop_copies_share_breaks_only_where_nothing_changed`).

**In the browser (this machine, 300 frames per view, one run each).** Firefox headed with hardware WebGL2, after fixes 1 to 3: frame mean 27.2 ms, median 14.1, p95 136.8, p99 174.3; 336 of 1199 frames over 16.7 ms. After fix 4: mean 26.8, median 13.7, p95 136.1, p99 146.8; 319 over (94 with a tick over 16.7 ms, 7 with the client alone over, 85 whose own work fits but the browser's frame does not, the rest with tick and client together over). The client (work minus tick) is 9.6 ms on average (p95 12.6), against 5.1 natively; ticks are slower than natively (29.8 ms a tick in the late run against 18.8, 16.7 ms on average over timed frames). Chromium headless renders WebGL2 in software (SwiftShader) here: 156 ms a frame, GPU-bound, so it says little.

**A rough iGPU estimate.** This machine has a software Vulkan adapter (lavapipe, `VK_DRIVER_FILES=<its ICD file>`), nothing slower in hardware. On it the GPU timestamps are about 15 ms a frame on average at 1080p (battle 17.9, open 10.5), against 0.43 ms on the discrete GPU; its frame mean is 27.7 ms (median 20.9), with the map's recording at 4.6 ms and the submit at 7.5 ms of CPU, since it renders on the CPU. A current laptop iGPU is perhaps 10 to 15 times slower than this discrete GPU, which would put the map's GPU work near 4 to 7 ms a frame: under the budget, but not by much, and the browser's WebGL2 adds its own cost (here Firefox's submit and present takes 1.6 ms against 0.5 natively). The CPU side is the larger risk: the client costs about 9.6 ms a frame in Firefox on this desktop CPU, and a laptop CPU at a lower clock would take more; ticks already exceed the budget in busy late-game moments on any machine. Trust it little: it is a scaling guess, not a measurement, and only the lead's run on the iGPU machine answers it.

Seams: the late-game tick spikes (simulation; frames over budget in every view); the browser's slower ticks; translucent drawings that overlap still need their own pass; the per-frame redraw (`mapview::live`'s scan of the grid and painting of mobile objects and buildings) is the client's largest remaining part (about 12 % of a sample profile); the benchmark has no zoomed-out view because the client has no zoom.

#### The tick's cost (M8 C4 part 2)

The tick benchmark (`crates/tools/src/tick_bench.rs`, our own tooling: nothing in it is game behaviour) plays the frame benchmark's game (the same setup through `game::bench::start_headless`: map index, seed, AI houses) without a client, timing every `Game::step` with the host clock. With `probe=on` it also passes a probe (`glasnost_sim::probe::Probe`) into the step: the step marks the start of each phase of the fixed tick order (S-0203), each object's turn by kind and each part of each brain's think, and the probe reads the clock at each mark. The simulation never reads the probe: the step's default is the unit probe `()`, whose marks compile to nothing, and the probe's run hashes the same as the plain run (`the_probe_leaves_an_ai_game_unchanged`, and every 500th tick's state hash of the runs below). A probed tick is a few per cent slower, so the plain run gives the times and the probed run the shares.

```sh
# Real data (local lane; DIR is the install folder; nothing of it is copied):
GLASNOST_GAME_DIR=DIR cargo run --release -p glasnost-tools --bin glasnost-headless -- tick-bench map=9 seed=7 players=8 ticks=11399 probe=on
# Synthetic, CI-cheap (the scripted test game; prints its summary and state hash):
cargo run --release -p glasnost-tools --bin glasnost-headless -- tick-bench synthetic 2000 probe=on
```

Words: `map` (9), `seed` (7), `players` (8), `ticks` (9000), `probe` (off), `hash` (a state hash every N ticks, 500), `save` (save and load the game at this tick and go on from the loaded game), `worst` (worst ticks listed, 20), `searches` (off; on lists every path computation, M8 C4 part 5), `out` (`local-lane/m8/c4-2`), `label` (`ticks`). Outputs: `<label>.txt` (mean, median, p95, p99, worst and ticks over 16.7 ms; the same by 1000 ticks with the object count and the update order's length; with the probe the share of each phase and part, the turns a tick by kind and the worst ticks' largest parts), `<label>.csv` (one row per tick) and `<label>-hashes.txt`.

**Where the time went (this machine; map 9, seed 7, 8 AI houses, 11399 ticks: the frame benchmark's 9000 ticks before timing and one tick for each of its 2399 timed frames (the frame benchmark's own end hash differs from this game's, so the two do not play exactly the same ticks); main 8dcc429 with the probe).** Plain run: mean 45.9 ms a tick, median 3.0, p95 355, p99 763, worst 1068; 1524 of 11399 ticks over 16.7 ms. The first 4000 ticks average 4 to 6 ms; from tick 4000 the mean is 55 to 79 ms with spikes of 0.4 to 1.1 s. The spikes fall on two tick classes: the AI's think (tick ≡ 0 mod 15, the main parts' period) and Movement on ticks ≡ 4, 9 and 14 mod 15.

| Part (probe on, share of the probed time) | share | mean µs a tick | worst tick µs |
|---|---|---|---|
| AI think, all houses | 61.5 % | 28 200 | 1 068 000 (the whole tick: eight thinks together) |
| of which `Script::think` (the base and army planner; share in the spanned run below) | 57 % | | |
| of which superweapons, deploys, defence, filters and the rest (same) | 5 % | | |
| Movement (the vehicle group update; almost all of it path computations) | 33.9 % | 15 500 | 712 000 |
| Behaviour, before and after movement | 3.3 % | 1 530 | |
| The object turns themselves (all kinds; 356 turns a tick) | 0.9 % | 430 | |
| Houses, the turn pass, the strike sweep, every other phase | 0.5 % | 190 | |

Inside the think (temporary spans, in a build that is not committed; the spans inflate small calls, so read them as proportions; 11399 ticks): `Script::place_cell` (the spiral search for a building's cell) is 97 % of the planner. It was called 44 195 times, and each call ran up to three passes over the spiral (gap 2, 1, 0: 624 cells, 4224 for a naval building), asking every cell the place order's legality (`placement::place_legal`, 86 M calls), whose adjacency scan (`placement::adjacent`) built a building's whole placement record (`place_type`, 1.2 G calls) for every building it met. Most of the calls came from the tech climb (`Script::climb` → `unblocker` → `placeable`), which asks the same building types' cells again for every army type it looks at, and from `water_goal`; the answers cannot change during a think (the brain reads the world by reference). The free-gap test (`Script::free_gap`, a scan of every object per cell) is 5 % of the placement time.

Inside Movement: 10 977 path computations ran 20 491 cell searches (all but 4 inside a corridor); 12 558 found no path, and the searches expanded 51 M pairs, about 2 500 a search. Per expansion the cost is spread over the neighbour loop: the entry outcome (`path::step_outcome`, about 40 % of the search), the layer and height rules, the step cost's soft-float arithmetic and the pending heap. The object update order (A5's suspect) costs nothing measurable: the turn pass outside the turns is 0.1 %.

**The fixes (the same game, tick for tick).** Each one does the same work in fewer steps and keeps nothing that is saved, hashed or compared:

- `placement::adjacent` reads the two flags it needs of a building's type (`support_flags`) instead of building the type's whole placement record for every building it meets.
- `Script::place_cell` keeps its answer for each type for the rest of a think (`PlaceMemo`, filled between `Script::begin_think` and `Script::end_think`, which `Brain::think_with` calls; `#[serde(skip)]`, empty outside a think, so a loaded game starts with nothing kept). The spiral asks each cell's legality and cell test once across the three gap passes; only the free-gap test is asked per pass.
- During that search the adjacency answer of each cell is kept (`placement::SupportMemo`, read through `place_legal_kept`, which gives `place_legal`'s answer), since neighbouring top-left cells' rectangles overlap.
- A think also keeps `tech::buildable` and `Script::unblocker` by type (and depth), so the tech climb no longer asks the same blocked types again for every army type.
- The path search (S-0212) reads the mover once per search (`occupancy::Mover`: its unit type, whether it crushes, a walker's arms, mission target and master), works out the step cost for b × τ = 1 once per direction, keeps each pending entry's estimate for the stale test, and with `quirks.path_no_reopen` passes over a candidate that already has a record (not the destination) before asking its entry outcome: claim 11 forbids recording it again, so nothing below would use it.

The proof is the same game: every 500th tick's state hash of the 11399-tick game is the same in every run before and after (also through a save and load at tick 6000); the M6c, M7a and M7b sweeps (seeds 1 to 16) and the oracle arena give the same counters and files; the goldens are unchanged and `cargo xtask gate` passes. The numbers below were measured on main 8dcc429; after the map's special flags landed (26f7de4, which changes the game) the fixes were proved again the same way against that main, and one pair of runs there gave the same picture (mean 45.2 ms before, 10.0 after). `scripted::place_cell_tests` compares the kept answers with a script that keeps nothing, on a cramped and an open fixture map.

**After (this machine; the same 11399 ticks, probe off; three interleaved runs each, before and after, on the same four cores; other work on the machine took the load average from 45 down to 4 over the runs, so read the ranges, and the CPU time as the steadiest ratio).**

| Tick time | before (main 8dcc429) | after |
|---|---|---|
| mean ms | 42.4 to 48.6 | 9.2 to 15.8 |
| median ms | 2.7 to 3.6 | 2.7 to 4.5 |
| p95 ms | 340 to 386 | 60 to 109 |
| p99 ms | 728 to 800 | 86 to 151 |
| worst ms | 872 to 1474 | 98 to 167 |
| ticks over 16.7 ms | 1519 to 1534 | 1366 to 1476 |
| user CPU s for the run | 487 to 559 | 110 to 187 (4.4 times less in the quietest pair) |

With the probe after the fixes (mean 12.5 ms): Movement 41 %, the planner (`Script::think`) 20 %, the other brain parts 19 %, Behaviour and the movement steps around it 15 %, everything else 6 % (rounded).

What is still over budget: 1421 of 11399 ticks in the probed run (1524 before). They are of two kinds. Every think tick (tick ≡ 0 mod 15, 760 of them) is still over: the eight houses think in the same tick, a median of 57 ms together (544 before) and at worst 125 ms (1066). And 658 Movement ticks (761 before), mostly on ticks ≡ 4 mod 5: their mean is 23 ms (72 before) and the worst 145 ms (712). The worst ticks are now all Movement, about 135 ms each, every 10 ticks over stretches of about 100 ticks (ticks 6569 to 6649 and 9739 to 9879): the same cost repeating, which looks like the same search being asked again (not looked into).

The frame benchmark (native offscreen, default spec, one run each, before on main 8dcc429 and after): the 9000 late ticks took 37.8 ms a tick before and 8.7 after; the timed frames (all views) went from mean 77.8 ms, median 20.7, p95 399, p99 549, worst 750 to mean 29.7, median 19.4, p95 86, p99 94, worst 167. The tick part of a frame went from 60.8 to 13.8 ms on average. Same state hash at the end, and the four views' pictures are byte-identical. Every timed frame is still over 16.7 ms, before and after, for a reason outside the simulation: the HUD's draw list now takes 9.6 to 10.4 ms a frame, against 0.11 ms in C4 part 1's runs (86416da). That came in between those two commits and is not looked into here.

Seams: the HUD draw list's cost since C4 part 1 (client); the think tick (eight brains in one tick); the repeated late-game Movement cost; the path search's per-step cost (the entry outcome is still most of a search).

#### The HUD draw list (M8 C4 part 3)

**The cause.** The frame benchmark's "draw list (hud)" lap times `Client::frame()`, and that includes the live radar's picture (`mapview::radar::Radar::rgba`, S-0410), which the session composes again on every frame whose tick changed: every benchmark frame ticks. The drawing of the interface itself takes 0.6 to 0.9 ms of it (most of that the psychic intent lines); the rest was the radar's composition, about 5 to 9 ms a frame. Its largest part was finding each pixel's cell (`minimap::cell_at_pixel`, S-0410 F3, whose divisions run in soft float): about 4.5 ms for the whole picture, against about 0.3 ms for the shroud test. Bisecting with the benchmark (`git bisect --first-parent` from C4 part 1's merge ebbd866 to 8dcc429, bad when the draw list is over 2 ms at tick 9000) gives merge d250715 (the M9 rehearsal's lost starts: a deploy order drops a driving vehicle's path; no crate runs for the yard vehicle), whose parent 3562ebc is good (0.18 ms). That merge changes no client code: it changes the game (its goldens move), and in the benchmark's game after it the watched house has radar at tick 9000 (`econ::radar::available`, S-0410 claim 1), so the radar is composed on every timed frame; before it the house had none, and a radar without it is black and composes nothing. So the cost was always there for a house with radar, and the benchmark started to show it.

**The fix.** The composition must still follow the state on every drawn frame (S-0410), so it is made cheaper, not rarer:

- The radar keeps each pixel's cell, worked out once when it is made (`minimap::pixel_cells`: `cell_at_pixel` for every pixel in row order, with the rules' `quirks.radar_pixel_cell_shift`). The cell of a pixel depends only on the grid, the picture's size and that switch, none of which changes during a game; if the switch read at drawing time differs, the radar composes the plain way.
- `minimap::compose_kept` composes with the kept cells. It shares one body with `minimap::compose` (`compose_by`, which takes the pixel-to-cell lookup), so the friendly gap, shroud and terrain rules have one implementation; `compose` passes `cell_at_pixel` itself and its output is the same as before.
- That body asks for an object's colour (`Arrivals::winner`) only at pixels where some object is listed (S-0410 claim 8: only such a pixel can have a winner), instead of at every pixel.

These are in `crates/deterministic/sim/src/econ/minimap.rs` (the radar's pure composition) and `crates/app/src/mapview/radar.rs`. Nothing kept is saved or hashed: the radar is client state, and the change touches no simulation state, no tick phase and no golden (`cargo xtask gate` passes; `crates/sim-gate` is unchanged).

How drawing was checked unchanged: in the A/B below every run plays the same game (the same state hash at the end), and all 24 view pictures (six runs, four views) are byte-identical (`cmp`); each picture includes the sidebar's radar, with terrain, shroud and objects on it. The listed-pixel test cannot change a pixel: `Arrivals::winner` looks only at arrivals listed at the pixel asked, so without one it has no winner. `mapview::radar_tests::radar_compose_matches_the_sim` compares `compose_kept` with `compose` for every pixel on a synthetic game with towers, moving vehicles and shroud, for both settings of `radar_pixel_cell_shift`, for houses 0 and 1 and both settings of the flat-grid shroud quirk, with flashing objects, first without and then with gap generators for both houses (it checks that the gap fields exist and cover each owner's own cells); keeping the cells with the switch flipped makes it fail. The S-0410 claim tests pass unchanged.

**Before and after (this machine, native offscreen, default spec: 4 views of 600 frames at tick 9000; three interleaved runs each, before on main 639ff51 and after; load average 2.4 to 9.3).**

| Frame time, all views (ms) | before | after |
|---|---|---|
| mean | 28.81 to 30.21 | 20.73 to 23.41 |
| median | 18.94 to 19.95 | 10.92 to 11.68 |
| p95 | 83.67 to 87.70 | 75.72 to 80.06 |
| p99 | 85.79 to 90.60 | 77.92 to 122.19 |
| worst | 114.2 to 122.5 | 109.4 to 242.9 |
| frames over 16.7 ms (of 2399) | 2399 | 437 to 659 |
| draw list (hud), mean | 9.54 to 9.85 | 1.51 to 1.72 |

The tick (13.2 to 14.9 ms a frame on average), the scene (3.2 to 3.6) and every other part are the same before and after within the runs' spread. Of the frames still over 16.7 ms, 401 to 410 have a tick over 16.7 ms by itself; the rest (35, 43 and 248, rising with the load) are frames where the tick and the client together pass the budget. The run with the highest load (after 3) has most of those and the worst p99. The "after" binary of these runs was built from the change before it was committed (its summary reads 639ff51 "+dirty"). Proved again after rebasing onto main 9ef3f68, with clean builds of main and the branch, two interleaved runs each (load 3.5 to 12.9): the same state hash as above in all four, the 16 pictures byte-identical, draw list 10.93 to 10.97 → 1.57 to 1.79 ms, frame mean 29.84 to 29.91 → 20.85 to 23.93, median 20.02 to 20.27 → 10.87 to 12.33, frames over 16.7 ms 2399 → 420 to 475.

**In the browser (this machine; Firefox headed in a headless `cage` compositor, hardware WebGL2; 4 views of 300 frames; three interleaved runs each of 639ff51's and ec7d999's `dist/web`; load average 3.3 to 8.5).** Frame mean 52.1 to 53.6 → 36.8 to 38.1 ms, median 34.6 to 35.5 → 19.4 to 20.4, p95 147.4 to 151.9 → 132.2 to 136.5, p99 153.7 to 157.3 → 137.1 to 149.1, worst 195.7 to 206.0 → 170.7 to 186.8; frames over 16.7 ms 1199 → 1137 to 1198 of 1199. The draw list goes from 17.6 to 18.0 ms to 2.5 to 2.6; every other part is unchanged, and all six runs end with the same state hash. Almost every frame is still over budget in the browser: 203 have a tick over 16.7 ms, and in the rest the client alone (work minus tick, about 14 ms on average after the fix, 29 before) with a tick of about 5.7 ms passes it. The client's largest parts there are the scene (5.0 ms), the draw list (2.5), the submit and present (1.8), the map's recording (1.5), the radar's object update (1.45) and the light alpha (1.2). Ticks average 23 ms a timed frame (14.8 ms a tick in the late run against about 8.2 natively).

**Other client costs since part 1.** Against C4 part 1's run (a41bfbc, a different game: 1498 objects at tick 9000 against 1543 now) the parts after the fix are: draw list 0.11 to 1.5 ms (the radar above, which part 1's game never composed), scene 2.43 to 3.23, play 0.09 to 0.13, the radar's object update 0.23 to 0.30, light alpha, map recording and encode the same, submit and present 0.49 to 0.66. None of these is one clear regression: temporary spans in the scene build (not committed) spread its time over the mobile objects' painting (about 0.9 ms), the watched house's own buildings (about 0.9), the overlay scan (about 0.6) and smaller parts, which grow with the game's objects.

Seams: the radar's per-pixel shroud test and the composition that remains (about 0.8 ms a frame); the psychic intent lines (about 0.5 ms of the interface's drawing in this game); the radar's object update (`Arrivals::observe`, which finds each object's position in the update order by a scan, about 0.3 ms); the scene build's mobile and building painting; the client's cost in the browser (about 14 ms a frame here, over budget with any tick).

#### The browser client's frame cost (M8 C4 part 4)

**Where the time went (this machine; Firefox headed in a headless `cage` compositor, hardware WebGL2; the frame benchmark's default spec with 4 views of 300 frames; main bfa4575).** Two runs of the same build were used to find out. One ran under Firefox's sampling profiler (started through its `MOZ_PROFILER_STARTUP` environment variables, 1 ms interval, JS and wasm frames). The other ran with a page script that counts WebGL2 calls, buffer bytes and texture pixels per frame. Neither change is committed: both are our own tooling. The profiler makes frames slower (mean 44.9 ms against 36.8 to 38.1 in part 3's runs), and the counting script makes the submit slower, so the shares come from the profile and the counts from the counting run. The milliseconds below scale each share to the client's 14 ms a frame in part 3's unprofiled runs, so read them as estimates. The profile has 25 304 samples of client work (every timed frame's sample not under `Game::step`); a share is that part's samples over those.

| Client part (Firefox, share of client samples) | share | about ms a frame |
|---|---|---|
| The scene build (`mapview::live::Live::redraw`) | 31.3 % | 4.4 |
| of which the buildings' painting (`Painter::buildings`, almost all `Builder::building_items`) | 9.1 % | 1.3 |
| of which the mobile objects' painting (`Painter::mobiles`) | 8.9 % | 1.2 |
| of which SHP parsing (`fmt::shp::Shp::parse`, again for every sprite drawn: most of it from `Builder::place_sprite`) | 7.1 % | 1.0 |
| of which INI lookups (`ini::Document::value`: animation slots read per frame by `slots::read_anim`, 3.4 %, and art values) | 4.4 % | 0.6 |
| of which the overlay scan, the voxel vehicles and the tick's bookkeeping (`live_overlays`, `vehicle_voxels`, `after_tick`) | 5.4 % | 0.8 |
| The radar's picture (`Radar::rgba` → `minimap::compose_kept`, S-0410; in the draw list) | 10.7 % | 1.5 |
| of which the friendly gap test (`in_friendly_gap`), the objects' colour (`Arrivals::winner`) and the shown cells | 6.0 % | 0.8 |
| The radar's object update (`Arrivals::observe`, each object's place in the update order by a scan) | 9.1 % | 1.3 |
| The map's instance list (`map_gpu::MapRenderer::render_lit`, its own code) | 10.0 % | 1.4 |
| The queue submit (wgpu's GL backend replaying the frame's commands into WebGL2) | 12.5 % | 1.7 |
| of which texture uploads (`texSubImage2D`), buffer uploads (`bufferSubData`), vertex attribute setup and buffer binds, the final blit | 7.4 % | 1.0 |
| The interface's drawing (`Session::draw`) | 6.5 % | 0.9 |
| of which the psychic intent lines (`psychic::intent_lines`: `sensors` again for each unit) | 5.1 % | 0.7 |
| of which the sidebar (`hud::model`, with `sidebar::darkened` 1.1 %) | 1.4 % | 0.2 |
| The covers of shroud and fog (`mapview::cover::covers`, every cell of the grid) | 3.9 % | 0.5 |
| The light alpha (`render::shroud::light_alpha`) | 3.9 % | 0.5 |
| The command encoder's finish, the present, the sound mixer, the benchmark's save | 4.4 % | 0.6 |
| Memory allocation and freeing (`dlmalloc`, spread over the parts above) | 2.4 % | 0.3 |

Per frame, the counting run saw about 12 000 WebGL2 calls: 345 instanced draws, 361 program changes, about 1 720 each of vertex attribute pointer, divisor and enable calls and 1 756 buffer binds (wgpu's GL backend sets up five instance attributes again for every draw, because WebGL2 cannot start a draw at an instance other than 0), about 335 each of depth, stencil and colour mask changes, 87 framebuffer attachment changes, 12 copies of the target and one blit. Uploads were 240 KB of buffer data and 20.7 thousand texture pixels a frame. Nothing is read back.

So the costs that are cheap natively and dear in wasm are work done again every frame on data that does not change during a game: SHP headers parsed again for every sprite, INI values looked up by name again, and the whole grid scanned for covers. Next come the scans in the radar and the psychic lines, which are pure functions in the simulation crate (part 3 changed `minimap` the same way, with the main session's approval). The WebGL2 calls cost about as much as one of the larger client parts, and most of them come from wgpu's GL backend rather than from our draws.

**The fixes.** Each one does less work for the same result; none changes what is drawn.

- **Sprite files** (`mapview::build`): the painter keeps each SHP file's canvas size and frame headers, parsed on first use, and each frame's decoded scene image (`Painter::shp_heads`, `Builder::sprite_frame`). The file's bytes do not change during a game. Asset lookups upper-case the name on the stack (`with_upper`) instead of allocating.
- **Animation fields and type art** (`mapview::build`): an animation type's playback and drawing fields, read from its own art section (S-0007 claim 34), are kept by registration index (`Builder::anim_fields`); a type's art is borrowed from the import (`Builder::type_art`) instead of cloned for every drawing.
- **Covers** (`mapview::cover::covers`, S-0110): only the cells whose canvas can reach the view are asked. A canvas's column depends on *x* − *y* and its row on *x* + *y* and the level (S-0110 F2, S-0101 F1), so the candidates are a band of each, widened by every level a cell can have (none with the flat-grid quirk). Every candidate still takes the full view test, the result is sorted as before, and each cell's explored state is read once.
- **The map's instance list** (`render::map_gpu::MapRenderer::render_lit`): its instance and rectangle buffers are kept between frames, and a stack's first copy is drawn from the item itself rather than from a copy of it.
- **The live map** (`mapview::live`): the overlay comparison walks a list of the grid's legal cells made once, and the map buildings that keep their cell-pass drawing are found through a set rather than a scan per building.
- **The radar and the psychic lines** (`crates/deterministic/sim`, approved by the main session; client-only callers, no tick phase calls them). `minimap::Arrivals::observe` finds each object's place in the update order through a map of each id's first place, the same first match as the scan. `minimap::compose_by` asks `Arrivals::winner`'s test (now `winner_among`) with the arrivals listed at that pixel, grouped once a composition (`Arrivals::by_pixel`), not by scanning all arrivals per pixel. `minimap::in_friendly_gap` returns at once when no gap field exists (S-0410 claim 6), else runs the full test (`in_friendly_gap_cells`). `psychic::intent_lines` finds the viewer's sensors once for all units (`intent_line_with`, S-0610 claims 9 and 10); `intent_line` keeps its meaning.

How drawing was checked unchanged:

- **Natively**: all 24 view pictures of the six runs below are byte-identical (`cmp`), and the 8 pictures of two short runs (60 frames a view, one with part and one with all of the client fixes) match those of a short run of main. Every run ends with the same state hash. The per-frame renderer counts in the CSV (map items, instances, vertices, draw calls, passes, backdrop copies and copied pixels, uploads and their bytes, batch quads, vertices, draws and uploads, rebuilds), with each frame's view, tick and object count, are equal row by row.
- **In Firefox**: all six runs end with the same state hash, and their CSVs' renderer counts are equal row by row in the same way. A canvas screenshot taken by Playwright at the end of each run (our own tooling, not committed) is byte-identical across all six.
- **Tests**: `mapview::cover::tests::covers_of_the_view_band_match_the_full_scan` compares the band with the old full scan (kept in the test) for levels −128 to 127, both settings of the flat-grid quirk, and many view origins and sizes. `econ::minimap::tests::kept_places_and_pixel_lists_match_the_scans` compares the kept places and pixel lists with the old scans, with an id twice in the update order, an id missing from it, a dead id and moving carts over several rounds for both houses. `econ::minimap::tests::friendly_gap_short_cut_matches_the_full_test` checks the gap short cut. `claims_s0610::intent_lines_with_the_sensors_found_once` compares the intent lines with the old one-by-one form for both settings of the visibility quirk, over rounds with a sensor building switched off and with the houses allied. Making the first-match test or the band wrong makes these fail.

**Before and after, natively (this machine; native offscreen; default spec: 4 views of 600 frames at tick 9000; three interleaved runs each of main bfa4575's binary and this branch's at 179fb64, both clean builds; load average 4.7 to 17.9).**

| Frame time, all views (ms) | before | after |
|---|---|---|
| mean | 21.78 to 26.33 | 19.32 to 19.73 |
| median | 11.40 to 12.54 | 8.84 to 9.08 |
| p95 | 79.65 to 93.53 | 78.76 to 81.12 |

| client (work minus tick), mean | 7.64 to 9.15 | 5.13 to 5.23 |

Of the frames still over 16.7 ms after, 203 have a tick over 16.7 ms by itself; in the rest the client (about 8 to 9 ms at the median) and a tick of about 6 to 8 ms pass the budget together. The after run with 551 frames over budget (after 2) ran while the load rose from 6 to 15.6.

**What is left (Firefox profile of this branch's build, same spec; 15 455 client samples; ms scale the shares to the client's 9.3 ms mean).** The queue submit (wgpu's GL backend replaying commands into WebGL2) 19.3 %, about 1.8 ms; the map's instance list (`render_lit`, which walks about 38 700 items a frame, of which about 2 800 are drawn) 13.2 %, about 1.2 ms; the radar's composition (`compose_kept`: the per-pixel shroud and terrain work) 10.7 %, about 1.0 ms; the buildings' painting (`Builder::building_items`: art and rules values, protection masks and the deployed image looked up again for every building every frame) 7.3 %, about 0.7 ms; the light alpha (a 2 MB buffer made and filled each frame) 5.6 %, about 0.5 ms; the mobile objects' painting 4.8 %, about 0.45 ms. SHP parsing and the animation fields' reading are gone from the profile (under 0.1 %); finding a sprite file's kept headers by name (`Painter::shp_heads`) takes 2.2 %, about 0.2 ms; the radar's object update is 2.6 %, the covers 1.3 %, the intent lines 1.0 %.

Seams: the GL backend's per-draw attribute setup (inside wgpu); a spatial index for the map's items; the radar's per-pixel composition; per-type caches for `building_items`' lookups; a cheaper key for the kept sprite headers; reusing the light-alpha buffer. A web-only release profile (`lto = "fat"`) was not tried.

#### The Movement tick (M8 C4 part 5)

The tick benchmark's `searches=on` (our own tooling) switches on the movement trace (`glasnost_sim::trace`, which the simulation never reads) and writes `<label>-searches.csv`: one row per path computation with its tick, unit, kind, owner, start, goal, the mode asked and run, whether the corridor was used, the expansions of each cell search, the result, and for a failed one whether the unit gave up and its retry tick. A run with it on ends with the same state hash as a run without. Lane outputs: `local-lane/m8/c4-5/` (cells and type indexes stay there).

**Where the time went (this machine; the tick benchmark's game, map 9, seed 7, 8 AI houses, 11 399 ticks, probe on; main 0b935c1, after B7's think spread).** Mean 4.40 ms a tick, median 2.15, p95 13.4, p99 18.0, worst 68.0; 143 ticks over 16.7 ms. Movement is 12.3 % of the probed time but has 97 of those 143 ticks; the AI's thinks have 38, the houses' phase 5 (one of 58 ms at tick 7750), the regions' rebuild 2 (about 36 ms) and the crates' phase 1 (about 36 ms).

| Over-budget Movement ticks (97) | ticks | Movement in each | what runs |
|---|---|---|---|
| ticks 4247 to 4939, at a regular interval | 49 | 35 to 44 ms | one vehicle of house 3: a failed computation of 5 corridor searches, about 5 350 expansions each (about 26 700 in all) |
| ticks 6366 to 6716 at a regular interval, and 6789, 6796 | 38 | 25 to 66 ms, rising as more units join | two to five infantry units of house 0 (one type, standing within two cells of each other, the same goal): a failed computation each, of 3 corridor searches of about 5 930 expansions |
| ticks 5087 to 5104 | 3 | 22 ms | another vehicle of house 3, failed, 3 searches |
| the rest (6 ticks) | 6 | 12 to 23 ms | a single found computation of 12 000 to 24 000 expansions, or a group of found ones |

- In the whole game 3 414 computations ran 4.55 M expansions. The 206 that failed (149 by infantry, 57 by vehicles; 134 in house 0) ran 3.62 M of them, 80 %. All used the corridor, and none of their cell searches ran without it; 189 of them ran 3 or 5 cell searches (5 is the limit of S-0212 claim 13). Every unit gave up (claim 23 row 2: its destination cleared, guard queued).
- **Why they are asked again.** Each of these units is in guard with a target it cannot reach. Guard's processing (S-0215 claim 24) runs the approach step (claim 37), which gives the unit a destination towards its target again; the computation fails, the destination is cleared and the target stays (S-0212 claim 23). The repeats come at the guard processing's rate, not at the retry delay. This is the specified behaviour, so the searches stay.
- **The same question, the same world?** 89 % of all expansions fall in (unit, start, goal, mode) keys asked more than once. But of the 188 failed computations asked again with the same key, 170 expanded a different number of pairs in some search than the previous ask (5 933 against 5 932, say): something in the searched area had changed between the two asks, so the world the search read was not the same, and no cache could have served the earlier answer. That leaves 18 that may have read the same world, and showing that they did would need every outcome input of every cell the search touched. A cache kept across ticks is therefore not built.
- **The cost of one expansion.** A stack-sampling profile (our own, not committed: a scratch program timing these computations again and again on the same world, sampled with `eu-stack`) of the vehicle's computation: the entry outcome is 60 % of the time, and most of it (37 % of the whole) is `occupancy::primary_warhead_flag`, which clones the weapon's name, its warhead's name and the whole warhead record for every candidate cell, to read one flag of the mover's own weapon. Of the infantry's: the corridor's exemption test (`corridor::exempt`, S-0212 claim 13: eight neighbours' overlays and object lists) 35 %, asked for every candidate outside the corridor from each neighbour and in each of the 3 searches; the entry outcome 22 %; the pending heap about 10 %; the soft-float step cost about 10 %.

**The fixes.** Each one does less work for the same answers; none changes which searches run, in what order, or what they return. Nothing is kept from one path computation to the next.

- **The mover's weapon, read once** (`occupancy`, S-0209 claims 4 and 5). `primary_warhead_flag`, `primary_hits_ground` and `walker_armed` borrow the rules instead of cloning names and records, and `occupancy::Mover` keeps what the outcomes read of its primary weapon (`Primary`: has one, harms walls, harms wood, hits ground) from the first cell that asks; a mover is read once per cell search.
- **The corridor test before the outcome** (`path::counted_search`, S-0212 claim 7). A candidate other than the destination that lies outside the corridor is passed over before its entry outcome is asked: a blocked outcome and an outside candidate both only skip it, and neither question changes anything. The destination's outcome is still asked first, because a blocked destination ends the search from a neighbour (claim 15).
- **One computation, one world** (`path::corridor_search`, `path::SearchScratch`). The cell searches of one computation with the corridor (S-0212 claim 13: up to 5, with retries) all read the world the computation was asked on, which does not change while it runs; they share the start pair and the destination. So the search scratch keeps, for that computation only, each cell's exemption (`corridor::exempt`), each cell's estimate h, and the entry outcome of each step from a search pair in each direction. `corridor_search` starts a computation stamp before its searches and closes it after; a search outside a computation asks everything afresh. The scratch is not saved or hashed, starts empty on load, and a copy of the world starts with empty scratch (`impl Clone for SearchScratch`).

How the answers were checked unchanged: the search listing (`searches=on`) of the benchmark game is byte-identical before and after (`cmp`), so every computation ran the same cell searches with the same number of expansions each and the same result and steps; the state hashes every 500 ticks are identical, also with a save and load at tick 6000; and the identity runs below. Tests: `claims_s0209::a_mover_read_once_answers_as_a_mover_read_afresh` asks a mover read once and one read afresh about walls, trees, enemy and allied units and an empty cell in every order, for walkers and vehicles whose weapons harm walls, wood or neither, or whose projectile does not hit ground. `claims_s0212::a_computation_keeps_nothing_for_the_next` moves a stone (a second car) round the map between computations, with and without a wall of enemy huts that makes the computation retry and an enemy hut on the destination (claim 15), alternates two destinations, and compares every answer with a copy of the world, whose scratch is empty; a computation stamp that does not move, an outcome slot that is not cleared for a new computation, or an estimate kept across destinations each make it fail.

**The identity runs (main 0b935c1 against this branch; the base's binaries built from 0b935c1's simulation).** The oracle arena (`glasnost-headless arena --record all --threads 8 --out …`) writes byte-identical `ours-M-0001.csv` to `ours-M-0003.csv` on both (M-0001 31 of 96 pairings failing, 152 of 172 stock outcomes reproduced; M-0002 13 of 24, 43 of 47; M-0003 2 of 35, 61 of 64). The M6c, M7a and M7b seed sweeps 1 to 16 print the same output line for line (smallest minimum M6c 4, M7a 1, M7b 3, the drain orders). B4's 32-seed default margins sweep writes a byte-identical CSV. The goldens are unchanged and `cargo xtask gate` passes; the save and AI formats are unchanged. All of it was run again after rebasing on main 23d450b (whose own commits change no output of these runs): the search listing, the hashes with and without the save, the sweeps, the margins and the arena outputs are again identical on both sides, and the gate passes.

**The search's own time (the scratch program again, the same computations on the same world, ms a round; main, then each fix in turn).**

| Computations | main | weapon read once | + corridor test first, exemption kept | + outcomes kept | + estimates kept |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| the four infantry units of the large ticks (3 corridor searches each) | 49.6 | 50.5 | 30.7 | 26.7 | 24.5 |
| the vehicle of the regular ticks (5 corridor searches) | 34.9 | 20.2 | 13.2 | 10.5 | 8.8 |
| every unit with a goal at that tick (mostly found at once) | 2.97 | 2.71 | 2.57 | 2.56 | 2.82 (noise) |

**Before and after (this machine; the tick benchmark's game as above; main 0b935c1's binary and this branch's on it, interleaved, pinned to the same four cores; load average 8.5 to 17.2, other sessions' runs on the machine).** The Movement phase's own time, which the load moves little, is the measure; the whole tick's figures move with the load.

| Movement (probe on) | before | after |
|---|---|---|
| share of the probed time | 12.3 % (load 2.6 to 3.8), 12.5 % | 7.4 % |
| mean a tick | 539 µs, 612 µs | 329 µs, 332 µs |
| worst tick's Movement | 65.8 ms, 73.4 ms | 37.5 ms, 33.4 ms |
| ticks whose Movement alone is over 16.7 ms | 95 | 21 |

| Whole tick, probe off (three pairs, run in the order A B A B A B) | before | after |
|---|---|---|
