The third *after* run (412 ticks over, median 2.75) ran while the load rose from 8.5 to 12.3; a fourth pair, under a load of 8.9 to 17.2, gave 318 before and 354 after. Under the first two pairs' load (9.0 to 11.4) the after runs had 134 and 154 ticks over against 180 and 181. All runs end with the same state hash.

The vehicle's regular ticks (4247 to 4939, 38 to 47 ms of Movement before) are no longer over budget, nor the other vehicle's (5087 to 5104), the infantry group's earlier ticks (6366 to 6506, 26 to 34 ms) or the six scattered ticks of 18 to 25 ms later in the game. All 21 left are the infantry group's ticks between 6516 and 6716, at 19.5 to 37.5 ms of Movement (42 to 73 ms before): four or five units' failed computations of 3 corridor searches each in one tick still cost more than a frame.

**The native game benchmark** (`--game --bench default --offscreen`, three interleaved runs each; load 4.2 to 18.4). All frames, before: mean 7.83 to 7.86 ms, median 5.94 to 6.00, p95 18.2 to 18.6, p99 20.9 to 22.0, worst 47.0 to 47.2, 179 to 186 frames over 16.7 ms of 2399. After: mean 7.75 to 9.24, median 5.99 to 7.23, p95 18.3 to 20.2, p99 20.7 to 23.3, worst 47.9 to 54.2, 177 to 239. The third after run's load rose from 4.2 to 9.3; the first two match the before runs. The benchmark times frames from tick 9000, after the Movement spikes, so its frames were not expected to move. Its 9000 untimed ticks ran in 41.9 to 42.6 s before and 39.5 to 41.2 s after. Every view's picture is byte-identical before and after, and every run ends with the same state hash.

Seams: the infantry group's ticks (soft-float step costs and the pending heap are now the larger parts of a search; spreading one tick's failed computations over several ticks would change behaviour and needs a spec); the AI's think ticks (the largest group of over-budget ticks after this step); the houses' phase (57 to 61 ms, five ticks a game), the regions' rebuilds (40 to 46 ms, twice) and the crates' phase (about 38 ms, once).

#### The planner and the house-wide spikes (M8 C4 part 6)

The think's own parts in the probe: `Script::think_with` (our own tooling) marks seven parts of the scripted plan, `ai:script:watch` (the income watch, selling when broke, the field memory), `ai:script:deploy`, `ai:script:buildings` (the done building's placement and the next buildings), `ai:script:harvesters`, `ai:script:army_types`, `ai:script:army_units` and `ai:script:waves`; the brain passes its probe on. `ai:script` itself now covers only the time before the first mark (and the done building's placement on the ticks between thinks). Like every mark, they read nothing and change nothing: the run hashes the same with and without them. Lane outputs: `local-lane/m8/c4-6/`.

**Where the time went (this machine; the tick benchmark's game, map 9, seed 7, 8 AI houses, 11 399 ticks, probe on; main a32dbdc with the marks only; load average 12.9 to 10.6).** Mean 4.47 ms a tick, median 2.38, p95 12.9, p99 16.8, worst 62.9; 121 ticks over 16.7 ms. The map has 32 604 legal cells (a coordinate square of 65 536), about 1 500 objects, 967 regions and 8 942 / 3 270 / 1 677 sectors at the corridor's three levels.

| Over-budget ticks (121), by their largest part | ticks | that part in each | what runs |
|---|---|---|---|
| `ai:script:buildings` | 96 (95 of them house 0's thinks, phase 0) | 6.0 to 10.8 ms, mean 7.9; with `ai:script:watch` 1.9 ms, `ai:build_superweapon` 1.9, `ai:plant_first` 1.0, `ai:expand` 0.8 | the placement spiral searches of `Script::search_place_cell`, below |
| Movement | 18 | 7.2 to 25.7 ms | path search (C4 part 5's infantry group); not this step's |
| Houses | 4 (ticks 1315, 2382, 3711, 4603) | 54.9 to 56.4 ms | a house's defeat: the whole-map reveal and its discovery, below |
| Crates | 1 (tick 5306) | 39.8 ms | a crate's random placement asks for the regions, which a category change has made out of date: their rebuild, below |
| Regions | 1 (tick 4754) | 37.9 ms | the regions' rebuild after a category change (S-0212 claim 5), below |
| `ai:script:watch` | 1 (tick 8493) | 4.5 ms, with 4.5 ms of buildings | the income watch's first ask of every type's buildability (below) |

- **The think's buildings part.** A stack-sampling profile (our own, not committed: a scratch program playing the benchmark game to a tick and asking one brain's think again and again on the same world, sampled with `eu-stack`) of house 0's think at tick 7095 (17.3 ms, no orders; 97 samples): 59 % in the placement spiral search, `search_place_cell`. 34 % is the naval yard's, in the buildings part: `water_goal` asks whether a naval yard can be placed, which searches the wide spiral (32 rings, about 4 200 cells) at three gaps and finds no cell, so the whole search runs in every think. 25 % is the superweapon's (`ai:build_superweapon`) and the next building's (`ai:plant_first`). Within a search, most of the time is the adjacency test (S-0406 claim 4: for each cell, every building of the house and its allies is asked whether it lies within reach), asked before the cell test even where the cell test (claim 3: a naval yard on land) fails at once; then the free-gap test (`free_gap`), which walks every object of the world for every cell and gap. The rest: `sell_when_broke`'s first ask of every type's buildability (21 %, kept for the think and read by the later parts), the tech climb's unblocker search (about 5 %) and `far_field`'s scan of the fields (about 5 %).
- **The houses' spikes.** A house is defeated (S-0409 claim 15): the explored-only whole-map reveal (S1 row 10) explores the 30 000 or so cells it had not, then the discovery (S-0409 Terms, *discovered by H*) compares, for each of those cells, the cover-piece keys (S-0110 claims 1 and 2) of its nine cells before and after. Each key reads nine cells' explored state and asks for each whether the reveal explored it, in an ordered set of the 30 000 cells. A cell is asked again from each of its explored neighbours, so about 280 000 cells are compared, each twice, and the set is asked about 5 million times. That loop and the set's building are nearly all of the phase.
- **The regions' and the crates' spikes.** The same rebuild, `regions::rebuild` with the corridor's `corridor::build` (S-0212 claims 3, 4, 5 and 13b): the region labels go through an ordered map keyed by union-find root, and for each of the three corridor levels the sector labels through another and every connection pair through a third ordered map (`(sector, sector)` → straight), entered once for each neighbouring cell pair across sectors (about 68 000 connections at level 0). The map entries are about half of the rebuild.

**The fixes.** Each one does less work for the same answers: no answer, order or tick changes, and nothing is kept from one think or tick to the next (no cache crosses a tick; C4 part 2's per-think `PlaceMemo` and `Kept` are unchanged, its adjacency memo is replaced, below).

- **One placement search a type, the cell test first** (`placement::PlaceSearch`, `Script::search_place_cell`; S-0406 claims 3 to 6, 8 and 12). A search reads the type's placement fields once and, for a building, counts the cells that support adjacency (claim 4: the house's and its allies' buildings, the S-0406 F1 rectangle around the foundation less the foundation) with running sums over the spiral's window widened by the adjacency reach, so that a cell's answer is two rectangle counts (the rectangle around the foundation has a supporting cell the foundation does not); a cell outside the window, an upgrade, or a reach over `TABLE_MARGIN` (64 cells, our own bound) is asked the old way. The cell test is asked before the place order's conditions: both only read the world, so either order gives the same answer, and a naval yard's spiral is mostly land, where the cell test fails at once. The free-gap test reads the objects the house knows (its own, and those on cells it has explored), listed once for the search (`Script::known_objects`, `free_among`), not every object for every cell and gap. `cell_test_with`, `explored_with` and `adjacent_by` ask without building lists. `SupportMemo` and `place_legal_kept` (C4 part 2's adjacency memo) are gone.
- **A reveal's discovery compares each cell once** (`shroud::discover`; S-0409 Terms, S-0110 claims 1 and 2). A cell's two cover-piece keys, before and after the reveal, do not depend on which flipped neighbour asks, so each cell is compared once and lists once among the changed cells; the discovered objects are a set, so the order of the changed cells does not matter. A reveal of more than `DENSE_REVEAL` (256, our own bound) cells keeps its cells and the compared cells in grid-sized tables, a smaller one (a unit's sight) in sets, so a sight's reveal does not clear a table of the whole grid. `explored_only_reveal` builds its set of flipped cells at once from the list.
- **The regions' and sectors' rebuild without ordered maps** (`regions::rebuild`, `corridor::build`; S-0212 claims 3, 4 and 13b). The region and sector labels go through a table by union-find root (a root's label is still the count of roots met before it, in the same cell order); the connection pairs go into one list, bucketed by sector and each bucket sorted and merged alone (`merge_met`: one connection per other sector, ordered by it, straight when any of its pairs was); each cell's level, usable-area answer and neighbours are read once for the three levels; `block_of` shifts, the block size being a power of two.

Tests: `place_cell_tests::the_placement_search_answers_every_cell_as_the_place_order_and_the_cell_test` asks a search for every house and building type about every cell of four synthetic maps (a roomy base, a cramped one, a sea along one side, a strait), inside its window and outside, at three points of a game, against the place order and the cell test asked afresh; `the_kept_and_once_asked_placement_answers_match_the_three_passes` now also plays the sea and the strait and checks the free gap against the test as it was. `shroud::discover_tests::small_and_whole_map_reveals_discover_as_before` runs a small reveal (sets) and a whole-map one (tables) over a patchy explored state against the discovery as it was. `corridor::build_tests::the_sectors_are_built_as_before` compares every level's sectors, parents, categories, blocks and connections with the build as it was over a map of mixed categories and levels (it has no bridge links; the links' pairs go through the same merge).

**The identity runs (main d4a79ba against this branch; the base's binaries built from d4a79ba).** The bench game's state hashes every 500 ticks are identical in every run: main's five (three plain, one with the probe, one with a save and load at tick 6000, whose hashes equal the plain runs'), the branch's six (the same, with a second probe run) and the marks-only build's one; every run ends with the same hash. The oracle arena (`glasnost-headless arena --record all --threads 8 --out …`) writes byte-identical `ours-M-0001.csv` to `ours-M-0003.csv` and oracle reports on both (M-0001 31 of 96 pairings failing, 151 of 172 stock outcomes reproduced; M-0002 13 of 24, 43 of 47; M-0003 2 of 35, 61 of 64). The M6c, M7a and M7b seed sweeps 1 to 16 print the same output line for line. B4's 32-seed default margins sweep writes a byte-identical CSV. B6's quick lane (`GLASNOST_AI_QUICK=1`, seeds 7 to 14, maps 8, 2 and 0, Yuri (house 1) against Allied and against Soviet: 48 games) prints the same output for every game but its timings: the same tick counts and winners. The goldens are unchanged (`git diff main -- crates/sim-gate/golden` is empty) and `cargo xtask gate` passes; the save and AI game formats are unchanged. On the earlier main a32dbdc the hashes, the sweeps, the arena and the quick lane were identical too.

**Before and after (this machine; the tick benchmark's game as above; main d4a79ba's binary and this branch's, interleaved A B A B A B, pinned to the same four cores; load average 11.3 to 19.9, other sessions' builds and runs on the machine).**

Seams: Movement (C4 part 5's infantry group, not this step's); the regions' rebuild (17 to 18 ms, once a game) and the crates' phase, which asks for the same rebuild (15 to 19 ms, once): the union-find and the corridor's three level passes are what is left, and keeping the regions up to date as categories change instead of rebuilding them would be a larger change; in the think, `sell_when_broke`'s first ask of every type's buildability (the watch part's 5 to 8 ms on its worst ticks).

## Electric bolts (M8 step C5 part 1)

S-0316 revision 27 (answers Q-1701). Ledger [docs/m8-claims/c5-S-0316.md](m8-claims/c5-S-0316.md).

- **Play.** `combat::fire::shot` makes a bolt after the shot's line report when the shot put its projectile on the map and the weapon has the electric-bolt flag without the laser flag (`combat::effects::makes_bolt`). `combat::effects::bolt` reports `Effect::Bolt` (firer, weapon, slot, burst counter, the fire point of that slot and counter, the bolt end from `combat::range::target_point` of the shot's own, possibly redirected, target, a building firer's position, whether the firer is a vehicle on the map) and then makes one system of the game-wide spark type at the bolt end (`emitters::electric_bolt_sparks`, the S-0616 seam, now filled). The bolt flag also stops a magnetic-beam weapon's wave (claim 2). The bolt itself takes no scenario draw; the spark system takes S-0616's. No new hashed state: save format 70 and AI game format 31 stay.
- **Import.** `IsElectricBolt`, `IsAlternateColor`, `IsLaser` and `IsRadBeam` are weapon booleans in `sim_combat::WEAPON_COMBAT` (absent no); `WeaponRules` gains `electric_bolt`, `alt_colour`, `laser`, `rad_beam`. The absent-`DefaultSparkSystem` warning names electric-bolt sparks.
- **Drawing.** `app::mapview::bolts` holds S-0316's shape (F1 to F3), depth terms (F4), colours (general palette row 26: B and C entry 10 or 5, A entry 15) and pixels (S-0514 claim 25's flash pixels, single-pixel lines). `beams::Lines` keeps the bolts: `take_bolt` draws the phase from the cosmetic stream and decides attachment, `after_tick` drops bolts after their 17th frame and moves attached starts to the firer's current fire point (the attachment ends when the firer leaves `World::objects`), `bolt_spots` draws them newest first and skips, with no draws, one whose straight screen line misses the battlefield view (`Live::battlefield`, set by the session before each drawing; `None` counts as in view). `Live::draw_lines` pushes lasers, bolts, then beam flashes.
- **Interim rules.** One drawn frame per tick (frame *n* is the bolt's age in ticks; a second redraw within a tick redraws frame *n* with new draws). Interior pixels are the beam flash's nearest-integer line, with the piece's leftmost end's unclipped bias. The bolt draws come from the view's cosmetic stream, which sound does not share, so claim 13's orders against the sound draws hold trivially.
- **Shrapnel fragments.** Claim 8's fragment bolts come since M8 A3 intake 6: S-0318 bursts a detonation into fragments, and an electric fragment calls `combat::effects::bolt` (`claims_s0318::sh1m_bolts`).
- **Checks.** The sim-gate goldens did not move (synthetic data has no electric-bolt weapon), and the M6c, M7a and M7b seed sweeps (seeds 1 to 16) print the same counters before and after. Oracle arena: M-0001 33 of 96 pairings failing before and after, side-wise unanimous stock outcomes reproduced 151 of 172 before and 150 after (one pairing against the stock electric-bolt tank goes from 12 draws to 2 row wins and 10 draws; attributed, not isolated, to the bolt's spark systems taking scenario draws, S-0616 claim 2); M-0002 13 of 24 and 43 of 47, M-0003 3 of 35 and 61 of 64, unchanged. Lane (`m7b_lane` scenes `bolt-vehicle`, `bolt-infantry`, `bolt-building`; pictures in `local-lane/m8/c5/`): ten stock weapons carry the flag; a stock vehicle and a stock infantry type with one fire at an enemy vehicle, each bolt drawn with 225 to 436 pixels per frame and one spark system at its end, the vehicle's bolt attached; the stock building type, given a power plant beside it and a target, fires at tick 48, its bolt drawn with 530 to 559 pixels per frame and one spark system at its end (without the power plant it is not in operation and never fires).

## Debris pieces with voxel models (M8 step C5 part 2)

S-0317 revision 34 (answers Q-1702), revision 57 since M8 A3 intake 32 (answers Q-1834 to Q-1836).
Ledgers [docs/m8-claims/c5-S-0317.md](m8-claims/c5-S-0317.md) and
[docs/m8-claims/intake32-S-0317.md](m8-claims/intake32-S-0317.md).

- **Play.** `glasnost_sim::pieces` holds the pieces in `World::pieces`. A piece is game state:
  saved, hashed and entered in the update order.
  - **Creation.** `pieces::create` takes claims 3 and 4's draws. It makes the attached system
    directly after the piece (`emitters::voxel_animation_system`, the S-0616 seam, now filled) and
    reports `Effect::PieceSound`.
  - **Turns.** `pieces::turn` runs from `tick::object_turn`: the count-down, the trailer on even
    ticks (F7), the flight with its deck, ground, building and cliff contacts and the rebound, the
    stop test (F6), and the bounce animation and one-cell hits of a ground impact.
  - **End.** Claim 11's rows, claim 12's further pieces and new resource, then the removal and
    the marking of the system.
  - **Producers.** A death (`combat::death::deal_debris`, with the object's house), a warhead's
    debris and an exploding overlay's barrel debris (no house) all make pieces through
    `pieces::create_named`.
  - **Contacts (r57).** F8's rebound off the cell's slope (claim 17), F9's cliff turn (claim 18),
    claim 19's building cells, claim 20's off-map cell, no placement at (0, 0, 0) (claim 21).
  - **Pose (r57).** `Piece::turns` and `Piece::reversed` keep claim 22's net turn count.
  - **Formats.** Save format 72, AI game format 33; 82 and 44 since intake 32.
- **Switches.** New: `quirks.voxel_debris_deck_impact_layer`, `voxel_debris_deck_over_water`,
  `voxel_debris_trailer_height` and `voxel_debris_expiry_needs_animation`; at intake 32
  `voxel_debris_deck_rebound_slope` and `voxel_debris_cliff_step_faults`. Wired:
  `chop_rounding`, `random_magnitude_overflow`, `update_order_removal_skip` and
  `table_root_distance`.
- **Import.** `convert::sim_pieces` reads the voxel-animation sections, and warns for zero spans,
  SpawnCount 2147483647, a resource flag without a resource overlay at 127, and an empty splash
  list; since intake 32 also for MaxXYVel 256 or more (claim 18) and a VoxelIndex outside the own
  model (claim 23). `art::voxel_animation` gives each type its own model I ‖ .vxl with no motion
  file (S-0007 claim 30).
- **Sound.** The start sound plays at the creation point and its anchor follows the piece
  (`glasnost_audio::events`, `Effect::PieceSound`).
- **Drawing.** `mapview::live` puts each piece in the air layer (S-0102), and
  `build::live_piece` draws claims 22 to 28: the VoxelIndex section turned by
  `voxel::piece_rotation` (no view), lit by F16's diffuse term, its F11 shadow
  (`voxel::draw_piece_shadow`) first, claim 25's palettes, claim 26's depth.
- **Image slots (design).** A pose is drawn afresh, so each piece holds one body and one shadow
  image, rewritten in place (`mapview::piece_pool`, `Scene::replace_image`, re-uploaded by
  `MapRenderer::sync`); a piece leaving play frees its slots. No interims remain.
- **Seams.**
  - S-0007 claim 30's shared part (a share flag draws nothing and warns), and with it
    `quirks.voxel_anim_share_afresh`.
  - The warhead's light flash at a piece's end, which is drawing only.
- **Sprite debris.** The sprite path (bouncing animations) was unchanged here. It is built at M8
  step A3 intake 15 part 1 (S-0319 r9, Q-1762 answered in part; section *Bouncing animations*
  below). Play notes 6, 10, 12 and 13: see [m8-claims/a4-play-notes.md](m8-claims/a4-play-notes.md).

## Music (M8 step C1 part 1)

S-0703 revision 34 (music playback; a partial answer to Q-1700), and revision 51 at M8 step A3 intake 12 (the start of music, the end screen, the options menu, files that do not play and the settings' defaults; answers Q-1831 to Q-1833, and Q-1830 in part). Ledgers [docs/m8-claims/c1-S-0703.md](m8-claims/c1-S-0703.md) and [docs/m8-claims/intake12-S-0703-S-0701.md](m8-claims/intake12-S-0703-S-0701.md). Music is presentation: it reads nothing of the game but the local player's side at a game's start, and its shuffle draws take the client's unsynchronised stream (S-0202 claim 11). `music::tests::state_hash_with_music` (`crates/audio/src/music_tests.rs`) and the lane show the state hash is the same with and without music.

- **Files (`glasnost_fmt::wav`).** `WavInfo::parse` reads a WAVE header up to the data chunk: 8-bit and 16-bit PCM and IMA ADPCM (format 0x11, mono or stereo, any block size), with the `fact` frame count capping IMA's frames. `frames(file_len)` counts from the data present (a file cut short ends where its data ends, and data beyond the header's size is not played, claim 8a); `decode_chunk` decodes one IMA block or 4096 PCM frames, so a track is decoded as it plays. `ima_wav` writes our own IMA test files; the synthetic fixtures `wav/tone-*` come from `glasnost-fixtures`. `Pcm::from_wav` uses the same reader.
- **Database and rule (`glasnost_audio::music`).** `MusicDatabase::from_ini` reads `[Themes]` and each entry's section (Import mapping: cut to 31, 255 and 127 bytes, leading `$` and `#` dropped from `Sound`, `.wav` added, an identifier listed again in any case kept at its first listing, empty values no entry). `availability` gives claim 2 (file present, `Normal`, side restriction), `side_warnings` the Edge cases' warning. `next_track` is the Formulas' pick (repeat, list order, shuffle with 1000 ranged draws, the fallback), with `MusicQuirks::fallback_entry_zero` for `quirks.music_fallback_entry_zero` (default on). The switch is kept in the audio crate, not in the rules' `Quirks`, so the simulation crates and the gate's rules hash do not change; it has no configuration of its own yet.
- **Player (`Music`).** One music update at the end of every audio pass (`Mixer::service`'s pass), after that pass's sound choices (claim 10). A track completes at the first pass with C ≥ C0 + T, T from S-0701 claim 30 with p = 100, counted from the pass at which the pick started it, also when its file is read later (the web). A failed start (claim 8a: the file cannot be read, or `music::playable` finds no RIFF WAVE file with its format chunk before its data chunk, format 1 or 17 and sample data) is silent and the following pass picks again; it stays the ended track (claim 4). A found file that does not parse stays present and available, with an importer warning naming the entry (`VfsTracks::warnings`, read once at install). Phases (`music::Phase`): `Loading` (the loading track `LOADING` started directly, no pick), `Armed` (the game started, no music update yet), `Started` (from the first music update: the map theme's entry started directly, else an ordinary pick with no ended track, claim 11), `Score` (the score track `SCORE` started directly; when it finishes, it starts again under the repeat setting or its own flag, otherwise silence; a failed start is not tried again, claim 12), `Menu` (the menu track `INTRO`, played once) and `Off` (the in-game music setting off). Direct starts make no ranged draw. `MusicVoice` streams the decoded track to the mixer's music voice, resampled to the output rate by linear interpolation like the other voices.
- **Install (`music_files`, `game::install`).** `InstallMusic::read` reads `thememd.ini` through the game's search order (S-0005 Import mapping), the settings file `ra2md.ini` as a loose file only (`Vfs::loose_only`, S-0005 claim 5; the native client now opens that one loose file too) and finds each entry's file once (`VfsTracks::find`, claim 8: presence does not change after startup). A missing music database gives no music, where S-0005 claim 16 has the original refuse to start (the sound and speech databases are already read the same way). The side warnings are given when the install is read, against the rules data's sides, and at each game start against that game's sides (`Music::start_game`).
- **Client.** A game started from the menus (skirmish or network) stops whatever plays at once and starts the loading track (`Client::loading_music`, claim 11). At the game's start `Client::start_audio` sets the game's sides (from the import with the game's layers), the map theme (`[Basic]` `Theme`, read in the app layer into `Loaded::theme`, cut to 127 bytes and matched ignoring case) and a new `Music` before `Audio::start`, which stops the loading track; the first start is made at the music update of the game's first audio pass. Audio passes, music updates and picks go on through the end delay. The end screen opens at the end of the cycle that runs its final tick, after that cycle's pass, and `Audio::game_over_music` stops the track and starts the score track; on the end screen the score track is timed by the output (`Audio::menu_frame`, no audio pass, claim 12). Leaving the end screen, or leaving through the options menu, starts the menu track (`Audio::main_menu_music`, claim 13). Since intake 25 (S-0701 r35 claim 30's *Music time*, S-0703 r58 claim 13) the music plays on in a skirmish's options menu and on our own settings screen opened from the game (`Audio::frame_in` with `Stand::Menu`): sound stands still there (no audio pass, `Mixer::hold_sounds`), music time *M* runs on the host clock's readings, and a music-only pass at each reading with *M* − *K* > 33 ms runs the music update, so a track that ends in the menu is followed by its pick there. Lost focus in a skirmish (`Stand::Unfocused`), menu open or not, holds sound and music (`Mixer::hold_music`), and the unfocused stretch adds nothing to *M* (`stand::HeldClock`). A network game stands still for neither (S-0201 claims 15 and 16), so its audio stays play. A surrender leaves the music as in play until the game is over. The music volume of our settings (`music`, 0 to 100) scales the music voice linearly (`music::music_gain`, our own UI; S-0703 leaves the scaling open); music skips the master level and the sound option.
- **Design choices (Edge cases; the lead accepted both on 2026-10-23).** Picks at zero music volume: tracks are picked and started at every volume, silently at zero (`Music::update` never reads the volume). Side restriction matched by name: the local player's side name, ignoring case (`MusicDatabase::availability`). Neither has a switch.
- **Departure (Edge cases; accepted by the lead, revision 58; no switch).** The end screen when the game is over (the original plays on through its real-time wait for the result announcement, with picks). Revision 51's other departure, the music held in the options menu, is gone: the music plays on there as in the original (intake 25).
- **Settings.** `MusicSettings::from_ini` reads `IsScoreShuffle`, `IsScoreRepeat` and `InGameMusic` (S-0003 claim 2's booleans); a missing file or key, or a value it does not recognise, keeps the default: shuffle and repeat off, in-game music on (Import mapping). With in-game music off no track is picked or started during a game; the loading and score tracks still play. Q-1830 to Q-1833 are answered by revision 51 and their interims are retired.
- **Web.** The music share (`game::music_share`, our own design, PLAN.md §5): each track read goes through a copy kept in the origin-private file system (folder `glasnost-music`, `manifest.txt` written last), keyed by the file's name, length and a hash of its first 64 KiB in the picked file. `persist()` is asked for once. Refused, full or evicted storage, or a copy that does not read back whole, falls back to the picked file with a log note. Until the rest of the install is kept between visits (Stage B), the folder is still picked each visit and the share only saves the track reads. Reads in the browser finish a few frames after the pick (`Load::Pending`).
- **Seams.** No in-game music controls (next, previous, stop): our own UI, not built. S-0701 claim 30's logical cycle-end clock is not built: passes run on host time, with the stand-stills left out of it (the sound clock leaves out the options menu and lost focus, music time only lost focus). The host clock is read in whole milliseconds, so the fraction carry of *Music time* is the host count's own. Natively the loading track is not heard, since the start job blocks the frame loop until the game is loaded. How the menu track goes on after it ends is open (Q-1700 question 7): it plays once. PCM other than 8 and 16 bits is a failed start in our engine, where claim 8a counts every format code 1 file as playable. Our engine offers no restart. Natively a track's whole file is read in the frame that starts it (`VfsTracks::open`): the lane's track-start updates took up to 62 ms with the install in the page cache, against 15 ms for the slowest other update (up to about 280 ms on a cold first read); a read in the background or a streamed read would remove the hitch. The track's display name (`Name`) and the campaign's `Scenario` are not read (Q-1700 question 4, campaigns). On the web a copy in the share can serve a track whose picked file went after startup (claim 8 partly).
- **Lane.** `crates/app/tests/c1_lane.rs` (ignored, `GLASNOST_GAME_DIR`): a skirmish through the windowless client with the settings forced to list order, 700 updates a second apart; the tracks start in list order over the available entries, each at the first pass at or after the last one's logical end, and the state hash equals that of the same game without music. Counts and timings only (`local-lane/m8/c1/music.txt`).

## Settings (M8 step C2)

Our own settings screen (`glasnost_ui::settings`, driven by `game/client_settings.rs`), reached from the main menu ("Settings", no install needed) and from the in-game options menu (not in a network game, whose lockstep must keep being pumped; Escape or Back returns to the paused game). Settings are presentation and input choices of the person at this machine. They live in `glasnost-app` (`settings.rs`), never reach the simulation crates (`xtask deps` and the determinism lint pass) and never change a state hash (`client::tests::state_hash_is_the_same_under_any_settings` plays one game under default and under extreme settings and compares every tick's hash).

What is stored (all fields optional on load):

| Setting | Key | Range, default | Used by |
|---|---|---|---|
| Sound-effects volume | `sound` | 0 to 100 percent, 100 (S-0701 F3, option at full) | `Mixer::set_volume`, level = percent x 16384 / 100 |
| Voice volume | `voices` | 0 to 100, 100 (S-0701 claim 35) | `Mixer::set_voice_volume`, scales the announcement voice (new) |
| Music volume | `music` | 0 to 100, 100 | `Mixer::set_music_volume`, scales the music voice linearly (step C1, "Music (M8 step C1 part 1)") |
| Scroll speed | `scroll` | index 0 fastest to 6 slowest, 3 (S-0101 Terms and Import mapping) | `Session::scroll_index`, replacing the fixed default in the edge, drag and arrow scroll |
| Display scale | `scale` | 100, 125, 150, 200, 300 percent, 100 (our own) | the interface scale of step C3 (see "Accessibility (M8 step C3)") |
| Command keys | `key.stop`, `key.scatter`, `key.guard`, `key.deploy`, `key.cheer` | a lower-case letter or digit each, S X G D C (S-0213 claim 22) | `client::bound_with` |
| Relay address | `relay` | text, empty | the multiplayer screen's field, set from it when a connection is tried; the command line's `--relay` still wins for a run |
| Game to rejoin | `rejoin` | `CODE SLOT TICKET RELAY` (the ticket as 32 hex digits), none | the multiplayer screen's **Reconnect** (M9 N5): written when a network game begins, removed when it ends or the person leaves it; a wrong line is ignored |

Fixed keys, listed on the screen and not rebindable: Escape (options menu) and the arrows (scroll), S-0213 claim 23. Rebinding: click a key button, press the new key; a key another action has is refused with a note and the button keeps waiting (no swapping); Escape cancels; "Reset to defaults" restores everything except the relay address and the game to rejoin. A file with clashing or non-alphanumeric keys loads the default keys together (the other fields still load). The scroll speed is shown as "n of 7" with 7 fastest. The scroll speed's table and index range follow S-0101 F9; the screen is our own.

Format: UTF-8 text, one `key=value` per line, first line `version=1`. A field that is missing (an older file), unknown (a newer file) or wrong takes its default or is ignored; numbers clamp to their range; control characters are removed from the relay. Version 1 is the first; bump `settings::VERSION` only when a field's meaning changes (adding a field needs no bump).

Where: natively `<config folder>/glasnost/settings.txt` (`XDG_CONFIG_HOME` or `~/.config` on Unix-like systems, `~/Library/Application Support` on macOS, `%APPDATA%` on Windows; a relative or missing folder means no store and the defaults, with nothing written); written beside the file and renamed over it. Never in the repository or the game folder. On the web, `localStorage` under `glasnost.settings`; a blocked storage reads as empty and a failed write shows a note on the screen. Both go through the `settings::Store` trait (`FileStore`, `KvStore<K: KeyValue>`); tests: `settings_tests.rs` (format, defaults, clamping, conflicts, native file round trip, config path per platform, the web layer's logic with a blocking mock) and `client_tests.rs` (screen, persistence into a new client, rebinding, remembered relay, refusing store). The web build compiles `web-sys`'s `Storage`; a browser test is not added (the existing web harness has no storage case).

Seams: none left for music (C1 uses the music volume); the display scale is used since C3, which also added the `captions` and `colour_blind` fields. The in-game screen is not offered in network games. The screenshot test `crates/app/tests/settings_shot.rs` writes `settings.png` when `GLASNOST_SHOT_DIR` is set.

## Accessibility (M8 step C3)

Four settings of our own, all presentation and input of the person at this machine: none reaches the simulation crates and none changes a state hash (`client::access_tests::state_hash_is_the_same_under_every_accessibility_setting` plays the same game at every scale, with captions and colour-blind colours on and off, and compares every tick's hash; `a_building_is_built_and_placed_by_keyboard` shows the keyboard gives the same orders, replay and hashes as the pointer). They are stored with the C2 settings (`scale`, `captions=` on by default, `colour_blind=` off by default; adding fields needed no version bump) and shown on the settings screen, which now has two columns.

**Interface scale.** `glasnost_ui::geom::Scale`: every screen lays itself out in logical pixels on a logical screen of `size × 100 / percent`, and `DrawList::scaled` (a `Prim::Layer`) draws it at `percent / 100` screen pixels per logical pixel, quads clipped first and scaled after (`paint.rs`). `Scale::fit` lowers a scale until the logical screen keeps at least `MIN_UI` = 640 × 480 (and never below 100 %); the settings screen names the scale actually used when it was lowered. Pointer positions go back through `Scale::to_ui`, the exact inverse, so every control is hit where it is drawn at every scale (`targets_hit_at_every_scale`). In the game the sidebar, the options menu, the chat, the network overlay and the captions scale; the battlefield keeps one screen pixel per art pixel and simply gets the room the sidebar leaves (the radar's origin and zoom, S-0410 F9, are given in logical pixels inside the scaled sidebar, equal to screen pixels at 100 %). Status lines in the battlefield (S-0213 claim 25) and the cursor stay unscaled.

**Keyboard.** Our own keys, none of them one of S-0213 claim 22's command keys; Escape and the arrows keep S-0213 claim 23's meaning (an arrow still scrolls, and its release always reaches the scrolling).

| Where | Keys |
|---|---|
| Every screen outside the game (menu, setup, room, multiplayer, settings, end) | Tab / Shift+Tab or Down / Up move the focus over the screen's controls in reading order; Left / Right step a choice as its left or right half does; Enter or Space press; Escape goes back where the screen has a back control. The first key only shows the focus. A text field takes Enter and Escape only while it types. |
| Options menu (paused game) | the same keys over its controls; arrows also scroll as before |
| Sidebar | Tab / Shift+Tab move the focus over the sidebar's controls; Page Up / Page Down the previous / next tab with icons; Space presses the focused control as a left click (Shift+Space as with Shift held); Backspace as a right click (hold, cancel), or ends aiming |
| Placing a building, aiming a targeted action | Ctrl+arrows move the keyboard cell one cell in that screen direction; Enter (or Space) places or fires at it |

The keyboard cell (`Session::aim_cell`) starts at the cell under the view's centre and is drawn as the pointer's cell is (the placement outline). Its steps (`session::aim_step`) zig-zag on the diamond grid by the cell's parity so Right and Left move half a diamond sideways and Down and Up half a diamond up or down on the screen (two presses: a whole diamond straight that way) and every cell is reachable; a diagonal neighbour can take three presses (`aim_tests::keyboard_cell_steps_follow_the_screen_and_reach_every_cell`, S-0101 F1). The focus is drawn as a light outline (`colour::FOCUS`) and is not stored; it resets when the screen changes. Window: Tab, Space, Page Up and Page Down are mapped; Backspace repeats only while a text field types.

**Captions.** When on, each announcement that starts playing (`glasnost_audio::events::Audio::take_started`, after S-0701 claim 27's queue) shows a line low over the battlefield for 4 s, at most 3 at once, newest last (`game/captions.rs`); turning captions off clears them. The text is S-0701 claim 26's (revision 25, answering Q-1735 and retiring its interim): the entry's `Text` key in the person's announcement data, spelled exactly so and read as the caption text itself, when present with a nonempty value, and otherwise the entry's name; the same for every voice set, with no length limit beyond the line limit (`glasnost_audio::speech::Line::caption`). Claim 26 lets the 4 s, 3 at once and newest last stand as our interface choices. Nothing of the data is in the repository; tests use invented names and words.

**Colour-blind house colours.** When on, a player house in lobby colour slot 0 … 7 shows our own colour definition (`mapview::build::COLOUR_BLIND_SLOTS`: the Okabe–Ito colours as H, S, V on S-0103's scales, white last) instead of the slot's definition, shaded by the same S-0103 ramp; map-defined and neutral owners keep theirs (`colour_blind_colours_replace_slot_definitions_only`). The setup screen and the room show the matching swatches (`install::colour_blind_swatches`). A game takes the setting when it starts (`Session::start`'s `colour_blind`); a change shows from the next game.

Tests: `game/client_access_tests.rs` (9: scales, keyboard over every screen, the options menu, a building built and placed by keyboard against the pointer, Backspace, captions, colour-blind swatches and start, hashes), `aim_tests`, the settings and ui tests of the new rows and stops. Pictures: `crates/app/tests/access_shot.rs` (synthetic; `GLASNOST_SHOT_DIR`) writes the menu at 100 % and 200 %, the settings at 150 %, the setup with both swatch sets, a game at 200 % with the sidebar focus, the keyboard cell and a caption, and the options menu at 200 %. The local lane `crates/app/tests/access_lane.rs` (ignored, `GLASNOST_GAME_DIR`; output `local-lane/m8/c3/`) starts a real skirmish with and without the colour-blind colours and builds and places the first building by keyboard at 200 %.

Seams: placement and aiming move the keyboard cell with Ctrl+arrows, not plain arrows, because plain arrows keep S-0213 claim 23's scrolling; some diagonal neighbours of the keyboard cell take three presses; the colour-blind setting applies from the next game; the interface's glyphs are scaled pixel by pixel, so at 125 % and 150 % some glyph columns are a screen pixel wider than others; captions sit at a fixed place above the chat; Escape does not close the options menu (S-0213 claim 23 opens it; closing by Escape is unspecified); the focus is not remembered across screens; art-less placeholder cameos (tests only) draw their hatch slightly past the card (older).

## Play-note fixes (M8 step A4, part 1)

Notes 2 to 5 of the lead's play notes ([m8-claims/a4-play-notes.md](m8-claims/a4-play-notes.md)); the table there has each note's status. Requests Q-1754, Q-1755 and Q-1756 (filed by this step), with Q-1753, Q-1760 and Q-1761 (filed at triage) still open at the time; Q-1753 and Q-1754 are answered since (A3 intake 7), Q-1755 and Q-1756 too (S-0501 revision 40, A3 intake 9). Save format 70 (was 69); AI game format 31 unchanged (the AI save embeds the world save with its own version).

**The deploy key (note 2; S-0213 *The deploy rule*, S-0214 Terms).** `targeting::key_command` hands the deploy key to `deploy_command`. A garrisoned building of the player's first in the selection gets the only order. Otherwise a selection that holds both deployed and undeployed in-place deployers skips its deployed members, and every member that is a unit or building, movable or able to fire, not berserk, and passes `deploy_check` and `deploy_readiness` gets an order. Deployed now covers vehicles (`econ::inplace::form`) and infantry in the deployed, deployed-fire and deployed-idle sequences. The interims Q-1753 (a member still deploying or undeploying counted as neither) and Q-1754 (the timer that follows an infantry deploy not modelled) are retired: S-0213 revision 46 and S-0214 revision 49 answer them, built at M8 step A3 intake 7 (below). Tests: `tests/claims_s0213_deploy_rule.rs`.

**Multi-turret vehicles (note 3; S-0105 claims 12, 16, 26; S-0007 claim 16).** `Mobile::turret_index` carries the selected turret index, which `mapview::build::selected_turret_index` gives. For a type `multi_turret` accepts (a vehicle with the turret flag, a turret count of 1 or more and no rotating-barrel stages, the importer's test), the turret block draws `turret{k}` and `barrel{k}` and puts *k* in the turret key. Without an index it draws no turret, which is what IFVs showed before. No approved spec said what sets the index (Q-1760), so the function returned none for every object and nothing changed on screen. The numbered model took claim 16's vehicle turret frame with its own frame count (the claim's interim). Both interims are retired since: S-0105 r35 claim 43 answers Q-1760, and claim 16 draws a numbered turret in frame *f_b* (A3 intake 19, below). Test: `live_tests::multi_turret_draws_the_models_of_the_selected_index`, with the index supplied by the test.

**Row B7 (note 4; S-0214 table B).** A building whose first-slot weapon's projectile is not anti-ground, a pulse cannon, or a building being sold gets *nothing* on a click, whatever the target, aircraft included (Q-1761 asks about airborne targets). Retaliation was unchanged until Q-1761 and Q-1763 were answered; S-0306 revision 33 and S-0309 revision 38 answer them (M8 step A3 intake 5, below), and Q-1761's question 3 on airborne targets stays open. Tests: `claims_s0214_combat.rs`, `row_b7_*`.

**Aircraft that lose their target (note 5; S-0501 claims 6, 32; T1, T2).** Claim 6's cruise-height replacement is now fixed when the point is accepted: `AirState::raised` records it, `flight_point` uses the stored flag for the held destination and the current state for any other destination, and the flag clears with the destination. A flag was chosen over a stored height because a destination can move (an object target): the held point's height is the terrain under its current position plus cruise height while the flag is set. A target whose object no longer exists is now dropped too (claim 32, the glossary's *removed*), so an aircraft whose target is removed without a kill flies home. Diagnostic tests in `tests/claims_s0501.rs` (`lost_target_*`, `removed_target_*`, `target_taken_inside_is_kept`, `claim_6_held_point_height_fixed_at_acceptance`, `spent_no_dock_types_holds_over_the_last_firing_spot`; `target_taken_inside_is_kept`, the spent no-dock-types test and the no-dock-types and not-landable `lost_target_*` tests were replaced at A3 intake 9, below).

What the tests showed at this step, by the specs then approved (A3 intake 9, below, changes the second, third and fifth points):
- One dock type, the target killed during the approach: before, the aircraft dipped toward the old spot during the done delay (the height recomputed without a target), then climbed and flew home. Now it keeps the held height, enters its pad with contact held, touches down and docks.
- No dock types, armed and landable: T2's guard row moves it to its last firing spot, where it now holds at cruise height for good. Before it landed there, by the recomputed height. The same holds after an ordinary attack that spends its ammunition, so such a type now never lands after any attack. The local lane counts 2 armed, landable aircraft types with no dock types in the installed data. Q-1756 asks whether it should land; its interim keeps the held point.
- Not landable: it is visiting and hunts at cruise height after the loss (T2).
- Every docking place reserved: it moves to an F8 landing spot, lands and guards (T2). A pad-bound one crashes (S-0501 claim 46).
- A target taken off the map (`objects::take_inside`): the target is kept (Q-1755 interim), so T1 decides the run: with the re-approach flag off (`[General] CurleyShuffle`, S-0501 claim 14) the aircraft stays in its firing behaviour forever with full ammunition; with it on, the aircraft chooses new firing spots, spends its rounds without firing and goes home. That may be the "circle at random" of the note.

**Local lane (counts only; `glasnost-local-lane`, `a4_counts` in `crates/tools/src/local_lane.rs`; output `local-lane/m8/a4-1/`).** Multi-turret vehicle types: 2, each with a `turret0` part and no plain `turret` part, none discarded. Buildings whose primary weapon's projectile is anti-air and not anti-ground: 2, both imported with anti-ground no (no importer bug; the count reads the primary weapon, row B7 the first-slot weapon). Aircraft types: 12, of which 10 have no dock types, 2 name a registered building, none an unresolved one; 7 landable, 2 airport-bound; 2 armed and landable with no dock types; 2 have a dock value in the rules and none loses it on import.

**Arena and sweeps (before: main 4ec9aa9; after: this branch on main 09659b3, which differs only by a docs row).** Oracle arena unchanged, with byte-identical CSVs: M-0001 33 of 96 pairings failing (151 of 172 unanimous outcomes reproduced), M-0002 13 of 24 (43 of 47), M-0003 3 of 35 (61 of 64). M7a and M7b sweeps (seeds 1 to 16) unchanged in every counter: M7a smallest minimum 1, M7b drain minimum 3. The M6c sweep moves, all of it from the aircraft commit (a build of that commit alone prints the same seed lines as the branch): early ends were seeds 9 and 15, now seed 5 (tick 4891); the smallest minimum stays 0 (jumpjet counters); deploy orders min/median 44/87 then 55/81, changes of deployed form 4/7 then 4/8, crate orders 22/160 then 46/174, radiation exits 2/32 then 8/43, sabotage orders 0/20 then 2/18; 23 of 29 counters move a little. Goldens regenerated: duel (from about tick 17 680), duel-diagonal (about 18 040), duel-m6c (about 70) and skirmish (about 11 720); combat, duel-m7a, duel-m7b, duel-naval and scripted unchanged. The goldens hold only state hashes, so the moves come from the hashed `AirState::raised` and from the aircraft behaviour it changes.

Seams:
- `objects::remove` and `objects::take_inside` clear only aircraft's targets (`air::targeters_lose`, S-0501 claim 32, since A3 intake 9); `death::mark_killed` clears every actor's. Ground units may still hold a removed target or one taken off the map: the loss every actor has is S-0309's (S-0501 Changes for other specs), not yet revised.
- S-0501 claim 24: on the docking tick the behaviour is *guard* and *sleep* follows a tick later. The spec has *sleep* current with *guard* queued. This was so before and after this step. Also, with full ammunition, claim 26's release leaves the aircraft in *guard* with contact held.
- `transport::in_tube` is always false (no tunnel travel yet), so the deploy check's inside-a-tunnel case and deploy readiness's inside-a-tunnel case never apply; the tunnel-cell test of deploy readiness does.
- The deploy key no longer sends a deploy order to a unit with no way to deploy (before, every movable or armed unit that was not a building got one): S-0214's deploy readiness now decides.
- The vehicle in-place rows V2, V3, VC4 and VC5 of S-0214 are not built yet.
- The multi-turret index waited for Q-1760, so IFVs still drew no turret. Answered by S-0105 r35 and built at A3 intake 19 (below).

### Targeting modes, retaliation and death lists (M8 step A3 intake 5)

S-0306 revision 33, S-0309 revision 38 and S-0107 revision 22; ledger [m8-claims/intake5-S-0306-S-0309-S-0107.md](m8-claims/intake5-S-0306-S-0309-S-0107.md). The firing check has S-0306 claim 23's water and land rows (`combat::check::targeting_mode_illegal`): by the firer's type's naval-targeting mode (S-0308 table N's "none": mode 0 against an underwater target that is not uncloaked, mode 2 against a target that is not underwater, mode 6 against all) for an object on water or beach that is neither airborne nor on a bridge, and by land-targeting mode 1 for a floor-bound object on land or on a bridge and for a land cell. Both answer *illegal* at rank 4, after the rank-4 answers the claim puts first and before the magnetic-beam rearming; the delayed-shot rearming's rank was interim Q-1770 (the rows win), and Q-1770 is now answered: the pending shot's rearming comes first (S-0301 r36 claim 9, intake 8). So retaliation, a building's *attack* and the cursor all refuse such targets (S-0309 claim 18's first case). Retaliation's F7 sight limit now reads a building type's `Sight` (it was 0 for buildings). The importer reads `DeathAnims` as a list of animation names like `DeadBodies`, so no-type and empty items are left out; row 3 takes the list as imported. On the installed data 2 of 2 anti-air-only building types have land-targeting mode 1, so play note 4's ground targets are now refused (lane scene `aa-only`, [a4-play-notes.md](m8-claims/a4-play-notes.md) note 4).

### Group destinations and the deploy press (M8 step A3 intake 7)

S-0213 revision 46 and S-0214 revision 49; ledger [m8-claims/intake7-S-0213-S-0214.md](m8-claims/intake7-S-0213-S-0214.md). They answer Q-1750, Q-1753 and Q-1754 (play notes 1 and 2). **Group destinations** (`orders::spread`, claim 7): the interim rules (a look of 6 points for each member, the first one cell out, the last-taken-cell fallback with no further search) are now the claim's rules. Two changes: a member standing in the leader's cell gets the common cell through the leader before any look (it used to run a look in the zero direction), and the *obstructed* class's entry check takes the common cell's height level, plus 4 on a high-bridge cell, as its reference (it used the member's own). The new switch `quirks.group_spread_no_detour` (default on, the original) is not consulted: a search after a spoiled look is a question for the lead. **The deploy press** (`targeting::deploy_command`): every selected in-place deployer is sorted at the press, part-way through a transition by the side it is heading to (`press_counts_deployed`: infantry in a deploy-kind sequence, *deploy* included, counts as deployed, in *undeploy* as not; a deploying vehicle as deployed, an undeploying one as not), including a member that then fails another condition. **Deploy readiness** (`targeting::deploy_readiness`): an infantry type with an undeploy delay of 0 or more lacks it in every deploy-kind sequence, in *undeploy*, and while its rearm countdown (`combat.rearm_until`) runs; a negative delay keeps it in every sequence. A vehicle's readiness never read its transition state, and row VD2 is unchanged. What a mid-transition member then does (finish, then go the other way or stay) follows from the rules already built; tests `claims_s0213_group.rs` and `claims_s0213_deploy_rule.rs` (Scenarios O10 part f, O6 part B, T24). No hashed state, formats 71/32 and goldens unchanged; the arena and the three sweeps are identical before and after.

### Network speed and the delayed building shot (M8 step A3 intake 8)

S-0201 revision 23, S-0209 revision 44, S-0414 revision 16, S-0301 revision 36, S-0306 revision 34, S-0308 revision 16 and S-0525 revision 4; ledger [m8-claims/intake8-S-0201-S-0301.md](m8-claims/intake8-S-0201-S-0301.md). They answer Q-1806 and Q-1770.

**Network games (S-0201 claims 15 to 19).** The options menu stops no tick. Lost focus stops none either and adds no wait (claim 16, our engine's departure). While the menu is open, or the window lacks the focus, the machine draws no new battlefield frame; closing the menu redraws it. The menu is refused once the game has ended, and in a network game after a surrender through the menu until the house is defeated. In a network game the speed control is hidden once the local house is defeated; a skirmish shows it after the defeat too (intake 24 below). The menu's control starts at the index in force, and the change is sent when the menu closes, as the new order `Order::GameSpeed { speed }`. The order is applied with the house's other orders and sets `World::game_speed`; an index above 6 is ignored. A speed order is not refused by the order block of the first ticks. In a network game it goes through the lockstep and runs on every machine in one tick. Every machine but the sender's shows "<name> changed the game speed.". The pacing then follows the new index: ⌊1000 / cap⌋ ms with the cap 60, 45, 30, 20, 15, 12, 10 (`glasnost_ui::setup::net_period_ms`; 16, 22, 33, 50, 66, 83 and 100 ms). The lockstep's period follows through `Session::set_tick_ms`. A skirmish keeps its own period table (`period_ms`, claim 2). The active clock runs throughout a network game. In a skirmish it pauses only for the options menu (S-0414 claim 23 revision 16: lost focus and the end delay count; our client has no freeze). The relay's rejoin grace and the bot and soak pacing now use the network table.

**The delayed building shot (S-0301 claims 9 and 14, F21; S-0306 claim 23; S-0308 claim 14).** A building's pending delayed shot answers *rearming* at rank 4, after S-0405 table T1's answers and ahead of every other reason, the water and land rows among them. At the end of the delay the check runs again with the slot chosen at the start (`Combat::delayed_slot`, new state) and the target as it then stands. A refused shot makes nothing, and the target is kept. An attack order during the delay starts no new delay. A fragment weapon's radiation level has no effect (S-0525 claim 1 revision 4; the S-0318 test now sets one). S-0209 revision 44's cell-layer term is cited where the *obstructed* class reads it.

Formats: save 72 → 73, AI game 33 → 34, wire 7 → 8 (the order in a frame). The goldens are regenerated once, for the new hashed state. Tests: `claims_s0301_delayed.rs` (8, scenario F21 and claim 9's ranks), `claims_orders.rs` (2 speed-order tests), the app's options-menu tests (`a_skirmish_speed_change_is_sent_when_the_menu_closes`, `a_network_speed_change_goes_through_the_lockstep_orders`, `the_menu_is_refused_after_the_end_and_offers_the_speed_after_the_defeat` and `a_network_menu_offers_the_surrenders_while_two_humans_stand` (intake 24; were `the_menu_is_refused_after_the_end_and_after_a_surrender`), `a_skirmish_clock_counts_lost_focus_but_not_the_menu`), the speed change in `two_clients_meet_chat_and_play_in_lockstep`, `network_pacing_follows_the_cap_table` and `options_menu_without_the_speed_control`. Seams:

- A skirmish's `game_speed` changes at the end of the next tick (our order path), while the paced period changes at once.
- The relay's grace and the lockstep's resend interval keep the start's period.
- The render-layer row of S-0306 claim 23 is still not modelled.
- The client clock counts no freeze.
- The notice names the house by its player's name.
- The pending-order limit (S-0213 claim 2) also counts speed orders.

### Aircraft after the run (M8 step A3 intake 9)

S-0501 revision 40 (answering Q-1755 in part, Q-1756, Q-1775 and Q-1776, play note 5), S-0318 revision 13 (Q-1825) and S-0503 revision 16; ledger [m8-claims/intake9-S-0501-S-0318-S-0503.md](m8-claims/intake9-S-0501-S-0318-S-0503.md). **After the run** (`air::run_end`, claim 17): every run end that does not go straight into another run gives the aircraft a cell on its house's computed edge, with S-0522 claim 4's draws, before the fall-back. A pad found at the fall-back replaces it; an armed aircraft with no dock types keeps it (T2's *guard* row, claim 45), and claim 40's selection now runs at the first processing of every ordinary *move* (`air::move_`; it ran when the destination changed), so a human player's aircraft gets a usable cell near the edge, lands and rests, and a computer player's keeps the edge cell in *area guard* (`air::area_guard`, new: the fall-back at its flight height with a delay of 1 tick; below it S-0215 claim 26 with *k* = 2, now applied to aircraft in `process::process_area_guard`). **A lost target** (claim 32): an aircraft loses as its target an object that is killed, taken inside a transport or building, or removed, at that moment (`air::targeters_lose` from `objects::take_inside` and `objects::remove`; `death::mark_killed`), with claim 37's *U*(4, 8) draw of the targeting timer when more than 10 ticks are left (`air::target_loss_draw`); T1's no-target rows of *approaching*, *firing* and *refiring* give *done* after 1 tick. **Nowhere to land** (claim 46, `air::crash_fall_back`): a pad-bound aircraft in flight with no pad granting contact at its fall-back is destroyed without a hit (health 0, a loss of its own house, credited to nobody), then falls as a dead aircraft; T2's after-the-row pad search runs after every row, the visiting rows included, and on the ground a pad-bound aircraft with no pad keeps its current behaviour. A pad that is gone, or a refusal with nothing queued, clears the destination first (T3). A planted bomb rides the fall (its timer test before the aircraft's movement) and is dropped at impact under the new switch `quirks.crash_drops_planted_bomb` (off: it goes off at impact). **Exit flights** (claim 34, `air::queue_ignored`, also in `orders::apply_behaviour`): an aircraft that is not a strike aircraft, in *retreat* or a paradrop or spy-plane behaviour, ignores a request to queue any other. **Hover stop request** (S-0503 claim 10): the stop request no longer clears the order-level destination; `HoverState::stopped` marks the locomotor's *D* as cleared while that destination stays. S-0318 revision 13 states the spread of fragment hits over several ticks that the engine already gave (Q-1825); only tests changed. Requests filed: Q-1837 (area guard above the flight height; interim: read as at it) and Q-1838 (a visiting type placed on the ground retreats at once and then ignores its orders; the rules are built as written, the tests use landable types). Save format 73 → 74 and AI game format 34 → 35, on top of intake 8 (new hashed `HoverState::stopped`); the goldens are regenerated for it and for the aircraft behaviour changes.

### Kept targets, fragments and the sloped shroud test (M8 step A3 intake 10)

This intake takes in six spec revisions: S-0519 revision 17, S-0513 revision 34, S-0203 revision 40, S-0306 revision 38, S-0202 revision 36 and S-0215 revision 76. The ledger is [m8-claims/intake10-S-0519-S-0215.md](m8-claims/intake10-S-0519-S-0215.md). S-0513 revision 34 answers Q-1740. No simulation logic changed: every behaviour these revisions state was already what the code did. The intake adds tests and citations.

**A building's kept target (S-0215 claim 32, scenario B16 case e).** An attacking non-SAM building acts on its firing check's answer. *Rearming* (S-0301 claim 9: rank 4 for a pending animation-delayed shot, rank 5 for the countdown) comes ahead of the range test (rank 10). So a target that leaves range while the building rearms is kept. It is dropped, and *guard* starts, at the first processing that answers *out of range* (`combat::behave::building_process`; buildings never take the approach step of claim 37). Tests: `claims_s0215_r76.rs` (countdown) and `pending_shot_keeps_a_target_driven_out_of_range` (delayed shot).

**Fragments (S-0306 revisions 35 to 38, S-0203 revision 40).**
- `combat::shrapnel` already treats a fragment as no shot. Its speed is the weapon speed *S*_w, uncapped; it starts at the burst point and climbs at the fixed elevation. The burst point is the one left before claim 20 (c).
- A projectile whose first turn a removal costs takes it in the first later tick in which no removal costs it, which can be *n* + 2. The update order already did this (`update::tests::o8_lethal`, `sh1a_removals_cost_fragments_their_first_turn`).
- New test: `s0306_fragment_speed_is_the_weapon_speed_uncapped`.

**Debris pieces and the bouncing draws (S-0202 revision 36).** Claim 10's bouncing row covers sprite animations only. A voxel-model debris piece takes S-0317's draws (`pieces::create`; `claim_3_falling_piece_creation` checks the exact stream).

**The sloped shroud test (S-0513 claim 29b, SW10 part i6).** `live::slot_cell_shrouded` keeps the surface height at the centre. That was the interim, and it is now the claim: a valley or steep slope at level 0 reads its north-west neighbour, an edge slope only itself (`protection_slot_cell_shroud_on_a_sloped_cell_uses_the_surface_height`).

**The bomb on a falling carrier (S-0519 claims 5 and 7).** Intake 9 builds S-0501 claim 46's dead fall-back crash, which carries this: the bomb is kept through the fall, can go off by its timer or the detonate order, and is removed silently at impact under `quirks.crash_drops_planted_bomb`. This intake only ledgers it.

No format or golden change.

### Start of music, the end screen and the options menu (M8 step A3 intake 12)

This intake takes in S-0703 revision 51 and S-0701 revision 27, both audio. The ledger is [m8-claims/intake12-S-0703-S-0701.md](m8-claims/intake12-S-0703-S-0701.md). S-0703 revision 51 answers Q-1831, Q-1832 and Q-1833, and Q-1830 in part. The behaviour is described under *Music (M8 step C1 part 1)* above.
- The loading track plays while a game loads.

- The end screen plays the score track once the game is over.

- A found file that does not parse fails its start and warns.

Two of these were departures waiting on the lead (S-0703 Edge cases): the end screen, accepted, and the hold in the options menu, rejected and replaced at intake 25. The simulation crates are unchanged, and the state hash is the same with and without music. No format or golden change.

### Area guard height, the visiting mark, hits without a warhead and the house order (M8 step A3 intake 13)

S-0501 r41 (Q-1837, Q-1838), S-0302 r39, S-0312 r44 and S-0009 r44 (Q-1805). The ledger, [m8-claims/intake13-S-0501-S-0302-S-0312-S-0009.md](m8-claims/intake13-S-0501-S-0302-S-0312-S-0009.md), holds the notes.

### A moving hover's deploy order (M8 step A3 intake 14)

S-0503 r27 claim 19 (Q-1773). The ledger, [m8-claims/intake14-S-0503.md](m8-claims/intake14-S-0503.md), holds the notes.
- The deploy order's clear reaches a hover as the stop order's point-equality rule: midway it stops at the next cell centre, on the final leg it completes the move. Balloon-hover retention reaches hovering types.
- A hover's readiness asks whether it is moving now, and its deploy attempt into a building waits until it holds no point. Its cell test now ignores the hover's own claims, so a hover's deploy attempt can succeed.

### Aircraft as tracked flying objects (M8 step A3 intake 16)

S-0501 r45 claim 47 (Q-1845). The ledger, [m8-claims/intake16-S-0501.md](m8-claims/intake16-S-0501.md), holds the notes.
- Tracking changes only at claim 47's events in `air.rs`: `take_off` and `placed` (in the air, at least 208 leptons, with the counts-as-aircraft flag) start it; an accepted `touchdown`, `impact`, `released_docked`, `leaving_map` and the off-map case of `sync_listing` end it. The Q-1845 interim (tracked exactly while in flight) is gone, and `sync_listing` keeps only the cell listing.
- `sector_update` runs at the start of each movement update, before the crash block and the forward step, and does nothing at a carried speed fraction of 0. A tracked aircraft goes through `flying::moved`; an untracked one in flight starts tracking, except in sector (0, 0) with `quirks.aircraft_corner_sector_untracked` on (now consulted).
- Our take-off and first climb fall in one movement update, so S-0303 claim 6's dual membership at take-off never lasts past that update.
- The counts-as-aircraft flag left out of an aircraft type now reads yes (`econ::tech`), so such an aircraft also ranks as air in the sidebar (S-0004 claim 11). The client's shadow rule still reads S-0105's no.
- No new state: save format 76, AI game format 37.

### Aircraft released above a pad (M8 step A3 intake 17)

S-0501 r52 claims 24, 40, 47 and 48 (Q-1841). The ledger, [m8-claims/intake17-S-0501.md](m8-claims/intake17-S-0501.md), holds the notes.
- Docking by landing no longer puts the aircraft on its docking position at touchdown. `enter` sets the docking stage at the processing that finds it landing on its pad, and `docking_step` then moves it horizontally toward the docking position by at most 5 leptons per axis per processing, keeping the landing height. Pad guidance (`point_with`) reads only the docking position's horizontal location.
- A factory release with `quirks.aircraft_released_above_pad` on (now consulted) puts the aircraft at its docking position, height included. Above the pad it rests with the `released` flag: in flight, in neither cell layer, not tracked, filed in the ground render layer (`ground_filed`). Its first movement update toward a destination descends by F2 and gives no forward step; it is tracked from its second. Its contact ends when it takes a destination other than the pad (`air::destination_taken`, from `assign_destination` and the behaviour orders).
- `ground_filed` is cleared at every S-0102 claim 4 re-file moment (`combat::effects::layer_refile`). The map-wide search's ground-layer pass reads it. The renderer files such an aircraft in the ground layer at placement (it used to read the height there, which put it in the top layer).
- `guard` compares height with the cruise height for equality only (claim 48 (d)).
- The take-off test reads height (Terms, claim 8). An aircraft at height 0 that is neither settled nor landing takes off at a new destination. The gate's M6c air-strike aircraft does this, which changed duel-m6c and the M6c sweep: its smallest minimum fell from 4 to 2, with no counter at 0.
- Goldens duel, duel-diagonal, duel-naval, duel-m6c and skirmish regenerated (causes in the ledger). The arena CSVs are byte-identical, and so are the M7a and M7b sweeps.
- Q-1875 (the released aircraft's object-present mark) was answered by S-0501 r55; the interim is retired at intake 23 (below).
- New hashed state (`released`, `ground_filed`): save format 77, AI game format 39.

### Object-present flag and height-0 run of a released aircraft (M8 step A3 intakes 23, 28 and 31)

S-0501 r55 claim 48 (e) (Q-1875), r59 claim 48 (c) *At height 0* (Q-1905), and r62 claim 48 (c) *Low release on a move* and *Pad contact* (Q-1940 to Q-1942). The ledgers, [m8-claims/intake23-S-0501.md](m8-claims/intake23-S-0501.md), [m8-claims/intake28-S-0501.md](m8-claims/intake28-S-0501.md) and [m8-claims/intake31-S-0501.md](m8-claims/intake31-S-0501.md), hold the notes.
- `released_docked` sets no flag for an aircraft it leaves above its pad, and clears the object-present flag of the docking position's cell's ground record outright (`others = 0`), whatever another object had contributed, because the flags are not counts (S-0209 claim 1). A release at or below the terrain, or with `quirks.aircraft_released_above_pad` off, still rests holding its flag there.
- `air::tick` notes whether the aircraft was released before its movement update, and at the end of that update `released_mark` brings the flag in line: none above height 0, and at height 0 the flag in the cell that then holds it, moved out of a cell it has left. That covers the low release, which stays at height 0 with a target fraction below 1, and its touchdown.
- The Q-1875 interim (the mark kept on the docking cell until the first update toward a destination) is gone.
- F7 (`landing_cell_ok`) accepts a cell whose first listed occupant is the lander itself, as F7 states; our search used to skip the lander, so its own flag refused the low release's touchdown. A refused touchdown now clears the flag as take-off restarts.
- Intake 28 (r59, Q-1905) made the low release run unsteered at height 0 until its destination's halving zone or a target fraction of 1. Intake 31 (r62) replaces that run; the Q-1940 to Q-1942 interims are retired.
- Intake 31, *Low release on a move* (Q-1942): claim 40 now gives the selected destination, kept or replaced, at the move's first processing, and the move's next processing gives its flight point once more (`air::regive`, `AirState::regive` = the move's start tick). Both go through `air::give_point`, the locomotor's acceptance that `tick_flight` uses for a new destination, so an aircraft then on the ground by its height, or landing, takes off (claim 8). A low release on a move (order or rally point) thus reaches height 0 in its first movement update and takes off before its second, tracked at the take-off and re-filed into the top render layer in that update. The release's rally move is now queued with the destination assigned, not started at once and not an order (claim 48 (d)).
- Intake 31, *At height 0* (Q-1941): claim 5's approach target replacement and claim 6's close-approach trigger are gated on a positive height for every aircraft, as guidance is (claims 5 and 42); the exact-cell trigger applies at any height. `low_release_at_height_0` now guards only claim 8's no-destination landing (a low release at height 0 is on the ground by Terms).
- Intake 31, *Pad contact* (Q-1940): claim 8's rule at `take_off` now also reads the cell (`pad_cell`) for a building contact, so a tethered type released outside the foundation keeps its contact as it takes off; the tether still ends. Our design for Open question 26 (`// design:`): a low release whose destination came with another behaviour (the attack of claim 48 (d)) takes off at the start of its next movement update at height 0 unless *move* is current, and intake 28's design for a contact held in flight stays.
- Intakes 23 and 28: arena CSVs and the M6c, M7a and M7b sweeps 1–16 identical before and after; no golden changes. Intake 31: arena CSVs and the M7a and M7b sweeps identical; goldens duel, duel-diagonal, duel-naval and skirmish moved by the new hashed field only, duel-m6c also by Q-1941's height gates; the M6c sweep moved too, cause not isolated (smallest M6c reference minimum 2 → 4).
- Save format 78, AI game format 40 at intake 23; 79 and 41 at intake 28; 81 and 43 at intake 31 (`AirState::regive`).

### Turret table and selected turret index (M8 step A3 intake 19)

S-0105 r35 claims 12, 16, 31 and 43, state table S3, the turret table pairs and two switches (Q-1760; play notes 3 and 14). The ledger, [m8-claims/intake19-S-0105.md](m8-claims/intake19-S-0105.md), holds the notes.
- The importer reads the 17 turret table pairs (`glasnost_convert::turrets`) into `Import::turret_tables`, by vehicle registry index. Each pair's absent index is its stem's default (Repair 1, MachineGun 2, Flak 3, all others 0), and its absent weapon number is −1. Pairs apply in order in every layer that has the section, the table is never cleared, and the renderer reads it.
- The importer warns about a weapon number outside 0 … 17 other than −1, and ignores that pair. It also warns about an index outside −1 … *N* − 1, which is kept and draws nothing.
- Two switches, both on by default, live in `glasnost_convert::sim::SimQuirks`, because the importer applies both:
  - `turret_table_fixed_type_only`: only the fixed type name reads its pairs.
  - `turret_pair_no_weapon_sets_charge_flag`: for the fixed type name, the last pair with no weapon number in each layer overrides that layer's `IsChargeTurret`. The flag is on unless the index is 0 or a multiple of 256. It decides the imported `is_charge_turret`, so S-0308 claim 5 and S-0301 claim 16 follow it.
- `mapview::build::selected_turret_index` derives the index from simulation state that already exists:
  - a gunner type that is not charge-turret gives *T*[weapon number];
  - a charge-turret type gives S-0301 claim 16's *c* from its rearm countdown, 0 from creation;
  - any other type gives *none*.
- The static map view uses `creation_turret_index`. A numbered turret is drawn in frame *f_b*, and an index outside 0 … *N* − 1 draws neither turret nor barrel.
- The renderer's `ConsideredAircraft` default is now yes for an aircraft type and no otherwise, which closes the intake 16 seam.
- A sprite-body vehicle with the turret flag draws its single voxel barrel (`sprite_body_barrel`). Since intake 27 (S-0105 r39, Q-1885 answered; ledger [m8-claims/intake27-S-0105.md](m8-claims/intake27-S-0105.md)), the barrel is shifted by trunc(δ / 8) model units and always drawn in HVA frame 0. The body frame, barrel and turret frame are composed into one image (`composed_item`), in claim 11's order by the 4-step turret facing. That image takes the voxel vehicle's row *U*, upright, a reader, at every height, and its rectangle is the exact union of its parts. Without a barrel part, the body and turret frames are still composed. The shadow keeps *U* − 2, flat. The intake 19 interim (the sprite row *U* − 2, the voxel-turret barrel frame) is retired. Seam, our design: the tall-image split (S-0102's, second condition open) is never made.
- Seam: the derived charge index also follows the countdown in ticks without a turn. The view cannot see a turn, so this is a limit of the view, not a case the spec leaves open.
- No new state: save format 78, AI game format 40.

### Bouncing animations (M8 step A3 intakes 15 part 1 and 33)

S-0319 r9 (Q-1762 answered in part), then r26 at intake 33 (Q-1846 and Q-1847, the rest of Q-1762). The ledgers, [m8-claims/intake15a-S-0319.md](m8-claims/intake15a-S-0319.md) and [m8-claims/intake33-S-0319.md](m8-claims/intake33-S-0319.md), hold the notes.
- A sprite animation whose type is a bouncing type at its creation carries a flight (`glasnost_sim::bounce`, `Anim::flight`). Its creation takes the three raw values of S-0202 claim 10's row and places it (F1, F2). Each turn takes the motion step first; a stopping or impact landing ends it in the same turn with its effects (claims 6 to 10). This fills A5's "bouncing motion" seam: sprite debris now ends (play notes 6, 10, 12 and 13).
- Any animation type that names a trailer makes it on ticks divisible by its separation (claim 12), bouncing or not; 0 or absent acts as 1 and the importer warns. A zero span takes the remainder 0 and the importer warns (claim 14); the importer checks the vertical span under both settings of `quirks.anim_fixed_upper_vertical_speed`, which it cannot see.
- All nine S-0319 switches are consulted (three new at intake 33). `pieces::land_impact`'s hits are shared as `pieces::ground_hits`; since intake 33 it takes a `walk` flag (S-0317's pieces pass false).
- Intake 33 (r26): the building cell, F6's sloped rebound and F7's cliff turn are S-0317's helpers (`pieces::building_cell`, `rebound`, `cliff_turn`); the cliff line is −0.0002. The impact hits walk the live listing (`quirks.bouncing_anim_impact_hits_walk`). A resource offer repeats on a cell that received nothing (a terrain object, a refused vein or end-marker placement, a place past the list) and places through `overlays::place_in_play`. Three new switches, all default on: `bouncing_anim_rebound_faults`, `bouncing_anim_impact_hits_walk`, `anim_resource_no_growth_entry`. No animation is placed at (0, 0, 0). The start sound follows the animation (audio). Importer warnings for the bound *H* ≥ 256 and for a spawn overlay type near the list's end.
- **Seams.** A place of 255 or more within the overlay list places nothing (cells hold a one-byte overlay). The warhead's light flash at the end is not drawn.
- Save format 78, AI game format 40 at intake 15; no new state at intake 33.

### A docked hover lost at a naval yard (M8 step A3 intake 18)

S-0314 r77 claim 21 (*Over water, at a naval yard*) and the Edge case *Hovering vehicle lost at a naval yard* carry the lead's ruling of 2026-10-24. The ledger, [m8-claims/intake18-S-0314.md](m8-claims/intake18-S-0314.md), holds the notes.
- `quirks.docked_hover_water_loss` is now consulted, in `hover::sink_test` (S-0503 claim 11's water test at a spin's end). On, the default, nothing changes: a hovering vehicle docked at a naval yard on water whose switch-off took the two draws is lost when its spin ends below 104 leptons, as anywhere.
- Off: a spin end that comes while the vehicle has depot contact (`depot::link`) or is settled at a depot (`depot::settled_at_depot`, r88) makes no water test, so no bomb mark and no fall. The draws, the spin and its rotation are the same in both positions. The test belongs to the spin's end, so a spin that ends after the contact and being settled have ended is tested as usual.
- Q-1876 (answered by r88, intake 30): a disabling away from any depot (no contact, no depot in its cell) makes the vehicle settled but not settled at a depot, so with the switch off a hover disabled over open water is tested in both positions (H4 part i run 7). The intake 18 interim is retired.
- Whether an ordinary repair visit reaches the loss stays open in the spec (claim 21). The code keeps to the stated rule: the switch-off at the movement arrival is in *enter* and takes no draws.
- No new state: save format 78, AI game format 40. Goldens unchanged.

### Slot power, disabling and re-enabling (M8 step A3 intake 20)

S-0108 r75 (claims 10, 11, 13 and 26 to 28; answers Q-1772), revised by r81 in intake 29 (answers Q-1890). The ledgers, [m8-claims/intake20-S-0108.md](m8-claims/intake20-S-0108.md) and [m8-claims/intake29-S-0108.md](m8-claims/intake29-S-0108.md), hold the notes.
- Slots read their power flags (`mapview::slots`, claim 13: the first of freeze, light and effect that is on counts; *pre-production*, *production* and *turret* have none). `mapview::slot_play::Slots` keeps the disabled state: a disabling freezes every running freeze slot, a re-enabling unfreezes them (`ViewQuirks::enable_unfreezes_unpowered`, now consulted; off, only when the building is in operation right after), and a frozen slot is held through `glasnost_sim::anim_clock::Player::update_held` (S-0109 claim 23: its clock keeps running). The loss and return columns apply only to a type that needs and draws power.
