Since intake 55b (M-0014) each change of a tick acts in its place in the tick (S-0108 claim 13): `Slots::prepare` applies, before the slots play, the re-enablings that come before any object's turn (a trigger's or an occupation's change of owner of a type that needs an engineer, and a pulse's end), and `Slots::update` applies the rest after them: a change of owner that the simulation reported with an announcement (`Effect::Captured`, an engineer's capture, a takeover or a mind control: the turn pass, our design, Q-2186), a pulse's start, and the power switch.
- A map-placed building that needs an engineer and is not manned is disabled at its placement and drawn live from the start; every ownership change re-enables it (an ownership change of a type that needs no engineer is no re-enabling, r81), and the held slots resume from the frame they held (claim 26). Its light and effect slots, and slots with no power flag on, keep playing (claims 13 and 27). Switching off and a pulse disable, switching on and a pulse's end re-enable (claim 28). Q-1772's interim (`Live::unmanned_tech`, the still map) is retired.
- A building placed in play, or a code-130 replacement, opens at the completion of its construction: the slots' *completed* reads the seen behaviour (`econ::placement::seen_behaviour`, S-0406 claim 17), so a queued *construction* counts (claim 11). Code 125 is not in the simulation (seam).
- **Placed switched off (intake 29, r81 claims 13, 27 and 28; Q-1890's interim retired).** A building the map places switched off (only a type with drain or the needs-power flag, the simulation's `map_off_applies`) is disabled at the end of its placement, after its set-up and opening slots start, so its freeze slots hold through any ownership change (a needs-engineer type resumes at its capture) until its switch-on re-enables it; the slots resume from the held frame with the first advance in the next tick, and in a house short of power keep advancing while the building is out of operation. Each disabling and re-enabling acts at once; events seen in one view update apply in the tick order ownership change, pulse, switch (our design: update order within the turn pass is not visible to the view), and the needs-and-draws-power reaction comes last.
- **Seams.** Claim 14's special outage and the *low-power* slot are not played (also at a switch-on); `power::under_pulse` always answers no until pulses reach the simulation; two toggles in one tick, and events collapsed by a rejoin's catch-up, are not visible to the view.
- No new state: save format 78, AI game format 40. Goldens unchanged. Intake 29: no simulation change (save format 79, AI game format 41, as on its base); goldens unchanged.

### Units placed in play (M8 step A3 intake 21)

S-0216 r20 (units placed after the game has started; answers Q-1711 questions 2 and 3 and Q-1713 questions 2 and 3). The ledger, [m8-claims/intake21-S-0216.md](m8-claims/intake21-S-0216.md), holds the notes and the table of placement paths.
- `objects::place_in_play` is the play-time placement: an infantry unit on a usable cell takes S-0208 claim 3's choice for a desired point of class centre (no position and no draw when a vehicle or object refuses; otherwise one R(0, 3), refused with the draw kept when the row is full), then the entry test (only *clear* accepts), then a visceroid-flagged vehicle's R(0, 29), then the placement with facing 256 × *v* for body and turret, and a unit ready to change has its resting behaviour current (claims 1 to 5).
- No placing rule of the game is a play-time placement: S-0216 Terms names only the measurement harness and a rule whose own spec says so, and excludes absorbing, sale survivors, unloading, paradrops, garrisons, parasites and map entries by name. Every path keeps its own spec (factory exits, crates, undeploying, survivors and the rest; set-up stays S-0009's and S-0208's, claim 5). The users are ours: `testkit::spawn_in_play` and the synthetic arena.
- The change to S-0215 (a ready unit has its resting behaviour current at placement) is built on S-0216's side only; the placement fall-back of every other placement still only queues it.
- The oracle arena keeps A1's interim (Q-1711 question 5 open; M-0006 settles only that the units are in play at tick 0, intake 52b): centre point, no draws, no entry test, *hunt* forced. Its facings already are 256 × *v* (claim 1) and claim 6 already holds for its computer-controlled houses.
- **Interim rules, our design.** Outside the usable area: no choice, no draw, the cell's centre, then the entry test. Infantry facing: 256 × *v*. A refused unit stays off the map (the harness discards it). Always the ground layer. A cell that is not a legal map cell refuses.
- No new state: save format 78, AI game format 40. Goldens unchanged.

### House-credited kills and the menu after the defeat (M8 step A3 intake 24)

S-0201 r31 and S-0414 r30; ledger [m8-claims/intake24-S-0201-S-0414.md](m8-claims/intake24-S-0201-S-0414.md).
- **Result screen** (`econ::stats::destruction` takes a `Credit`): a hit that S-0302 claim 14 credits to the attacking house alone (gas particles, the mutation superweapon, a projectile or area detonation whose firer is gone) now gives that house the kill and the destruction score, the score with no not-scored or insignificant exclusion, and a not-scored victim's house a loss (S-0414 claims 21 and 22). An insignificant building still gives no kill or loss.
- **Skirmish options menu** (S-0201 claims 1, 15 and 17): it shows the speed control after the person's defeat (the lead's ruling of 2026-10-24), and opens again after our engine's surrender, which it offers (design) only while the house is undefeated and has not surrendered. The network menu's leave choices are under *The client (N3)* below.
- No new state: save format 78, AI game format 40. The goldens are regenerated for the new scores.

### Pathing reports (M8 A4)

A pathing report is what the lead saves while playing when a unit moves strangely; the replay tool explains it from the simulation's own decisions. Everything here is our own diagnostics (`// nocite: our own diagnostics`): no game rule changes, the save format stays 70 and the AI game format 31, and every golden is unchanged.

**The ring** (`crates/app/src/game/capture.rs`, `Ring`; held by `Play::capture`). The client keeps a few snapshots of the game, each its own save bytes (`Game::save`, which hold the brains and the whole replay). `Play::step_by` calls `Ring::after_tick` right after every tick, before any order of the next, so a snapshot is a tick boundary. The ring covers at least `WINDOW_MS` = 20 s of game time at the game's speed (`window_ticks(speed)`: 20 000 ms over `glasnost_ui::setup::period_ms(speed)`, rounded up; the session sets `Ring::speed` before each tick, so a network game uses the room's speed). A snapshot is taken every `spacing(speed)` = ⌈window / 2⌉ ticks, and the oldest is dropped once the next one already covers the window, so the oldest kept is one to one and a half windows old and the ring holds 2 or 3 snapshots (at most `MAX_SNAPSHOTS` = 6). Orders need no ring of their own: every person's order of a skirmish or of a network game goes through `Game::issue` into the game's replay, and the report takes those since its snapshot. The ring also keeps the last 8 battlefield clicks (tick, button, cell, object; `Play::click_map`) and up to 32 marks. A new game (and a rejoin's `Session::replace_game`) starts a new ring. Its cost, measured in the local lane (release build, a stock two-player map, game speed index 1, so a window of 1250 ticks and a snapshot every 625): a snapshot is about 1.1 MB and takes about 6 ms, against a mean tick of 1.2 ms, so the ring adds under 0.01 ms a tick on average and holds 3 snapshots, about 3.4 MB. A snapshot's few milliseconds fall on one tick in 625; we do not spread it.

**The keys** (`settings::Action::PathReport` and `Action::Mark`, labels "Save pathing report" and "Mark (pathing report)" on the settings screen, defaults P and M; neither is a command key of S-0213 claim 22). A settings file written before them that already gives P or M to a command keeps it, and the new action takes the first free letter or digit. *Mark* notes the present tick ("Marked tick N for the pathing report."). *Save pathing report* makes the bundle and stores it through `Client::save_path_report`, the same kind of function as the desync report's (`SaveReport`): natively `capture::save_file`, a file `path-reports/path-<capture tick>-<n>.gpath` under the working directory (a taken name gets `-2`, `-3` before the extension); in the browser the shell's download (`GameOptions.report`). The status line says "Pathing report of ticks A to B saved to PLACE.", or why not. `path-reports/`, `desync-reports/`, `*.gpath` and `*.gdsync` are git-ignored: a report holds a save of the game.

**The bundle** (`capture::Bundle`). The file is the magic `GPTH`, the format version (`capture::VERSION`, 1; little-endian u32), the SHA-256 of the body, then the postcard body: the build id (`capture::build_id()`: the client's build string with the save and game format versions), the rules hash, the map's content hash, the setup hash (`glasnost_net::start::setup_hash`), whether it is a network game, the snapshot (ticks run, tick number, bytes), every person's order since it (tick, house, order), the capture (ticks run, tick number, state hash), the marks inside the window, and what the person looked at (`Look`: house, selection in order, the view's origin and size and the cells under its corners, the cell under the pointer, the clicks, the game-speed index). A reader refuses, with a sentence for a person, a file too short for its header, another magic, another version, a checksum that does not match (damaged), contents that do not read, trailing bytes, and a capture before its snapshot. Bump the version whenever the layout changes.

**The trace** (`crates/deterministic/sim/src/trace.rs`). A read-only record of what the movement code decided: path computations (S-0212: mode asked and run, start, destination before and after substitution, congestion-marked cells, corridor, each cell search's expansions, recorded pairs, steps found and cost g as the bits of the simulation's own single-precision value, the result and the stored steps), what followed a failed computation, step checks that did not give clear (driving, walking and hovering: the cells, the entry outcome and the objects listed in the asked cell), blocked-step re-paths and whether the retry timer held them, give-ups, give-way requests and their answers, and group-destination spreading. It lives in `World::trace`, a `#[serde(skip)]` slot that is empty unless `trace::switch_on` fills it and that a clone of the world leaves empty; nothing in the simulation reads it. Recording copies values the movement code has in hand plus pure reads of the cell lists, so a traced run takes the same steps: `sim/tests/trace.rs` runs the determinism gate's scripted replay with and without it and compares the state hash after every tick, and checks that it recorded path computations and refused steps and is gone after a save and load. The only changes to the simulation crates are this module, the slot and the recording calls in `path.rs`, `drive.rs`, `walk.rs`, `hover.rs`, `scatter.rs` and `orders.rs`; `path::search` and `path::compute` gained untraced inner functions with the old bodies.

**The tool** (`crates/tools/src/path_report.rs`):

```sh
glasnost-headless path-report FILE [--out DIR] [--unit ID]... [--tick T] [--names] [--any-build]
```

It reads the report (exit 2 with the reason if it cannot), takes the rules and the tile catalogue from the synthetic fixture when the report's rules are its, else from the install in `GLASNOST_GAME_DIR` (the map found by its content hash, the rules checked against the report's hash), refuses a report of another build unless `--any-build`, loads the snapshot and checks its map, setup, tick and ticks run against the report, switches the trace on, and replays: at every tick the report's orders of that tick, then the tick, up to the capture, where it issues the capture tick's orders taken before the capture and compares the state hash. Exit 0 when it matches; 1 when it does not (the outputs are still written, headed "NOT reproduced"). The units of interest are the selected, those ordered in the window and any `--unit`, at most 32. Outputs go to `--out` or a folder named after the file next to it:

- `report.txt`: the build, game, window (ticks and orders), whether it reproduced, marks, selection, view, pointer cell and clicks; a summary of blockages (per unit, by what and by whom, with the ticks); then per unit its state at the snapshot and the capture, its track as runs of cells (`(x,y) ticks a-b`), a timeline (orders, path computations with the cells found or why none (a failed one names the destination asked for, since the trace has the searched-for cell only with a stored path), the cell searches' sizes and costs, whether the 5th failed cell search inside a corridor ended the computation (S-0212 claim 13's cap), and the likely reason of each re-path, refused steps with the objects in the way, where they stood and whether they were moving (a moving-blocker refusal whose cell lists no object names instead the vehicles whose vehicle-present flag is set on it at the end of the tick, S-0209 claim 1: for example both cells of a two-cell curve; pathing report 2), a run of identical refused steps on consecutive ticks as one line, blocked holds, waits, give-ups, give-way requests made and answered, group spreading, and changes of its behaviour, destination and locomotor state) and its totals. Units are named by id, kind, type index and house; `--names` adds type names (so a lane run's text holds no game data).
- `paths.png`: the cells around the tracks and paths, top down (x right, y down; the game's view turns this 45 degrees), 12 pixels a cell, at most 120 cells across, from the simulation's state at the capture only: passability category colours (lighter for higher ground), buildings, the other units, each unit of interest's track (thick), its searched paths (thin) and its place, crosses on the cells it was refused a step into, and a legend in the interface's own font.
- with `--tick T`, `state-T.txt`: every field of each unit of interest when the world reached tick T.

Tests (synthetic, CI): `crates/tools/tests/path_report.rs` plays the cramped fixture map with eight of the person's vehicles placed in the clearing, sends them all through its three-cell land bridge at once, marks and saves through the client model; the tool reproduces the capture hash, names a unit refused a step and the unit in its way, writes its outputs and the dump; damaged, truncated, foreign, other-build, other-rules and other-setup reports are refused with their reason, and a wrong capture hash reports "NOT reproduced". `the_ring_covers_the_window_and_a_failed_save_says_why` checks the coverage and the failure texts; `settings_tests` the keys' defaults and an older file's bindings, and `client_access_tests::the_mark_and_report_keys_save_a_pathing_report` presses M and P in a game and reads the saved bundle's mark, selection and capture hash.

Local lane (`lane_capture_on_the_install`, ignored; `GLASNOST_GAME_DIR`): a skirmish on the install's first two-player map with ten starting units, played for a window and two spacings (2500 ticks at speed index 1), every unit of the person's sent to one cell 12 east and 4 south of the first, a mark 150 ticks later, a report 150 ticks after that, and the tool on it. Writes `local-lane/m8/a4-capture/report.txt`, `paths.png`, `state-<mark>.txt` and `ring-cost.txt` (ids, cells, counts and timings only; the `.gpath` is deleted). On 2026-10-22: 11 units ordered, the report 1.1 MB and made in 10 ms, the tool (release) about 5 s for a window of 1550 ticks, the capture hash reproduced, 860 trace events; the summary names the crowd's refused steps by blocker (one unit held 48 ticks behind another moving one at the goal).

### A sinking vessel's lean (M8 A3 intake 26; S-0504 r26 claims 15, 16; Q-1871 answered)

Replaces the note 20 interim (`mapview::sinking`, an id-hashed list by depth), now deleted. **State:** `naval::NavalState` keeps the pitch (`pitch: F32`, soft float) and the end that goes down (`lean: Option<Lean>`), serialized and hashed (save format 79, AI game format 41). Each descent of a sinking vehicle (`naval::sinking_turn`, after claim 4's 5 leptons) takes one step (`naval::pitch_step`, F4): 0.01 added in double, narrowed to single under the rules mode, held at the full lean 0.785398185253143310546875; the end is fixed at the first step from the body facing's 8-direction (`naval::lean_of`: 1 to 5 bow down, 0, 6, 7 stern down) and kept through turns and a second start. No random draw. The 79th step reaches the full lean; a vessel from height 0 leaves play at the 81st descent. Every sinking vehicle steps (`// design:`: the simulation does not know which bodies are voxels; only drawing reads the pitch). The simulation's roll stays level: rocking is the view's (section below), which seeds its own lean from a rocked pitch and adds the roll.

**Drawing:** `live::attitude` returns `Attitude::Sinking { pitch, roll, drawn }` from `naval::pitch`, with `drawn` for a type locomotor (S-0207 claim 14) of driving, ship, hovering or jumpjet (claim 16). A sinking vehicle draws no shadow from the first frame. While |p| or |r| ≥ 0.005 the body is posed by `voxel::vehicle_parts_rocking` (F5: *D* = Z(lift) · Rz(θ) · X(sx) · Y(sy) · Rx(r) · Ry(p), lift and shifts truncated from table sin and cos, *W* and *H* from the first section of the main body model), with the turret and barrel on that *D*. A rocking image is kept in a cache of its own keyed by the exact pitch and roll bits (S-0105 claim 26, S-0106 claim 13: drawn fresh; the cache only bounds memory, with the same pixels). `// design:` F5 also for the hovering and jumpjet locomotors (their order and lift are open in S-0504; they take the driving order), the type's own locomotor for a lift or teleport carrier, and no clipping (both open in S-0504). Not built: slope tilt *T* in F5, and S-0313 claim 5's passenger fire point on the leaned body (passengers fire from a point built from the heading alone). Tests: `claims_s0504.rs` (F4, claim 15), `voxel::tests::f5_lift_and_shifts`, `mapview::sinking_tests`; lane `sinking-list` in `m7b_lane.rs`. Ledger: [m8-claims/intake26-S-0504.md](m8-claims/intake26-S-0504.md).

### Body rocking (M8 A4 rocking, intake 53; S-0113, S-0303 claim 20, S-0614 claims 9 and 10, S-0601 F4)

**Simulation:** reports only, no state. `combat::area::detonate` pushes `Effect::Rock { damage, pushed }` after its particle system for a warhead with the rocking flag when *D* ≥ 31 (min(*D* × 0.01, 4.0) > 0.3): the objects of the detonation's cell layer in the 7 × 7 cells around the centre (only the centre when *S* ≤ 0), row by row, each with the point it is pushed from (the source object's position for the centre cell, else *P*; no source: *P*, S-0113 claim 11). A nullified detonation returns before it. `parasite::deliver` reports `DroneRock { host, side }` with its existing side draw, `parasite::push_start` `GrapplePush { host, way }`, and `mind::overload_turn` `OverloadRock { controller, level, draw }`. `World.effects` is not saved or hashed, so no format, golden or draw changes.

**View:** `mapview::rocking::Rocking`, kept in `Live` and stepped once per game tick in `take_effects` (never per drawn frame; at most 120 ticks caught up), in the simulation's soft floats and rounding mode so the rules can move into the simulation unchanged. In each tick the bodies step first (S-0113 claim 19: the rocking step is the first part of the turn) and the tick's reports then set the rates, so a push shows from the next step. Pushes are kept for vehicles only (`kept`: an aircraft draws the angles only while crashing, and buildings and infantry are sprites). A crash starts a body of its own: `Effect::CrashTumble` reports S-0312 claim 16's three draws (presentation only, from `air::start_crash_hit` and `jump::fly::start_crash`), `crash_rates` sets both rates by S-0113 F5, and a crashing aircraft or jumpjet steps by table S's crashing row (`glasnost_sim::air::crashing` or `jump::crashing`); `build.rs` draws a crashing aircraft at strictly positive signed height by `voxel::crashing_body_pose` (S-0113 r17 claim 20, Q-2236, intake 67): *V* · Rz(θ_flight) · Rx(φ) · Ry(ψ′) with no lift or shift, ψ′ = `voxel::crash_pitch` (the tumble pitch plus the type's drawing-pitch angle when the speed fraction is above the threshold, summed in double and stored in single, the angle not narrowed first), never the bank; the tumble's exact angles (`Rocking::exact_angles`, not held to the drawing step) at every size, its images kept apart under the F1 table steps of ψ′ and φ, which fix the drawn transform, as a rocket's fresh pitch is; at zero or negative height the heading alone; the shadow on the heading alone with level flight's key. `push_rates` is F1 (both rates replaced, *k* = (0.04 − 2.5 × 10⁻⁵ *d*) × *s* ÷ *W*, at least 0.01 and at most 0.05, *h* = ½ for area and drone pushes, the side sign test and the table roots under S-0204's `quirks.table_root_distance` and `quirks.table_direction`); `area_strength` is F2; the drone point is F3 through `walk::step_point`; an overload sets the roll rate to S-0601 F4's value. `axis_step` is table R for an unlinked body (move, hold at ±*L* with π/10 for the pitch while crush-slowed, the pull of 0.005 or 0.002 by the angle's side of the level band, past π/2 away from level, the stop band), with `quirks.rocking_limit_escape` (S-0113 claim 15, default on: an angle at its limit is not held again). `Rocking::step` is table S: a body in a warp before arrival hold holds; a sinking body keeps its pitch rate and steps its roll (its lean is seeded from the rocked pitch and stepped by `naval::pitch_step`, S-0504 claim 15); a crashing body adds its rates (balloon-hover bounds); otherwise the roll, then the pitch. A body past ±π is dropped (claim 9's destruction is a seam), and a body whose four values are all exactly 0 leaves the list. A grappling parasite's host (`parasite::grappling`) has its roll set to 0 at the grab, at each strike and when the parasite leaves, and in a push phase written after the step by F4 (no write in turn 1, *k* = ⌊turn ÷ 4⌋ in turns 2 to 39, 0 in turn 40); the rate is never touched. `live::attitude_with` turns the angles into `Attitude::Rocking` (or the roll of `Attitude::Sinking`); drawn angles are truncated to multiples of 0.005 rad, so a rock draws few distinct images, and a rocking body that does not sink keeps its shadow. `Effect::Rock` does not say which point is the source's, so `source_points` reads it (one point: the source's when another object stands exactly on it; two: the one whose bodies stand in the other's cell); a misreading changes only a push's magnitude. Seams (ledger): the angles are not game state (claim 19, fire points), claim 9's destruction, direct rocking and the melee link (claim 12), the angles an aircraft would carry into its crash from earlier pushes, crush tips (claim 17), the psychic dominator ring, weight of non-vehicles. Tests: `claims_s0303_rocking.rs`, `claims_s0614.rs` claims 9 and 10, `claims_s0601.rs` claim 16, `mapview::rocking_tests` (S-0113's State and ordering table among them). Ledgers: [m8-claims/a4-rocking.md](m8-claims/a4-rocking.md), [m8-claims/intake53-S-0107-S-0108-S-0113.md](m8-claims/intake53-S-0107-S-0108-S-0113.md).

### Pathing and other fixes (M8 step C7)

*Pathing and other fixes* is our own rules patch layer, `data/layers/play-fixes.jsonc` (`glasnost_rules::layers`). It sets chosen quirk switches to their off position: the one their spec states, or, for our own pathing improvements (part 4), one of our design. It holds thirteen: `corridor_retry_no_fallback` (S-0212 claim 13; pathing reports 1 and 2, Q-1771 and Q-1870) and `corridor_free_part_ignores_height` (S-0212 claim 13a, a ledge; part 3, which dropped `corridor_split_probe`); `depot_loss_strands_hover` (S-0314 Edge cases; part 2), which frees a hover that would never move again after its depot contact ends; `unload_without_contact_stalls`, `absorber_stale_contact_kept` and `hover_landing_request_stands` (part 3, each a stuck state its spec's Edge cases name); part 4's `path_through_stalled_blockers`, `unreachable_move_dropped` and `group_spread_no_detour`, part 5's `rally_point_shared`, `deadlock_no_yield` and `harvest_cell_shared` (below); and `ungranted_super_reads_clock` (S-0108 claims 11 and 18, intake 53), so a superweapon building whose owner holds no grant opens and stays on *super-1* rather than *super-3*. It is a box in the skirmish setup and the network room (the host sets it), on by default (m8-plan.md, decisions of 2026-10-25). It is part of `sim_rules_hash`, so the network start, saves and replays carry it, and the pathing and desync reports name it. The defaults in `quirks.rs` stay the original's. The arena, sweeps, gates and tick bench stay on the reference ruleset; `GLASNOST_PLAY_FIXES=1` puts the layer on for the seed sweeps and the skirmish lane, including the quick lane. The app's local lanes start through the setup screen, so they play with the layer on. The ledger [m8-claims/c7-play-fixes.md](m8-claims/c7-play-fixes.md) has the admission rule, the rejected candidates, the plumbing, the lead's two reports replayed with the layer, the runs with it on, and the identity runs; its *Part 2* the hover cases, the switch's catch-up in `depot::vehicle_turn` for a contact broken off elsewhere, and their runs.

### Our own pathing improvements (M8 step C7 parts 4 and 5)

The lead's decision of 2026-10-29 lets us depart from the original's pathing. Each improvement is our design, marked `// design: C7-P4 <name>` instead of a spec citation, behind a quirk switch whose default (on) is the original's, and the play-fixes layer sets it off; the layer's admission rule takes them without a spec's off position. Three so far. *Stalled blockers* (`path_through_stalled_blockers`): stalled units that wait on each other's cells form a deadlock (a wait-for graph, queues whose head still moves taken away), and a stalled unit in one first searches round the others' cells (bounded, no corridor) before the original's re-path; two groups head-on in a 2-wide corridor now both arrive. *Nearest reachable cell* (`unreachable_move_dropped`): a failed move tries once more towards the nearest cell the unit can stand on in its own reach region, so a click on rock or into a ring of buildings moves the units next to it. *Group spread* (`group_spread_no_detour`): a group member whose look runs into an obstacle gets the nearest open cell round the common cell instead of a cell already given, so groups by buildings or water do not crowd and give up. None draws on the simulation RNG and none adds saved state. The synthetic scenario set (`sim/tests/pathing_common`, `pathing_c7p4.rs`, its ignored `measure` prints the table) and the before and after numbers, the open cases (two closed rooms still deadlock; harvesters and a factory's rally point) and a yield proposal are in [m8-claims/c7-pathing.md](m8-claims/c7-pathing.md).

Part 5 (marked `// design: C7-P5 <name>`) adds three more switches and a cheaper wait-for graph. *Rally spread* (`rally_point_shared`, `orders::rally_cell`): a unit a factory releases with a cell rally point gets the rally cell while no other unit of the house has it, else the group spread's nearest open cell round it. *Yield* (`deadlock_no_yield`, `path::give_way` and `path::yield_turn`): in a deadlock with no way round (a two-sided fill from the unit and its goal, with the units heading against it as walls, does not meet), the units heading against the deadlock's lowest-id unit go to the nearest free cells off the other side's lane and resume their orders once it has passed (or after 900 ticks); a deadlock with a way round keeps the detour. *Field cells* (`harvest_cell_shared`, `econ::harvest::ring_search`): a harvester's ring search skips ore cells its house's units are heading for. The yield's state, `PathState::yielding`, is saved and hashed (save format 86, AI game format 48). `stalled_ring` builds no graph for a unit that waits on no stalled unit and peels it in one pass. The ignored `cost` test in `pathing_c7p4.rs` prints the fastest ns per tick of every scenario for the reference and the layer. None of the three draws on the simulation RNG. The two closed rooms now clear; the harvesters' docking queue and the shore's maximum stay open (the ledger's *Part 5*).

### The map's special flags (M8 map flags)

The importer reads `[SpecialFlags]` `Inert` and `HarvesterImmune` (booleans, absent: no; S-0303 Import mapping) into `Map::basic` (`inert`, `harvester_immune`). `load::new_game` copies them to `World::map_flags` (`world::MapFlags`, serialized and hashed; save format 76, AI game format 37). Consulted at: `combat::area::detonate` (S-0303 claim 2: an inert map's detonations are *missed* before any scan, wall hit, overlay or draw; the projectile's animation and debris are not suppressed), `combat::area::harvester_immune` (claim 9, scanned cells, and the tracked flying candidates when `quirks.flying_candidates_unfiltered` is off), `combat::damage::modified` (S-0302 claim 6 and F3 case (ii): m0 is 0 for a hit that does not ignore defences, which also gives the berserk duration of 0, S-0610 P3) and `combat::acquire` (S-0309 table C, harvester immunity). `DestroyableBridges` is not read (a skirmish ignores it, S-0511 claim 1). Ledger: [m8-claims/mapflags.md](m8-claims/mapflags.md). The sim-gate goldens moved by the two added bytes of hashed state only: the seed sweeps and the oracle arena CSVs are identical before and after.

### Projectile start point and absent muzzle positions (M8 step A3 intake 51; S-0306 r45, S-0313 r37; Q-2111 answered)

`combat::projectile::start_point` gives S-0306 claim 8's start point. A placed projectile whose fire point (or claim 5's start position) is not above the terrain at its *x* and *y* starts 1 lepton above that terrain; a bridge deck is ignored. `launch`, `airburst_children`, `shrapnel::burst` and `superweapons::nuke::missile` call it. `Projectile::fire_point` keeps the unraised point: it is the line-of-fire walk's *X*₁, and claims 5 to 7 work out direction, pitch and velocity from it. `placed_at` and `omega` take the start point. An invisible projectile is still moved to *K* afterwards, without a raise. `rocket::place` (S-0506's rocket missiles, which are objects) is not raised. S-0313 claim 1a: in `combat::data::type_combat`, an absent elite muzzle position takes the same slot's non-elite one, and for numbered weapons elite weapon *n* takes weapon *n*'s. The importer fills gaps in the elite numbered list the same way. A type with numbered weapons and no numbered muzzle key still reads the primary and secondary keys (interim, Q-2146). Ledger: [m8-claims/intake51-S-0306-S-0313.md](m8-claims/intake51-S-0306-S-0313.md).

### The crush response and a seized unit's approach step (M8 step A3 intake 52a; S-0210 r29, S-0215 r88, S-0216 r31; Q-2005 and Q-2113 answered)

`damage::apply` wraps the hit rules (`apply_hit`) and then calls `crush::respond_to_hit` for every result but *now dead* and *post-mortem* (S-0215 claim 39), so the response comes after every other effect of the hit: after a berserk entry (its queued *hunt* is replaced by *move*) and after a retaliation. A vehicle responds to a hit's attacker when it is not tethered, its owner is not allied with the attacker's, its owner is allowed (`crush::response_allowed`), it is a crusher, the attacker is crushable by it and does not appear disguised to its house, and the 3-D centre distance (approximate root) is at most the crush range. The attacker object becomes its destination through `econ::assign_destination`; *move* is queued only when `econ::accepts_destination` says the destination is taken. The owner: a house with `human = false` (neutral, special, test-only computer houses such as the oracle arena's) takes the original's row, skill at least `general.iq_auto_crush` (`[IQ] AutoCrush`, absent 2) and difficulty index not 2; a player house never responds while `quirks.auto_crush_computer_only` is on (default, the original), and with it off responds when `general.player_auto_crush` is on or the type has `AutoCrush`. The switch is not a play fix (ledger). `approach::step` no longer skips a seized unit whose target is its lifter (S-0215 r88 claim 37 (c)); `lift::steer` ignores the destinations the step gives it, and a cleared one ends the grip (interim, Q-2151). Ledger: [m8-claims/intake52a-S-0210-S-0215-S-0216.md](m8-claims/intake52a-S-0210-S-0215-S-0216.md).

### Held targets that leave play, and the arena's stock rows (M8 step A3 intake 52b; S-0309 r53, S-0210 r31, S-0405 r47, M-0005, M-0006, M-0016; Q-2006, Q-1710, Q-2114 answered, Q-2112 in part)

`combat::held` holds S-0309 claim 27. `holders_drop` runs for an object that is destroyed (`death::mark_killed`, at the killing hit), goes off the map (`objects::take_inside`) or is removed (`objects::remove`, a crush included). Every holder, in key order, which is creation order (S-0309 r53 Terms: `World::new_id` gives keys out in creation order and never reuses them), first takes `timer_draw`: with more than 10 ticks left on its targeting timer, one `ranged(4, 8)` from the scenario stream. Then its target is cleared, or S-0215 claim 10's revert restores the target put aside; a removal clears a restored target again with no second draw. `timer_draw` replaces the aircraft-only `air::target_loss_draw`, and `air::targeters_lose` is gone. The DECIDED rule is `firing_target` (a held object off the map, or dead but still in play, is no target in the firing steps of `combat::behave`) and `turn_start_drop` (called in `tick::object_turn` after the deactivated-vehicle sparks: the holder drops it with no draw and no revert). A building's pending delayed shot with no target fires nothing and is cleared. Claim 28: `cloak::machine::drop_attackers` takes the same draw for each holder it drops. A warp departure drops no holder at all and takes no draw (Q-2156, answered by S-0513 r43 at intake 60, below). A crush removes its victims one at a time in the cell layer's listing order (`crush::at_cell_centre`); a crushed vehicle first takes a killed vehicle's two animation picks (`death::vehicle_animations`, S-0210 r31 claim 4, S-0312 claim 9), then its removal takes its holders' draws. `path::failed` drops the target at a give-up of S-0212 claim 23 row 2 (S-0309 r53 claim 7), and at any other failed route search of a vehicle with the driving locomotor drops a target that is not within reach (`acquire::within_reach`, claim 23) and sets the limited-scan mark (claim 22); the hovering and ship locomotors set no mark (not examined in the spec). No state is added; the save format stays 89 and the AI game format 51. S-0405 r47 needed no code (`hover.rs` already made claim 13's arrival). The arena scores the SREF and V3 pairings through M-0016's stock rows (`oracle::load_stock_replays`) and puts the units stated on the five cliff cells on M-0006's measured cells (`oracle::load_placements`). Ledger: [m8-claims/intake52b-S-0309-S-0405-M-0005-M-0006-M-0016.md](m8-claims/intake52b-S-0309-S-0405-M-0005-M-0006-M-0016.md).

### The holders of a departing warp member (M8 step A3 intake 60; S-0513 r43; Q-2156 answered)

S-0513 r43 claim 39: a warp member's departure is off the map, but it is neither S-0309 claim 27's drop nor S-0207 claim 20 e's own jump. `superweapons::warp::depart` no longer calls `teleport::targeters_drop`, so the departure clears no holder's target, takes no holder draw, reverts no override (S-0215 claim 10) and leaves a projectile in flight aimed at the member (S-0306 claim 17). `teleport::targeters_drop` is now the own jump's only caller and takes no draw. A member that the landing check kills is destroyed through `combat::death`, so its holders take claim 27's draws after the warp-out animation's draws. Throughout the leaving stage (`Member::leaving`, `superweapons::warp_leaving`) `combat::check::check` answers *illegal* for the member unless the weapon is temporal. The test sits just before the held-spawner *impossible*: after every other rank-4 reason, ahead of the spawner answers, the S-0306 claim 23 rows, the live-effect *impossible*, the magnetic-beam *rearming* and ranks 5 to 16. So an opportunity target is dropped at the holder's next search with no claim 27 draw. Interims:

- **Q-2211**: a holder's own turn drops a departed member with claim 27's DECIDED turn-start drop.
- **Q-2212**: a projectile's own update drops the off-map target object (replaced by S-0306 r57 at intake 64, below).
- **Q-2213**: a member held by a temporal beam gets the same answer.

No state is added: save format 91, AI game format 53. Every lane is unchanged (sweeps, arena, quick lane). Tests: `claims_s0513_r43.rs`. Ledger: [m8-claims/intake60-S-0513.md](m8-claims/intake60-S-0513.md).

### A projectile's firer, T1's first row and the switched-off hover lead (M8 step A3 intake 61; S-0306 r53 and r54, S-0607 r37; Q-2157, Q-2158 and Q-2160 answered)

`combat::projectile::has_firer` is S-0306's *firer* (Terms): the projectile still carries its attacker (a kill clears it) and the firer object still exists, so a firer gone inside a transport is still the firer and a removed or killed one is not (a firer that started to cloak is still counted, a seam). T1's first row (`turn`) detonates a projectile that has a firer only at the deck crossings while `quirks.owned_shell_ground_pass` is on (the default); below the terrain, the building band (any house's building, an open gate included, not a laser-fence section or a vehicle-like building; the point at the terrain height) and the wall overlays (only with no building in the cell) apply only to one with no firer. Off, the whole row applies to every projectile, except that the firer and buildings allied with its house do not stop one with a firer. A shell with a firer that passes below the ground flies on to another row (in open ground the deep-fall row). The proximity row's firer exclusion reads `has_firer` too. `projectile::led` decides claim 3's lead: a hovering target is led while S-0503 counts it as moving (`hover::moving`: a destination or a heading point), switched off or not (`quirks.switched_off_hover_lead`; off, a switched-off hover is not led, and the play-fixes layer sets it off). The lead's speed estimate takes a hover's speed fraction from the hover state (`hover::speed_fraction`, S-0503 claim 4) rather than the drive state, which a hover does not use: before, a hover's *u* was 0 and no hover was led. S-0306 r54's claim 17 (a warp departure keeps the projectile's target) is S-0513 claim 39's, built at intake 60. S-0607 r37 (claims 3 and 20, scenario L19) was already as built. Tests: `claims_s0306.rs` (T1 row 1, P32 f, i and j, P44 b and c, claims 3 b and 6 a), `claims_s0607_r37.rs`. Ledger: [m8-claims/intake61-S-0607-S-0306.md](m8-claims/intake61-S-0607-S-0306.md).

### A projectile aimed at a departed warp member (M8 step A3 intake 64; S-0306 r57; Q-2212 answered)

S-0306 r57 claim 17: a projectile keeps a superweapon warp member as its target object from its departure until it appears (`superweapons::warp::departed`: stage `Blocked` or `Away`, off the map at its kept position). The turn-start drop of an off-map target object in `combat::projectile::turn` skips it, so the steering, T1's steering-distance row and claim 20's detonation point read the departure position; the area damage and T1's proximity row find objects through the cells and so miss it. From its first turn after the member appears, a steering projectile turns to the landing point; the phase-in does not stop a hit. `projectile::target_airborne` answers claim 17's airborne question (T1's anti-air row, claim 20 (c), the loss in `target_leaves` and `death::mark_killed`): any other object keeps S-0307's airborne; a departed member counts by its height above the terrain beneath its kept position (`quirks.warp_deck_departure_airborne` on, the default) or above the deck it departed from (off), and is on a deck while a passed landing check for a deck cell waits (`warp::passed_for_deck`). So a landing-check kill gives the departure cell, or nothing for a member counted as airborne (a steering shot then climbs). `target_leaves` acts for a departed member too. Interims, both answered by S-0306 r62 (intake 65, next section): **Q-2231** (`projectile::effect_target`: no special hit of S-0514 claim 20 and no disguise acts on a departed member, the selected row still replaces damage; claim 20 (c)'s render layer is the kept state's), **Q-2232** (a refused placement after a passed deck check counts by height again). The switch is not a play fix (C7 ledger). No state is added: save format 91, AI game format 53. Tests: `claims_s0306_r57.rs`, `claims_s0513_r43.rs`. Ledger: [m8-claims/intake64-S-0306.md](m8-claims/intake64-S-0306.md).

### Special hits on a warp member and its render layer (M8 step A3 intake 65; S-0306 r62; Q-2216, Q-2231 and Q-2232 answered)

S-0306 r62 claim 17: the special rows of S-0514 claim 20 and S-0516 claim 31's disguise act on the projectile's target object, so a superweapon warp member receives them off the map and while phasing in, each by its own claim's tests; intake 64's `projectile::effect_target` interim is gone. Two engine-design rules, no switch: `lift::seize_hit` does nothing to a member from its departure until its arrival hold ends (`warp::phasing`), after the firer's old grip ends; and the deck count after a refused placement stays as intake 64 built it (`warp::passed_for_deck`: only while a passed deck check awaits placement). `warp::in_ground_layer` puts a member in the ground render layer while its member state exists (launch to hold end); claim 20 (c)'s threshold and S-0514 claim 1's test in `temporal::deliver` read it, while `temporal::ground_layer` and S-0607's seize test do not. Claim 3 (b) and T1's flak row needed no code (intake 61 built them); new tests cover the held fraction 0, the arrival that clears a switched-off hover's heading point, and P45 part b. Interim of the spec's Open question: an erasure lock on a member off the map waits for the hold, since S-0513 claim 51 makes the warp update its whole turn. No new quirk and no state: save format 92, AI game format 54. Tests: `claims_s0306_r62.rs`, `claims_s0306.rs`. Ledger: [m8-claims/intake65-S-0306.md](m8-claims/intake65-S-0306.md).

### Capture and sabotage processing (M8 seam; S-0215 r91 claim 28; Q-2251 and Q-2252 filed)

`process::process_capture` is claim 28's table, chosen by the destination and target at the start of the processing. With no destination, a building taker (`process::building_taker`: charges, an engineer, an occupier, an assaulter or an infiltrator) whose target is a building takes it as its destination. Any other actor falls back to rest (claims 14 and 15) and, when it then stands on a cell holding a building, is sent a scatter request with no threat point, forced and not move-anywhere (S-0208 claims 11 and 13 for infantry, S-0207 claim 16 for a vehicle). With a destination nothing changes: the hunt rule never applies in our engine (the claim's own divergence, not a quirk). The delay is always the standard delay, where before the behaviour fell through to "not implemented yet" with 450 ticks. *Sabotage* uses the same processing (claim 5, T1) for infantry, vehicles and aircraft (`air::process`). *Eaten* (`structures::grinder::process_eaten`) calls it with the narrower building-taker test (charges and engineers only). A building in any of the three has no processing rule (claim 4). An aircraft is sent the scatter request with no effect until **Q-2251** answers. Separately, the pad-bound route game of S-0213 O8 x (6) leaves its unarmed aircraft hovering under *move* above its F8 spot next to the pad after P2, as S-0501 claim 6 and T2 give it. That is pinned for **Q-2252**. No state is added: save format 92, AI game format 54. The `duel-m6c` golden moved. Tests: `claims_s0215_capture.rs`, `claims_s0213_r89.rs`. Ledger: [m8-claims/seam-S-0215-claim28.md](m8-claims/seam-S-0215-claim28.md).

## Arena mode (M7b)

PLAN.md §5 (M7 exit: "Arena win rates within tolerance of the stock oracle") and §6 layer 2B (unit-arena win rates and time to kill across seeds, two-proportion and KS tests). Code: `crates/tools/src/oracle.rs` (the oracle arena: the lead's records M-0001 to M-0003, their setup and the comparison), `crates/tools/src/arena.rs` (the synthetic arena on our own flat map, with its own record form), `crates/tools/src/stats.rs` (the statistics), `crates/tools/tests/arena.rs`. The arena is tooling: it touches no simulation state, so the save format and goldens are unchanged.

### The oracle arena (step 4c, second part)

**The records.** An oracle arena record is a measurement record `measurements/<topic>/M-xxxx-*.md` with a CSV of the same stem beside it whose header is exactly `row,col,row_side,seed,winner,end_frame` (one line per game: row and column type, the row's side `A` or `B`, seed 0 to 5, winner `row`/`col`/`none`, the sample frame at which the game was over; the records give no army shares since their revision of 2026-10-29). The Setup's `- Map:` line names the map in back quotes and gives its `sha256`. `oracle::load_records` reads every such pair; `arena_records_parse` (CI) checks that each parses and has 6 seeds per side per pairing, and fails on a record CSV with any other header. Today: M-0001 (Yuri vehicles, 96 pairings), M-0002 (Yuri infantry, 24), M-0003 (Allied and Soviet vehicles, 35); 1860 games in all.

**M-0017 (M8 step A3).** The comparison leaves out the 7 MIND against E2 games of M-0001 that crashed in the stock game (`oracle::crashed`: winner `none` with an end frame short of the limit sample; our games of the same side and seed are left out too), so that pairing is judged on its 5 side-B games, n = 5, as the record's Tolerance says. The report also splits its totals into pairings whose stock rows M-0017 confirms and the SREF and V3 pairings it does not cover (`oracle::UNCONFIRMED_TYPES`); since intake 47 those are shown, not scored. Ledger: [m8-claims/intake-M-0017.md](m8-claims/intake-M-0017.md).

**The setup we play (measure: M-0001 to M-0003, Setup and Procedure).** The record's map, read from `GLASNOST_GAME_DIR` and checked against its sha256 (never committed), with its rules layer; W = start waypoint 2. Side A plays the first country at start 0, side B the second at start 1, skirmish setup with no bases, superweapons and crates off, game speed fastest (index 0). Per side round(9000 / cost) units (an exact half to even, as the CSV denominators showed before the records' revision; kept while Q-2099 is open), at least 1, infantry at most 60, unit *i* on (W.x ∓ 6 ∓ ⌊i/8⌋, W.y − 4 + (i mod 8)), A facing east, B west; every unit starts in the hunt behaviour and no order is given. Samples every 304 frames; the game is over at the first sample where at most one side owns anything, or at the first sample at or after frame 6000. A side's share (the old CSVs' last two columns, gone since the revision) was the summed cost of what its house owns at the end over its group's cost at the start. The houses are test-only computer-controlled player houses (`human = false`; glossary revision 58, S-0215 revision 69 Terms, S-0309 revision 23 *Test-only exception*): the records' houses are hard computer players, so ours take the original's computer-owner rules everywhere the neutral and special houses do, with the oracle house's skill level (the rules' maximum, `[IQ] MaxIQLevels`, S-0412 claim 3) and difficulty index 0, and no teams; hard also gives the easy row through the handicap (S-0412 claims 4 and 5), and as computer players they stop the unused-house cleanup (S-0009 claim 28). No brain of ours is attached.

**Setup differences** (what we cannot or do not reproduce; each can move a result):

- The original's skirmish computer players were in the stock games; in ours no AI runs (our own AI takes no part, D14), and any effect the stock computer players had on the hunting units is absent.
- No observer slot and no tech-level setting: our skirmish setup has neither, and nothing in the arena reads the tech level.
- `--ai-houses` plays the houses as human-controlled AI houses of our engine instead (the earlier default); it changed which pairings pass (below) and is kept for comparison.
- Side A's group is created first, so it is first in the update order (S-0203 claim 3); the records do not say in which order the groups were placed.
- A unit its stated cell does not take (a unit there, or an entry outcome other than *clear*) stands on the first cell around it that does, ring by ring in S-0212 claim 25's scan order (`oracle::placed_cell`; our design, Q-2096). Since intake 52b a unit stated on a cell of M-0006's placement table stands on the cell it measured, even where a unit already stands (`Ground::placed`, read from the record by `oracle::load_placements`). Infantry stand at the cell's centre standing point, with no standing-position draw, and every unit starts with *hunt* forced: A1's interim (Q-1711 question 5, Q-2097). The facings agree with S-0216 claim 1.
- Our end read: when the game finishes, the state is read at the next multiple of 304 frames, as the procedure samples.
- Rocket missiles in flight (a launcher's spawned missiles) are not counted as owned objects (`oracle::owned`); counting them gave launcher shares above 1 that the stock CSV does not show.

**Running it** (local lane, release build; run from the repository root):

```sh
export GLASNOST_GAME_DIR=<install>
cargo build --release -p glasnost-tools --bin glasnost-headless
target/release/glasnost-headless arena --record M-0001 --threads 8   # about 6 min (1152 games)
target/release/glasnost-headless arena --record M-0002 --threads 8   # about 1.3 min (288 games)
target/release/glasnost-headless arena --record M-0003 --threads 8   # about 45 s (420 games)
target/release/glasnost-headless arena --record all --threads 8      # all three, about 8 min
# Options: --pairing ROW,COL (repeatable; replays only these and merges them into the stored CSV), --out DIR (default local-lane/m7b/arena),
#          --compare-only (compare the stored ours-<ID>.csv without playing), --groups (list each type's kind, group size and cost),
#          --ai-houses, --watch ROW,COL,A|B,SEED (play one game and print per-sample counts and every unit's final state),
#          --spread ROW,COL,A|B (play the side's six seeds and print, per seed, the first tick and the state that differs from seed 0's),
#          --trace ROW,COL,A|B,SEED (one game, every unit's changes tick by tick: berserk time, target, shots, how it leaves play), --trace-summary (its per-unit totals only),
#          --seed-range ROW,COL,A|B,FIRST-LAST (play the side over more seeds than the records' 0 to 5 and print each winner and the counts: a noise check of ours, M8 A6).
cargo test --release -p glasnost-tools --test arena arena_matches_the_oracle -- --nocapture   # the same as a test; GLASNOST_ARENA_RECORD=M-0003 picks one record
```

Outputs (git-ignored, never committed): `local-lane/m7b/arena/ours-<ID>.csv` (our games, in the record's CSV form) and `oracle-<ID>.txt` (the table, the summary, the failing pairings largest discrepancy first, and every game of ours). Without `GLASNOST_GAME_DIR` or without records, `arena_matches_the_oracle` prints why it is skipped and passes.

**The comparison** (each record's *Tolerance for tests*; since [intake 47](m8-claims/intake47-arena-restated.md) only the stock pairings, no SREF or V3, are scored; since [intake 52b](m8-claims/intake52b-S-0309-S-0405-M-0005-M-0006-M-0016.md) the SREF and V3 pairings are scored too, their changed rows replaced by M-0016's stock rows (`oracle::load_stock_replays`), so every pairing is scored: 96, 24 and 35). Per scored pairing, pooled over both sides (n = 12): the row's win proportion with a two-proportion z test, failing at p < 0.01 (`oracle::ALPHA`), and, for every side on which the stock games show the same winner for all six seeds, whether ours do too; a pairing passes when both hold. (Until intake 47 the share KS tests below also counted.) `oracle_records_are_compared` (CI) runs the path on the synthetic ground with a temporary record.

**Results** (release, computer houses, after M7b step 1c with its second batch; `local-lane/m7b/arena-1c/`, the first batch's in `arena-1c-first-batch/`; the rerun after the difficulty-indexed auto-deploy delay, the test house's area guard and full refund wrote byte-identical CSVs to the run before it, kept in `arena-1c-second-batch/`):

| Record | Pairings | Fail win test | Fail row-left KS | Fail column-left KS | Pairings failing any test | Side-unanimous stock outcomes reproduced |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| M-0001 | 96 | 6 | 26 | 16 | 35 | 145 of 172 |
| M-0002 | 24 | 4 | 9 | 3 | 16 | 43 of 47 |
| M-0003 | 35 | 1 | 3 | 2 | 4 | 59 of 64 |

Before step 1c (step 4c part 2): 37 of 96, 17 of 24 and 6 of 35 failing; 146, 43 and 55 unanimous outcomes reproduced. Step 1c (S-0405 revision 20, S-0502 revision 18, S-0309 revision 21 and the other revisions of [step1c-m7b.md](m7-claims/step1c-m7b.md); nothing tuned to the records) made four pairings pass and none fail anew: M-0001 YTNK against ROBO, M-0002 BRUTE against E1, M-0003 ROBO against TTNK and ROBO against HTK (both now won by the column side in all 12 games, as in the stock games; ROBO is a powered unit and the arena houses have no control centre, so S-0405 revision 20's disabling on a cell arrival is the likely cause, not traced game by game). LTNK against ROBO still fails the win test (stock 5 of 12 row wins, ours 12). Those counts were 36, 16 and 4.

The second batch of step 1c (the test-only computer-controlled player houses of glossary revision 58, S-0215 revision 69 and S-0309 revision 23, with the oracle house's maximum skill level and difficulty index 0; S-0301 revision 26 and the rest of [step1c-m7b.md](m7-claims/step1c-m7b.md); the S-0611 merge) gives the table above: M-0001 LTNK against E1 now passes (its column-left KS test; our E1 share left now varies with the seed, 0.56 to 0.98, where it was 0.89 to 0.98), and TELE against MTNK still passes but one of our 12 games (side A, seed 1) now ends `none` at frame 6080, so one side-unanimous stock outcome is no longer reproduced (146 to 145). M-0002 and M-0003 do not move. The skill level is the likely cause for E1 (S-0209 claim 11's scatter threshold now holds for the arena houses), not traced.

Before the fixes below (and with human-controlled houses) the counts were 37, 13 and 9 failing pairings, and 142, 41 and 51 unanimous outcomes reproduced. What moved, by pairing: M-0001 CAOS against MGTK and DISK against HTK now pass, MIND against MTNK and SREF now fail on the row-left KS test (winners unchanged); M-0002 BRUTE and YURI against E1 and YURI against MTNK now fail on a share KS test (winners unchanged), and INIT against E2 now fails the win test (ours `none` in all 12, side A on the cliff cells of Q-1502); M-0003 MGTK against HTNK, HTK and V3 now pass. The last step (S-0309 claim 7, S-0306 claim 20 c) alone took M-0001 from 36 to 37 failing: DISK against HTK passes, DISK against FV and TELE against MGTK fail. Running the three records takes about 8 minutes on 8 threads. Our games vary much less with the seed than the stock ones (often identical for all six seeds), so many share KS tests fail although the winner and the mean share agree.

**Fixed against approved specs** (with synthetic tests): a jumpjet between lift-off and landing is a tracked flying object (S-0303 Terms, S-0502 claim 29 since revision 18, which answered Q-1500), so detonations above the terrain hit it; a jumpjet is in the air for weapon choice by the height test (S-0502 claim 30, answering Q-1501); a range or area search for aircraft first scans the tracked flying objects (S-0309 claim 9, table C row "class"); the map-wide search's second pass takes only objects of the ground render layer, so it never finds a hovering jumpjet (S-0309 claim 7); a projectile's claim 20 c threshold is 128 for a target airborne in another render layer (S-0306 claim 20 c). Tests in `claims_s0303_jumpjet.rs` and `claims_s0306.rs`. Hunting units run only map-wide searches, so the claim 9 change alone moved no arena count; the claim 7 and claim 20 c changes turned DISK against HTK into a pass, and DISK against FV and TELE against MGTK into failures (below). The computer houses fixed a group that could not approach (S-0215 claim 37's keep table keeps a hunt target for a computer house).

**Open discrepancies** (largest first; type IDs as in the committed records):

- *Units on impassable cliff cells*: five placement cells of side A are rock cliffs of land type 3 on `Arena.mmx`; infantry there belong to no reach region and never move, so infantry pairings on side A end `none` or lose (YTNK/MIND/INIT/VIRUS against E1 or E2). The lead's note f81fc29 confirms that the stock harness placed units there too. S-0309 revision 21 (Q-1502 answered, step 1c) makes two no-region cells one region and keeps every other search region test, which is built; these pairings did not move (INIT against E2 still `none` in all 12 of ours). What still differs is not explained by an approved spec; no request yet. S-0208 revision 34 claim 24 (step 1d) confirms it in part: a scatter destination gives such a walker no escape (`claim_24_scatter_is_no_escape`).
- *Hunting ground units against hovering jumpjets*: S-0309 revision 21 (Q-1504 answered) confirms the built rule (a hunting unit's map-wide search never finds a hovering jumpjet; retaliation needs the hunt behaviour's retaliate property). DISK against FV still fails (stock FV 12 of 12, ours 0 of 12). Not yet explained; no request yet.
- *Steering anti-air projectiles* (DISK against APOC, MGTK against APOC): the APOC's anti-air missiles detonate next to the launcher right after launch. Not yet explained (S-0306 steering rows); no request yet.
- *Mind control* (MIND against MGTK and the MIND shares, ours lower): our MIND capture fewer units and die. Not yet explained (S-0601, S-0610).
- *Magnetron* (TELE pairings): our TELE seize targets that then neither lift nor take damage while the beam keeps firing (TELE against MGTK now ends `none` in all 12 games: in a watched game the MGTK end in guard at full health, never firing back, while the TELE fire about 2000 shots at one of them; why the ground-layer fixes changed this pairing is not verified); V3 shares end slightly above 1 against TELE. Not yet explained (S-0607). M8 A3 intake 40 (M-0015, [ledger](m8-claims/intake40-M-0015.md)): against BFRT each seized unit was dropped, set down unhurt and then stayed seized for good, because in *hunt* it never fell back to rest; Q-2035. Intake 45 (S-0607 r14, [ledger](m8-claims/intake45-S-0607.md)) answers it: the restoration test runs at the end of each turn, so a set-down fortress is restored at touchdown and hunts again. With it, TELE against MGTK, HTNK, TTNK, TNKD and SREF ends as the stock does, and against BFRT and APOC the outcomes come close to the stock's (M-0001 failing pairings 30 to 25 of 96). TELE against V3 got worse: the restored V3s are seized again and again, so most games reach the tick limit, where the stock's V3s win; not yet explained. Intake 49 (S-0607 r18, [ledger](m8-claims/intake49-S-0607.md), answering Q-2113): a lifter takes no approach step against its gripped unit, so its grip no longer ends with a destination of claim 12 row 2 but at the unit's arrival (claim 8). TELE against BFRT now ends `none` as the stock's do (seed range 60 of 64) and its M-0015 hold counts come close to the stock's; TELE against APOC still fails by one seed (within noise); TELE against V3 is 0/6/6 against the stock's col 12. Intake 52a (S-0215 r88 claim 37 (c), [ledger](m8-claims/intake52a-S-0210-S-0215-S-0216.md)): a seized unit whose target is its lifter takes the approach step too; no TELE pairing moved.
- *CAOS pairings* (ours too strong: it wins or survives more than in the stock games against SREF, BFRT, E1, TTNK and HTNK): not yet explained. Against BFRT, intake 52a ([ledger](m8-claims/intake52a-S-0210-S-0215-S-0216.md)) builds Q-2005's answer, the crush response of S-0215 claim 39: the berserk fortresses now drive over the drones that gas them, and ours end 0/8/4 against the stock's 0/7/5 (before: 1/0/11).
- The drain of S-0608 (the `draining()` seam in `combat/approach.rs` on this branch) landed on main in step 3.3, not on this branch.
- MIND against E2: the stock CSV shows `none` before the frame limit, which the procedure does not explain; noted for the lead.

### Spread audit (M8 step A1)

[m8-claims/a1-arena-spread.md](m8-claims/a1-arena-spread.md) holds the audit: where the arena's seed goes (only the scenario stream of S-0202 draws in play, and it is seeded from `Setup::seed`; the map stream is 0 by claim 13, the lobby stream is unused), the table of the 72 draw sites an arena game reaches with their status, the failing pairings ranked by how far our seed-to-seed spread falls short of the stock games', and the requests Q-1710 to Q-1714. `glasnost-headless arena --spread ROW,COL,A|B` (`oracle::spread`) shows, per seed, the first tick at which a game differs from seed 0's: tick 1 or 2 in the ROBO and infantry pairings, tick 30 to 90 in the vehicle pairings. The seed reaches play; the outcomes often do not follow, because in most of the worst pairings one side wins (or the game stalls) so clearly that the draws have nothing to vary.

Built: S-0309 claim 25 (g), the computer-owner houses' draw at a blinking disguise (`acquire::admit`; it was the seam `disguise::neutral_sees_through`), and S-0107 claim 17's corpse pick and row 3 (the importer now reads `DeadBodies` and `DeathAnims`; `combat::death::corpse`, the seam `death_anims_row` is gone; no-type entries by the interim rule of Q-1715 until S-0107 revision 22 answered it at M8 step A3 intake 5: the importer leaves no-type and empty items out of both lists). Left as seams: the animation playback of S-0109 (claims 11, 14 with μ > 0, 15, 17, 28, 29; `load::anim_created` now notes when an animation with those parts is created, which happens in nearly every pairing) and the smudges of S-0312 claim 26.

Arena after both builds: failing pairings 34 of 96, 16 of 24 and 3 of 35 (were 35, 16 and 4); side-unanimous stock outcomes reproduced 148 of 172, 43 of 47 and 61 of 64 (were 145, 43 and 59). Claim 25 (g) moved only MGTK pairings (M-0003 MGTK against APOC and M-0001 YTNK against MGTK now pass); the corpse pick changed 37 games of M-0001 and 37 of M-0002. The sim-gate goldens and the seed sweeps 1 to 16 are unchanged. The results table above is from before this step.

### Failing-pairing triage (M8 step A6)

[m8-claims/a6-arena-triage.md](m8-claims/a6-arena-triage.md) classifies the 17 scored pairings that fail under intake 47's scoring: none is a departure of ours from an approved claim, 5 rest on rules the specs leave open (DISK against APOC: Q-2111, a steering missile fired from a muzzle at the surface; DISK against FV: Q-2112, ground hunters and hovering jumpjets; TELE against BFRT: Q-2113, a lifter whose gripped unit comes inside its minimum range; LTNK against ROBO and ROBO against APOC: Q-1710), and 12 are within noise. Q-2114 (the targets of a crushed unit) came up on the way. Interim rule for all of them: the approved rules as built; nothing in the simulation changed. Q-2113 is answered by S-0607 r18 (intake 49) and S-0215 r88 claim 37 (c) (intake 52a, [ledger](m8-claims/intake52a-S-0210-S-0215-S-0216.md)).

The noise test is ours. `glasnost-headless arena --record ID --seed-range ROW,COL,A|B,FIRST-LAST` (`oracle::seed_range`) plays one side over more seeds than the records' 0 to 5 and prints each game and the counts. A side-outcome failure counts as within noise when our share *p* of the stock's 6-of-6 outcome over seeds 0 to 63 gives *p*⁶ ≥ 0.01, the records' α.

### The synthetic arena

**The scenario.** Two groups, A and B, each a list of type IDs and counts (infantry or vehicles), meet on our own flat map (`testkit::empty_map`: clear, flat cells, no objects, no EA bytes). The houses are hostile player houses in a sandbox setup (no start positions, starting units or bases; not AI houses), of the scenario's countries, else both of the rules' first country that is not passive. Each group stands in rows of 5 cells across the map's x axis, one unit per cell; the middle cells of the two front rows are `start-distance` cells apart, centred on the map's middle cell; further rows stand one cell further back; every unit is placed by S-0216's play-time placement (`testkit::spawn_in_play`, since M8 step A3 intake 21): it faces the nearest facing value of the direction to the enemy front cell, an infantry unit stands at the position its draw gives, and a unit rests in its resting behaviour until the order tick; a refused placement is an error of the scenario. **Alternation:** in a trial with an odd seed group A is house 0, stands on the lower-x side and is created and ordered first; a trial with an even seed is swapped, group B taking all four (`arena::swapped`). Being created first puts a group first in the update order (S-0203 claim 3), so it takes its turns first; the two sides differ in their geometry (with an odd start distance the fronts are not equally far from the middle, with an even one the middle cell is crossed differently from the two sides). Unalternated, these favoured one group of a mirror matchup by up to 100 % (see the findings below); alternating balances both over an even number of trials, starting unswapped with the default first seed 1. In the first tick in which orders are taken, every unit is ordered to attack-move to the enemy front cell through `orders::issue` (an order a person could give); every 60 ticks after that, each idle unit (guard or area guard with no target and no destination) is ordered to attack-move to the cell of the nearest enemy unit. A unit *of a group* is one of the objects created for it that still exists with health above 0 and is still owned by its house (a unit under mind control counts for no group; a lifted or carried one still counts). A trial ends after the first tick at whose end a group has no units (the other wins; neither: a draw), or as a timeout `tick-cap` ticks after the order tick. **Time to kill** = ticks from the order tick (the first) to the end tick, both included. **Survivors** = units of each group at the end, and their summed health in per-mille of the group's summed health at creation. Seeds are the simulation seed: trial *i* uses seed `first-seed + i`. Row width, the re-order period and the defaults (map 32, distance 10, cap 6000 ticks, 32 seeds, the rules' lobby game speed) are our tool's choices.

**Running it.**

```sh
# Synthetic ruleset (the default; the synthetic combat types CART, CRUSHER, BUGGY, WALKER):
cargo run --release -p glasnost-tools --bin glasnost-headless -- arena --seeds 32 --ticks 6000 "A:CART*2" "B:WALKER*4"
# Options: --first-seed K, --size S, --distance D, --speed G, --countries X,Y, --synthetic (ignore GLASNOST_GAME_DIR),
#          --watch (shots per group, the firing check's answers per group and slot, and every unit's final state in the first trial), --types (list the type IDs a matchup can name).
# Reference ruleset of a real install (local only; also writes the git-ignored local-lane/arena.txt):
GLASNOST_GAME_DIR=<install> cargo run --release -p glasnost-tools --bin glasnost-headless -- arena --types
GLASNOST_GAME_DIR=<install> cargo run --release -p glasnost-tools --bin glasnost-headless -- arena --seeds 32 "A:<type>*5" "B:<type>*5"
# Every fenced-block arena record's matchup (the alternative format below; none exists today):
GLASNOST_GAME_DIR=<install> cargo run --release -p glasnost-tools --bin glasnost-headless -- arena --records [--seeds N]
```

The reference ruleset is the local lane's import (the game's search order, rules and art, temperate theater, no map layer), resolved; a stock map is never loaded, so no map's rules layer changes the matchup. The import's default game mode is skirmish, so the skirmish game-mode layer is applied: the arena's rules are a skirmish's without the map layer. Each matchup prints N, the wins of A and B in per cent, draws and timeouts, the mean, standard deviation, minimum and maximum of the time to kill over the decided trials, the survivors of each group (mean units and mean health in per-mille, with their standard deviations), and every trial as a record trial line with its seed. 32 trials of a 5-against-5 matchup take about 3 s (release). Which matchups are available: any pair of infantry or vehicle type IDs of the loaded rules (`arena --types` lists them with kind, tech level and owning sides; on the reference ruleset that list is local output only). Matchups run in M7b on the reference ruleset (Yuri infantry and vehicles against Allied and Soviet ones, N = 32) are kept in `local-lane/m7b/arena/`, git-ignored; type IDs of the stock rules are not written in this file.

**Arena measurement records (alternative format).** No record uses this form today (the lead's records use the CSV form above); it is kept, documented, for small matchups on the synthetic arena's flat map. `arena --records` reads every record `measurements/<topic>/M-xxxx-*.md` (see `measurements/TEMPLATE.md`) that holds a fenced block with the info string `arena`. Each block is one matchup, one `key: value` per line (`#` starts a comment):

| Key | Required | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `group-a`, `group-b` | yes | The groups: `TYPE*n, TYPE*n` (type ID and count; count 1 when `*n` is left out) |
| `country-a`, `country-b` | no | The houses' countries (default: the rules' first country that is not passive, for both) |
| `map-size` | no | The map is `map-size` × `map-size` cells (default 32) |
| `start-distance` | no | Cells between the two front cells (default 10) |
| `tick-cap` | no | Ticks from the order tick before a trial is a timeout (default 6000) |
| `game-speed` | no | The lobby game speed, 0 to 6 (default: the rules' lobby speed) |
| `n` | yes | Number of trials; must equal the number of `trial` lines |
| `wins-a`, `wins-b`, `draws`, `timeouts` | no | Cross-checks: must match the trial lines when given |
| `trial` | n lines | `<winner> <ticks> <survivors-a> <survivors-b> [<health-a> <health-b>]`: winner `A`, `B`, `draw` or `timeout`; time to kill in ticks (for a timeout, the cap); units of each group left; optionally their health in per-mille of the group's full health. Integers only. |

Example (synthetic types, invented numbers):

````text
```arena
group-a: CART*2
group-b: WALKER*3
map-size: 24
start-distance: 8
tick-cap: 3000
game-speed: 4
n: 3
trial: A 500 1 0 300 0
trial: A 640 2 0 700 0
trial: timeout 3000 1 1
```
````

The record's procedure must say how the stock game was set up to match this scenario (the same groups, placement, map and orders), including the game mode (the arena applies the skirmish game-mode layer) and the alternation: the trial lines in order, the 1st, 3rd, … with group A as the first house, on the lower-x side and created first, the 2nd, 4th, … with group B so; an even N. Per-trial lines are needed: the KS test compares the distributions of the times to kill, which a mean and a spread cannot give. When the front matter says `value-visibility: local`, the outcome keys (`n`, the win counts, `trial`) may come instead from the git-ignored `measurements/local/<id>.json`: `{"arena": [ {"n": 32, "wins-a": 20, "trial": ["A 812 3 0", …]}, … ]}`, one object per `arena` block in order, with the same keys (integers or strings; `trial` an array of trial-line strings); a key may be in the record or in the local file, not in both. Win rates under stated settings are not rules data, so arena records will usually be `committed`.

**The comparison of the alternative format.** `arena --records` runs each fenced-block record's matchup on the reference ruleset over the record's N seeds (`--seeds N` overrides), and tests, at α = 0.01 per test (`arena::ALPHA`, our test design choice): each group's win rate with a two-proportion z test (pooled variance; equal rates with a pooled variance of 0 give p = 1), and the times to kill of the decided trials with a two-sample Kolmogorov–Smirnov test (ties handled; asymptotic p-value with Stephens' correction, λ = (√nₑ + 0.12 + 0.11/√nₑ)·D; no comparison when neither side has a decided trial, a failure when only one has). It prints, per matchup, both rates, the p-values and pass or fail. `arena_records_parse` (in CI) also checks that any record in this form parses. The statistics are unit-tested against hand computations in `stats.rs`; `records_are_run_and_compared` runs the whole record path on the synthetic ruleset over invented records in a temporary folder.

**Findings of the first reference runs (M7b step 4c, N = 32).** Outcomes vary with the seed (standard deviations of the time to kill of 9 to 69 ticks), so the KS test has material. The first runs found two engine defects and one arena defect, all fixed in step 4c. (1) **Infantry whose fire frame equals its firing sequence's frame count never fired** (two stock infantry types, even one against one: every trial a timeout). The tick ran the completion follow-up of S-0107 claim 8 before the firing rules of claim 11, so the stage never equalled the fire frame; S-0107 *State and ordering* puts the firing rules before the completion follow-up ("judged against the sequence that is current before that tick's completion follow-up"; "When the fire frame equals *C*, the shot is still released, on the tick the sequence completes"), and the follow-up before the movement rule. The infantry turn is now fear rules (`infantry::fear_turn`), firing (`behave::infantry_step`), then completion and movement (`infantry::sequence_turn`); tests `s0107_fire_frame_equal_to_the_count_still_fires` and `s0107_attack_state_ends_the_tick_after_completion` (`claims_s0301.rs`; both fail with the old order). The determinism goldens did not change. (2) **A weapon whose projectile is named in another letter case had no projectile**, so its shots did no damage (one stock vehicle type: 38 shots, the target group at 1000 ‰), and against a heavy tank it took no target at all (0 shots; with the fix it fires). The importer copied a weapon's `Projectile` and `Warhead` as written; S-0004 claims 2 and 13 make them type references that find the existing type without regard to letter case. They are now references, written as the named type's key (test `weapon_references_ignore_letter_case`, `crates/convert`). A local check of the reference import found no case mismatch in the other name-only fields that the simulation looks up by exact name (`DeploysInto`, `UndeploysInto`). (3) **Mirror matchups were not even** before the alternation (above): synthetic, N = 512, three carts a side, B won 74 %; reference, N = 256, five of one infantry type a side B 55.9 % and three of one tank type a side B 82.4 %. Swapping only the sides and swapping only the creation order gave exactly mirrored counts (226 to 30 out of 256 for the carts), so the house order plays no part; the place and the creation order do, in a direction that depends on the type (one walker a side favoured the lower-x side). With an odd start distance the fronts are not equally far from the middle and the farther side won every trial (carts, distance 9). Being created first means taking turns first (S-0203 claim 3), which is game behaviour; the geometry is the arena's. With the alternation, B's rate at N = 256: carts 50.8 %, five walkers 52.3 %, three crushers 46.1 %, five crushers 51.6 %; reference, five infantry 49.2 %, three tanks 54.3 %, five of a second infantry type 45.7 % (all within 1.4 standard errors of 50 %). The results with the fixes, and the outputs from before them, are in the git-ignored `local-lane/m7b/arena/` (`summary.txt`, `before-fixes/`).

## Multiplayer (M9)

M9 adds multiplayer by lockstep: every peer runs the whole game from the same start values, and the peers exchange only the persons' orders, their delay proposals and state hashes. Rules data and game state never cross the network. Our AI plays the computer slots inside the tick on every peer, so a computer slot's orders never cross the network either. The protocol, the frame rule, the tick rule and the delay rule below are our own netcode design; no spec governs them, and their constants are marked `// nocite: our own netcode`. Nothing in the game rules changes, so the step needs no spec request. Plan: [m9-plan.md](m9-plan.md).

### The lockstep core (N1)

The crate `glasnost-net` holds the protocol and one peer's lockstep session. It does no I/O: it reads no clock, opens no socket and starts no thread. The caller passes its clock in milliseconds into every call and moves the bytes. `crates/net/clippy.toml` forbids clocks, threads, locks, sockets, files and hashed containers in the crate, and `xtask deps` forbids it every socket or async runtime crate (the list `FORBIDDEN_EXTERNAL`, which now also names the WebSocket, WebRTC and HTTP crates). The simulation crates' determinism lint now also forbids `std::net::TcpListener`, with a canary.

**Wire format** (`wire.rs`). A message is a 4-byte header, then a postcard body. The header holds the magic `GN`, the protocol version (`WIRE_VERSION`, now 5: N2 reserved the sender id 255, `wire::RELAY`, for the relay, N3 added chat lines to the lobby messages that share the version, N4 added the desync report's digest to the frames message, and N5 added `Body::Serve`, `Body::Resume`, `Frame.resume`, the grace period in the start values, and the lobby's `Rejoin`, `Snapshot`, `CaughtUp`, `Rejoined`, `Begin.ticket` and new refusals) and the sender's slot. A receiver reports a message with another version as a refusal on the field *protocol*. Trailing bytes, a short header or a body that does not decode are reported as `Event::BadMessage`, and never cause a panic (`hostile_bytes_never_panic`). The body is one of three kinds:

- `Start`: the sender's start values.
- `Frames`: its frames not yet acknowledged by every live peer, and its checkpoint hashes not yet acknowledged. The message also carries the ticks it has run, its acknowledgements of every other slot's frames and hashes (how many it holds from 0 without a gap), its time stamp, an echo of every other live peer's latest stamp with the time it held that stamp (since N7 only a live peer's: a dropped slot's stamps belong to a process that may be gone), and, once it has written one, its desync report's tick and digest (N4).
- `Dropped { slot, kept }`: the relay's report that a slot left (N2). A session acts on it only from the sender `RELAY`; from any other sender it is a bad message, as is any other body from `RELAY`.

Each message carries the whole unacknowledged window, so a lost message costs nothing once a later one arrives. A receiver keeps the first copy of each (slot, frame) and ignores duplicates. It also ignores frames beyond the window: two largest delays past its own tick.

**Session start** (`start.rs`). The start values are the build id, the rules hash, the map hash, the seed, the slot table, the hash of the whole `GameSetup`, and the lockstep parameters: the initial, smallest and largest delay, and the checkpoint period.
